# Audit ErgoptiPlus (AHK) — Bugs, races & frappes perdues

> **Date** : 2026-06-14 · **Périmètre** : driver Windows AutoHotkey v2 sous `static/ergopti_plus/windows/`. · **Méthode** : un agent d’audit par module + des agents transversaux (end-to-end), chaque finding repassé à une **vérification adversariale** indépendante (lecture du code réel + reproduction empirique contre AutoHotkey64.exe v2 quand pertinent). Chaque finding porte sa **sévérité effective** (post-vérification) et son **verdict** (`confirmed` / `uncertain` / `unverified-low`).

Fil conducteur : le modèle de concurrence AHK v2 — un seul pseudo-thread coopératif ; `Suspend` ne désarme que les hotkeys/hotstrings (les `SetTimer`, `InputHook`, `OnMessage` **le contournent**) ; un `try` sans `catch` avale l’exception ; un appel bloquant (WinHTTP synchrone, SQLite, `fh.Write` derrière un antivirus, COM/UIA) tenu sur le thread clavier peut dépasser `LowLevelHooksTimeout` (~300 ms) et **faire tomber une frappe physique**.

> **Total : 170 findings actifs.** (Le « 123 » d’une première version tronquée du rapport était un décompte erroné de l’agent de synthèse ; le jeu de données réel en contient 170.)

---

| Catégorie | Nombre |

| Fuites de pause / suspend | 22 |
| Fuites de ressources | 19 |
| Erreurs avalées silencieusement | 9 |

| Divers | 2 |

### Les findings les plus graves

- [`dispatcher-boundfunc-ptr-drops-subscribers`](#f-dispatcher-boundfunc-ptr-drops-subscribers) (**critical**, lost-keystroke) — HookDispatcher.Register silently DROPS every subscriber after the first per event type (BoundFunc has no .Ptr)
- [`sensors-bypass-pause-suspend`](#f-sensors-bypass-pause-suspend) (**critical**, suspend-pause-leak) — All peripheral sensor timers/OnClipboardChange keep logging while the script is paused
- [`keylogger-telemetry-ignores-suspend`](#f-keylogger-telemetry-ignores-suspend) (**critical**, suspend-pause-leak) — Keylogger keeps collecting and writing PII telemetry to disk while the driver is paused (KL_AppendLog lacks an A_IsSuspended guard)
- [`gesture-release-disables-shared-lbutton`](#f-gesture-release-disables-shared-lbutton) (**high**, lost-keystroke) — Gesture click-hold release does Hotkey("~LButton", ..., "Off") which turns OFF the shared button hotkey for every other subscriber
- [`layout-poll-blind-reload`](#f-layout-poll-blind-reload) (**high**, lost-keystroke) — 1 s layout-poll timer does an unguarded Reload() that drops in-flight typing on any transient HKL change
- [`llm-autotype-no-hse-suppress`](#f-llm-autotype-no-hse-suppress) (**high**, race-condition) — LLM accept and inline auto-type feed injected text back into the hotstring InputHook (no suppression)
- [`changelog-infinite-resolve-timeout`](#f-changelog-infinite-resolve-timeout) (**high**, hot-path-blocking) — Changelog GitHub fetch uses a 0 (infinite) WinHTTP resolve timeout on the main thread — can freeze all keyboard remapping
- [`uia-selection-query-on-hot-path`](#f-uia-selection-query-on-hot-path) (**high**, hot-path-blocking) — Synchronous UIA COM query runs on the keyboard thread for every common symbol keystroke, even with no selection
- [`uia-selection-blocks-keyboard-thread`](#f-uia-selection-blocks-keyboard-thread) (**high**, hot-path-blocking) — Synchronous UIA selection query runs on the keyboard thread on every wrap-symbol keypress
- [`ingest-tick-blocks-keyboard-thread`](#f-ingest-tick-blocks-keyboard-thread) (**high**, hot-path-blocking) — 5 s ingest tick (and dashboard live-push) runs SQLite rebuild + multi-MB file I/O on the keystroke thread, dropping keys
- [`bypass-dispatch-critical-starves-keyboard-hook`](#f-bypass-dispatch-critical-starves-keyboard-hook) (**high**, hot-path-blocking) — Bypass dispatch runs heavy callback (full hotstring re-registration) under unbalanced Critical, starving the keyboard hook
- [`ingest-prefetch-blocks-keyboard-thread`](#f-ingest-prefetch-blocks-keyboard-thread) (**high**, hot-path-blocking) — Metrics ingest tick runs a full SQLite build + n-gram projection synchronously on the keyboard thread, dropping keystrokes while a dashboard is open
- [`remote-poll-no-deadline-cap`](#f-remote-poll-no-deadline-cap) (**high**, hot-path-blocking) — Remote-API async poll re-arms a 50 ms timer forever with no deadline — a wedged WinHTTP request saturates the message pump and drops keystrokes
- [`layout-poll-reload-unpauses`](#f-layout-poll-reload-unpauses) (**high**, suspend-pause-leak) — Automatic layout-poll Reload silently un-pauses the driver (suspend state not persisted across Reload)
- [`dispatcher-unregister-never-removes`](#f-dispatcher-unregister-never-removes) (**high**, suspend-pause-leak) — HookDispatcher.Unregister can never detach any subscriber, defeating every feature Stop()
- [`warmup-retry-bypasses-suspend`](#f-warmup-retry-bypasses-suspend) (**high**, suspend-pause-leak) — Warmup-retry timer keeps POSTing to Ollama while the script is paused (pause = tout éteint violation)
- [`halflife-tick-no-suspend-guard`](#f-halflife-tick-no-suspend-guard) (**high**, suspend-pause-leak) — KL_Roi_HalflifeTick writes PII-candidate / half-life events to disk while the script is PAUSED
- [`textsend-clip-destroys-nontext`](#f-textsend-clip-destroys-nontext) (**high**, resource-leak) — TextSend clipboard branch destroys non-text clipboard content (images/files) and loses formatting
- [`textpresskey-drops-string-modifiers`](#f-textpresskey-drops-string-modifiers) (**high**, logic-bug) — TextPressKey silently drops modifiers passed as a string ("Shift", "Ctrl", "Ctrl Shift", "Blind", "Win")
- [`word-delimiters-not-applied-live`](#f-word-delimiters-not-applied-live) (**high**, logic-bug) — Word-delimiter edits never take effect until the next Reload, yet the UI reports success
- [`dpapi-decrypt-fail-wipes-token`](#f-dpapi-decrypt-fail-wipes-token) (**high**, logic-bug) — Failed DPAPI decrypt returns "" and silently destroys the stored token on next save
- [`download-no-integrity-partial-safety`](#f-download-no-integrity-partial-safety) (**high**, logic-bug) — Self-update downloads and swaps the new exe with no integrity check and no partial-download guard

---

## Feuille de route priorisée (checklist)

> Conventions impératives : une branche `fix/<id>` ou `feat/<id>` par lot ; **jamais de push/merge sans validation** ; historique **linéaire** (squash + un seul commit conventionnel) ; **chaque correctif embarque son test de non-régression** (règle 5.9) ; fichiers `.ahk` en **UTF-8 BOM + CRLF**, suites de test **ASCII-only** (`Chr(0xNNNN)`). Lancer la suite headless `run_all.ahk` en `/ErrorStdOut` après chaque lot.

### P0 — Critical (à corriger en premier) (3)

- [x] [`dispatcher-boundfunc-ptr-drops-subscribers`](#f-dispatcher-boundfunc-ptr-drops-subscribers) — HookDispatcher.Register silently DROPS every subscriber after the first per event type (BoundFunc has no .Ptr)  
  _Fix :_ Compare callbacks by object identity, not .Ptr. Replace `if (existing.Ptr = callback_fn.Ptr)` with `if (existing == callback_fn)` (verified: `==` is identity-equal for the same BoundFunc and not-equal for distinct BoundFuncs). Apply the same change in Unregister (see related finding).
- [x] [`sensors-bypass-pause-suspend`](#f-sensors-bypass-pause-suspend) — All peripheral sensor timers/OnClipboardChange keep logging while the script is paused  
  _Fix :_ Add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` as the first line of every standalone sensor entry point: KL_Sensors_Tick, KL_Net_WifiTick/ReachTick/VpnTick, KL_AV_FastTick/SlowTick, KL_Topo_Tick, KL_Mouse_ParkTick, KL_Mouse_FlushScroll, KL_Clip_OnChange, KL_Clip_OnPaste (and KL_Roi_HalflifeTick). The cleanest single-chokepoint fix is to add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` to KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk, right after the `initialized` check) and KL_LogSystemEvent so EVERY non-hook event source inherits the pause gate for free, mirroring how MF_ShouldFilter is the single privacy chokepoint. Keep the per-callback guards too for callbacks that mutate state before logging (mouse park distance bump).
- [x] [`keylogger-telemetry-ignores-suspend`](#f-keylogger-telemetry-ignores-suspend) — Keylogger keeps collecting and writing PII telemetry to disk while the driver is paused (KL_AppendLog lacks an A_IsSuspended guard)  
  _Fix :_ Add a single early-return guard `if A_IsSuspended return` at the very top of KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618, right after the `if !Keylogger.initialized return`). This mirrors the HookDispatcher.Dispatch single-gate pattern and silences EVERY telemetry source's disk write in one canonical place, since all of them funnel through KL_AppendLog. Additionally (defence in depth + to stop wasted polling), add `if A_IsSuspended return` at the top of each recurring tick (KL_Sensors_Tick, KL_Net_*Tick, KL_AV_*Tick, KL_Topo_Tick, KL_Watchers_IdleTick, KL_Mouse_ParkTick, KL_Roi_HalflifeTick) and skip the FileAppend in KL_IngestOnce while suspended so no data.sql commit happens during pause. Keeping the guard in KL_AppendLog as the authoritative gate ensures any future telemetry source is covered automatically.

### P1 — High (19)

- [x] [`gesture-release-disables-shared-lbutton`](#f-gesture-release-disables-shared-lbutton) — Gesture click-hold release does Hotkey("~LButton", ..., "Off") which turns OFF the shared button hotkey for every other subscriber  
  _Fix :_ Give the gesture's dynamic LButton watcher its own #HotIf context (e.g. #HotIf GestureRightClickHeld) so its On/Off only affects that distinct variant, never the shared global ~LButton. Better: subscribe the gesture release to HookDispatcher (Register/Unregister EVT_MS_LDOWN) so it cannot disable anyone else's binding. The Hotkey(...,"Off") at release must never touch the variant other modules depend on.
- [x] [`layout-poll-blind-reload`](#f-layout-poll-blind-reload) — 1 s layout-poll timer does an unguarded Reload() that drops in-flight typing on any transient HKL change  
  _Fix :_ Guard the Reload behind (a) `if A_IsSuspended return` (a paused driver must not auto-reload), and (b) a quiescence check: require the HKL to stay changed for two consecutive polls AND that no send/expansion is in flight (HSE_Suppressed / _PrefixWatcherSuppressed both false) and the user is idle (A_TimeIdlePhysical above a small threshold, e.g. > 400 ms) before reloading. This converts a transient flicker into a no-op while still adapting to a genuine, sustained layout switch.
- [x] [`llm-autotype-no-hse-suppress`](#f-llm-autotype-no-hse-suppress) — LLM accept and inline auto-type feed injected text back into the hotstring InputHook (no suppression)  
  _Fix :_ Wrap the TextSend in LLM_Bridge_OnAccept and the inline-autotype TextSend in HSE_Suppress(true)/PrefixWatcherSuppress(true) for the burst, releasing on the same deferred SetTimer pattern HSE_DispatchMatch uses (negative timer ~ release delay), and reset _PrefixBuffer afterwards. Alternatively raise the SendLevel of the auto-type and the InputHook's MinSendLevel so synthetic predictions are inherently invisible to the hook.
- [x] [`changelog-infinite-resolve-timeout`](#f-changelog-infinite-resolve-timeout) — Changelog GitHub fetch uses a 0 (infinite) WinHTTP resolve timeout on the main thread — can freeze all keyboard remapping  
  _Fix :_ Replace SetTimeouts(0, 10000, 20000, 20000) with finite values for every phase, reusing the updater's constants (UPDATER_HTTP_RESOLVE_TIMEOUT_MS, …CONNECT…, …SEND…, …RECEIVE…) so the resolve phase is bounded (e.g. 5000 ms). Longer term, move the changelog fetch to the same async WinHTTP + poll-timer pattern used by _Updater_FetchLatestJsonAsync so it never runs synchronously on the main thread at all.
- [x] [`uia-selection-query-on-hot-path`](#f-uia-selection-query-on-hot-path) — Synchronous UIA COM query runs on the keyboard thread for every common symbol keystroke, even with no selection  
  _Fix :_ Gate GetUIASelection() so the UIA round-trip only runs when a selection plausibly exists. Cheapest: skip the UIA query entirely unless GetKeyState modifiers or a cheap heuristic indicate selection; better, check selection state without a full TextPattern walk, or move the UIA query off the synchronous path (e.g. only attempt the wrap on an explicit gesture, or cache 'this app never exposes TextPattern' results per-process so repeated misses cost nothing). At minimum, wrap GetUIASelection in a hard timeout (run it via a short-lived worker / ComCall with a deadline) and treat a timeout as 'no selection', and extend the WinActive("Code") fast-bail into a small allowlist/denylist of process names known to be slow so the query is never even attempted there.
- [x] [`uia-selection-blocks-keyboard-thread`](#f-uia-selection-blocks-keyboard-thread) — Synchronous UIA selection query runs on the keyboard thread on every wrap-symbol keypress  
  _Fix :_ Do not block the keyboard thread on UIA. Options, in order of preference: (a) Only attempt the UIA selection probe when a selection is plausible — gate it behind a cheap heuristic (e.g. require a recent Shift-drag / Ctrl+A, or skip the probe entirely when the active app is known-slow such as Electron, which is already partially done via WinActive("Code")). (b) Wrap the entire GetUIASelection() in a hard timeout using a worker (e.g. a short-lived ComCall on a separate AHK process, or a SetTimer-driven async probe that falls back to plain SendNewResult if it has not answered within ~30 ms). (c) Add ComObjFlags / IUIAutomation timeout configuration and a defensive A_TickCount budget so the function returns "" after a small cap. At minimum, document and measure the worst-case latency and bound it well under LowLevelHooksTimeout.
- [x] [`ingest-tick-blocks-keyboard-thread`](#f-ingest-tick-blocks-keyboard-thread) — 5 s ingest tick (and dashboard live-push) runs SQLite rebuild + multi-MB file I/O on the keystroke thread, dropping keys  
  _Fix :_ Never run KLR_BuildDatabase / KLPF_BuildAndWrite synchronously on the timer sharing the keystroke thread when it can exceed a few ms. Either move the SQLite rebuild + projection to a worker process/child AHK script over a file/pipe, or chunk it so each timer slice does bounded work and yields (one 4 MB chunk per tick; time-budget RebuildAggregates) and gate the live-push so it only fires when the keystroke thread has been idle (A_TickCount - KLHook.last_tick > threshold). At minimum drop the per-call FileAppend debug logging in KLR_BuildDatabase from the hot cadence and gate KL_Hook_LivePush behind 'no dashboard open'.
- [x] [`bypass-dispatch-critical-starves-keyboard-hook`](#f-bypass-dispatch-critical-starves-keyboard-hook) — Bypass dispatch runs heavy callback (full hotstring re-registration) under unbalanced Critical, starving the keyboard hook  
  _Fix :_ Scope Critical to only the timestamp compare-and-set, then release it BEFORE invoking the user callback: read CurrentLastFire and write `_MenuDispatchLastFire[ItemId] := A_TickCount` under `Critical "On"`, then `Critical "Off"`, THEN `Callback.Call(...)`. The callback must run interruptibly exactly like AHK's native dispatch path. (Optionally also move RebuildHotstringsLive's heavy work off the click thread, but at minimum do not hold Critical across it.)
- [x] [`ingest-prefetch-blocks-keyboard-thread`](#f-ingest-prefetch-blocks-keyboard-thread) — Metrics ingest tick runs a full SQLite build + n-gram projection synchronously on the keyboard thread, dropping keystrokes while a dashboard is open  
  _Fix :_ Take the heavy build off the keyboard thread. Either (a) spawn the SQLite projection in a curl-style detached child / separate AHK process (mirroring how the LLM dispatch moved to a curl child) and harvest the result via a poll timer, or (b) chunk the work and yield with periodic `Sleep -1` between phases so the message pump and InputHook can interleave, or at minimum (c) gate the live/full rebuild behind a coalescing one-shot that runs at most every N seconds AND skips entirely when KL_Hook recorded a keystroke within the last ~500 ms (defer until the user pauses), so a build never starts mid-burst. The fast manifest-only path (~20 ms) can stay inline; the n-gram/full projection must not.
- [x] [`remote-poll-no-deadline-cap`](#f-remote-poll-no-deadline-cap) — Remote-API async poll re-arms a 50 ms timer forever with no deadline — a wedged WinHTTP request saturates the message pump and drops keystrokes  
  _Fix :_ Mirror api_ollama.ahk: thread an absolute `deadline := A_TickCount + LLM_REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS + slack` (and/or a max-polls budget like the updater's UPDATER_ASYNC_MAX_POLLS) through _LLMRemote_PollRequest. On `A_TickCount >= deadline`, call `try entry["http"].Abort()`, delete the registry entry, fire on_fail() once, and stop re-arming. Also have LLM_RemoteCancelAllAsync call `.Abort()` on the http object (it currently only sets cancelled := true, leaving the WinHTTP request live).
- [x] [`layout-poll-reload-unpauses`](#f-layout-poll-reload-unpauses) — Automatic layout-poll Reload silently un-pauses the driver (suspend state not persisted across Reload)  
  _Fix :_ Persist the suspend state before any automatic Reload and restore it on boot: e.g. write a one-shot marker (a temp flag file or a [script] runtime key) when A_IsSuspended is true and re-apply Suspend('On') early in the auto-execute section if the marker is present (clearing it after). Minimally, guard CheckKeyboardLayoutChange with `if A_IsSuspended return` so a paused driver never auto-reloads on layout change. The marker approach is preferable because user-triggered Reloads (menu) also drop pause today.
- [x] [`dispatcher-unregister-never-removes`](#f-dispatcher-unregister-never-removes) — HookDispatcher.Unregister can never detach any subscriber, defeating every feature Stop()  
  _Fix :_ In Unregister replace `arr[idx].Ptr = callback_fn.Ptr` with `arr[idx] == callback_fn`. Separately, fix keyboard_hook.ahk to bind once and store the reference (e.g. global _KH_CHAR_FN := _KH_DispatchChar.Bind() created in KHStart, reused in KHStop) so Register and Unregister pass the identical object, matching the KLHook.cb_char / _LLM_Bridge_DispatcherCharFn pattern.
- [x] [`warmup-retry-bypasses-suspend`](#f-warmup-retry-bypasses-suspend) — Warmup-retry timer keeps POSTing to Ollama while the script is paused (pause = tout éteint violation)  
  _Fix :_ Add `if A_IsSuspended { LLM_OllamaCancelWarmupRetry(); return }` at the top of LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryTick (or, better, cancel it from Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter via try LLM_OllamaCancelWarmupRetry() and try LLM_Engine_StopGeneration() so in-flight generation and warmup both stop on pause, then let Ergopti_OnSuspendResume re-arm warmup if needed).
- [x] [`halflife-tick-no-suspend-guard`](#f-halflife-tick-no-suspend-guard) — KL_Roi_HalflifeTick writes PII-candidate / half-life events to disk while the script is PAUSED  
  _Fix :_ Add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` as the first line of KL_Roi_HalflifeTick (after the RequireInit guard). For defence-in-depth and to cover any future timer-driven caller, also add an `if A_IsSuspended return` guard at the top of KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618) so no background timer can ever persist a metric while paused — this is the single choke-point all 20+ KL_Log* entry points funnel through.
- [x] [`textsend-clip-destroys-nontext`](#f-textsend-clip-destroys-nontext) — TextSend clipboard branch destroys non-text clipboard content (images/files) and loses formatting  
  _Fix :_ Make the Clipboard port snapshot/restore preserve all formats: add CB_SaveAll()/CB_RestoreAll() (or change CB_Save to return ClipboardAll() and CB_Restore to assign it back) and have TextSend's clipboard branch use them, mirroring SendInstant. Keep CB_Read/CB_Write text-only for the read/write contract, but the save/restore pair must use ClipboardAll so non-text content survives.
- [x] [`textpresskey-drops-string-modifiers`](#f-textpresskey-drops-string-modifiers) — TextPressKey silently drops modifiers passed as a string ("Shift", "Ctrl", "Ctrl Shift", "Blind", "Win")  
  _Fix :_ Add a string branch in TextPressKey: if Modifiers is a non-empty String that is not "Down"/"Up", split on spaces and either map known modifier tokens via _TextSenderModifierPrefix or pass through AHK-native prefixes/words (e.g. "Blind" -> `{Blind}` prefix). Simplest robust form: for a string, build Prefix by tokenising and translating each token; emit `Prefix . "{" . Key . "}"`. Keep Array and Down/Up handling intact.
- [x] [`word-delimiters-not-applied-live`](#f-word-delimiters-not-applied-live) — Word-delimiter edits never take effect until the next Reload, yet the UI reports success  
  _Fix :_ Make HotstringsSetWordDelimiters update the runtime global in lock-step with the consumed-delimiter setter: add `global HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS` and `HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS := HotstringsGetWordDelimiters()` (i.e. the effective string, falling back to the default when Delimiters=="") right after _SaveGlobalWordDelimiters, mirroring lines 252-254. Note _PREFIX_WORD_BOUNDARIES (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:100) is also derived from HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS at load; if the watcher must honour the new set live too, recompute it as well. Then correct the docstring (no Reload is performed).
- [x] [`dpapi-decrypt-fail-wipes-token`](#f-dpapi-decrypt-fail-wipes-token) — Failed DPAPI decrypt returns "" and silently destroys the stored token on next save  
  _Fix :_ Make decrypt failure distinguishable from an empty token. Either (a) have LLM_ApiToken_Decrypt return the original `stored` (still-encrypted) string on failure so a round-trip re-persist preserves the ciphertext, plus log a WARN that the token can't be decrypted on this machine; or (b) return a distinct sentinel and have the persister skip rewriting the Token field (preserve the existing on-disk value) when decrypt failed. At minimum, guard the persister: never overwrite a non-empty on-disk Token with an empty one that resulted from a failed decrypt.
- [x] [`download-no-integrity-partial-safety`](#f-download-no-integrity-partial-safety) — Self-update downloads and swaps the new exe with no integrity check and no partial-download guard  
  _Fix :_ Before swapping: (1) verify the downloaded file size against the asset's 'size' field from the release JSON (available in the assets array next to browser_download_url); (2) ideally verify a SHA-256 (CryptoSha256 already exists in this unit) against a digest published in the release; (3) keep a backup copy of CUR_EXE in the swap .cmd and restore it if 'start' of the new exe is not confirmed. At minimum, reject the swap when FileGetSize(NewExe) != expected asset size.

### P2 — Medium (42)

- [x] [`clickhold-inputhook-drops-keys`](#f-clickhold-inputhook-drops-keys) — Click-hold InputHook suppresses every keystroke and re-sends it, losing/reordering keys and ignoring pause  
  _Fix :_ Do not suppress: install the watcher as a non-consuming hook (InputHook with 'V' so keys pass through normally, or arm ~*$<key> style passthrough hotkeys) and merely RELEASE the held button on the first KeyDown without swallowing/re-sending. If suppression is truly required to release-before-the-key-acts, replay must reconstruct full modifier state and the watcher must be torn down atomically under Critical before the click release so no second key is seen. Also add 'if A_IsSuspended { ih.Stop(); GestureReleaseLeftClick(); GestureReleaseRightClick(); return }' at the top of GestureOnKeyDown so pause silences this hook (InputHooks bypass Suspend per project invariant).
- [x] [`mouse-hotkey-clobber`](#f-mouse-hotkey-clobber) — Three subsystems register Hotkey("~LButton"/"~RButton"/"~MButton") in the same context — later ones silently clobber the dispatcher fan-out  
  _Fix :_ Route ALL mouse-button observation through the HookDispatcher subscriber API instead of re-registering the raw hotkey. PrefixWatcher should HookDispatcher.Register(EVT_MS_LDOWN/MDOWN/RDOWN, _OnMouseClickReset) and the LLM pointer-watch should Register(EVT_MS_LDOWN/.../wheel, _LLM_PointerWatch_ActivityFn) rather than calling Hotkey("~LButton", ...). The dispatcher already owns the single Hotkey() set and fans out to every subscriber, so each module then coexists. Where a module must keep its own Hotkey (e.g. it can run with metrics off and no dispatcher), give it a dedicated #HotIf context so it occupies a distinct hotkey variant rather than clobbering the shared one.
- [x] [`boolean-suppression-released-early-on-overlapping-fires`](#f-boolean-suppression-released-early-on-overlapping-fires) — Suppression / synthetic flags are booleans released by independent one-shot timers; back-to-back fires clear them early  
  _Fix :_ Replace the three boolean flags with small integer counters (suppress depth) incremented on enter and decremented on the deferred release, un-suppressing only at zero; OR arm ONE named release function (a module-level function reference, not a fresh lambda each call) via negative SetTimer so successive fires re-arm the single timer to the latest +60 ms instead of stacking independent early-firing timers. Apply the same to KL_ClearSynthetic (use a counter so an early clear cannot un-mark a still-in-flight burst).
- [x] [`activitysim-global-collision`](#f-activitysim-global-collision) — GestureToggleActivitySimulation collides with the keep-awake system in shortcuts/win.ahk over the shared ActivitySimulation global  
  _Fix :_ Make the gesture action call the canonical ToggleActivitySimulation() from shortcuts/win.ahk instead of re-implementing it: change GESTURE_ACTIONS["activity_simulation"].Fn to (*) => ToggleActivitySimulation(), and delete GestureToggleActivitySimulation/GestureSimulateActivity entirely. If win.ahk's feature can be disabled (Features["shortcuts"]["move"] false) the gesture must guard for that and surface a message rather than silently writing the shared global.
- [x] [`pending-entries-cross-thread-race`](#f-pending-entries-cross-thread-race) — _pending_entries pushed on the keystroke thread (via KL_LogShortcut) while the ingest tick drains-and-clears it  
  _Fix :_ Atomically swap, never read-then-clear: as the FIRST thing in KL_IngestOnce do `Critical("On"); snapshot := Keylogger._pending_entries; Keylogger._pending_entries := []; Critical("Off")`, then iterate `snapshot` outside Critical so the keystroke hook is starved for nanoseconds only. Equivalently wrap the Push in KL_AppendLog in Critical.
- [x] [`metrics-focus-cache-cross-thread-race`](#f-metrics-focus-cache-cross-thread-race) — Privacy filter focus cache is read/written across keyboard, mouse, clipboard and sensor threads without Critical — torn focus read can log PII  
  _Fix :_ Make MF_RefreshFocus update atomic from a reader's perspective: build the new values into locals first, then publish them in one shot and set last_at LAST so a reader either sees the fully-previous or fully-new snapshot. Better: wrap the publish in 'Critical "On" ... Critical "Off"' around the field assignments (kept short — no Send/Sleep/blocking call inside), or store the snapshot as a single immutable object reference (proc/title/class in one object) and swap the reference in one assignment so readers get a consistent tuple. Keep the WinGet* calls (which can block) OUTSIDE the Critical region.
- [x] [`space-tap-dispatch-not-critical`](#f-space-tap-dispatch-not-critical) — Space-tap hotstring dispatch fires HSE_DispatchMatch outside Critical, unlike the OnChar fire path  
  _Fix :_ Wrap the feed->dispatch region of _SpaceTap in `Critical('On')`/restore exactly like _OnPrefixChar (save/restore so it nests safely; HSE_DispatchMatch already drops Critical for its Notepad Sleep branch). Order the literal-space emission AFTER (or fold it into) the expansion decision so the buffer state and the screen state cannot diverge mid-gap.
- [x] [`roi-map-mutation-during-enumeration-race`](#f-roi-map-mutation-during-enumeration-race) — Half-life tick enumerates KLRoi.trigger_last_use / word_counts while the keystroke thread mutates them — torn read / throw  
  _Fix :_ Wrap the read side in Critical: in KL_Roi_HalflifeTick snapshot the map under `Critical('On')` (copy keys/values into a local Array, then `Critical('Off')`) and iterate the snapshot — never call KL_AppendLog (which blocks on fh.Write) while holding the live map open. Likewise guard the word_counts prune in KL_Roi_ProcessWord: build the `prune` list under Critical, then Delete under Critical, keeping the section tiny (no I/O inside it). Mirror the HookDispatcher.Register pattern (Critical On/Off around list mutation) that the codebase already established.
- [x] [`deferred-queue-not-cancelled-on-live-rebuild`](#f-deferred-queue-not-cancelled-on-live-rebuild) — Live section toggle during the post-boot deferred drain double-registers every remaining emoji/symbol row  
  _Fix :_ Make the deferred queue lifetime owned by the registration: in RebuildHotstringsLive() (and at the top of RegisterAllHotstrings when DeferHeavy=false, since that path registers emoji/symbols whole) cancel the deferred pass before re-registering — `global _HS_DeferredQueue` then `_HS_DeferredQueue := []` and `try SetTimer(_HsDeferredChunkTick, 0)`. Symmetrically, have RegisterEmojisSymbolsDeferred() guard against a concurrent live rebuild having already registered the sections (e.g. skip arming if HSE already holds the emoji/symbol groups). Disarming the timer (SetTimer fn,0) is the established idiom in this codebase (tooltip.ahk, prefix watcher).
- [x] [`spotlight-blocks-keyboard-thread`](#f-spotlight-blocks-keyboard-thread) — Spotlight overlay runs a 3–5 s synchronous Sleep poll loop inside the triggering hotkey/gesture thread  
  _Fix :_ Make the dismiss watcher non-blocking: create the overlay windows, store the hwnds + StartX/StartY + deadline in module-level state, and drive dismissal from a `SetTimer(SpotlightTick, DISMISS_POLL)` that checks elapsed/mouse-move and tears the windows down + shuts GDI+ down when done. Return from SpotlightMouseAt immediately so the hotkey thread ends. Guard the tick timer with `if A_IsSuspended` (stop + dismiss) so pause hides the overlay too.
- [ ] [`sync-winhttp-blocks-keyboard-on-user-check`](#f-sync-winhttp-blocks-keyboard-on-user-check) — User-initiated update check/download runs synchronous WinHttp on the main thread, freezing keyboard remapping for up to ~80s while the user types  
  _Fix :_ Route the user-initiated check and the asset download through the same WinHTTP-async + SetTimer(WaitForResponse(0)) poll pattern already implemented in _Updater_FetchLatestJsonAsync/_Updater_PollAsync. Show a non-blocking 'Vérification…'/'Téléchargement…' state in the menu/dialog and complete via the async callback. For the download, stream to disk from the async request once WaitForResponse(0) signals completion. This makes every updater network call non-blocking, matching the background path.
- [x] [`sqlite-wrapper-no-bounded-blocking-on-main-thread`](#f-sqlite-wrapper-no-bounded-blocking-on-main-thread) — SQLite FFI wrapper offers no way to bound or yield during multi-second projections invoked from a timer on the keyboard thread  
  _Fix :_ Expose an optional sqlite3_progress_handler-backed callback (or a row/step budget) in SQLite_Query/SQLite_Exec that lets the caller insert a periodic 'Sleep(-1)' yield so the message pump and keyboard hook can run between batches. Strategically, the keylogger projection should run off-thread (a separate process or a worker) — but at minimum the wrapper should support cooperative yielding so a long query cannot monopolize the keyboard thread.
- [x] [`logger-sync-double-write-on-warning`](#f-logger-sync-double-write-on-warning) — Every WARNING/ERROR forces two synchronous FileOpen/Write/Close round-trips on the main thread — can stall typing when an LLM/HTTP timer logs a warning mid-interaction  
  _Fix :_ Do not force a synchronous flush for WARNING — reserve synchronous, file-handle-closed writes for ERROR only (the level that actually precedes a crash). For WARNING, push to the pending queue and let the 500ms batched _LoggerFlush handle it like INFO. Also collapse the errors-only-file write into the same single forced flush rather than a second independent open/close (e.g. append the errors-file write to the same ForceFlush routine, or only force it for ERROR). This removes the per-warning double blocking write from any thread that can interrupt typing.
- [x] [`kl-synchronous-disk-write-on-keystroke`](#f-kl-synchronous-disk-write-on-keystroke) — Keylogger writes to today.log synchronously (fh.Write) on the keyboard thread for shortcut / app-switch / window-switch events  
  _Fix :_ Make ALL keystroke-thread log writes deferred, mirroring the existing _HSE_QueueFireLog pattern in the prefix watcher: KL_AppendLog should push the entry to Keylogger._pending_entries (it already does) and the actual fh.Write should happen ONLY on a SetTimer drain (the flush/ingest tick), never inline. Shortcut/app-switch/window-switch logging from the InputHook should enqueue an O(1) record and arm a short one-shot drain timer, so the disk touch always lands off the keystroke. At minimum, gate the inline fh.Write behind a check that it is not being called from within an InputHook callback.
- [x] [`deadkey-inputhook-blocks-thread`](#f-deadkey-inputhook-blocks-thread) — DeadKey() blocks the keyboard thread on InputHook.Wait() with no timeout  
  _Fix :_ Add a timeout to the InputHook (e.g. `InputHook("L1 T2", ...)`) and handle the timeout branch by sending the bare dead-key char and clearing InDeadKeySequence, so an abandoned sequence resolves deterministically and releases the captured key stream. Verify InDeadKeySequence is reset in a finally so an exception in SendNewResult cannot leave it latched.
- [x] [`wraptext-sendinstant-sleep-hotpath`](#f-wraptext-sendinstant-sleep-hotpath) — WrapTextIfSelected / UIA-wrap runs blocking Sleep+clipboard on the keyboard thread mid-typing  
  _Fix :_ Keep the clipboard write synchronous but defer the restore via a negative SetTimer (as TextSend already does) instead of an inline Sleep, so the keyboard thread returns immediately after the paste. For UIA, cap/timebox the GetUIASelection call and/or move it behind the existing 100 ms active-app cache so it cannot stall the hook. Document that no Critical may be held across SendInstant (the prefix watcher already releases Critical before this branch — good).
- [x] [`keepawake-bypasses-pause`](#f-keepawake-bypasses-pause) — Keep-awake timers and mouse-cancel InputHook are not gated by A_IsSuspended — they keep running while the driver is paused  
  _Fix :_ Add `if A_IsSuspended` early-returns at the top of SimulateActivity, AwakeCheckMouseMoved, AwakeReturnToOrigin, and AwakeCancelOnKeypress/AwakeCancelOnMouse (return without acting). Better: hook the pause transition to call StopActivitySimulation() so the InputHook and timers are fully released on pause and re-armed only if the user re-triggers Win+M after resume.
- [x] [`mouse-park-distance-no-gate`](#f-mouse-park-distance-no-gate) — Mouse park timer accumulates mouse_distance_px with no initialized/suspend/filter gate  
  _Fix :_ Guard the distance accumulation: at the top of KL_Mouse_ParkTick add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return`, and move the KL_BumpMouseDistance call to AFTER the `if !filtered and Keylogger.initialized` gate (or evaluate MF_ShouldFilter once at the top of the tick and skip both distance and park work when filtered). Still update prev_x/prev_y so the next live tick computes a correct delta rather than a huge jump.
- [x] [`warmup-retry-ignores-suspend`](#f-warmup-retry-ignores-suspend) — Warmup-retry and deps poll timers keep hitting Ollama over HTTP during Suspend (pause is not 'tout eteint')  
  _Fix :_ Add 'if A_IsSuspended { re-arm a short check-back timer and return }' at the top of LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryTick and _LLM_Deps_OnPollProbeResult/LLM_Deps_PollServerReady, mirroring the FirePrediction guard. Re-arm rather than cancel so warmup resumes when unpaused.
- [x] [`timer-scheduler-no-pause-guard`](#f-timer-scheduler-no-pause-guard) — TimerScheduler repeating callbacks keep firing while the driver is paused (project pause invariant violated)  
  _Fix :_ Add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` at the top of both `_Repeating` (before the `Fired` check) and `_OneShot` (before invoking BoundFn), mirroring HookDispatcher.Dispatch and prediction_engine's guard. For one-shot timers, prefer re-arming or dropping per the port's documented semantics rather than silently consuming the firing; at minimum repeating timers must not invoke the callback under suspend.
- [x] [`keylogger-watchers-bypass-pause`](#f-keylogger-watchers-bypass-pause) — Keylogger idle/session/power timers and OnMessage handlers write to disk while the driver is paused  
  _Fix :_ Add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` at the top of the idle_check_timer callback and both OnMessage handlers in keylogger_watchers.ahk, OR — more robustly and in one place — add an `if A_IsSuspended` guard at the top of KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618) so EVERY keylogger event source is silenced under pause uniformly (matching how HookDispatcher.Dispatch centralises the guard). The central guard is preferable; document that KL_Stop's final flush path must run BEFORE/independent of it so a pause does not block shutdown flushing.
- [x] [`spotlight-gdiplus-token-leak-on-error`](#f-spotlight-gdiplus-token-leak-on-error) — GDI+ startup token and overlay windows leak if any DllCall/window create throws (no try/finally)  
  _Fix :_ Wrap the body from after GdiplusStartup through the dismiss/destroy in `try { ... } finally { GR_DestroyWindow(CircleHwnd); for h in CrossHwnds GR_DestroyWindow(h); DllCall('gdiplus\GdiplusShutdown','ptr',pToken) }`. Initialise CircleHwnd:=0 and CrossHwnds:=[] before the try so the finally is always valid. (This also dovetails with the non-blocking refactor: shutdown happens in the final tick / on suspend.)
- [x] [`toml-batchwrite-nonatomic-config-loss`](#f-toml-batchwrite-nonatomic-config-loss) — TOML_BatchWrite deletes config.toml before renaming the temp file — a crash/kill in that window destroys all user settings  
  _Fix :_ Replace the delete-then-move with a single atomic replace: `FileMove(tmp, Path, true)` (overwrite=true) — one rename-with-replace call, no zero-file window. Remove the FileDelete(Path) lines. Update the comment to reflect that the temp+replace is what provides the atomicity, and on FileMove failure leave the original Path intact (do not delete it first).
- [x] [`webview2-userdata-temp-dir-leak`](#f-webview2-userdata-temp-dir-leak) — WebView2 changelog/update windows create a new A_Temp user-data dir per open and never delete it  
  _Fix :_ On G.OnEvent('Close')/G.Destroy(), explicitly tear down the WebView2 controller (close the CoreWebView2/controller so its process exits) and DirDelete(udir, 1) the user-data folder. Alternatively reuse a single stable user-data dir per window type instead of an A_TickCount-suffixed one, and clean it on script exit via OnExit.
- [x] [`webview2-udir-temp-leak`](#f-webview2-udir-temp-leak) — Seven WebView2 hosts leak a unique per-launch temp profile dir on every open/reload  
  _Fix :_ Mirror the keylogger_ui.ahk sweep in every WebView2 host: immediately before DirCreate(udir), do `loop files, A_Temp . "\ergopti_<feature>_wv*", "D" { try DirDelete(A_LoopFileFullPath, true) }` (best-effort — a dir still locked by a lingering Edge helper is retried next launch). Additionally, have each Close path attempt `try DirDelete(udir, true)` after controller.Close()/Gui.Destroy(). Factor the sweep into a shared helper (e.g. WebView_SweepStaleProfiles(prefix)) so all seven sites stay consistent.
- [x] [`dispatch-swallows-subscriber-errors`](#f-dispatch-swallows-subscriber-errors) — Dispatch swallows every subscriber exception with no log — silent hot-path failures  
  _Fix :_ Add a catch that logs at WARNING with throttling, e.g. `try cb(args*) catch as e { try LoggerWarn("HookDispatcher", "Subscriber for '{1}' threw: {2}.", event_type, e.Message) }`. Keep the loop continuing to the next subscriber. Optionally dedupe identical messages so a per-keystroke fault does not flood the log.
- [x] [`timer-callback-errors-to-outputdebug`](#f-timer-callback-errors-to-outputdebug) — Timer-callback exceptions are logged to OutputDebug, not the file logger — invisible in the audit trail  
  _Fix :_ Replace the OutputDebug calls with `try LoggerError("TimerScheduler", "one-shot callback threw: {1}", Err.Message)` (and the repeating equivalent), guarded with `try` so a logger failure cannot re-crash the timer thread. Keep OutputDebug only as an additional channel if desired.
- [x] [`json-unicode-escape-raw-throw`](#f-json-unicode-escape-raw-throw) — Malformed \uXXXX JSON escape throws a raw 'Chr requires a Number' error, not the parser's descriptive Error — silently zeroes untrusted LLM/WebView2 parses  
  _Fix :_ Validate the 4 hex digits before decoding and align position to what was actually consumed: `hex := SubStr(text, pos, 4); if !RegExMatch(hex, "^[0-9A-Fa-f]{4}$") throw Error("JSON: invalid \\u escape at position " . pos . ".", -1); pos += 4; out .= Chr("0x" . hex)`. This converts the cryptic Chr type error into the parser's own descriptive, position-bearing Error (which callers already expect), and prevents `pos += 4` from overshooting on a truncated escape.
- [x] [`healthcheck-recordwarn-never-called`](#f-healthcheck-recordwarn-never-called) — HealthCheck warn/err counters are never incremented — diagnostic and crash reports always report 0 warnings/0 errors  
  _Fix :_ Call HealthCheck_RecordWarn() from the logger's WARNING emit and HealthCheck_RecordError(Msg) from the ERROR emit (guarded with `if IsSet(...)` since the logger loads before healthcheck). Alternatively, derive warn_count/err_count directly by counting [WARNING]/[ERROR] lines in the ring buffer inside HealthCheck_Run (the snapshot is already taken for recent_issues), removing the dead counters entirely.
- [x] [`wrap-symbol-disabled-state-roundtrip-loss`](#f-wrap-symbol-disabled-state-roundtrip-loss) — Disabling a wrap pair whose opening token carries whitespace (« ») is lost across restart  
  _Fix :_ Stop trimming significant characters: on load, do not Trim() the per-token whitespace (only skip genuinely empty tokens), or — more robustly — change the persistence format to one disabled entry per line (e.g. a [[disabled]] block with char = "…" like [[custom]]) so commas and spaces are never ambiguous. Whichever is chosen, keep _WS_EscapeToml/_WS_UnescapeToml in the loop so the stored token is byte-exact. Add a guard in _WS_Save that escapes/avoids the comma delimiter if the per-line format is not adopted.
- [x] [`screenshot-region-clipwait-clobber`](#f-screenshot-region-clipwait-clobber) — GestureScreenshotRegion blanks and overwrites the user clipboard without saving/restoring it  
  _Fix :_ Wrap the capture in OldClip := ClipboardAll() / try ... finally A_Clipboard := OldClip, mirroring GetSelection. On timeout/cancel restore the clipboard. Consider lowering the 30s ClipWait or making it fire-and-forget like the clipboard-mode branch.
- [x] [`av-mute-heuristic-and-doc-mismatch`](#f-av-mute-heuristic-and-doc-mismatch) — Volume mute detection is a fragile vol<=0.01 heuristic and contradicts the COM docstring  
  _Fix :_ Either implement the documented IAudioEndpointVolume path (CoCreateInstance MMDeviceEnumerator -> GetDefaultAudioEndpoint -> Activate IAudioEndpointVolume -> GetMasterVolumeLevelScalar + GetMute) wrapped in try, or fix the docstrings to describe the winmm heuristic and stop double-calling GetMasterVolume (pass the already-read vol into the mute decision). Prefer the COM path so mute is real. Cache the COM interface across ticks rather than re-creating it.
- [x] [`ergo-roi-count-synthetic-keystrokes`](#f-ergo-roi-count-synthetic-keystrokes) — Ergonomics and ROI counters include hotstring/LLM synthetic keystrokes as user typing  
  _Fix :_ Pass the synthetic flag into the fan-out and skip: in KL_Hook_OnChar guard `if !Keylogger.synth_active { KL_Ergo_OnKeystroke(...); KL_Roi_OnChar(c) }`, or pass is_synth and early-return inside KL_Ergo_OnKeystroke / KL_Roi_OnChar when set. Keep delay accounting consistent (treat the first manual key after a synth burst with a fresh delay).
- [x] [`no-profile-delete-path`](#f-no-profile-delete-path) — Custom LLM profiles can never be deleted — the only delete affordance is a dead Cancel branch  
  _Fix :_ Implement the delete path: on the Cancel branch (or, better, replace the tri-state MsgBox with an explicit 'Delete' confirm like _LLM_Tray_RemoveActiveApiEntry already does for API entries), find the profile by id in _LLM_Tray["user_profiles"], RemoveAt it, fix up _LLM_Tray["profile_id"] if it pointed at the deleted profile (fall back to a built-in), then LLM_Tray_SaveConfig() + LLM_Engine_Init(LLM_Tray_BuildOpts()) + LLM_Tray_Build(). Re-run LLM_Tray_BindProfileHotkeys() since the hotkey order changes.
- [x] [`nav-val-modifiers-not-wired`](#f-nav-val-modifiers-not-wired) — nav_modifiers / val_modifiers settings only relabel the tooltip hint — the real slot-nav hotkeys are hardcoded and collide with Ctrl+<n>  
  _Fix :_ Drive the slot-nav bindings from configuration like the profile hotkeys do: add an LLM_Tray_BindNavHotkeys() that, under HotIf(LLM_Tooltip_GetText() != ''), registers Up/Down and 1..9 with the prefix derived from nav_modifiers/val_modifiers (reusing LLM_Tray_ShortcutToAhk's modifier parsing), Off-ing the previous set on each change. Call it from LLM_Tray_Init and after LLM_Tray_PromptNavModifiers/PromptValModifiers. Also detect and warn on the ctrl collision with the profile hotkeys.
- [ ] [`appstate-orphaned-parallel-state`](#f-appstate-orphaned-parallel-state) — app_state.ahk AppState Map and all its accessors are included but never used by production — a single-source-of-truth trap with parallel standalone globals  
  _Fix :_ Either (a) finish the cut-over: make the standalone globals (NumberOfRepetitions, LastSentCharacterKeyTime, ActivitySimulation, OneShotShiftEnabled, RemappedList) thin readers/writers of the AppState Map (or delete the globals and route all sites through AppState_* accessors), so there is exactly one copy; or (b) if the cut-over is abandoned, delete app_state.ahk's unused fields/accessors and the #Include, per rule 5.6 (no dead/duplicate state). Do not leave both alive.
- [ ] [`pathstoml-read-missing-utf8-encoding`](#f-pathstoml-read-missing-utf8-encoding) — ReadPathsToml reads paths.toml without the UTF-8 encoding arg, so a non-ASCII custom config dir is mis-decoded and the user's config silently goes missing  
  _Fix :_ Change line 512 to `FileRead(FilePath, 'UTF-8')` to match the writer and every other reader in the unit. Consider also reading via ReadTomlFile for cache consistency, but at minimum fix the encoding.
- [ ] [`traymenu-separator-addstandard`](#f-traymenu-separator-addstandard) — TrayMenu setMenu renders a 'separator' item as the entire AHK standard menu  
  _Fix :_ Replace `try A_TrayMenu.AddStandard()` with `try A_TrayMenu.Add()` (no arguments) in the separator branch.
- [ ] [`uridecode-multibyte-utf8-corruption`](#f-uridecode-multibyte-utf8-corruption) — UriDecode mangles non-ASCII paths: byte-wise %XX → Chr() yields NUL/replacement chars for multibyte UTF-8 sequences  
  _Fix :_ Decode into a byte Buffer and StrGet as UTF-8: accumulate consecutive %XX bytes (and literal ASCII bytes) into a Buffer via NumPut('UChar', value, buf, i), then `return StrGet(buf, byteLen, 'UTF-8')`. This correctly reassembles multibyte UTF-8 sequences while leaving ASCII unchanged. Keep the lone-'%' passthrough behaviour the existing tests assert.
- [ ] [`onkeydown-dead-llm-branch`](#f-onkeydown-dead-llm-branch) — Unreachable LLM-feed branch in prefix-watcher OnKeyDown swallows Tab/Enter/Escape LLM flushes  
  _Fix :_ Collapse the LLM feed into the live branches: call `LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive(VK)` for Enter inside the Tab/Enter clause (after the Tab-accept early return), and remove the dead third else-if. Mirror the macOS flush-on-Enter contract.
- [ ] [`paste-plain-destroys-nontext-clip`](#f-paste-plain-destroys-nontext-clip) — Paste-without-formatting (Ctrl+Shift+V) and GesturePastePlain destroy non-text clipboard via A_Clipboard := A_Clipboard  
  _Fix :_ Guard the strip: only do `A_Clipboard := A_Clipboard` when CB_Read()/A_Clipboard is non-empty text (i.e. DllCall IsClipboardFormatAvailable(CF_UNICODETEXT) or a non-empty A_Clipboard). When the clipboard is non-text, fall through to a normal SendFinalResult('^v') without the destructive round-trip.
- [ ] [`inline-autotype-not-synthetic`](#f-inline-autotype-not-synthetic) — Inline-autotype predictions are typed without KL_MarkSynthetic and never appended to the rolling buffer  
  _Fix :_ In the inline branch of LLM_Engine_OnResults, mirror LLM_Bridge_OnAccept: call KL_MarkSynthetic("llm") before TextSend, schedule SetTimer((*) => KL_ClearSynthetic(), -SYNTH_CLEAR_DELAY_MS) after, and append the typed text to _LLM_Bridge_Buffer (or route the inline insertion through a shared helper that LLM_Bridge_OnAccept also calls, so the synthetic-tag + buffer-advance logic lives in exactly one place per rule 5.2).

### P3 — Low (96)

- [ ] [`traymenu-setmenu-raw-add-drops-clicks`](#f-traymenu-setmenu-raw-add-drops-clicks) — TrayMenu adapter setMenu uses raw A_TrayMenu.Add, bypassing the mandatory RegisterMenuItem dispatch-bypass  
  _Fix :_ In TrayMenuSetMenu, route actionable items through `RegisterMenuItem(A_TrayMenu, ItemTitle, ItemFn)` instead of `A_TrayMenu.Add(ItemTitle, ItemFn)`. Keep raw Add only for separators/submenus/disabled headers. Since A_TrayMenu.Delete() wipes items, also prune the corresponding entries from _MenuDispatchCallbacks/_MenuDispatchLastFire (or accept stale-ID growth) to avoid unbounded Map growth on repeated setMenu calls.
- [ ] [`winorder-unbounded-and-cross-thread`](#f-winorder-unbounded-and-cross-thread) — _GestureWinOrder grows unbounded and is reassigned non-atomically across the WinEvent callback / cycle threads  
  _Fix :_ Cap _GestureWinOrder length (e.g. keep the most-recent N=64) when prepending in _GestureOnForeground, and/or prune entries whose HWND no longer exists at insert time. Add an A_IsSuspended early-return so the tracker does not churn while paused.
- [ ] [`password-cache-torn-write`](#f-password-cache-torn-write) — KL_AsyncPasswordDetect writes the password cache in 3 unsynchronised steps the keystroke thread can read mid-update  
  _Fix :_ Write the cache fields under Critical in KL_AsyncPasswordDetect, or publish-after-fill: write last_val first and last_hwnd LAST so a reader's hwnd-match implies last_val already corresponds to that hwnd (hwnd is the commit flag, written last).
- [ ] [`klpf-writeatomic-delete-window`](#f-klpf-writeatomic-delete-window) — KLPF_WriteAtomic deletes the destination before FileMove, leaving a window where the prefetch file is absent  
  _Fix :_ Make KLPF_WriteAtomic use the same MoveFileExW(MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING | MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH) + single-retry primitive as KL_WriteAtomic (factor it into a shared helper) so the rename is a true atomic directory-entry swap with no absent-file window.
- [x] [`synthetic-flag-global-race`](#f-synthetic-flag-global-race) — Global synthetic flag with fixed 80 ms clear can leak onto manual typing or be cleared early under rapid Tab-accepts  
  _Fix :_ Replace the boolean with a refcount or a generation id: KL_MarkSynthetic increments a counter and returns a token; KL_ClearSynthetic decrements only when the token matches the latest, so overlapping bursts keep the flag set until the LAST one clears. Alternatively clear synchronously after TextSend returns (TextSend with SendMode Input is largely synchronous) rather than on a fixed-delay timer.
- [ ] [`wpm-ring-buffer-cross-thread-race`](#f-wpm-ring-buffer-cross-thread-race) — WPM ring buffer mutated on the keyboard thread while the tick timer enumerates it  
  _Fix :_ If exactness ever matters, wrap the Push tail (the few lines that mutate _ring/_ring_head) and the Calc enumeration in 'Critical "On"/Critical "Off"'. The regions contain no Send/Sleep/blocking call so Critical will not starve the hook. Lower-cost alternative: have Calc snapshot the array length once and iterate by index up to that length.
- [ ] [`dispatcher-map-never-cleared-stale-misfire`](#f-dispatcher-map-never-cleared-stale-misfire) — Menu-dispatcher callback Maps never cleared on rebuild → reused Win32 IDs can fire the WRONG callback  
  _Fix :_ Clear both Maps at the very start of RebuildTrayMenu() (and BuildTrayMenuDeferred()) BEFORE A_TrayMenu.Delete()/InitSubMenus()/initMenu() repopulate them: `global _MenuDispatchCallbacks, _MenuDispatchLastFire` then `_MenuDispatchCallbacks := Map()` / `_MenuDispatchLastFire := Map()`. Expose a `MenuDispatcher_Reset()` in menu_dispatcher.ahk and call it from the rebuild entry point so the ownership stays in the dispatcher module. This guarantees no stale ID survives a rebuild, so a reused ID always maps to the current item or is absent (and _DispatchIfMissed's `Has(ItemId)` guard then no-ops correctly).
- [ ] [`remap-emit-critical-uneven`](#f-remap-emit-critical-uneven) — Layout remap serialization via Critical is applied to RemapKey output but NOT to the digit-row / dead-key / DigitShift hotkeys, leaving cross-handler interleave possible  
  _Fix :_ Route the digit-row and _DigitShiftSend emissions through the same serialized emit primitive (wrap their SendEvent in Critical("On") like _RemapEmit, since there is no Sleep on those paths), or convert them to a shared `_LayoutEmit` helper so every layout-layer emission shares the one atomicity contract. Leave the dead-key path as-is only if it is made non-blocking (see deadkey finding).
- [ ] [`textsend-restore-timer-race`](#f-textsend-restore-timer-race) — TextSend clipboard restore timer can clobber a clipboard a later TextSend just wrote  
  _Fix :_ Serialize clipboard injection: bump a module generation counter on each clipboard-mode TextSend, capture it in the restore closure, and have the restore no-op if the generation has advanced (another injection took over). Or cancel the previous pending restore timer before arming a new one. Sharing one clipboard-injection helper between TextSend and SendInstant would also remove the duplicated, uncoordinated logic.
- [x] [`hse-register-torn-read-vs-onchar`](#f-hse-register-torn-read-vs-onchar) — HSE_Register mutates the last-char bucket and the star indices non-atomically; an interrupting OnChar reads a half-registered Spec  
  _Fix :_ Make each HSE_Register call atomic with respect to the OnChar reader by wrapping the index-mutation block (the body from HSE_SeqCounter++ through the group-index Push, i.e. lines 259-371, or at minimum the live-index block lines 350-363) in Critical("On")/Critical(restore). There is no Sleep or blocking call in that block, so holding Critical is safe and cannot starve the hook. Save/restore the prior Critical state (PrevCrit := Critical("On") ... Critical(PrevCrit)) so it nests cleanly if a future caller is already Critical. This guarantees an OnChar either sees the Spec fully indexed in all four maps or not at all. (Alternative, lighter: reorder so the by-trigger/prefix indices are updated BEFORE the last-char bucket push, AND publish via a single atomic swap — but Critical is simpler and matches the reader-side pattern already in the codebase.)
- [x] [`rebuild-hotstrings-live-clear-rebuild-window`](#f-rebuild-hotstrings-live-clear-rebuild-window) — RebuildHotstringsLive clears then rebuilds the whole registry non-atomically; OnChar mid-rebuild reads an empty/partial registry so every expansion no-fires  
  _Fix :_ Serialize the swap against the reader. Two options: (a) wrap the HSE_RegistryClear()+HSE_HardReset()+RegisterAllHotstrings() trio in Critical("On")/restore — but RegisterAllHotstrings does file I/O on the first call, and Critical must not span blocking I/O, so first call HotstringsCacheEnsure() OUTSIDE Critical, then take Critical only around the clear+register. Better: (b) build the new registry into FRESH local Maps and publish them with a single atomic pointer swap under a brief Critical, so the reader never sees an empty registry — the old registry stays live and matchable until the instant the new one is ready. Option (b) eliminates the dead window entirely. At minimum, gate HSE_FindMatchAtEnd via a module-level `HSE_RebuildInProgress` generation flag set true across the rebuild so the reader can fall back gracefully instead of silently no-matching.
- [ ] [`deferred-menu-critical-file-io`](#f-deferred-menu-critical-file-io) — BuildTrayMenuDeferred holds Critical across InitSubMenus/initMenu, which read TOML files from the (possibly cloud-synced) config dir  
  _Fix :_ Do the file I/O OUTSIDE Critical: pre-read/parse all TOMLs into in-memory structures first (no Critical), then take Critical only around the pure Win32 Menu.Add/RegisterMenuItem calls that must be one uninterrupted pass. Alternatively warm the personal/section caches on a normal (non-Critical) deferred tick before BuildTrayMenuDeferred so InitSubMenus hits only caches under Critical. _HS_PreScanPersonal already has a cache flag (_HS_PreScanPersonalCacheLoaded) — populate it off-Critical first.
- [ ] [`error-handler-heavy-diagnostics`](#f-error-handler-heavy-diagnostics) — Global error handler runs WMI queries + git RunWait (x3) synchronously while modifiers may be stuck  
  _Fix :_ Collect the heavy diagnostics (WMI CPU, git hash) ONCE and cache them at boot (they don't change during a session), so the error handler reuses cached values instead of re-querying. Call HealthCheck_Run() at most once per crash and pass the result into CrashReport_Build. Consider dropping the git RunWait entirely in compiled mode (no repo) and giving every RunWait a finite path / timeout.
- [ ] [`textsend-clipwait-blocks-input-thread`](#f-textsend-clipwait-blocks-input-thread) — TextSend clipboard path can block the input thread up to 1s on ClipWait and fires callback before paste completes  
  _Fix :_ Reduce the ClipWait timeout to a small value (e.g. ClipWait(0.2)) and proceed/log on timeout rather than blocking a full second; or move the clipboard write+paste fully onto a SetTimer(-0) so it never runs synchronously on the input-gating thread. Fire Callback only after the paste has been issued, and document that completion is best-effort for the async clipboard path.
- [ ] [`tooltip-hide-inside-critical-finally`](#f-tooltip-hide-inside-critical-finally) — TooltipHide (GUI teardown) runs inside the outer Critical from _OnPrefixChar, risking message-pump starvation if it ever blocks  
  _Fix :_ Either move the tooltip hide out of the Critical region (defer it via the same SetTimer that releases suppression, accepting a 1-frame later hide) OR add an explicit invariant comment + a meta/static assertion that TooltipHide contains no Sleep/WinWait/blocking-await, mirroring the existing input-serialization meta-test that pins the Notepad branch releasing Critical before its Sleep.
- [ ] [`send-instant-sleep-clipboard-on-keyboard-thread`](#f-send-instant-sleep-clipboard-on-keyboard-thread) — SendInstant blocks the keyboard thread for 200 ms and round-trips the clipboard inside a wrap  
  _Fix :_ Serialise clipboard-using sends (a process-wide reentrancy guard / Critical-bracketed clipboard section that still yields for the paste but blocks a second clipboard dance from starting), and/or replace the fixed 200 ms Sleep with a ClipWait-style confirmation bounded by a small timeout. Prefer sending the wrapped text via SendEvent("{Text}") when the payload is small enough that the clipboard route is unnecessary (the clipboard route is only needed for large payloads per the module header).
- [ ] [`activate-hotstrings-sleep-on-keyboard-thread`](#f-activate-hotstrings-sleep-on-keyboard-thread) — ActivateHotstrings injects a 50 ms blocking Sleep on the keyboard thread for Shift French-punctuation keys  
  _Fix :_ Avoid the synchronous space/backspace+Sleep poke for layout punctuation: have the hotstring engine commit the pending buffer via a direct API call (a 'flush pending hotstring now' function) instead of the Sleep-based space dance, or reduce/parameterise the delay. If the dance is unavoidable, gate it so it only runs when there is actually a pending hotstring abbreviation in the buffer (cheap to check) rather than on every Shift-punctuation press.
- [x] [`simulateactivity-timer-sleep-blocks-and-leaks`](#f-simulateactivity-timer-sleep-blocks-and-leaks) — GestureSimulateActivity Sleeps up to 6.4s on its timer thread and the recurring timer is never disarmed on toggle-off  
  _Fix :_ Superseded by the activitysim-global-collision fix (delete this function and delegate to win.ahk, which already uses a self-rescheduling single tick without a 6s Sleep loop). If kept standalone: replace the Sleep loop with a single MCSetPos per tick, re-arm via SetTimer(GestureSimulateActivity, -Random(...)) (negative = one-shot, self-rescheduled), and call SetTimer(GestureSimulateActivity, 0) in the toggle-off branch.
- [ ] [`tooltip-teardown-on-keyboard-thread`](#f-tooltip-teardown-on-keyboard-thread) — Per-keystroke prediction dismissal runs synchronous DWM/GDI window teardown on the InputHook thread  
  _Fix :_ Defer the hide off the hook: in OnChar, set the visibility flags to hidden immediately (so logic is consistent) but schedule the actual window teardown via SetTimer(..., -1) so the GDI/DWM work runs on a fresh thread after the hook returns. Mirror what the auto-hide TimerFn already does (it runs off-thread). Keep the buffer update + LLM_Engine_OnKeystroke synchronous (cheap), only defer the expensive window destruction.
- [ ] [`stream-final-flush-sleep-blocks-thread`](#f-stream-final-flush-sleep-blocks-thread) — Streaming final-flush loop blocks the AHK thread up to 200 ms with synchronous Sleep(40)  
  _Fix :_ Replace the in-callback Sleep loop with a re-armed one-shot timer: if acc is still empty and the file is non-empty, schedule another _LLM_Ollama_StreamPoll tick (e.g. -40 ms) instead of Sleeping. This keeps the message pump live between reads.
- [ ] [`remote-api-validate-blocks-message-pump`](#f-remote-api-validate-blocks-message-pump) — Saving an API entry runs a synchronous 2s WinHTTP ping on the main thread (LLM_RemoteIsReady)  
  _Fix :_ Use an async WinHTTP probe (Http.Open(..., true) + poll, like LLM_OllamaIsRunning_Async) and surface the success/failure TrayTip from the async callback, so the menu/save path returns immediately and the message pump keeps running. At minimum drop the per-phase timeout (e.g. 1500ms total) to shrink the worst-case stall.
- [ ] [`textsend-clipboard-blocks-and-races`](#f-textsend-clipboard-blocks-and-races) — TextSend clipboard branch blocks the keyboard thread on ClipWait and races a 150 ms restore  
  _Fix :_ Do the clipboard round-trip off the keyboard thread: schedule the CB_Write/ClipWait/^v sequence on a one-shot SetTimer(-1) so the hotkey thread returns immediately; or lower/abort the ClipWait timeout and guard restore with a generation counter so a newer clipboard write is never overwritten by a stale restore. Match the hotstring engine pattern (snapshot via ClipboardAll, restore in finally).
- [ ] [`kl-refresh-context-blocks-on-keystroke`](#f-kl-refresh-context-blocks-on-keystroke) — KL_Hook_RefreshContext runs blocking Win32 window queries (and a synchronous flush+writes) on the keystroke that crosses the 1 s cache boundary  
  _Fix :_ Move context refresh off the keystroke thread entirely: run KL_Hook_RefreshContext (and MF_RefreshFocus) from a dedicated low-frequency SetTimer (e.g. 250-500 ms) rather than lazily from inside OnChar/OnKeyDown. The hook callbacks then read the cached Keylogger.session_app/title with zero Win32 cost. Ensure the app/title-switch flush and its KL_AppendLog writes also run on that timer (ties into kl-synchronous-disk-write). Where a synchronous WinGet* is unavoidable, the title/process queries already tolerate failure (wrapped in try) but should also be bounded — prefer DllCall(GetWindowThreadProcessId)+QueryFullProcessImageName style probes that do not pump the target window's message queue.
- [ ] [`textsend-clipwait-blocks-kbd-thread`](#f-textsend-clipwait-blocks-kbd-thread) — TextSend clipboard branch blocks the keyboard thread with ClipWait(1) and ignores its timeout  
  _Fix :_ Check ClipWait's return and bail loudly on timeout (LoggerError + abort the paste / fall back to SendText) instead of pasting blindly. Better: avoid the blocking wait on the keyboard thread entirely — write the clipboard, then schedule the paste via a negative SetTimer so the keyboard thread returns immediately, or cap to a much smaller timeout. At minimum, never SendInput('^v') when ClipWait returned false.
- [ ] [`getselection-blocks-and-eats-keys`](#f-getselection-blocks-and-eats-keys) — GetSelection blocks the keyboard thread up to 2s on ClipWait and silently swallows a failed copy  
  _Fix :_ Check ClipWait's return; on false, log and return '' so callers no-op instead of pasting stale clipboard. Lower the timeout for an interactive chord (e.g. 0.3-0.5 s) since a real selection copies near-instantly. Optionally short-circuit when the active-app cache indicates an app known to be slow/unresponsive to programmatic copy.
- [ ] [`roi-full-map-prune-scan-on-hot-path`](#f-roi-full-map-prune-scan-on-hot-path) — KL_Roi_ProcessWord runs an O(n) full-map prune scan on the keystroke thread once word_counts exceeds 500 entries  
  _Fix :_ Make the prune bounded and guaranteed-shrinking: when over cap, evict to a target (e.g. keep the top MAX_TRACKED_WORDS/2 by count, or evict the lowest-count half) so the map is always brought strictly below the cap and the scan amortises. Better, move the prune off the hot path entirely — run it from the (off-thread) ingest tick or a low-frequency SetTimer that snapshots under Critical. At minimum, gate the prune behind a counter so it runs at most once per N words, not on every boundary while saturated.
- [ ] [`oneshotshift-lalt-lshift-stuck`](#f-oneshotshift-lalt-lshift-stuck) — LAlt one_shot_shift arms LShift Down across an unbounded KeyWait — LShift latches if the chord is interrupted  
  _Fix :_ Guard the LShift Down/Up pair with try/finally: TextPressKey('LShift','Down'); try { KeyWait('SC038') } finally { TextPressKey('LShift','Up') }. Add a timeout to the KeyWait so a lost up-event cannot block forever (e.g. KeyWait('SC038','U T5')) and still release LShift. Apply the same finally guard to rctrl.ahk 7.3.
- [ ] [`deadkey-inputhook-no-timeout-no-suspend-guard`](#f-deadkey-inputhook-no-timeout-no-suspend-guard) — Dead-key InputHook has no timeout and no A_IsSuspended guard — InDeadKeySequence can latch and eat a later keystroke  
  _Fix :_ Set a bounded ih.Timeout (e.g. a few seconds) or pass a timeout to ih.Wait(); on timeout, set InDeadKeySequence := false and emit the bare dead-key glyph (or nothing). Add an A_IsSuspended check at the top of DeadKey() to no-op when suspended, and stop/cancel any in-flight dead-key InputHook in Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter() while clearing InDeadKeySequence in Ergopti_OnSuspendResume(). Keep a reference to the live ih so resume can call ih.Stop().
- [ ] [`pointer-watch-timer-bypasses-suspend`](#f-pointer-watch-timer-bypasses-suspend) — Pointer-dismiss 50 ms poll timer keeps running while paused and is not stopped on suspend  
  _Fix :_ Add `if A_IsSuspended return` at the top of _LLM_PointerWatch_OnMoveTick, OR call _LLM_PointerWatch_Stop() from Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter and _LLM_PointerWatch_Start() from Ergopti_OnSuspendResume (only when the bridge is active).
- [ ] [`dequeue-poll-no-suspend-guard`](#f-dequeue-poll-no-suspend-guard) — Dequeue poll timer repaints the tooltip on screen for up to ~500 ms after an external Suspend  
  _Fix :_ Add 'if A_IsSuspended { return }' at the very top of _TooltipDequeuePollFn (mirroring TooltipShow:277 and WPMWidget_Tick:857). Optionally also early-out in _TooltipDequeueRebuild for defence in depth. Leaving the items in place (rather than clearing them) is fine — the items will simply be re-evaluated on resume, or torn down by the watchdog's TooltipHide.
- [ ] [`health-probe-timer-not-suspend-guarded`](#f-health-probe-timer-not-suspend-guarded) — Background 10s LLM health-probe SetTimer keeps pinging Ollama while the script is suspended (pause invariant)  
  _Fix :_ Add `if A_IsSuspended return` at the top of _LLM_Tray_FireHealthProbe (and skip the LLM_Tray_Build() in _LLM_Tray_OnHealthProbeDone when A_IsSuspended), matching the pattern already used in hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk / tooltip.ahk / keylogger_hook.ahk.
- [ ] [`no-suspend-guard-on-background-tick-and-focus-poll`](#f-no-suspend-guard-on-background-tick-and-focus-poll) — Updater background tick and PLC focus poller ignore A_IsSuspended — pause does not silence them (project pause invariant)  
  _Fix :_ Add 'if A_IsSuspended return' at the top of PLC_Poll (after re-arm is not needed since SetTimer is periodic) and at the top of Updater_BackgroundTick AFTER re-arming the next tick (so the loop survives pause and resumes cleanly) — i.e. re-arm the timer, then 'if A_IsSuspended return' before the network dispatch. For PLC_Poll, simply early-return so no callbacks fire while suspended.
- [ ] [`llm-pointer-watch-not-stopped-on-suspend`](#f-llm-pointer-watch-not-stopped-on-suspend) — LLM pointer-dismiss poll timer keeps firing (MouseGetPos every poll) while the driver is paused  
  _Fix :_ In Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2009 region) add `try _LLM_PointerWatch_Stop()` alongside the existing LLM_Tooltip_Hide / LLM_Engine_CancelTimer calls, and re-arm it from Ergopti_OnSuspendResume only if the bridge is active (or simply add `if A_IsSuspended return` at the top of _LLM_PointerWatch_OnMoveTick so the poll is inert while paused). Stopping the timer is preferable since it also frees the recurring thread.
- [ ] [`no-onexit-keylogger-flush`](#f-no-onexit-keylogger-flush) — No global OnExit handler — keylogger buffer + pending events lost on every Reload/exit  
  _Fix :_ Register a single global shutdown handler in ErgoptiPlus.ahk's auto-execute section (after the keylogger is started), e.g. OnExit(Ergopti_OnShutdown) where Ergopti_OnShutdown(reason, code){ try KL_Stop(); try HotstringPrefixWatcherStop(); try HookDispatcher.Stop(); try KLWV_CloseAll(); try OllamaWV_Close(); return 0 }. KL_Stop is already idempotent (guards on Keylogger.initialized) and already flushes+ingests+saves, so wiring it to OnExit is sufficient for the data-loss part. Keep the handler fast and fully try-wrapped (an OnExit callback that throws is swallowed and can hang exit).
- [x] [`metrics-timers-bypass-pause`](#f-metrics-timers-bypass-pause) — Sensor / ingest / flush / live-push timers keep running and emitting under pause (A_IsSuspended) — violates the 'pause = tout eteint' invariant  
  _Fix :_ Add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` at the top of KL_Sensors_Tick, KL_IngestOnce (after the initialized check, before any disk work), KL_Hook_Tick and KL_Hook_LivePush — or, cleaner, centralise it: have these ticks early-return when A_IsSuspended so they re-arm but do nothing (matching the TimerEvery contract asserted in tests/test_timer_scheduler.ahk:233). Note: KL_IngestOnce on Stop still needs a final drain, so guard the periodic-tick entry, not the explicit Stop-path call.
- [ ] [`prefix-index-rebuild-no-suspend-guard`](#f-prefix-index-rebuild-no-suspend-guard) — HotstringPrefixWatcherRebuildIndex runs (clear + repopulate of the index OnChar reads) with no A_IsSuspended guard and no atomicity vs OnChar  
  _Fix :_ Add `if A_IsSuspended { return }` (or a short re-arm) at the top of HotstringPrefixWatcherRebuildIndex, mirroring _OnPrefixChar's guard, so the rebuild does not run during Pause. For the atomicity gap, build the new _PrefixIndex/_TriggerSet into locals and assign them in one statement each at the end (Map() clear last), so the reader never observes an empty index mid-rebuild.
- [x] [`webview-udir-temp-leak`](#f-webview-udir-temp-leak) — Changelog and Healthcheck WebView2 user-data dirs are created per-open in %TEMP% and never deleted  
  _Fix :_ On window close (_CLW_OnClose/_CLW_Reset and the healthcheck Close handler), after Destroy()/Controller release, try DirDelete(udir, true) wrapped in try (the controller must be torn down first to release the handle). Or reuse a single stable per-feature user-data dir (e.g. A_Temp\ergopti_changelog_wv) and only fall back to a tick suffix if the stable dir is locked, so at most one dir persists.
- [ ] [`dispatcher-stop-not-wired-and-leaks`](#f-dispatcher-stop-not-wired-and-leaks) — HookDispatcher.Stop is never registered with OnExit and never clears _subscribers (InputHook leak across non-Reload exit)  
  _Fix :_ Either wire `OnExit((*) => HookDispatcher.Stop())` at startup (after Start) or correct the docstring to say cleanup relies on process teardown/Reload. In Stop(), clear `_subscribers := Map()` (or document deliberate persistence) and reset the _hk_* references to unset so Start() rebuilds cleanly.
- [ ] [`altgr-shortcut-hotkeys-reregister-unguarded`](#f-altgr-shortcut-hotkeys-reregister-unguarded) — _RegisterAltGrShortcutsHotkeys re-binds SC138 combos with no idempotence guard or logging  
  _Fix :_ Add a module-level `_AltGrShortcutsRegistered := false` guard: warn+return if already true, else set true. Wrap registration in a LoggerStart/LoggerSuccess pair so any future double-call is visible in the log (errors thrown here would otherwise be swallowed).
- [ ] [`kl-stop-dead-no-exit-flush`](#f-kl-stop-dead-no-exit-flush) — KL_Stop() is never called (no OnExit hook); a Reload loses the unflushed buffer and unsaved state, risking event-id collisions  
  _Fix :_ Register `OnExit(KL_OnExit)` in KL_Init where KL_OnExit(*) calls KL_Stop() (already idempotent, guarded by Keylogger.initialized) so flush+save+close run before teardown. Separately harden against id reuse: at startup derive next_event_id as MAX(persisted next_event_id, 1 + max id already present for this device) rather than trusting state.json alone. Remove the dead-code ambiguity by wiring KL_Stop rather than leaving it dangling.
- [ ] [`klw-ctx-unbounded-hist-growth`](#f-klw-ctx-unbounded-hist-growth) — Per-app walker ctx hist array grows unbounded within a day and the full ctx is JSON-serialised into state.json each tick  
  _Fix :_ Cap ctx["hist"] to the deepest n-gram order the walker reads back (~8, matching the heptagram depth), trimming with RemoveAt(1) past the cap like recent_typing. Optionally prune KLW.ctx of apps not seen recently.
- [ ] [`webview-temp-dir-and-com-leak-on-reload`](#f-webview-temp-dir-and-com-leak-on-reload) — WebView2 per-launch temp dirs and COM controllers are never released across Reload  
  _Fix :_ Mirror the Edge sweep: before DirCreate(udir) in KLWV_Open, `loop files, A_Temp . '\ergopti_webview2_*', 'D'` and best-effort DirDelete each (skipping the just-allocated path, as the Edge path does). Register KLWV_CloseAll() via OnExit so Reload/ExitApp closes controllers and destroys Guis. Optionally delete entry['udir'] in KLWV_Close after the controller closes.
- [x] [`remote-async-no-timeout-deadline`](#f-remote-async-no-timeout-deadline) — Remote async poll has no deadline — a stuck WinHTTP request re-arms the 50 ms poll forever  
  _Fix :_ Mirror the Ollama fix: thread a deadline (A_TickCount + LLM_REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS + slack) through _LLMRemote_PollRequest and, when A_TickCount >= deadline, try http.Abort(), delete the registry entry, and call on_fail() once. Bind the deadline on first entry exactly like _LLM_Ollama_PollGeneric.
- [ ] [`winhttp-no-abort-on-poll-timeout`](#f-winhttp-no-abort-on-poll-timeout) — Generic/remote WinHTTP poll timeouts return without Abort(), leaking the in-flight request until GC  
  _Fix :_ On poll timeout and on the cancelled path, call try http.Abort() before deleting the registry entry (the Ollama curl path already ProcessCloses its child symmetrically). Add an absolute deadline to _LLMRemote_PollRequest (A_TickCount + LLM_REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS + slack) so a never-responding accept can't keep the poll chain re-arming indefinitely.
- [ ] [`border-gdi-cleanup-broken-nesting`](#f-border-gdi-cleanup-broken-nesting) — _TooltipShowBorder GDI-failure cleanup never returns and leaks a DC, then proceeds with a null handle  
  _Fix :_ Replace with explicit braces and unconditional cleanup+return:
  if (!HBmp or !MemDC) {
      if (HBmp)
          DllCall("Gdi32\DeleteObject", "Ptr", HBmp)
      if (MemDC)
          DllCall("Gdi32\DeleteDC", "Ptr", MemDC)
      return
  }
so both handles are released regardless of which failed and the function always bails on failure.
- [x] [`wpm-webview-temp-dir-leak`](#f-wpm-webview-temp-dir-leak) — WebView2 graph user-data temp dir is created per attach and never cleaned up  
  _Fix :_ Mirror the keylogger_ui.ahk pattern: before DirCreate(udir), 'loop files, udir_root . "_*", "D" { try DirDelete(A_LoopFileFullPath, true) }' where udir_root := A_Temp . "\ergopti_wpm_wv2". Wrap in try since a locked dir from a still-running webview process cannot always be removed.
- [ ] [`menu-dispatch-callbacks-unbounded-growth`](#f-menu-dispatch-callbacks-unbounded-growth) — Menu-dispatcher callback maps grow unbounded across the very frequent full LLM-menu rebuilds (Menu.Delete + RegisterMenuItem churn)  
  _Fix :_ Give the dispatcher a prune step keyed off the live HMENU: either expose RegisterMenu_ResetForMenu(MenuObj) that, before a rebuild, drops _MenuDispatchCallbacks/_MenuDispatchLastFire entries whose ItemId is no longer in GetMenuItemID(HMENU) for that menu; or track item IDs per owning menu and clear that bucket at the top of LLM_Tray_Build() (and other rebuilders) right after _LLM_Tray_Menu.Delete().
- [ ] [`async-requests-not-cancelled-on-channel-reload`](#f-async-requests-not-cancelled-on-channel-reload) — Updater_SetChannel calls Reload without cancelling in-flight async checks or stopping the focus poller; relies on process teardown  
  _Fix :_ Call Updater_StopBackgroundChecks() (which already calls _Updater_CancelAsyncChecks and stops the timer) at the top of Updater_SetChannel before 'Reload', mirroring what Updater_SetCheckInterval already does for the in-process restart path.
- [x] [`cb-save-text-only-data-loss`](#f-cb-save-text-only-data-loss) — CB_Save snapshots clipboard TEXT only, so clipboard-mode TextSend destroys image/file/RTF content  
  _Fix :_ Add a binary-safe save/restore to the Clipboard port (e.g. CB_SaveAll/CB_RestoreAll backed by ClipboardAll()) and have TextSend's clipboard path use it, matching SendInstant/GetSelection in hotstring_engine.ahk. Keep CB_Save text-only for callers that genuinely want text.
- [ ] [`roi-current-word-unbounded-growth`](#f-roi-current-word-unbounded-growth) — KLRoi.current_word grows unbounded when the user types a long run with no word-boundary character  
  _Fix :_ Cap current_word: if StrLen(KLRoi.current_word) exceeds a sane bound (e.g. 2*MAX_WORD_LEN or a dedicated MAX_INPROGRESS_WORD_LEN constant) reset it (treat the over-long run as not-a-word and discard) before appending. Add the constant to the KLRoiConst block per the no-magic-numbers rule.
- [ ] [`uia-error-swallowed-silently`](#f-uia-error-swallowed-silently) — GetUIASelection swallows UIA/COM errors with a catch-less try and never logs  
  _Fix :_ Convert to `try { ... } catch as e { try LoggerWarn("Layout", "UIA selection probe failed: {1}", e.Message) }` (using the try-prefixed logger pattern used elsewhere in the layout files), then return "".
