- [ ] [`ui-launch-error-msgbox-on-timer-thread`](#f-ui-launch-error-msgbox-on-timer-thread) — Dashboard launch failures pop a modal MsgBox that can fire from a non-user thread  
  _Fix :_ Route both failure paths through the central logger (LoggerError) in addition to any user-facing message, and use a non-blocking notification (tray balloon / TooltipShow) rather than a modal MsgBox so the calling thread is never blocked. Confirm KLPF_BuildAndWrite at launch uses the documented 'manifest' fast mode, never a synchronous cold full build on the launching thread.
- [ ] [`coalesced-job-callbacks-dropped`](#f-coalesced-job-callbacks-dropped) — Coalesced Ollama async job silently drops both on_success and on_fail when superseded  
  _Fix :_ When overwriting _LLM_Ollama_Pending, invoke the displaced job's on_fail() (wrapped in try) before replacing it, so the async contract holds; or document explicitly that coalesced jobs may drop callbacks and have the engine never rely on them (it already uses request_id, so just call on_fail for safety).
- [ ] [`ext-builder-fn-dynamic-call-swallow`](#f-ext-builder-fn-dynamic-call-swallow) — Extension menu builders invoked via dynamic %BuilderFn% with errors swallowed and no validation of resolved value  
  _Fix :_ On catch, render a visible disabled placeholder row in ExtMenu (e.g. t('menu.extensions.load_error') with the ExtId) so the failure is user-visible, in addition to the LoggerError (upgrade from Warn). Validate that the resolved builder actually populated at least one item; if zero items were added after a successful call, also show the error/empty marker.
- [ ] [`plc-onfocuschange-drops-closures-boundfuncs`](#f-plc-onfocuschange-drops-closures-boundfuncs) — PLC_OnFocusChange/OnAppLaunch/OnAppQuit silently drop closures and BoundFuncs because they check Type=="Func"  
  _Fix :_ Replace 'if Type(Callback) = "Func"' with a generic callable check: 'if (Callback is Func) or HasMethod(Callback, "Call")' — or simply 'if HasMethod(Callback, "Call")'. Apply to all three registration functions. Log a WARNING when a non-callable is rejected instead of silently returning.
- [ ] [`json-number-misleading-error`](#f-json-number-misleading-error) — Bare/invalid number tokens ('-', 'e', 'x') surface AHK's 'Expected a Number but got a String' instead of a JSON-position error  
  _Fix :_ After building `s` in _JsonParseNumber, if `pos == start` (no characters consumed) or `s` does not match `^-?\d`, `throw Error("JSON: invalid number at position " . start . ".", -1)` before the `s + 0` coercion. Optionally wrap `s + 0` in try and re-throw the descriptive error.
- [ ] [`global-error-handler-sendevent-storm`](#f-global-error-handler-sendevent-storm) — Global error handler force-releases all 8 modifiers via SendEvent on every uncaught error, can interleave with user input  
  _Fix :_ Only release modifiers that are LOGICALLY (not physically) stuck, or skip the release entirely when the user is still physically holding them; and surface the error via a non-blocking tray notification instead of a modal MsgBox so the keyboard hook is never starved. Keep the file logging.
- [ ] [`healthcheck-init-dead-reference`](#f-healthcheck-init-dead-reference) — HealthCheck_Init() is documented as the uptime origin but does not exist — uptime is anchored at module-load instead  
  _Fix :_ Either implement HealthCheck_Init() (set _HealthCheckStartMs := A_TickCount) and call it once at a defined boot point near LoggerInit, OR delete every HealthCheck_Init reference from the comments/docstrings and document that _HealthCheckStartMs is captured at module load. Keep the single-source-of-truth principle.
- [ ] [`dispatcher-start-unguarded-hotkeys-leak-ih`](#f-dispatcher-start-unguarded-hotkeys-leak-ih) — Start() mouse-hotkey registration is unguarded; a single Hotkey throw leaks a started InputHook and leaves _started false  
  _Fix :_ Wrap each Hotkey() in `try` (logging a WARNING on failure) so a single unsupported mouse event cannot abort the whole Start, and set _started := true immediately after the InputHook is up (before the optional mouse hotkeys) OR use a try/finally so _started reflects the InputHook being live. Guard against re-creating the InputHook if _ih is already an InputHook.
- [ ] [`parser-ord-empty-token-crash`](#f-parser-ord-empty-token-crash) — Ord() on an empty token in the advanced parser anchor logic can throw inside an async callback (swallowed)  
  _Fix :_ Wrap LLM_Parser_ProcessPrediction's body (or _LLM_Engine_ParseSlots) in try/catch that LoggerWarn("LLM.parser", ...) the message + a snippet of the offending block and returns "" — so an edge-input crash degrades to a failed variant WITH a log line instead of a silent swallow. Defensive nil/empty guards before each Ord() call.
- [ ] [`personal-ext-scan-unbounded-recursion`](#f-personal-ext-scan-unbounded-recursion) — Unbounded recursive personal-hotstrings directory scan crashes on a symlink/junction cycle  
  _Fix :_ Pass a depth counter (cap at e.g. 16) and/or maintain a Map of canonical absolute paths already visited; skip a directory whose resolved path is already in the set, and stop descending past the cap with a LoggerWarn. Wrap the top-level _HS_ScanExt call in try/catch so a runaway scan degrades to 'no extension hotstrings' instead of crashing the menu build.
- [ ] [`numeric-prompt-throws-on-nonnumeric`](#f-numeric-prompt-throws-on-nonnumeric) — Numeric setting prompts crash on non-numeric input (Integer()/Float() throw) — only the Ollama-port prompt guards with IsInteger  
  _Fix :_ Guard before converting, mirroring LLM_Tray_PromptOllamaPort: in PromptNumeric `if !IsInteger(ib.Value) return` (or show a localized 'invalid number' TrayTip); in PromptMaxWords same; in PromptTemperature `if !IsNumber(ib.Value) return` and accept comma decimals via StrReplace(ib.Value, ',', '.') before Float(). Keep the existing range checks after conversion.
- [ ] [`time-activation-fails-open-on-missing-prev-char`](#f-time-activation-fails-open-on-missing-prev-char) — IsTimeActivationExpired defaults the previous-char timestamp to 'now', so the activation-delay gate fails OPEN when the prev char was never tracked  
  _Fix :_ When LastSentCharacterKeyTime has no entry for PreviousCharacter, treat it as expired (return True) for any positive OptionTimeActivationSeconds — fail closed, not open. I.e. default CharacterSentTime to 0 (or A_TickCount - large) so the elapsed time exceeds the window, matching the gate's intent ('only fire when we can prove the prior char was typed recently').
- [x] [`starcover-ci-suppresses-cs-endchar-overbroad`](#f-starcover-ci-suppresses-cs-endchar-overbroad) — Case-insensitive star trigger over-suppresses a case-SENSITIVE end-char trigger sharing the same body  
  _Fix :_ Tighten the CI cover-check so it only suppresses when a star trigger that the typed casing could actually extend toward exists — i.e. confirm the matched star prefix corresponds to a registered star trigger reachable from the typed EndChar in the relevant case bucket, rather than suppressing on any lowercased-prefix hit. Alternatively, record on each prefix entry whether it came from a CI or CS star and require a casing-compatible match.
- [ ] [`space-altgr-double-raalt-down`](#f-space-altgr-double-raalt-down) — _SpaceHoldAltGr sends '{RAlt Down}' twice and never the intermediate Up, risking a stuck/duplicated AltGr modifier  
  _Fix :_ Remove the redundant second '{RAlt Down}': send '{RAlt Down}' once, send the captured char with the existing '{RAlt}'-down context, then release in a finally: SendInput('{RAlt Down}'); try { if captured... SendInput(captured); KeyWait('SC039','U T2') } finally { SendInput('{RAlt Up}') }. Mirror _SpaceHoldShift's decision about whether to re-send the captured char to avoid double-translation.
- [ ] [`lalt-altgr-capslock-no-priorkey-min-duration`](#f-lalt-altgr-capslock-no-priorkey-min-duration) — CapsLock and LAlt layer/special blocks resolve tap via A_TickCount diff without the TapMinDurationMs floor, allowing spurious tap fires mid-chord  
  _Fix :_ Apply the same guards uniformly: in capslock 2.4 add 'and A_PriorKey == "CapsLock"' to the tap condition, and add a '(Now - CharTime) >= TapMinDurationMs()' lower bound to capslock 2.4, lalt 4.2 and lalt 4.8 so an ultra-fast chord brush is not counted as an intentional tap.
- [ ] [`capslock-led-multiple-owners`](#f-capslock-led-multiple-owners) — CapsWord, nav-layer, and hardware CapsLock states fight over the single physical CapsLock LED  
  _Fix :_ Make UpdateCapsLockLED the single LED owner and route ToggleCapsLock through it, computing desired LED = (CapsWordEnabled or LayerEnabled or real_capslock_toggle) consistently; or document that the hardware CapsLock toggle action and the CapsWord/layer LED are deliberately separate and never combined.
- [ ] [`config-shortcuts-array-parse-escape-bug`](#f-config-shortcuts-array-parse-escape-bug) — CS_CoerceValue array tokenizer mishandles escaped quotes / embedded commas in disabled-app names  
  _Fix :_ Fix the tokenizer to track in_str from the raw character stream with a proper escape flag (check the previous raw char, not the accumulator), and do NOT re-run CS_CoerceValue's string-unescape on already-extracted quoted elements (unescape exactly once). Add a comment that array elements may contain commas inside quotes.
- [ ] [`lock-workstation-lambda-implicit-concat`](#f-lock-workstation-lambda-implicit-concat) — The #+L LockWorkStation hotkey relies on implicit string concatenation to run two statements in one arrow body  
  _Fix :_ Replace the arrow with a named helper (e.g. _LockWorkstationEmit(Character)) that calls DllCall("LockWorkStation") then UpdateLastSentCharacter(Character) on explicit separate lines, ideally routed through the same Critical-serialised pattern as _RemapEmit, and bind it like the other handlers.
- [ ] [`gesturepickcolor-clipboard-clobber`](#f-gesturepickcolor-clipboard-clobber) — GesturePickColor and GesturePastePlain overwrite the clipboard without restore  
  _Fix :_ For GesturePickColor, optionally save/restore or just document. For GesturePastePlain, save OldClip := ClipboardAll() before the coercion and restore in a finally after the ^v settle, so the user's original (possibly non-text) clipboard survives.
- [ ] [`lost-tick-after-filtered-keystroke`](#f-lost-tick-after-filtered-keystroke) — last_tick is not updated when a keystroke is privacy-filtered, poisoning the next keystroke's delay/burst/session gap  
  _Fix :_ Update KLHook.last_tick (and drive KL_Watchers_OnKeystroke) BEFORE the MF_ShouldFilter early-return, since the physical key WAS pressed — only the content must be dropped, not the timing watermark. Move the watermark update to the top of both callbacks, guarded only by A_IsSuspended and Keylogger.initialized.
- [ ] [`topo-checkvirtualdesktop-stale-prev-hwnd`](#f-topo-checkvirtualdesktop-stale-prev-hwnd) — Virtual-desktop heuristic reads prev_hwnd after it was already overwritten  
  _Fix :_ Decide the reference window explicitly: compute `last_seen := KLTopo.seen_hwnds.Has(hwnd) ? KLTopo.seen_hwnds[hwnd] : 0` BEFORE updating seen_hwnds, pass it into the heuristic, and only then set seen_hwnds[hwnd] and prev_hwnd. Document that the alive-check is on the OUTGOING window and the gap is on the INCOMING window.
- [ ] [`av-focus-mode-dead-code`](#f-av-focus-mode-dead-code) — Focus-mode detection is fully wired but permanently disabled, so focus_mode_end on shutdown can never fire  
  _Fix :_ Either remove KL_AV_PollFocusMode, focus_active, and the focus_mode_end shutdown emit entirely (per rule 5.6) until a non-blocking focus-state source exists, or replace the registry walk with the non-blocking WNF/notification API and re-enable it. If kept disabled, leave a single explanatory comment, not the full machinery.
- [ ] [`stale-cache-survives-reset-and-pause`](#f-stale-cache-survives-reset-and-pause) — last_ctx / last_results survive reset and pause, allowing a stale prediction to re-render on identical context  
  _Fix :_ In LLM_Engine_StopGeneration (and from Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter), clear _LLM_Engine["last_ctx"]:="" and _LLM_Engine["last_results"]:=[] (and last_result:="") so the cache cannot replay across an explicit reset/pause. If a soft-cache across pause is desired, gate the cache branches on an epoch counter bumped by reset/suspend.
- [ ] [`remote-parse-first-content-match`](#f-remote-parse-first-content-match) — Remote response parser grabs the FIRST "content"/"text" string — can return wrong or partial text on multi-block / error bodies  
  _Fix :_ Parse with JsonParse and navigate the canonical path per format (openai: choices[1].message.content; anthropic: first content[] entry with type=='text'; gemini: candidates[1].content.parts[1].text), falling back to the current regex only if structured parse misses. Reuse the same JsonParse the Ollama path already uses.
- [ ] [`insert-id-discovery-label-collision`](#f-insert-id-discovery-label-collision) — RegisterMenuItemInsert resolves the new item's ID by visible text — duplicate labels in one menu bind the wrong ID  
  _Fix :_ Resolve the inserted item's position deterministically: BeforeItem already encodes the 1-based insert position (e.g. '1&'), so compute the inserted item's index from that and read its ID by position via GetMenuItemID, instead of matching text. If position cannot be derived, fall back to matching but require the match to be unique (count occurrences; return 0 and log if >1) so a collision degrades to native dispatch rather than binding the wrong ID.
- [ ] [`counttoml-overcounts-personal-meta-sections`](#f-counttoml-overcounts-personal-meta-sections) — CountTomlSection/CountTomlHotstrings count [_meta.sections.<name>] metadata keys as hotstrings, inflating personal-category counts in the tray  
  _Fix :_ Change the exclusion test from equality to a prefix/startswith check: skip any section whose lowercased name == '_meta' OR starts with '_meta.' (covers '_meta.sections', '_meta.sections.<x>', '_meta.section_delays'). e.g. `if (CurrentSec != '' and SubStr(CurrentSec,1,5) != '_meta')` plus the exact '_meta' case, mirroring ParseTomlFile's general '[_*] is metadata' convention used by toml_config_loader.
- [ ] [`strict-canon-does-not-drop-stale-keys`](#f-strict-canon-does-not-drop-stale-keys) — TOML_RunStrictCanonicalization/SaveFullConfig do NOT drop stale sections/keys as documented — BatchWrite merges rather than rebuilds  
  _Fix :_ Either (a) make the contract true — have SaveFullConfig pass a 'rebuild from scratch' flag to a variant of BatchWrite that starts from an empty Sections map (and explicitly re-add every section that must persist, including ahk.personal_editor), or (b) correct the docstrings on TOML_RunStrictCanonicalization and SaveFullConfig to state that untouched sections are PRESERVED (merge semantics) and remove the 'stale keys are dropped' claim. Pick one; do not keep the false claim.
- [ ] [`format-toml-stale-path-deadcode`](#f-format-toml-stale-path-deadcode) — TOML_FormatViaScript and the two personal_toml_editor formatters point at a non-existent repo layout (static\drivers\autohotkey + tools\format_toml.py) — the formatter never runs  
  _Fix :_ Fix the path to the real layout: derive RepoRoot by stripping `\static\ergopti_plus\windows$` and look for `tools\dev\format_toml.py`; OR if the Python pass is intentionally retired, delete all three RunWait blocks and their comments (rule 5.6). If kept and wired live, ensure it never runs synchronously inside SaveFullConfig's save path during typing — run it deferred/off-thread.
- [ ] [`parsetomlgroupconfig-missing-file-cache-key-mismatch`](#f-parsetomlgroupconfig-missing-file-cache-key-mismatch) — ParseTomlGroupConfig caches the missing-file Config under LowerCat instead of CacheKey, so explicit-path calls never cache-hit and invalidation cannot reach it  
  _Fix :_ Store the missing-file Config under CacheKey, not LowerCat: change line 479 to `HotstringGroupConfig[CacheKey] := Config`. This makes the cache key consistent across both branches and lets _ParseTomlGroupConfig_InvalidatePath reach it.
- [ ] [`reg-keyexists-false-negative-value-only-key`](#f-reg-keyexists-false-negative-value-only-key) — Reg_KeyExists returns false for an existing key that has values but no subkeys and no (Default) value  
  _Fix :_ After the subkey loop, also enumerate values: `Loop Reg, keyPath, 'V' { return true }` before falling back to the default-value RegRead. Only return false on Extra=2/3 from BOTH the K and V enumerations. This makes existence independent of whether a (Default) value is set.
- [ ] [`ssid-utf8-misdecode-and-signal-offset-fragility`](#f-ssid-utf8-misdecode-and-signal-offset-fragility) — NI WLAN SSID is decoded as UTF-8 from a raw DOT11_SSID byte buffer with hardcoded struct offsets — wrong hash on non-UTF-8 SSIDs and fragile across struct changes  
  _Fix :_ Hash the raw SSID octets (copy ssid_len bytes from pData+524 into a Buffer and feed the bytes to the hash) rather than StrGet-then-hash, so the digest is over the true network identifier regardless of encoding. Add a bounds check that cbData >= 580 before reading the signal offset, returning an empty Map otherwise.
- [ ] [`ni-isvpnactive-missing-return-on-no-match`](#f-ni-isvpnactive-missing-return-on-no-match) — NI_IsVpnActive falls off the end of the loop without an explicit 'return false' on the no-VPN path  
  _Fix :_ Add an explicit 'return false' after the while loop (before the closing of the try) and ensure all exit paths return a genuine boolean.
- [ ] [`updater-setcheckinterval-type-guard-rejects-non-integer`](#f-updater-setcheckinterval-type-guard-rejects-non-integer) — Updater_SetCheckInterval silently rejects any Seconds value whose Type is not exactly 'Integer'  
  _Fix :_ Coerce defensively: 'Seconds := Integer(Seconds)' inside a try, reject only genuinely invalid (negative or non-numeric) input, and LoggerWarn on rejection so a bad value is visible in the log rather than silently dropped.
- [ ] [`prefix-onkeydown-tab-llm-feed-gap`](#f-prefix-onkeydown-tab-llm-feed-gap) — PrefixWatcher OnKeyDown never feeds Tab/Enter/Escape to the LLM bridge — the LLM flush-on-nav path is structurally unreachable from the primary hook  
  _Fix :_ Fold the LLM feed into the live branches: in the 0x09/0x0D branch (line 924), after the Tab-accept early-return, call LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive(VK) before/with HSE_FeedReset; delete the unreachable else-if at line 936. LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive already maps 0x0D/0x1B->OnFlush and 0x09->TryAcceptTab-or-OnFlush, so routing every nav VK through it once is sufficient and removes the dead code.
- [ ] [`win-l-lock-no-watcher-reset`](#f-win-l-lock-no-watcher-reset) — Win+L (LockWorkStation) remap updates last-sent char but skips HSE/prefix-buffer reset, leaving stale word context after unlock  
  _Fix :_ In the Win+L handler call `HSE_FeedReset(false)` and `_ResetPrefixBuffer()` (and drop the misleading `UpdateLastSentCharacter('l')`, since no 'l' was emitted) so the lock is treated as the context-unknown boundary it is.
- [ ] [`per-entry-priority-divergence-cache-vs-toml`](#f-per-entry-priority-divergence-cache-vs-toml) — Individual per-hotstring `priority = N` is honoured only on the TOML fallback, never on the cached/rebuilt path  
  _Fix :_ Either (a) add a 9th priority column to the .tsv: in _HotstringsCacheBuildRows compute EntryPriority := _ParseEntryPriority(Line, _HSE_SourcePriority(Category) or the resolved section value) and push it into the row; serialize/deserialize it (it is a small integer, identifier-safe, no escaping); have _HsCacheRegisterSection set Opts["Priority"] from it so the cache reproduces LoadHotstringsSection 1:1 as the module docstring claims. Or (b) if bundled categories are intentionally NEVER to carry per-entry priority, document and assert that with a meta-test and make the build/registrar explicitly pass the resolved section priority so both paths provably agree. Bump the cache format version so old .tsv files are rebuilt.
- [ ] [`isrepeat-section-name-mismatch-latent-divergence`](#f-isrepeat-section-name-mismatch-latent-divergence) — Cache IsRepeat test matches the wrong section name and the TOML fallback never sets IsRepeat at all  
  _Fix :_ Pick one source of truth. Best: delete the IsRepeat computation from _HotstringsCacheBuildRows (and from LoadExtTomlFile) entirely if repeat is now fully owned by the engine-level HSE_TryRepeatKey fallback (it is — see hotstring_engine_main.ahk:701/750), storing IsRepeat=false unconditionally; OR if registered-hotstring IsRepeat is still meaningful, fix the section literal to "repeat_corrections" in BOTH places AND make LoadHotstringsSection compute and pass the identical IsRepeat so the two paths provably match. Either way add a comment that the cache and the TOML fallback must use byte-identical IsRepeat logic.
- [ ] [`freshness-same-second-edit-window`](#f-freshness-same-second-edit-window) — A bundled TOML edited in the same wall-clock second as the .tsv write is judged fresh and the edit is ignored until the next boot  
  _Fix :_ Treat equal-second mtimes as stale: change the comparison to rebuild when the TOML is `>=` the .tsv, i.e. require the .tsv to be strictly newer (`FileGetTime(TomlPath,"M") >= TsvTime => return false`). Equivalent to the i18n contract phrased the other way. The only cost is one extra rebuild on the rare equal-second boot, which is the safe direction. Optionally also stamp a content hash of the TOML set into the .tsv header and compare hashes instead of mtimes for an exact freshness signal.
- [x] [`clipboard-reads-entire-clipboard-into-ram`](#f-clipboard-reads-entire-clipboard-into-ram) — Clipboard sensor copies the full clipboard text into RAM on every copy  
  _Fix :_ Get the length without materialising the text: use the Win32 clipboard API — OpenClipboard/GetClipboardData(CF_UNICODETEXT)/GlobalSize then GlobalUnlock/CloseClipboard via DllCall, computing char count from the buffer size, never assigning A_Clipboard to a variable. Wrap in try (clipboard may be locked by another app). Also gate KL_Clip_OnChange on A_IsSuspended so no clipboard read happens while paused.
- [ ] [`curl-payload-pii-temp-leak`](#f-curl-payload-pii-temp-leak) — User-typed context (PII) written to A_Temp curl payload files is never orphan-cleaned on the active (non-streaming) path  
  _Fix :_ Call _LLM_Ollama_StreamCleanupOrphans() (or a shared sweeper) from _LLM_Ollama_DispatchAsync before writing the new payload, and/or run it once at LLM bridge init. Better: write payloads to a dedicated subdir under A_Temp and sweep that dir at startup. Optionally shorten the orphan window for payloads specifically.
- [ ] [`ollama-curl-temp-pii-plaintext`](#f-ollama-curl-temp-pii-plaintext) — Ollama curl dispatch writes the user's typed context (PII) to a world-readable plaintext temp file  
  _Fix :_ Write the curl payload to a per-instance subdirectory created with restrictive ACLs (or under %LOCALAPPDATA%\Ergopti with user-only access), delete it immediately after curl has opened it rather than waiting for the poll/orphan sweep, and consider gating LLM context capture through MF_ShouldFilter so password/private-browsing windows that the keylogger refuses to record also suppress sending their text to the model. At minimum, document the on-disk exposure and tighten the cleanup to fire as soon as the child has consumed the file.
- [ ] [`config-window-delay-write-per-keystroke`](#f-config-window-delay-write-per-keystroke) — Delay/priority Edit fields rewrite the override file (or personal TOML) on every digit typed  
  _Fix :_ Debounce the numeric-field persistence: on 'Change', cancel and re-arm a short SetTimer (e.g. -250 ms) that performs the single _HCW_SetOverride/_HCW_LoadCurrent once the user pauses; or persist on focus-loss / Enter instead of per-keystroke. Personal-file patches especially should not run per digit.
- [ ] [`parsetomlgroupconfig-missing-file-cache-key`](#f-parsetomlgroupconfig-missing-file-cache-key) — Non-existent TOML is cached under the wrong key, so missing personal/extension files re-stat on every cache-miss resolve  
  _Fix :_ Change line 479 to `HotstringGroupConfig[CacheKey] := Config` so the missing-file result is cached under the same key the lookup uses.
- [ ] [`loader-toml-injection-readfile-hotpath`](#f-loader-toml-injection-readfile-hotpath) — Tap-hold TOML loader runs on reload and trusts on-disk config; a malformed/huge file is parsed line-by-line with no size guard  
  _Fix :_ Write atomically: FileAppend to a temp path then FileMove/rename over the target (rename is atomic on NTFS), so the loader never sees a half-written file. Add a sanity check in LoadTapHoldToml: if FileExist(FilePath) but the parsed result has 0 keys AND inherit_defaults was not explicitly false, log a WARNING rather than silently returning empty (distinguishes 'user disabled everything' from 'file was truncated').
- [ ] [`klr-builddatabase-debug-fileappend-hot`](#f-klr-builddatabase-debug-fileappend-hot) — KLR_BuildDatabase unconditionally FileAppends 6+ debug lines to prefetch.log on every build (every 5 s / live tick)  
  _Fix :_ Gate every KLR_BuildDatabase / KLR_ApplyIncremental FileAppend behind a debug-level check (route through LoggerDebug which no-ops below threshold), or write them through a single cached FileObject handle like KL_OpenTodayFh rather than re-opening per line.

### P4 — Info / latent (10)

- [ ] [`disable-enable-group-unguarded-but-dead-path`](#f-disable-enable-group-unguarded-but-dead-path) — HSE_DisableGroup/HSE_EnableGroup do a wide non-atomic index rebuild (whole prefix set reset to empty) — currently unused by the live path  
  _Fix :_ When (and only when) these are re-wired to a live path, apply the same Critical-wrapped build-then-swap as proposed for HSE_Register/RebuildHotstringsLive: build replacement HSE_StarPrefixSetCI/CS and HSE_StarByTriggerCI/CS into locals and assign each in one statement, under a brief Critical, so the reader never sees an empty index. For now, leave a code comment flagging the requirement.
- [ ] [`suspend-watchdog-no-prefix-keywait`](#f-suspend-watchdog-no-prefix-keywait) — External/native Suspend toggles bypass the SC138 prefix-latch KeyWait that ToggleSuspend performs  
  _Fix :_ Either (a) route ALL suspend toggles through ToggleSuspend and document that nothing else may call Suspend() directly, or (b) factor the SC138 KeyWait into a small _SuspendDrainPrefix() helper and call it from any code path that can trigger suspend. Keep the existing AltGrShiftDispatch WARNING guard rail as the safety net.
- [ ] [`kh-intercept-dead-flag`](#f-kh-intercept-dead-flag) — KeyboardHook _KH_INTERCEPT is stored but has no effect (dead behavioral flag)  
  _Fix :_ Either remove _KH_INTERCEPT entirely (per the 'no unused fallback code' rule) or, if the capability is desired, log a WARNING when intercept=true is requested so the unsupported path is loud, and track it in the spec.
- [ ] [`delay-edit-non-integer-personal-truncation`](#f-delay-edit-non-integer-personal-truncation) — Sub-millisecond personal delays silently truncate to 3 decimals while the override store keeps full precision (UI/engine drift)  
  _Fix :_ Route both backends through one delay-serialisation helper, or document that delays are ms-quantised everywhere and clamp the override store the same way. Low priority — only worth doing if a non-integer-ms delay path is ever introduced.
- [x] [`wpm-graph-label-json-injection`](#f-wpm-graph-label-json-injection) — Graph update interpolates the WPM label into JS without escaping; JSON_Escape is defined but unused  
  _Fix :_ Route the label (and any other interpolated string fields) through the existing JSON_Escape() helper when building the JSON, or assemble the payload with a proper JSON builder; remove JSON_Escape if it is intentionally not needed.
- [ ] [`sensors-cpu-state-stale-across-reload`](#f-sensors-cpu-state-stale-across-reload) — Sensor CPU-delta baseline is a class static that survives logically but the first post-Stop/Start tick can emit a bogus 0% or skipped CPU sample  
  _Fix :_ Reset KLSensors.prev_idle/prev_kernel/prev_user to -1 in KL_Sensors_Stop (or at the top of KL_Sensors_Start) so the first tick after a restart re-establishes a fresh baseline and skips the bogus delta, exactly as it does on cold start.
- [ ] [`count-regex-vs-entry-pattern-divergence`](#f-count-regex-vs-entry-pattern-divergence) — Displayed hotstring counts use a looser regex than the one that actually registers entries, so counts can exceed registered rows  
  _Fix :_ Make _TomlWarmFileCounts count with the same _HOTSTRING_ENTRY_PATTERN used for registration (or, for sections that legitimately use the simple key="value" form handled by LoadExtTomlFile, count both shapes exactly as that loader registers them). Then the displayed count equals the registered/cached row count by construction.
- [ ] [`wpm-push-unguarded-debug-arg-build`](#f-wpm-push-unguarded-debug-arg-build) — Per-keystroke LoggerDebug in WPMWidget_Push builds its arg array without a LoggerIsDebugEnabled gate  
  _Fix :_ Wrap line 237 in 'if LoggerIsDebugEnabled()' so the arg array is built only when DEBUG is actually on, matching the prefix-watcher pattern.
- [ ] [`tint-test-stale-constants-comment`](#f-tint-test-stale-constants-comment) — Tint contract test seeds L=0.13/S=0.85 but its header comment claims the vectors were generated at L=0.10/S=0.40  
  _Fix :_ Update the comment at lines 37-39 to state L=0.13 / S=0.85 (matching the seeds and constants.toml), and ideally derive the seeds from UI_TINT_LIGHTNESS/UI_TINT_SATURATION loaded from the TOML rather than hard-coding, so comment and seed cannot diverge again.
- [ ] [`app-picker-t-variable-shadows-i18n`](#f-app-picker-t-variable-shadows-i18n) — AppPicker_BuildRows reuses `t` as a local window-title variable, shadowing the global t() i18n function name  
  _Fix :_ Rename the local to `winTitle` (it is already shadowed implicitly): `winTitle := WinGetTitle(...)` and use winTitle in the `if (winTitle = "")` guard. Trivial, removes the footgun.

---

## Findings détaillés

### Catégorie : Frappes perdues / réordonnées (6)

<a id="f-dispatcher-boundfunc-ptr-drops-subscribers"></a>
#### `dispatcher-boundfunc-ptr-drops-subscribers` — **critical** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : input-dispatch (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:135`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:136`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:141`
- **Description** : Register() de-duplicates by comparing existing.Ptr = callback_fn.Ptr for each already-registered callback. In AHK v2 a BoundFunc (the object returned by Func.Bind(), which is what EVERY caller passes) has no .Ptr property. The first Register() for a given event type works because the for-loop has nothing to iterate and never touches .Ptr, so the callback is Pushed. The SECOND distinct Register() for the same event type enters `for existing in ...`, evaluates `existing.Ptr` on the first (BoundFunc) element, THROWS 'has no property named Ptr', the enclosing `try {}` swallows it, control jumps past the Push — so the second subscriber is silently discarded.
- **Cause racine** : BoundFunc objects in AHK v2 do not expose a .Ptr member (empirically confirmed: `b1.Ptr` throws 'This value of type "BoundFunc" has no property named "Ptr"'). The dedup loop reads `existing.Ptr` inside `try {}`, so the exception aborts the whole Register body before `.Push()` runs. Reproduced: registering two distinct MyCb.Bind() for one event yields subscriber count 1, not 2.
- **Scénario** : When the keylogger is enabled it registers EVT_KB_CHAR + EVT_KB_DOWN first (KL_Hook_Start at boot). Later the LLM bridge takes its dispatcher-fallback path (_LLM_Bridge_RegisterDispatcherFallback, e.g. Ollama not yet warm / PrefixWatcher not up) and registers a SECOND EVT_KB_CHAR subscriber — which is dropped. The LLM fallback char feed never fires, so early predictions are dead until PrefixWatcher comes online. Equivalently, if KeyboardHook adapter (KHStart) and the keylogger both run, only whichever registered first per event type receives keystrokes; the other feature is silently blind to all typing.
- **Impact** : Whole features go silently deaf to keyboard input depending on registration order. This is exactly the user's #1 'lost keystroke / dropped feature' class — the keystrokes reach the app (visible hook) but a subscriber that was supposed to observe/act on them never sees a single one, with no error in the log.
- **Correctif** : Compare callbacks by object identity, not .Ptr. Replace `if (existing.Ptr = callback_fn.Ptr)` with `if (existing == callback_fn)` (verified: `==` is identity-equal for the same BoundFunc and not-equal for distinct BoundFuncs). Apply the same change in Unregister (see related finding).
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/: load hook_dispatcher.ahk, define two trivial functions A,B, register A.Bind() and B.Bind() (distinct objects) for EVT_KB_CHAR, then AssertEqual(HookDispatcher._subscribers[EVT_KB_CHAR].Length, 2). Then register A's SAME bound reference twice and assert Length stays at its prior value (dedup still works). Fails before the fix (count is 1), passes after.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : CONFIRMED by reading the real source and empirically reproducing against it with the installed AHK v2.0.19. The cited code in lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:128-146 (Register) dedups via `if (existing.Ptr = callback_fn.Ptr)` inside an enclosing `try {}` with no `catch`.

Empirical facts (AHK64 v2):
- T1: `b1.Ptr` on a BoundFunc THROWS 'This value of type "BoundFunc" has no property named "Ptr"'. Confirmed.
- T2 (broader than the finding): even a plain `Func` reference (`A.Ptr`) THROWS the same. So the bug is NOT BoundFunc-specific — every callable passed to Register triggers it the moment the loop body evaluates `.Ptr`.
- End-to-end against the ACTUAL hook_dispatcher.ahk (logger stubbed, file #Included): registering two distinct BoundFuncs for EVT_KB_CHAR yields _subscribers[EVT_KB_CHAR].Length = 1, not 2. For EVT_KB_DOWN, after Dispatch only the first callback fires (gotA=1, gotB=0). The second subscriber is silently dropped because the thrown exception unwinds past `.Push()` and the bare `try {}` swallows it.

Why the first Register works but the second fails (matches the finding exactly): on the first call the for-loop iterates an empty array, never touches `.Ptr`, and Push runs. On the second call the loop's first iteration evaluates `existing.Ptr`, throws, the `try {}` eats it, Push is skipped.

Real callers collide on the same event type — this is not hypothetical:
- modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:398-399 registers KLHook.cb_char / cb_down (= KL_Hook_OnChar.Bind(), KL_Hook_OnKeyDown.Bind()) for EVT_KB_CHAR / EVT_KB_DOWN.
- modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:120-121 registers _LLM_Bridge_DispatcherCharFn / _LLM_Bridge_DispatcherKeyFn (= ...Bind()) for the SAME EVT_KB_CHAR / EVT_KB_DOWN.
- adapters/keyboard_hook.ahk:68-69 also registers BoundFuncs for EVT_KB_CHAR / EVT_KB_DOWN.
All three pass BoundFuncs, so whichever registers second/third for a given keyboard event type is silently discarded and that feature receives ZERO keystrokes, with no log entry (the LoggerDebug "Subscriber registered" line is never reached for the dropped one, and the exception is swallowed). This is precisely the user's #1 'feature goes silently deaf to keyboard input' class.

The Dispatch path itself (lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:187-198) is correctly Suspend-gated and serialized — this is NOT a concurrency/race issue. It is a flat logic bug in the dedup comparison that AHK's cooperative model has nothing to do with; the Critical("On") wrapper does not save it because the throw is inside the try.

Companion bug confirmed by inspection: Unregister (line 163) uses the identical `arr[idx].Ptr = callback_fn.Ptr` inside its own `try {}`. With a non-empty array the first iteration throws and is swallowed, so Unregister NEVER removes a subscriber. _LLM_Bridge_UnregisterDispatcherFallback (llm_bridge.ahk:124-130) and the keylogger stop path therefore leave stale BoundFuncs firing after a feature is stopped.

Severity 'critical' is correct: it silently disables whole input-observing features depending on registration order, with no error surfaced.

<a id="f-gesture-release-disables-shared-lbutton"></a>
#### `gesture-release-disables-shared-lbutton` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-lost-keystroke (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:1633`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:1647`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:310`
- **Description** : GestureReleaseRightClick (and the symmetric left-click-hold path) arms Hotkey("~LButton", GestureReleaseRightClick, "On") while the hold gesture is active, then on release calls Hotkey("~LButton", GestureReleaseRightClick, "Off"). Because the gesture, the dispatcher, the prefix watcher and the LLM pointer-watch all share the single ~LButton hotkey variant (same context), the "On" first REASSIGNS the action to the gesture handler, and the "Off" then DISABLES that hotkey variant entirely.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 Hotkey(name, fn, "On"/"Off") toggles the enabled state of the ONE hotkey variant identified by (name + #HotIf context); it has no notion of per-callback ownership. So Hotkey("~LButton", X, "Off") disables ~LButton for the whole process, regardless of which callback is currently bound — and re-points it at X first. After the gesture ends, the shared ~LButton is left OFF and bound to a gesture handler that early-returns.
- **Scénario** : User triggers the right-click-hold gesture (e.g. AltGr gesture), then releases it. The release runs Hotkey("~LButton", GestureReleaseRightClick, "Off"). From then on, plain left clicks fire nothing: the LLM pointer-dismiss no longer dismisses a shown prediction on click, the keylogger stops counting clicks, and the PrefixWatcher caret-reposition reset never runs after a click — until the next Reload re-arms everything. The user perceives "my prediction tooltip won't go away when I click" and "clicking to move the cursor then typing gives weird hotstring previews".
- **Impact** : A single completed gesture permanently (until Reload) disables left-click handling for three unrelated features that legitimately need it. Combined with finding mouse-hotkey-clobber, the mouse-input path is fragile and order-dependent. This is lost input handling on the shared thread caused by toggling a shared resource as if it were privately owned.
- **Correctif** : Give the gesture's dynamic LButton watcher its own #HotIf context (e.g. #HotIf GestureRightClickHeld) so its On/Off only affects that distinct variant, never the shared global ~LButton. Better: subscribe the gesture release to HookDispatcher (Register/Unregister EVT_MS_LDOWN) so it cannot disable anyone else's binding. The Hotkey(...,"Off") at release must never touch the variant other modules depend on.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert that any Hotkey("~LButton"|"~RButton"|"~MButton", ..., "Off") call in gestures.ahk is inside a function that also established a unique #HotIf context (or that gestures use the dispatcher Unregister path), so a release can never disable the globally-shared button hotkey. headless-unit: stub Hotkey() into a recorder, simulate dispatcher.Start() then a gesture On->Off cycle, and assert the dispatcher's ~LButton variant is still enabled and still bound to _hk_ldown afterwards.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source. This is not an AHK-concurrency race; it is a hotkey-identity semantics bug, which makes it deterministic and easy to confirm by static reading.

AHK v2 identifies a hotkey VARIANT by (KeyName + #HotIf context) only — there is NO per-callback ownership. `Hotkey(name, fn, "On"/"Off")` operates on that single variant: if `fn` differs it REPLACES the variant's bound function, then On/Off toggles the variant's enabled state.

Grep shows four registrants of the identical no-#HotIf `~LButton` variant:
- lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:310  -> `_hk_ldown`  (the canonical owner; its whole design premise, header lines 9-20, is "exactly one mouse-button set for the process")
- lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:195 -> `_OnMouseClickReset`
- modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:402 -> `_LLM_PointerWatch_ActivityFn`
- modules/gestures.ahk:1633 -> `GestureReleaseRightClick`
The only SAFE `~LButton` is capsword.ahk:58, which sits under `#HotIf CapsWordEnabled` (line 42) — a distinct variant, correctly isolated.

Because the gesture, dispatcher, prefix watcher and LLM watch all share one variant, GestureToggleRightClick's `Hotkey("~LButton", GestureReleaseRightClick, "On")` (line 1633) first STEALS the variant (re-points it from `_hk_ldown` to the gesture handler), and on release GestureReleaseRightClick's `Hotkey("~LButton", GestureReleaseRightClick, "Off")` (line 1647) DISABLES that single variant outright, leaving it bound to a handler that early-returns once GestureRightClickHeld is false. From then on, plain LButton fires nothing — no keylogger LDOWN fan-out (keylogger subscribes via HookDispatcher.Register at keylogger_mouse.ahk:500, so it dies with the dispatcher's `_hk_ldown`), no prefix-watcher click reset, no LLM pointer-dismiss — until something re-arms `~LButton` (an LLM/prefix re-`On`, or a Reload). The symmetric left-hold path does the same to `~RButton` (lines 1544/1558).

The finding's mechanism, root cause and scenario are all accurate. The only refinement: this is a manifestation of a broader systemic violation of the HookDispatcher contract — even WITHOUT any gesture, these modules already clobber each other's `~LButton`/`~RButton` binding (last-writer-wins), because none uses #HotIf isolation and none routes through the dispatcher. The gesture's `"Off"` is just the most visible failure mode (leaves the variant disabled rather than merely re-pointed). The capsword guard via #HotIf proves the codebase already knows the correct isolation idiom; the three direct registrants simply don't use it.

<a id="f-layout-poll-blind-reload"></a>
#### `layout-poll-blind-reload` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-keystroke-pipeline (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2251-2259`
- **Description** : CheckKeyboardLayoutChange() runs every 1000 ms and calls Reload() the instant GetForegroundKeyboardLayout() differs from the cached HKL. It is not guarded by A_IsSuspended, not debounced, and not gated on whether the user is mid-word or a send burst / hotstring expansion is in flight.
- **Cause racine** : Reload() tears down and restarts the whole script. Any keystroke the user typed in the ~1 s window, the entire HSE_Buffer / _PrefixBuffer word context, and any expansion that SendInput just queued are discarded because module globals are re-initialised from scratch. GetForegroundKeyboardLayout() reads the FOREGROUND window's thread HKL, so it changes not only on a deliberate layout switch but also transiently: alt-tabbing to an app pinned to a different input language, an app that briefly raises an IME/candidate window, or a per-control language switch (common in browsers / Office). A SetTimer callback also interrupts the auto-execute/typing thread at the next rest point, so the Reload can land between two physical keystrokes.
- **Scénario** : User is fast-typing a French sentence; a background notification toast or an Office field momentarily reports a different keyboard HKL for the foreground thread. Within 1 s the poll fires, Reload() runs, and the half-typed word plus any armed magic-key expansion vanish — the user sees keystrokes 'eaten' and the tooltip disappear for no visible reason.
- **Impact** : Silent, hard-to-reproduce lost keystrokes and surprise driver restarts during normal typing. Exactly the user's #1 concern, and intermittent enough to be blamed on 'the app'.
- **Correctif** : Guard the Reload behind (a) `if A_IsSuspended return` (a paused driver must not auto-reload), and (b) a quiescence check: require the HKL to stay changed for two consecutive polls AND that no send/expansion is in flight (HSE_Suppressed / _PrefixWatcherSuppressed both false) and the user is idle (A_TimeIdlePhysical above a small threshold, e.g. > 400 ms) before reloading. This converts a transient flicker into a no-op while still adapting to a genuine, sustained layout switch.
- **Test** : headless-unit: extract the decision into a pure helper `_ShouldReloadForHkl(prevHkl, curHkl, suspended, suppressed, idleMs)` and Test() it: same HKL -> false; changed but suspended -> false; changed but suppressed (send in flight) -> false; changed + idle + not suppressed -> true; single-poll flicker that reverts -> false. Assert the current inline body delegates to it so a regression to the blind Reload fails the test.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2249-2259) and the helper it depends on. The finding holds up against the actual source and the AHK cooperative-threading model.

ACTUAL CODE: The timer body is exactly 8 lines:
  CheckKeyboardLayoutChange() { HKL := GetForegroundKeyboardLayout(); if (HKL != 0 and HKL != _LAST_KEYBOARD_HKL) { _LAST_KEYBOARD_HKL := HKL; Reload() } }
armed as SetTimer(CheckKeyboardLayoutChange, 1000) — a POSITIVE interval, so it is a recurring 1 s poll, not a one-shot. There is no A_IsSuspended guard, no debounce, no in-flight/idle check. The body matches the finding verbatim.

THE HKL IS GENUINELY VOLATILE: GetForegroundKeyboardLayout() (lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:83-93) does GetForegroundWindow -> GetWindowThreadProcessId -> GetKeyboardLayout(TID). It returns the FOREGROUND window's thread HKL, which changes on any focus/alt-tab to a thread carrying a different active input locale, and on per-control/IME locale switches. So the !=  comparison legitimately fires on transient focus flicker, not only on a deliberate layout switch. The finding's volatility premise is correct, not hand-waving.

CONCURRENCY MODEL CONFIRMS THE INTERRUPTION: AHK v2 is cooperatively threaded — the periodic timer preempts the auto-execute / typing thread at the next rest point (between keystroke-handler threads, or during a Send/Sleep yield inside an expansion). This is not a parallel data race; it is a control-flow interruption that runs Reload() between two physical keystrokes or mid-expansion-burst. Reload() restarts the whole script, and HSE_Buffer is a plain global (hotstring_engine_main.ahk:122 -> `global HSE_Buffer := ""`), reset to empty on restart, so the in-progress word/prefix context and any queued expansion are discarded. The lost-keystroke / lost-context mechanism is real.

VIOLATES A KNOWN PROJECT INVARIANT: Everywhere else, a timer/InputHook that bypasses native Suspend explicitly guards with `if A_IsSuspended return` — HookDispatcher.Dispatch (lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:191), the prefix watcher (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:659, 861), gestures (gestures.ahk:662), and the suspend watchdog. This poll is the one timer that omits the guard, so a PAUSED ("tout eteint") driver will still auto-Reload on an HKL flicker — and Reload re-arms every feature, effectively undoing the pause. That alone is a clear invariant breach independent of the lost-keystroke angle.

I could not refute it: there is no surrounding debounce, no Critical, no quiescence check anywhere near the call, and the suppression signals the fix needs (HSE_Suppressed at engine_main.ahk:195, _PrefixWatcherSuppressed at prefix_watcher.ahk:58) both exist as globals, so the proposed quiescence gate is implementable. The one thing keeping this from being universal is that the trigger requires a transient foreground-HKL difference to actually occur — on a strictly single-input-locale setup the HKL never differs and this never fires. That makes it intermittent and config-dependent, which is why it is hard to reproduce, not why it is benign.

<a id="f-clickhold-inputhook-drops-keys"></a>
#### `clickhold-inputhook-drops-keys` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : gestures (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:1571`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:1594`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:1605`
- **Description** : While a left- or right-click hold is active, GestureStartKeyboardWatcher installs an InputHook with KeyOpt("{All}","N") and NO 'V' (visible) flag, so EVERY physical key is swallowed by the hook. On the first key, GestureOnKeyDown stops the hook, releases the click, then re-Sends only that single vk/sc via Send("{Blind}{vk..sc..}"). Any key the user pressed in the same burst before AHK's pseudo-thread reached the OnKeyDown rest point is consumed by the hook but NOT re-sent (only the one vk/sc passed to the callback is replayed), and modifier state at re-send time is whatever happens to be down then, not when the key was struck.
- **Cause racine** : An InputHook with KeyOpt suppression ('N' notify + no 'V') consumes the key from the input stream; the design re-injects exactly one key from the OnKeyDown args. If the user types fast (multi-key rollover) the hook can have buffered/seen more than one transition before the cooperatively-scheduled callback runs, and the re-Send replays only the single reported key, dropping the rest. The {Blind} re-send also re-enters the keyboard hook at SendLevel 3, which is intentional but means the synthetic key competes with still-arriving physical input (SendInput-style replay reorder).
- **Scénario** : User triple-taps to start left_click_toggle (drag/select mode), then quickly types a word to, say, rename a selection. The first physical key releases the click and is re-sent, but characters typed in the same fast burst before the callback fires are silently eaten — the user sees a word with missing letters, or letters in the wrong order.
- **Impact** : Directly the user's #1 concern: lost/reordered keystrokes, but only while a click-hold toggle is engaged. Bounded to that mode, hence high not critical.
- **Correctif** : Do not suppress: install the watcher as a non-consuming hook (InputHook with 'V' so keys pass through normally, or arm ~*$<key> style passthrough hotkeys) and merely RELEASE the held button on the first KeyDown without swallowing/re-sending. If suppression is truly required to release-before-the-key-acts, replay must reconstruct full modifier state and the watcher must be torn down atomically under Critical before the click release so no second key is seen. Also add 'if A_IsSuspended { ih.Stop(); GestureReleaseLeftClick(); GestureReleaseRightClick(); return }' at the top of GestureOnKeyDown so pause silences this hook (InputHooks bypass Suspend per project invariant).
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub Send to record its argument list; set GestureLeftClickHeld:=True and GestureKeyboardHook to a fake with a Stop() method, call GestureOnKeyDown(fakeIh, vk_of('a'), sc) and Assert that GestureLeftClickHeld became False AND exactly the 'a' key was re-emitted. Add a second test that sets A_IsSuspended-equivalent flag (or a wrapper) and asserts no Send occurs and the hook is stopped. The pause gate is the realistically testable invariant; the multi-key drop needs live hardware to reproduce fully (flag as live).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code. The mechanism is real, with caveats on magnitude.

SUPPRESSION IS REAL (gestures.ahk:1571-1581). GestureStartKeyboardWatcher creates InputHook("L3") with KeyOpt("{All}","N") and NO 'V' flag. In AHK v2.0 an InputHook without 'V' is non-visible/suppressing by default — the project itself proves it knows this: HookDispatcher uses "V L0" (line 63, commented "V = visible (events pass through to apps)") and the prefix watcher uses "V" for pass-through, whereas the gestures hook deliberately omits V with the comment "No 'V' option: we want to suppress the key, release click, then re-send." So every physical key IS swallowed while a click-toggle is held. The finding's premise is correct, not a false positive.

PAUSE BYPASS IS REAL AND STATICALLY CONFIRMABLE. GestureOnKeyDown (line 1594) has no A_IsSuspended guard. This is a separate InputHook, NOT routed through HookDispatcher.Dispatch — which is the single place that enforces the project invariant « pause = tout éteint » via 'if A_IsSuspended return' (hook_dispatcher.ahk:191, with a comment explicitly noting Suspend does not stop InputHooks). The gesture hotkeys themselves (^#+F1.. line 1726) are native hotkeys disarmed by Suspend, so a toggle cannot be NEWLY engaged while paused; but if the user engages a toggle and then pauses, this InputHook keeps suppressing keystrokes until the next key tears it down. That is a one-keystroke invariant leak, but a genuine one, and exactly the class the project guards against elsewhere.

MULTI-KEY DROP IS PLAUSIBLE BUT TIMING-GATED. OnKeyDown re-sends only the single vk/sc in its args via Send("{Blind}{vk..sc..}"). Any second key suppressed in the window between key1's queued OnKeyDown thread and ih.Stop() is consumed but never re-sent. Under AHK's cooperative scheduler the callback normally runs within ~1-2 ms while the script idles in its message loop, so a drop needs fast rollover AND a momentarily-busy main thread. The codebase's own HSE memory note (the "abcd"->"acd" swallow when a suppress window stretches) shows this failure class is real here, but I cannot prove the magnitude by static reading — it needs live timing.

DOWNGRADES: the modifier-state sub-claim is weak. {Blind} intentionally preserves whatever modifiers are down; for the realistic single-key case the modifier is still physically held at re-send time, and the code comment (1603-1604) shows this is by design. So "wrong modifier" is largely a non-issue. The whole defect is also bounded to an actively-engaged click-toggle (deliberate gesture, auto-released on first key), which caps the blast radius.

<a id="f-mouse-hotkey-clobber"></a>
#### `mouse-hotkey-clobber` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-lost-keystroke (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:310`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:194`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:400`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1037`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2207`
- **Description** : HookDispatcher.Start() binds ~LButton/~RButton/~MButton (and the Up/wheel variants) to its fan-out handlers (_hk_ldown etc., which drive the keylogger mouse counters and any mouse subscriber). Then HotstringPrefixWatcherInit()->_InstallMouseClickResetHooks() re-binds ~LButton/~MButton/~RButton to _OnMouseClickReset. Then the LLM bridge's _LLM_PointerWatch_Start() re-binds ~LButton/~RButton/~MButton (+ XButtons/wheel) to _LLM_PointerWatch_ActivityFn. All three calls use the bare key name with NO #HotIf context active, so they all target the SAME hotkey variant.
- **Cause racine** : In AHK v2, Hotkey(KeyName, Action) for an already-registered KeyName under the same #HotIf context REASSIGNS the single action bound to that hotkey variant — it does NOT stack callbacks. The whole point of HookDispatcher (one mouse hotkey set fanning out to N subscribers) is therefore defeated for the buttons that other modules also bind directly: whoever calls Hotkey() last for ~LButton wins, and the dispatcher's _hk_ldown (hence the keylogger click metrics and the dispatcher's mouse_ldown fan-out) plus the prefix watcher's _OnMouseClickReset are overwritten by the LLM pointer-watch's single callback once the bridge arms.
- **Scénario** : User has metrics + the hotstring preview + the LLM bridge all enabled (the default rich setup). After boot, the LLM bridge attaches to the PrefixWatcher and arms _LLM_PointerWatch_Start. From that point a left click only fires _LLM_PointerWatch_ActivityFn: the keylogger's click counter never increments (session_clicks stays 0, mouse heatmap under-counts), and the PrefixWatcher's _OnMouseClickReset no longer runs — so after the user clicks to reposition the caret mid-word, the prefix buffer is NOT reset and the next keystrokes look up a stale prefix, surfacing a wrong/again-the-old tooltip and an HSE buffer that thinks the caret is somewhere it is not (can mis-fire or fail to fire an is_word trigger at the new caret).
- **Impact** : Silent loss of mouse-driven features: keylogger click/scroll metrics under-report; the caret-reposition buffer reset that prevents stale/incorrect hotstring previews and mis-fires after a click is dead. It is not a dropped KEY-stroke per se, but it is lost INPUT handling on the shared input thread and directly undermines the dispatcher's single-fan-out invariant the module was built to guarantee.
- **Correctif** : Route ALL mouse-button observation through the HookDispatcher subscriber API instead of re-registering the raw hotkey. PrefixWatcher should HookDispatcher.Register(EVT_MS_LDOWN/MDOWN/RDOWN, _OnMouseClickReset) and the LLM pointer-watch should Register(EVT_MS_LDOWN/.../wheel, _LLM_PointerWatch_ActivityFn) rather than calling Hotkey("~LButton", ...). The dispatcher already owns the single Hotkey() set and fans out to every subscriber, so each module then coexists. Where a module must keep its own Hotkey (e.g. it can run with metrics off and no dispatcher), give it a dedicated #HotIf context so it occupies a distinct hotkey variant rather than clobbering the shared one.
- **Test** : meta-static (tests/meta): grep every .ahk for Hotkey("~LButton" / "~RButton" / "~MButton" / their Up/wheel variants and assert at most ONE registration site per bare button name exists OUTSIDE hook_dispatcher.ahk, OR that every such call is preceded by a #HotIf in the same function — fails the moment a second module re-binds the shared button at global context. Complement with a headless-unit test that stubs Hotkey() into a recorder Map keyed by (name,context), replays the boot registration order (dispatcher.Start, prefix _InstallMouseClickResetHooks, _LLM_PointerWatch_Start) and asserts no key maps to a callback that overwrote a previously-registered different callback.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read all five cited sites and the surrounding wiring. The core AHK-v2 claim is correct: Hotkey(KeyName, Action) for an already-registered KeyName under the SAME #HotIf context REASSIGNS the single bound action — it never stacks callbacks. All four registrants use the identical bare string ("~LButton"/"~MButton"/"~RButton") with NO HotIf() set on their thread (grep confirms no #HotIf in llm_bridge.ahk or hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk; the Hotkey() calls are runtime calls in plain functions, so they all use the default always-active variant). They therefore all collide on one variant; last writer wins.

This is NOT a concurrency/race issue at all — AHK's cooperative threading is irrelevant here. It is a static registration-ordering defect, and it is UNCONDITIONAL in the default config:
1. metrics.enabled defaults true (_generated/features_manifest.ahk:144), so the auto-execute runs HookDispatcher.Start() (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1037) — Hotkey("~LButton", _OnLDown, "On") + L/M/R/wheel — then KL_Mouse_Start() registers the keylogger as a SUBSCRIBER via HookDispatcher.Register(EVT_MS_LDOWN, KLMouse.hk_ldown) (keylogger_mouse.ahk:500-509). The keylogger does NOT bind the raw hotkey; it relies on _OnLDown firing Dispatch(EVT_MS_LDOWN).
2. Later in the same auto-execute, HotstringPrefixWatcherInit() (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2207) -> _InstallMouseClickResetHooks() (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:195-197) calls Hotkey("~LButton"/"~MButton"/"~RButton", _OnMouseClickReset). This REPLACES the dispatcher's _OnLDown/_OnRDown/_OnMDown for those three buttons. From that point Dispatch(EVT_MS_LDOWN/RDOWN/MDOWN) never fires, so the keylogger's still-registered subscriber is dead — L/M/R click metrics silently stop, exactly as claimed. (Wheel events survive: only the dispatcher binds ~WheelUp/Down/Left/Right; the prefix watcher binds only L/M/R.)
3. If the LLM feature is enabled (Ollama installed+ready — gated by _LLM_Tray["enabled"] && LLM_Deps_IsReady, ui/tray_llm/actions.ahk:328), _LLM_PointerWatch_Start (llm_bridge.ahk:400-403) re-binds ~LButton/~RButton/~MButton/XButtons/wheel to _LLM_PointerWatch_ActivityFn, clobbering a THIRD time — now the prefix watcher's own _OnMouseClickReset is also dead, so the caret-reposition buffer reset stops, matching the stale-prefix/mis-fire scenario. I confirmed FeedReset/_ResetPrefixBuffer/LLM_Bridge_ResetPredictions all live only inside _OnMouseClickReset (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:200-212), so when it stops being the bound callback there is no other path that resets on a left click.

A FOURTH clobberer the finding missed: gestures.ahk:1544/1633 dynamically arm Hotkey("~RButton"/"~LButton", Gesture...) and, worse, disarm them with "Off" on release (1558/1647) — "Off" does not restore the previous owner, it disables the variant, so a gesture session leaves the shared button silenced until the next re-registration.

Two factual corrections to the finding, which is why I adjust severity down from high to medium: (a) it calls this the "default rich setup" via the LLM, but the LLM clobber requires Ollama installed and the feature enabled — not default; (b) the unconditional damage is the keylogger L/M/R click/scroll-button metrics plus the dispatcher fan-out for those buttons — the click-reset itself still works after step 2 (the prefix watcher becomes the new owner) and only dies once LLM takes over in step 3. So "silent loss of click metrics" is the always-true impact (analytics under-count, no lost keystroke), and the stale-prefix/mis-fire is LLM-conditional. Real, but analytics-degradation + a conditional correctness edge, not a guaranteed user-facing keystroke loss — medium fits better than high.

<a id="f-traymenu-setmenu-raw-add-drops-clicks"></a>
#### `traymenu-setmenu-raw-add-drops-clicks` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : adapters-os (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/tray_menu.ahk:67`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/tray_menu.ahk:51`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:50`
- **Description** : TrayMenuSetMenu builds actionable items with `try A_TrayMenu.Add(ItemTitle, ItemFn)` (line 67). The project has a documented, in-repo workaround (lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk) for an AHK 2.0 bug where ~30-50% of tray-menu WM_COMMAND callbacks are silently dropped; the invariant is 'Every actionable tray-menu item must use RegisterMenuItem ... Raw Menu.Add is only OK for separators/submenus/disabled headers.' This adapter ignores RegisterMenuItem entirely, so every item it adds is subject to the random click-drop.
- **Cause racine** : AHK 2.0 WM_COMMAND -> menu-callback dispatch intermittently fails to fire the registered callback (no error). RegisterMenuItem installs a parallel WM_COMMAND retry path keyed by Win32 item ID; raw Menu.Add does not participate, so dropped clicks are never recovered.
- **Scénario** : If the cross-platform shared layer (TrayMenu.spec.js) is wired to drive the Windows tray menu through the `setMenu` port — the explicit design intent of these adapters — the user clicks a tray toggle (e.g. enable a feature) and roughly one click in three does nothing, with no feedback and no log line. The user re-clicks, sometimes toggling twice.
- **Impact** : Intermittent, unexplained dead tray-menu clicks — the precise symptom the menu_dispatcher module exists to eliminate. Currently DORMANT because the live menu is built by ui/tray_menu.ahk (which correctly uses RegisterMenuItem) and `setMenu` has no production caller, but it is a primed trap: the adapter is the contract-sanctioned path and a future migration silently reintroduces the bug.
- **Correctif** : In TrayMenuSetMenu, route actionable items through `RegisterMenuItem(A_TrayMenu, ItemTitle, ItemFn)` instead of `A_TrayMenu.Add(ItemTitle, ItemFn)`. Keep raw Add only for separators/submenus/disabled headers. Since A_TrayMenu.Delete() wipes items, also prune the corresponding entries from _MenuDispatchCallbacks/_MenuDispatchLastFire (or accept stale-ID growth) to avoid unbounded Map growth on repeated setMenu calls.
- **Test** : meta-static test in tests/meta/: assert the source of adapters/tray_menu.ahk contains `RegisterMenuItem(` and does NOT contain a bare `A_TrayMenu.Add(` with two arguments (an actionable add) — only zero-arg/submenu adds. Pair with a headless-unit test that stubs RegisterMenuItem to record calls and asserts TrayMenuSetMenu([{title,fn}]) routed the item through it.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Code facts verified against source, not just the finding text. adapters/tray_menu.ahk:67 builds actionable items with `try A_TrayMenu.Add(ItemTitle, ItemFn)` — a raw two-arg Menu.Add. This violates the documented invariant in lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:50-67, which states EVERY user-actionable menu callback must go through RegisterMenuItem to participate in the WM_COMMAND retry bypass for AHK 2.0's silent ~30-50% click-drop bug. Separators are correctly handled via AddStandard() (line 60), so the violation is specifically the actionable-item path.

The invariant is genuine and consistently honored elsewhere: ui/tray_menu.ahk and ui/tray_llm/* register ~80 actionable items through RegisterMenuItem and reserve raw A_TrayMenu.Add only for submenu containers (e.g. lines 2462, 2495, 2603) and separators (2557, 2624). So the adapter is the one outlier.

Crucially, this is NOT an AHK-concurrency / lost-KEYSTROKE issue. The drop is in the WM_COMMAND mouse-click dispatch path, not the low-level keyboard hook, so the "lost-keystroke" category is mislabeled — no keystroke is dropped, and there is no race in the AHK cooperative-threading sense (the menu_dispatcher bypass is the project's chosen mitigation regardless of threading).

The bug is DORMANT, which I verified: grep shows TrayMenuSetMenu / ADAPTER_TRAY_MENU["setMenu"] has zero production callers in the Windows runtime — only test_adapter_compliance_new.ahk (compliance) and test_adapter_contract_vectors.ahk (calls it with an empty array). The live tray menu is built entirely by ui/tray_menu.ahk, which is compliant. So today no user click is dropped via this path; the risk only materializes if a future shared-layer migration wires setMenu to drive the real tray menu.

Net: a real, correctly-diagnosed latent contract violation, but unreachable in production and affecting mouse clicks rather than keystrokes. Real bug, wrong category and inflated severity.

### Catégorie : Race conditions (19)

<a id="f-llm-autotype-no-hse-suppress"></a>
#### `llm-autotype-no-hse-suppress` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-send-clipboard (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:480-509`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/prediction_engine.ahk:1119-1129`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:654-672`
- **Description** : LLM_Bridge_OnAccept (Tab hotkey) and LLM_Engine_OnResults inline-autotype both call TextSend(text, 0, 0), which for normal-length text uses _AHK_SendText -> SendText. They guard the keylogger via KL_MarkSynthetic but never call HSE_Suppress(true) / PrefixWatcherSuppress(true). The prefix-watcher InputHook is started with 'V L0' and is gated ONLY by _PrefixWatcherSuppressed / HSE_Suppressed (and A_IsSuspended) — NOT by KL_MarkSynthetic. Therefore every character of the auto-typed prediction re-enters _OnPrefixChar and is appended to HSE_Buffer and _PrefixBuffer as if the user typed it.
- **Cause racine** : Synthetic SendText input at SendLevel 0 is visible to the 'V L0' InputHook (MinSendLevel 0 sees all input). The hotstring engine relies on the HSE_Suppressed/PrefixWatcherSuppressed flags — not the keylogger synthetic flag — to keep its own output out of the buffer. The LLM paths only set the keylogger flag, so the hotstring side double-processes the injected text.
- **Scénario** : An LLM prediction whose text ends in a registered hotstring trigger plus a word-terminator (e.g. a prediction ending in 'asap ' or an apostrophe contraction) is accepted via Tab. As the prediction is typed back through SendText, the hotstring engine sees the trigger+terminator and fires a spurious expansion, mangling the auto-typed prediction. Also pollutes the prefix tooltip with the predicted text.
- **Impact** : Corrupted auto-typed predictions and stale/incorrect tooltip context; nondeterministic depending on prediction content. The HSE_DispatchMatch path explicitly added PrefixWatcherSuppress to fix exactly this class of desync for tap-hold callers, but the LLM accept path was not given the same guard.
- **Correctif** : Wrap the TextSend in LLM_Bridge_OnAccept and the inline-autotype TextSend in HSE_Suppress(true)/PrefixWatcherSuppress(true) for the burst, releasing on the same deferred SetTimer pattern HSE_DispatchMatch uses (negative timer ~ release delay), and reset _PrefixBuffer afterwards. Alternatively raise the SendLevel of the auto-type and the InputHook's MinSendLevel so synthetic predictions are inherently invisible to the hook.
- **Test** : headless-unit: register a star/end-char trigger in the HSE, then simulate LLM accept by calling the bridge accept with text containing that trigger while feeding the resulting characters through HSE_FeedChar; assert that with the suppression fix in place HSE_LastMatch stays '' (no spurious fire) and HSE_Buffer is not polluted. Encodes the root cause (auto-typed text must be suppressed from the engine).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Read the cited code. The mechanism holds under the AHK v2 cooperative model.

WHAT IS TRUE:
- The prefix-watcher InputHook is created with "V L0" (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:628), no "I0", and grep finds NO MinSendLevel set anywhere in the driver — so the hook uses the default and the "V" (Visible) flag means it observes synthetic input.
- _OnPrefixChar's only gates are A_IsSuspended (line 659) and `_PrefixWatcherSuppressed or HSE_Suppressed` (line 661). It does NOT consult the keylogger synthetic flag. After those gates it (a) feeds LLM_Bridge_FeedCharIfActive(Char) (line 674 -> llm_bridge.ahk:176 -> LLM_Bridge_OnChar) and (b) feeds HSE_FeedChar -> HSE_DispatchMatch (lines 735, 749), appending to _PrefixBuffer/HSE_Buffer.
- LLM_Bridge_OnAccept (llm_bridge.ahk:480-509) calls only `KL_MarkSynthetic("llm")` then TextSend(text,0,0); it never calls HSE_Suppress/PrefixWatcherSuppress. The inline-autotype branch (prediction_engine.ahk:1119-1129) is worse: it calls TextSend(text,0,0) with NO synthetic marking and NO suppression at all.
- TextSend (adapters/text_sender.ahk:104) routes normal-length text through `SendText(Text)`, which inherits the script-wide SendMode("Event") (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:114) at the ambient send level. This is the SAME class of synthetic emit as the reference path.

DECISIVE EMPIRICAL PROOF (not just docs reasoning): HSE_DispatchMatch — the canonical hotstring fire path — emits SendInput(Burst) at the ambient level and STILL must call HSE_Suppress(true) AND PrefixWatcherSuppress(true) around the burst (hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1261-1270), released on a deferred SetTimer (1382-1384). The comment at 1262-1267 documents this was added to stop the backspaces+replacement from polluting _PrefixBuffer and staling the tooltip "causing incorrect tooltip lookups afterward". If synthetic sends were invisible to this V-flagged hook, that suppression would be dead code. It is not — therefore synthetic sends DO re-enter _OnPrefixChar in this exact configuration, and the LLM paths, lacking those flags, are double-processed. This is the same desync class the project already fixed for tap-hold callers and never extended to the LLM accept/inline paths.

WHY IT IS NOT BLOCKED BY THE CONCURRENCY MODEL: there is no Critical or suppression spanning the LLM TextSend that would keep its synthetic OnChar out of the buffer. The send is fully synchronous on the accept thread, the Visible synthetic chars are delivered and re-enter the hook, and the gates at 659/661 are both open (not suspended, neither suppress flag set). So the buffer/LLM-context pollution is essentially deterministic whenever a prediction is accepted/auto-typed; a spurious HOTSTRING EXPANSION additionally requires the predicted text to contain a registered trigger + terminator.

ONE CORRECTION TO THE FINDING: its root_cause names "L0 = MinSendLevel 0 sees all input". That is technically wrong — in InputHook options "L0" is the LENGTH limit (0 = no length-based termination, per the comment at line 620), not the send level. The visibility comes from the "V" flag plus the absence of any suppression, not from L0. The conclusion is right; the cited reason is mislabeled.

NET: real defect, mechanism verified against source. Two distinct bugs: (1) buffer/tooltip/LLM-context desync (near-certain on every accept), (2) content-dependent spurious expansion (only when prediction ends in trigger+terminator). The inline-autotype path also pollutes the keylogger `chars` count because it omits KL_MarkSynthetic entirely.

<a id="f-boolean-suppression-released-early-on-overlapping-fires"></a>
#### `boolean-suppression-released-early-on-overlapping-fires` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : hotstrings-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1261-1262`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1382-1388`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:286-295`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:168-178`
- **Description** : HSE_Suppressed, _PrefixWatcherSuppressed and the keylogger synth_active flag are plain booleans. Each fire sets them true, then arms a SEPARATE one-shot SetTimer(...false / KL_ClearSynthetic, -60ms). If a second expansion fires within ~60 ms of the first (a magic-key roll cascade, fast repeated triggers, or a tap-hold Space fire landing just after an OnChar fire), the SECOND fire re-sets the flags true and arms its own release — but the FIRST timer (already armed at T+0) fires at T+60 and clears the flags while the second burst's BackSpace/replacement events are still draining through the InputHook.
- **Cause racine** : A boolean 'suppressed' state shared across thread boundaries needs reference counting (increment on suppress, decrement on release, only un-suppress at zero) OR a single coalesced release timer that is re-armed (not duplicated) on each fire. Negative-period SetTimer with the SAME callback re-arms a single timer, but here HSE_Suppress(false), PrefixWatcherSuppress(false) and KL_ClearSynthetic are passed as DISTINCT fresh closures ((*) => ...) every fire, so AHK treats each as a different timer object and they do not coalesce — the earliest one wins and clears the flag prematurely.
- **Scénario** : User types a magic-key sequence that cascades (e.g. a roll p'★ → ct then the ct★ expansion, or rapidly fires two adjacent text expansions within 60 ms). The first expansion's 60 ms release timer fires mid-second-burst, dropping HSE_Suppressed/_PrefixWatcherSuppressed to false. The second burst's own replayed BackSpace + replacement characters then re-enter HSE_FeedChar / _OnPrefixChar as if they were physical input, polluting HSE_Buffer and _PrefixBuffer (the exact 'c'était → Taïwan preview' / buffer-drift class the suppression exists to prevent), and the keylogger mis-attributes the tail of the burst as manual chars (synth flag cleared early).
- **Impact** : Buffer desync and spurious tooltips after fast/cascading expansions, plus keylogger PII/metric mis-attribution (synthetic replacement chars counted as manual typing). Not a hard crash, but it undermines the central invariant that the watcher never observes its own output, and it is most likely exactly when the user is typing fast — the scenario the whole Critical/suppression machinery targets.
- **Correctif** : Replace the three boolean flags with small integer counters (suppress depth) incremented on enter and decremented on the deferred release, un-suppressing only at zero; OR arm ONE named release function (a module-level function reference, not a fresh lambda each call) via negative SetTimer so successive fires re-arm the single timer to the latest +60 ms instead of stacking independent early-firing timers. Apply the same to KL_ClearSynthetic (use a counter so an early clear cannot un-mark a still-in-flight burst).
- **Test** : Headless-unit: with the _SendHook seam installed, drive two HSE_DispatchMatch calls back-to-back without advancing the (mockable) release timer, then run the release once and assert the flag is STILL true after the first release if a second suppress is outstanding (counter > 0). Concretely: suppress depth must be 2 after two fires, and a single release must leave it at 1 (still suppressed). Also assert PrefixWatcherSuppress/HSE_Suppress/KL_ClearSynthetic are re-armed on a single timer reference rather than via per-call lambdas (meta/static check that the SetTimer argument is a named function, not an inline closure, would catch the regression structurally).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read all four cited sites. The finding's two load-bearing claims both hold against the real source and the AHK v2 model.

1) The SetTimer non-coalescing claim is TRUE. At hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1382-1388 (and the identical raw-callback block at 1183-1187, plus hotstring_engine.ahk:526) each release is armed as a FRESH fat-arrow closure: `SetTimer((*) => HSE_Suppress(false), -60)`, `SetTimer((*) => PrefixWatcherSuppress(false), -60)`, `SetTimer((*) => KL_ClearSynthetic(), -60)`. AHK v2 keys timers on the callback object's identity; a new `(*) =>` lambda is a distinct object every call, so two fires arm two independent one-shot timers (one at fireT1+60, one at fireT2+60). They do not re-arm a single timer. The flags themselves are plain booleans: HSE_Suppressed (engine_main:195/643), _PrefixWatcherSuppressed (prefix_watcher:58/288), Keylogger.synth_active (keylogger:169/176). No reference counting.

2) The premature-clear is reachable. Critical DOES serialize each dispatch: _OnPrefixChar sets Critical("On") at prefix_watcher:723 before HSE_DispatchMatch (749), and the atomic SendInput burst is itself wrapped in Critical (engine_main:1341-1351). So each individual expansion is atomic and the deferred timers (armed in the finally while Critical is still On) cannot fire until _OnPrefixChar returns. That is exactly what the existing meta test (tests/meta/test_input_serialization.ahk) pins — and it is ORTHOGONAL to this bug. The release timers fire AFTER Critical clears, on the single AHK thread, between subsequent keystroke threads. Two physical chars typed <60 ms apart that each fire (the finding's magic-key roll cascade p'★→ct then ct★→c'était, or two adjacent expansions) each re-set the flags true and arm their own timer. Fire #1's timer fires at T+60 and drops the flags to false while fire #2's window should run to T+90.

3) The premise that this matters — that synthetic events re-enter the hook during the drain window — is established by the codebase itself, not just the finding. The watcher's InputHook is `InputHook("V L0")` (prefix_watcher:628): MinSendLevel 0, so it does NOT filter the script's own injected keys by send level.
