The whole suppression system exists for this: engine_main:1120-1124 ("wait... for the OS message loop to drain the BackSpace/Replacement events through the InputHook so they are filtered out instead of polluting the buffer"), prefix_watcher:662-666 ("Synthetic replacement chars are EXPECTED here... filtered out of the buffer by design"), and the OnKeyDown comment at 863-867 ("the BackSpace events the dispatcher fires via SendEvent reach this callback"). So burst #2's replayed BackSpace/{Text} events arriving at T+65 (after timer A cleared the flags at T+60) pass the `if (_PrefixWatcherSuppressed or HSE_Suppressed)` guard at prefix_watcher:661/868 as FALSE and are appended to _PrefixBuffer / fed to HSE_FeedChar as if manual — the buffer-drift class the suppression targets. The synchronous _ResetPrefixBuffer(true) in the finally (engine_main:1379) cannot help: it runs at fire-T2 (~T+30) and cannot un-pollute events that arrive at T+65. KL_ClearSynthetic has the same shape, so the tail of burst #2 is mis-attributed as manual chars in the keylogger metrics.

This is NOT a false positive: the Critical serialization is real but only guarantees per-dispatch atomicity; it deliberately does not address cross-fire release coalescing, which is a separate, unguarded mechanism. The finding correctly identifies that the earliest-armed timer wins.

<a id="f-activitysim-global-collision"></a>
#### `activitysim-global-collision` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : gestures (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:650`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:657`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/shortcuts/win.ahk:129`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/shortcuts/win.ahk:160`
- **Description** : Both modules read/write the SAME global ActivitySimulation but implement entirely different machines. shortcuts/win.ahk's StartActivitySimulation arms SimulateActivity, AwakeCheckMouseMoved, AwakeReturnToOrigin timers, three ~*$ mouse-cancel hotkeys and an AwakeInputHook, and StopActivitySimulation tears them all down. gestures.ahk's GestureToggleActivitySimulation just flips the flag and (when turning on) arms its OWN GestureSimulateActivity timer; when turning OFF it does nothing to stop win.ahk's machinery, and vice-versa.
- **Cause racine** : Two owners of one mutable global with no single source of truth (violates the project's single-source-of-truth rule). Turning the flag False via the gesture path leaves win.ahk's SimulateActivity/AwakeCheckMouseMoved timers running (they early-return on the flag, but the ~*$LButton/RButton/MButton cancel hotkeys and AwakeInputHook installed by win.ahk are never disarmed). Turning it on via the gesture path while win.ahk's keep-awake is active double-arms competing mouse-jiggle timers writing to the cursor.
- **Scénario** : User enables keep-awake via Win+M (win.ahk), then fires the 'activity_simulation' gesture. The gesture flips ActivitySimulation to False, which makes win.ahk's StopActivitySimulation never be called — the three ~*$mouse cancel hotkeys and AwakeInputHook stay installed indefinitely, intercepting every mouse click/keypress to (uselessly) re-check a now-false flag, and the gesture's own jiggle timer is NOT started. The user thinks keep-awake is off but stray hooks persist; or the reverse leaves two jiggle timers fighting over the cursor.
- **Impact** : Leaked global mouse/keyboard hotkeys and an InputHook that survive the toggle, plus contradictory cursor jiggling. Resource leak + behavioral corruption of a user-facing feature.
- **Correctif** : Make the gesture action call the canonical ToggleActivitySimulation() from shortcuts/win.ahk instead of re-implementing it: change GESTURE_ACTIONS["activity_simulation"].Fn to (*) => ToggleActivitySimulation(), and delete GestureToggleActivitySimulation/GestureSimulateActivity entirely. If win.ahk's feature can be disabled (Features["shortcuts"]["move"] false) the gesture must guard for that and surface a message rather than silently writing the shared global.
- **Test** : headless-unit: assert there is exactly one writer of ActivitySimulation that also owns timer teardown — e.g. a meta/static test asserting modules/gestures.ahk does not contain its own 'SetTimer(GestureSimulateActivity' once the fix lands, and that GESTURE_ACTIONS["activity_simulation"] dispatches to ToggleActivitySimulation. Plus a unit test: stub the timer/hotkey calls, call the gesture Fn twice and assert the flag flips and win.ahk's StopActivitySimulation teardown path is invoked on the off transition.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code. The defect is real, but it is NOT a race condition in the AHK concurrency sense — re-categorize it as a shared-state / single-source-of-truth violation.

Two independent state machines own the same global `ActivitySimulation`:
- win.ahk:129-176 — `StartActivitySimulation` arms `SimulateActivity`, `AwakeCheckMouseMoved`, `AwakeReturnToOrigin` timers, three `~*$LButton/RButton/MButton` cancel hotkeys, and an `AwakeInputHook` (L0 I, OnChar+OnKeyDown). `StopActivitySimulation` tears all of that down (SetTimer …,0; Hotkey …,"Off"; AwakeInputHook.Stop()).
- gestures.ahk:650-655 — `GestureToggleActivitySimulation` only does `ActivitySimulation := !ActivitySimulation` and, on the on-transition, `SetTimer(GestureSimulateActivity, Random(1000,5000))`. It NEVER touches win.ahk's InputHook or the three mouse-cancel hotkeys.

Both are user-reachable. The gesture function is wired at gestures.ahk:496 into GESTURE_ACTIONS["activity_simulation"], reachable two ways: (a) the touchpad DispatchGesture path (gestures.ahk:1702 `GESTURE_ACTIONS[ActionName].Fn()`) for any slot the user assigns to "activity_simulation"; (b) the configurable-keyboard layer — KEYBOARD_SHORTCUT_DEFAULTS["win_m"]="activity_simulation" (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:475) bound at ErgoptiPlus.ahk:989 as `Hotkey("#m", RunKeyboardShortcutAction("win_m"))`. win.ahk:115 separately does `AddShortcut("#","m",ToggleActivitySimulation)` → `Hotkey("#" . RetrieveScancode("m"), …)` and `ahk.shortcuts.move` defaults true (features_manifest.ahk:177). modules/shortcuts.ahk (hence win.ahk) is #Included at ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2186, AFTER the line-989 loop, so win.ahk's `#sc032` registration is the later one and wins the physical Win+M trigger — meaning via Win+M the correct full win.ahk machine runs and the gesture's win_m route is effectively dead. So the keyboard path is mostly self-protecting by accident of include order.

The genuinely broken, live path is the TOUCHPAD GESTURE. With AHK cooperative single-threading there is no torn read of the flag — each toggle body runs atomically between rest points — so the "race-condition" framing in the finding is inaccurate. But the ownership collision is real: user enables keep-awake via Win+M (full win.ahk machine + InputHook + 3 cancel hotkeys live), then a swipe mapped to activity_simulation flips the flag to False. win.ahk's StopActivitySimulation is never called, so its `AwakeInputHook` (global keyboard InputHook) and the three `~*$mouse` cancel hotkeys LEAK — they stay installed indefinitely, intercepting every click/keypress to re-check a now-false flag. win.ahk's SimulateActivity/AwakeCheckMouseMoved timers do early-return on the flag, so they are benign, but the InputHook and hotkeys are a true resource leak. The reverse (gesture on, then Win+M off, or gesture on while win.ahk on) can leave the gesture's own GestureSimulateActivity timer running with no off-switch from win.ahk's Stop path, jiggling the cursor with no way to cancel via the documented mouse/keypress cancels. This is the scenario the finding describes, and it holds.

So: confirmed real defect (leaked global InputHook + 3 mouse hotkeys, plus an orphaned jiggle timer), but mis-categorized as race-condition and somewhat over-rated at high, because it requires the non-default action of mapping a gesture slot to activity_simulation while keep-awake is on; the default keyboard Win+M path is shielded by include order.

<a id="f-pending-entries-cross-thread-race"></a>
#### `pending-entries-cross-thread-race` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : keylogger-core (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:643`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1217`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1220`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:277`
- **Description** : KL_AppendLog does `Keylogger._pending_entries.Push(entry)` (line 643). KL_LogShortcut -> KL_AppendLog is called INLINE from KL_Hook_OnKeyDown (keylogger_hook.ahk:277) on the keystroke pseudo-thread for every modifier chord. KL_IngestOnce (a separate 5 s timer pseudo-thread) drains with `for _, e in Keylogger._pending_entries { entries.Push(e) }` then immediately `Keylogger._pending_entries := []` (lines 1218-1220). The ingest tick can be interrupted by an incoming Ctrl/Alt chord at the rest point between finishing the for-loop and executing the `:= []` reassignment.
- **Cause racine** : Check-then-clear race under AHK cooperative threading. Thread B (ingest) reads the array into `entries`, is interrupted at a line boundary by thread A (keystroke OnKeyDown -> KL_LogShortcut -> Push) which appends a new entry, then thread B resumes and executes `_pending_entries := []`, discarding that just-pushed entry. No Critical brackets the drain; Array.Push and `:= []` are distinct interruptible statements.
- **Scénario** : User fires a shortcut (Ctrl+S, Ctrl+C, Win+key) at the exact moment the 5 s ingest tick is draining the queue. The shortcut event is silently lost from data.sql (today.log copy survives, but the in-RAM fast path — the one consulted first per the comment at lines 141-144 — drops it).
- **Impact** : Intermittently lost shortcut (and any other inline-logged) events under normal use; under-counts the shortcuts n-gram / agg tables. Hard to diagnose because today.log still looks complete.
- **Correctif** : Atomically swap, never read-then-clear: as the FIRST thing in KL_IngestOnce do `Critical("On"); snapshot := Keylogger._pending_entries; Keylogger._pending_entries := []; Critical("Off")`, then iterate `snapshot` outside Critical so the keystroke hook is starved for nanoseconds only. Equivalently wrap the Push in KL_AppendLog in Critical.
- **Test** : headless-unit: refactor the drain into KL_DrainPending() that atomically swaps+clears and returns the snapshot. Push N entries, call KL_DrainPending, push a new entry, call KL_DrainPending again, assert the second drain returns the new entry (regression target: 'drain must atomically swap, not read-then-clear').  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code; the finding's mechanics check out against the real source.

CALL PATH (confirmed): keylogger_hook.ahk:277 `KL_LogShortcut(sk, ...)` is called INLINE inside `KL_Hook_OnKeyDown`. That handler runs via HookDispatcher.Dispatch (lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:187-198) for the process-wide InputHook OnKeyDown. KL_LogShortcut -> KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:751-758, 618) -> `Keylogger._pending_entries.Push(entry)` (line 643). The drain is keylogger.ahk:1217-1220: `for _, e in _pending_entries { entries.Push(e) }` then `_pending_entries := []`, with NO Critical bracket. The ingest runs on a periodic 5 s SetTimer (line 1557, INGEST_TICK_MS=5000).

NO SERIALIZATION ON EITHER SIDE: Dispatch (hook_dispatcher.ahk:187-198) is NOT Critical — only Register/Unregister (129,153) hold Critical, and those merely guard subscriber-list mutation. So the per-keystroke fan-out that does the Push is fully interruptible, and the drain block is too. The Push at 643 and the `:= []` at 1220 are distinct interruptible statements with a line boundary between 1219 and 1220.

WHY THE DEFAULT-INTERRUPT GRACE WINDOW DOES NOT SAVE IT: AHK's default `Thread "Interrupt", 15, 1000` makes a new SetTimer thread uninterruptible only for its first 15 ms OR first 1000 executed lines (no override of Thread Interrupt exists anywhere in the tree — grep found none). But the drain at 1217-1220 runs AFTER `KL_ReadNewTodayLog()` (called at 1214), which does a blocking FileObject.Flush() (NTFS+AV syscall) plus FileOpen/Seek/ReadLine loop up to INGEST_BATCH_LINES=5000 with a KL_JsonDecode per line plus Close (keylogger.ahk:1161-1204). On a busy tick this exceeds both 1000 lines and 15 ms of wall-clock, so by the time execution reaches the drain the grace window is exhausted and the thread is interruptible at the 1219->1220 boundary. An OnKeyDown thread (a default-priority pseudo-thread dispatched from the message pump for a Ctrl/Alt/Win chord) will interrupt there, run the Push, then the ingest thread resumes and `_pending_entries := []` discards the just-pushed entry. This is a textbook check-then-clear / read-then-clear race, real under AHK's cooperative model.

CORROBORATION OF THE INVARIANT: tests/meta/test_input_serialization.ahk shows the project already Critical-serializes the keystroke hot path (_RemapEmit, _OnPrefixChar, HSE_DispatchMatch) precisely because high A_MaxThreads + InputHook callbacks interleave. The same model applies to _pending_entries, which is currently unguarded — an oversight, not an intentional safe path.

NOT A FALSE POSITIVE: I considered (a) Critical on Dispatch (absent), (b) the default 15 ms/1000-line interrupt grace (exhausted by the preceding disk read), (c) A_IsSuspended gating (only affects whether handlers run at all, not the race), (d) #MaxThreadsPerHotkey (irrelevant — the interrupter is an InputHook/timer thread, not the same hotkey). None close the gap.

SEVERITY DOWNGRADE to medium: the loss is real but bounded and partly self-healing. KL_AppendLog writes the entry to today.log on disk (fh.Write at 650) BEFORE/around the Push, and today_log_offset only advances after a successful data.sql append — so the lost entry is recovered on the next process start's cold-replay of today.log. The defect is limited to the steady-state in-RAM fast path: an intermittently dropped entry from the current process's data.sql/agg projection (under-counting the shortcuts n-gram/agg tables until a restart re-ingests). Intermittent, narrow one-line window, recoverable on restart -> medium, not high.

<a id="f-metrics-focus-cache-cross-thread-race"></a>
#### `metrics-focus-cache-cross-thread-race` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : tooltip-widget (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/metrics_filters.ahk:101`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/metrics_filters.ahk:161`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/metrics_filters.ahk:93`
- **Description** : MetricsFocusCache (static process_name/title/class/last_hwnd/last_at) is the shared state behind the per-event privacy gate. MF_RefreshFocus writes last_hwnd (113), then process_name (114), title (115), class (116), then last_at LAST (117). MF_ShouldFilter reads proc/title/class to decide whether to drop the event. MF_ShouldFilter is called from at least four distinct thread contexts: the keyboard InputHook OnChar (keylogger_hook.ahk:210), the low-level mouse hooks (keylogger_mouse.ahk:180/219/254/323/412), the clipboard handler (keylogger_clipboard.ahk:93/133), and sensor/window-topology paths (keylogger_sensors.ahk:94, keylogger_window_topology.ahk:121). None hold Critical.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 is cooperatively multi-threaded: a mouse-hook / clipboard / timer thread can interrupt the keyboard thread (or vice versa) at a rest point between any two lines. If thread B interrupts MF_RefreshFocus after it has updated process_name but before title/class (lines 114->115), and B's own MF_RefreshFocus finds last_at still within MF_FOCUS_TTL_MS (250 ms) and returns immediately, B's MF_ShouldFilter reads a MIXED snapshot: new process_name with stale title/class (or any partial combination). Because last_at is written last, the TTL check can also let the interrupted partial write be observed as 'fresh enough' to use.
- **Scénario** : The keylogger is enabled. The user moves to a private-browsing/incognito window (or a password field) and starts typing while the mouse is also moving (mouse hook firing). A mouse-hook MF_ShouldFilter interrupts the keystroke's MF_RefreshFocus mid-update, so the keystroke is evaluated against a torn focus snapshot whose title field still reflects the previous, non-private window. The private-browsing title pattern fails to match and the keystroke is logged to disk — exactly the data the privacy filter exists to suppress.
- **Impact** : Security/privacy: keystrokes from a private/password/system-auth window can leak into the metrics log during the narrow torn-read window. Also the reverse (spuriously dropping events). Narrow timing window and requires the opt-in keylogger to be on, but the affected code IS the privacy chokepoint, so correctness matters disproportionately.
- **Correctif** : Make MF_RefreshFocus update atomic from a reader's perspective: build the new values into locals first, then publish them in one shot and set last_at LAST so a reader either sees the fully-previous or fully-new snapshot. Better: wrap the publish in 'Critical "On" ... Critical "Off"' around the field assignments (kept short — no Send/Sleep/blocking call inside), or store the snapshot as a single immutable object reference (proc/title/class in one object) and swap the reference in one assignment so readers get a consistent tuple. Keep the WinGet* calls (which can block) OUTSIDE the Critical region.
- **Test** : headless-unit: directly exercise the publish helper. Refactor the field publish into a small MF_PublishFocus(hwnd, proc, title, cls) that sets all fields then last_at; assert that after it returns the four fields are mutually consistent. To encode the race intent, a meta/static test can assert MF_RefreshFocus assigns last_at after proc/title/class (ordering invariant) and/or that the publish path is wrapped in Critical.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read the actual cited code (lib/metrics/metrics_filters.ahk) and every caller. The finding's factual claims hold:

1) MF_RefreshFocus (lines 101-118) publishes MetricsFocusCache fields non-atomically: last_hwnd (113), process_name (114), title (115), class (116), last_at LAST (117). Each is a separate static-property assignment. MF_ShouldFilter (162-165) early-calls MF_RefreshFocus() then copies process_name/title/class into locals across three separate statements.

2) The four caller-context families are genuinely distinct, interruptible AHK pseudo-threads, NOT one serialized path:
   - Keyboard: InputHook OnChar/OnKeyDown via HookDispatcher.Dispatch (keylogger_hook.ahk:210/293).
   - Mouse: KL_Mouse_On* are Hotkey()-backed subscribers registered through HookDispatcher (keylogger_mouse.ahk:489-509), firing MF_ShouldFilter at 180/219/254/323/412.
   - Clipboard: ~^v / ~+Insert hotkeys (keylogger_clipboard.ahk:190-191 -> :93/:133).
   - Timers: SetTimer callbacks KL_Sensors_Tick (sensors:94) and KL_Topo_Tick (topology:121), plus the mouse park timer.

3) There is NO Critical anywhere in the keylogger module, and decisively HookDispatcher.Dispatch (hook_dispatcher.ahk:187-198) runs its fan-out loop WITHOUT Critical — Critical is only used to protect subscriber-LIST mutation in Register/Unregister (129/153), not the per-event Dispatch. So nothing serializes access to MetricsFocusCache. No global Thread "Interrupt" override exists, so AHK's default interruptibility applies.

The AHK cooperative model makes the race real, not a generic-threading false positive: a fresh thread is uninterruptible only for the ~15 ms / 1000-line default grace window, after which it can be interrupted at line boundaries. MF_RefreshFocus issues four cross-process WinGet* queries (WinGetID/ProcessName/Title/Class); WinGetTitle/WinGetText against a busy or unresponsive target can stall well past 15 ms, so the refreshing thread becomes interruptible mid-publish. A second event thread (mouse/clipboard/timer/keyboard) can then run, find MetricsFocusCache.last_at still within MF_FOCUS_TTL_MS (250 ms) from the PREVIOUS complete refresh, early-return from its own MF_RefreshFocus, and read a torn tuple — e.g. new process_name with stale title/class. Writing last_at last is exactly backwards for a reader: the stale-but-fresh last_at keeps the half-updated fields readable as "valid" for the entire in-flight refresh. On the privacy chokepoint this can let a private-browsing/password/system-auth keystroke be evaluated against a stale title that fails the MF_PRIVATE_TITLE_PATTERNS match and get logged to disk (and the symmetric spurious-drop). The project already treats this precise class of non-Critical interleave as a shipped, real bug (tests/meta/test_input_serialization.ahk), confirming the model and the established remedy (Critical-serialize or publish atomically).

Why not higher than medium: exploitability is narrow and probabilistic. It requires (a) the opt-in keylogger enabled, (b) an interrupt landing inside the brief refresh window after the grace period, AND (c) the interrupted partial snapshot to be specifically the one whose title field would otherwise have triggered the private filter. It is not a deterministic leak, but it does corrupt the exact code whose job is to never leak, so medium is appropriate (not low). I considered downgrading to low on rarity but the privacy-chokepoint placement and the bidirectional failure (leak AND spurious drop) justify keeping medium.

<a id="f-space-tap-dispatch-not-critical"></a>
#### `space-tap-dispatch-not-critical` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-keystroke-pipeline (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/space.ahk:97-116`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1341-1351`
- **Description** : _SpaceTap() calls `TextPressKey('Space')` (SendInput) then `HSE_FeedChar(' ')` and, on a match, `HSE_DispatchMatch(...)`. The OnChar fire path wraps the whole feed->dispatch region in Critical (_OnPrefixChar line 723) so no other key's remap thread can interleave; the space-tap path has no outer Critical — it relies solely on the inner Critical that HSE_DispatchMatch's atomic-send branch sets (line 1341).
- **Cause racine** : HSE_DispatchMatch's inner Critical only spans the single SendInput burst; the window between `TextPressKey('Space')` (which already injected the space) and the BackSpace+replacement burst is interruptible. A physical key pressed in that gap can be processed (its own remap SendEvent) before the expansion burst lands, so the just-typed space and the following char can interleave with the trigger deletion. The Notepad branch additionally Sleeps (clipboard) and explicitly drops Critical, widening the gap.
- **Scénario** : User holds nothing, taps Space to terminate a word that triggers an end-char hotstring (e.g. `ia`+space->`IA`), and immediately types the next letter. The space-tap path emits the literal space first, then runs the expansion non-atomically; a fast next keypress can splice between the space emission and the backspace+replacement, yielding a transposed/garbled result.
- **Impact** : Occasional interleave/transposition when an end-char expansion fires from the Space tap-hold rather than from a normally-typed terminator.
- **Correctif** : Wrap the feed->dispatch region of _SpaceTap in `Critical('On')`/restore exactly like _OnPrefixChar (save/restore so it nests safely; HSE_DispatchMatch already drops Critical for its Notepad Sleep branch). Order the literal-space emission AFTER (or fold it into) the expansion decision so the buffer state and the screen state cannot diverge mid-gap.
- **Test** : headless-unit with _SendHook recorder: seed HSE_Buffer with an end-char trigger body, call the space-tap dispatch helper, and assert the recorded burst is the atomic backspace+replacement (no interleave seam) and that HSE_Buffer mirrors the post-expansion screen. Assert the dispatch helper sets Critical (introspect via a Critical-state probe or factor the region into a testable function).  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I verified the cited code against the AHK v2 cooperative model and the finding holds.

ASYMMETRY IS REAL. The OnChar path wraps the match->fire->buffer region in Critical: hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:723 calls Critical("On") BEFORE HSE_FeedChar(Char) (line 735) and before HSE_DispatchMatch (line 749), so the whole keystroke including the expansion burst is uninterruptible. The space-tap path (space.ahk:97-116) has NO such wrap: _SpaceTap does TextPressKey("Space") (= SendInput("{Space}"), via text_sender.ahk:125-136), then HSE_FeedChar(" "), then HSE_DispatchMatch(...) with no outer Critical. The note at the finding crediting "_OnPrefixChar line 723" is slightly mis-cited (723 in the engine file is inside HSE_TryRepeatKey) but the actual Critical at watcher line 723 exists and does exactly what the finding claims.

THE GAP IS GENUINELY INTERRUPTIBLE AND NOT TINY. HSE_DispatchMatch's inner Critical (engine line 1341) only spans the single SendInput burst (1341-1351). Everything between dispatch entry and that point runs OUTSIDE Critical on the space-tap path: HSE_Suppress(true) (1261), PrefixWatcherSuppress(true) (1268), KL_MarkSynthetic (1275), a real SendEvent("{SC138 Up}") when _ALTGR_KANA_FIXUP (1277-1279, itself a rest point), plus the TextPressKey->FeedChar prelude. The space hotkey thread (SpaceTapHold -> _SpaceTapOrDispatch -> _SpaceTap) never calls Critical.

THE INTERRUPTER IS NOT GATED. Every remapped physical key fires _RemapEmit (layout.ahk:376-380) = Critical("On"); SendEvent(SendStr). SendMode is Event globally. _RemapEmit has NO HSE_Suppressed/A_IsSuspended guard, so the suppression flags set early in HSE_DispatchMatch do NOT stop it — that protection is watcher-only. Critical on the CURRENT thread is the sole barrier. Since the space-tap thread is not Critical, a next-key remap thread queued by the hook can interrupt at any rest point in the prelude and SendEvent its character BEFORE the backspace+replacement burst -> transposition/interleave. This is the exact "outpubct"/"cmooe" class the meta test test_input_serialization.ahk was written to prevent, and the space-tap path is the one keystroke entry that was never given the Critical that the other three (_RemapEmit, _OnPrefixChar, HSE_DispatchMatch atomic send) all received.

Scenario realism: the path is reached only on a genuine tap (KeyWait returns 1, Space already released) of a Space configured as a hotstring terminator, AND the user must type the next letter fast enough to land in the prelude window. Real but narrow, so medium is right — not high (only the end-char-from-Space-tap subset, not normal typing) and not low (it is a true correctness race in the keystroke hot path, and Notepad's branch widens it with a Sleep).

<a id="f-roi-map-mutation-during-enumeration-race"></a>
#### `roi-map-mutation-during-enumeration-race` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : gap-keylogger-roi-onchar-race-and-pause (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:233`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:134`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:188`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:204`
- **Description** : KL_Roi_HalflifeTick runs `for trig, last_tick in KLRoi.trigger_last_use` (line 233) and, inside the loop body, calls KL_AppendLog which performs fh.Write() (a blocking disk write = a thread rest point). At that rest point the half-life timer thread can be interrupted by the keystroke pseudo-thread (InputHook OnChar is interruptible — no Critical anywhere on the KL_Roi_OnChar path). The interrupting KL_Roi_OnHotstring does `KLRoi.trigger_last_use[StrLower(trigger)] := A_TickCount` (line 134, can insert a new key and trigger a rehash), and KL_Roi_ProcessWord mutates the sibling KLRoi.word_counts via Push (line 188) and Delete in the prune loop (line 204-209). Resuming the half-life enumerator over a map whose internal hash table was reallocated mid-iteration yields undefined behavior in AHK v2: skipped/duplicated entries, or a thrown error. Symmetrically, the prune loop in KL_Roi_ProcessWord (`for k,v in KLRoi.word_counts ... Delete`) can itself be interrupted by another OnChar that inserts into word_counts.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 cooperative threading: a SetTimer thread and an InputHook OnChar thread share KLRoi.word_counts / trigger_last_use with no Critical section. A Map enumeration whose backing store is resized (insert that grows capacity, or Delete) by an interrupting thread is undefined — the same 'modified during enumeration' hazard keylogger-sensors flagged for app_categories, on two maps that were never audited.
- **Scénario** : User is typing fast (≥8 keys/s, so OnChar fires continuously and word/hotstring counts are being inserted) at the moment the 1 h half-life timer fires. The timer's enumeration of trigger_last_use is interrupted during its fh.Write by an OnChar that inserts a new trigger key, rehashing the map. The half-life tick then either silently emits duplicate/garbled trigger_halflife rows or throws — and because the throw is inside a SetTimer callback it is SWALLOWED (never reaches the logger), so the corruption is invisible.
- **Impact** : Silent data corruption in the ROI metrics and a swallowed crash inside a timer callback. Hard to diagnose precisely because the error never surfaces. Cross-thread state race on two previously-unaudited maps.
- **Correctif** : Wrap the read side in Critical: in KL_Roi_HalflifeTick snapshot the map under `Critical('On')` (copy keys/values into a local Array, then `Critical('Off')`) and iterate the snapshot — never call KL_AppendLog (which blocks on fh.Write) while holding the live map open. Likewise guard the word_counts prune in KL_Roi_ProcessWord: build the `prune` list under Critical, then Delete under Critical, keeping the section tiny (no I/O inside it). Mirror the HookDispatcher.Register pattern (Critical On/Off around list mutation) that the codebase already established.
- **Test** : headless-unit: seed KLRoi.trigger_last_use with N entries, run a helper that emulates the interleave — start enumerating, mid-loop insert a new key (forcing a rehash) and Delete an existing one, and assert the tick completes without throwing and visits each surviving key exactly once. Assert that after the fix KL_Roi_HalflifeTick iterates a *copy* (e.g. expose KL_Roi_SnapshotHalflife() returning an Array) so the live map is never open across a write. A meta/static test can also assert KL_Roi_HalflifeTick and KL_Roi_ProcessWord contain 'Critical'.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : The mechanism is real against the AHK v2 cooperative model, but the finding overstates probability and severity. Verified against the actual source:

UNGUARDED PATHS CONFIRMED. There is NO `Critical` anywhere in the entire keylogger module tree (grep across modules/keylogger returned zero matches). `HookDispatcher.Dispatch` (lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:187-198) only checks `A_IsSuspended` then loops subscribers in try/catch — it does NOT hold Critical — so `KL_Hook_OnChar`→`KL_Roi_OnChar` and the hotstring-fire path `KL_Roi_OnHotstring` run fully interruptible. `ErgoptiPlus.ahk:111` sets `A_MaxThreads := 64`, explicitly permitting concurrent pseudo-threads. `KL_AppendLog` (keylogger.ahk:650) does call `fh.Write()`, a genuine blocking rest point. `KL_Roi_HalflifeTick` (roi:233) is a bare SetTimer callback enumerating `trigger_last_use`, and the `KL_Roi_ProcessWord` prune (roi:204-209) enumerates `word_counts` while OnChar can reenter and insert (line 188, can rehash) or the same loop can be reentered. AHK v2 Map enumeration with a concurrent insert-that-grows or Delete is genuinely undefined (skip/dup/throw), and a throw inside a timer/InputHook callback is swallowed — that part of the impact reasoning is correct. The codebase's one real precedent for the fix is `HookDispatcher.Register/Unregister` (Critical around list mutation, line 129-171); the meta-test tests/meta/test_input_serialization.ahk pins Critical-serialization ONLY on the remap/hotstring-engine path (_RemapEmit, _OnPrefixChar, HSE_DispatchMatch), NOT on the keylogger InputHook — so that existing protection does not cover these maps.

WHERE THE FINDING IS WRONG / OVERSTATED:
1) Path A severity is far lower than claimed. The blocking `fh.Write` rest point inside the half-life loop fires ONLY for entries with `age >= HALFLIFE_WARN_DAYS*86400000` (30 days). `trigger_last_use` is stamped with `A_TickCount` and only holds triggers seen THIS session, so reaching that branch requires a SINGLE continuous session running 30+ days. In any realistic session the loop body is pure arithmetic with no blocking call, so the high-probability "interrupted during its fh.Write" window the finding leans on essentially never occurs. Interruption between plain lines is still possible (that is why Critical exists) but is far less likely than at a blocking call.
2) The finding conflates the interrupting thread: it says "an OnChar that inserts a new trigger key" rehashes `trigger_last_use`. OnChar does NOT touch `trigger_last_use`; only `KL_Roi_OnHotstring` does (roi:134), and it is called from `KL_LogHotstring` (keylogger.ahk:795), i.e. a hotstring-fire thread, not a plain keystroke.
3) The cited "app_categories was already flagged/audited for the same hazard" precedent is inaccurate: `KL_AppCat_Save` (keylogger_app_categories.ahk:223-231) is a deferred-timer enumeration with a FileAppend blocking write and ALSO has no Critical — it shares the identical unguarded pattern rather than being a fixed precedent.

NET: the race is genuinely possible (most realistically the `word_counts` prune reentered during fast typing once 500 words accumulate), the swallowed-throw invisibility is real, and the proposed Critical-snapshot fix is the correct, idiomatic remedy. But it is a low-probability, narrow-window defect, not the high-likelihood data-corruption the title implies.

<a id="f-deferred-queue-not-cancelled-on-live-rebuild"></a>
#### `deferred-queue-not-cancelled-on-live-rebuild` — **medium** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : gap-hotstrings-cache-toml-fallback-parse-divergence (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_menu.ahk:2798`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/hotstrings.ahk:997`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/hotstrings.ahk:1025`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:356`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2245`
- **Description** : RegisterEmojisSymbolsDeferred (armed by SetTimer at ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2245, AFTER "ready") drains _HS_DeferredQueue across many self-re-arming _HsDeferredChunkTick ticks (1ms gap), registering the ~3000 emoji/symbol rows in 150-row slices via the cache registrar. RebuildHotstringsLive() (tray_menu.ahk:2798) — the live path for almost EVERY hotstring section toggle, since hotstring_live_toggle.ahk makes everything live-eligible by default — calls HSE_RegistryClear() + HSE_HardReset() then RegisterAllHotstrings() with DeferHeavy=false, which registers the emoji/symbol sections WHOLE and synchronously (_RegisterEmojisSymbolsSections at hotstrings.ahk:684-686). But RebuildHotstringsLive never clears _HS_DeferredQueue and never disarms _HsDeferredChunkTick. The pending timer is still armed, so its next tick continues from the stale Job.Cursor and registers the remaining emoji/symbol slices a SECOND time into the just-rebuilt engine.
- **Cause racine** : HSE_Register (hotstring_engine_main.ahk:356/371) always Pushes a new Spec with no dedup; HSE_RegistryClear resets the registry but the deferred SetTimer chain survives the reset because it is a separate AHK pseudo-thread that RebuildHotstringsLive does not cancel. AHK SetTimer chains are not bound to the registry lifetime; only an explicit SetTimer(_HsDeferredChunkTick, 0) or emptying the queue stops them.
- **Scénario** : User boots, starts typing immediately (the common case the deferral was designed for). Within the ~hundreds-of-ms deferred drain window they toggle, say, Autocorrection > errors off/on from the tray. RebuildHotstringsLive rebuilds the whole engine (including emoji/symbols, whole). The still-armed deferred timer then re-registers every emoji/symbol row from the old Cursor onward, producing duplicate Specs for those triggers in HSE_RegistryByLastChar / HSE_StarSpecs and a doubled prefix-watcher index. Expansions still fire (harmless output) but the registry and star indices are bloated and the live preview can show duplicate candidates; on the hottest tail-char scan path every duplicate Spec is re-evaluated on each terminator keystroke.
- **Impact** : Duplicate hotstring Specs leak into the live engine and the prefix index after any live toggle that races the deferred drain. Wasted per-keystroke scan work on the duplicated star/tail indices, doubled memory for ~thousands of rows, and confusing duplicate live-preview candidates. Worsens with each toggle during the window. No data loss but a real correctness/resource regression on the fast path that is invisible at boot.
- **Correctif** : Make the deferred queue lifetime owned by the registration: in RebuildHotstringsLive() (and at the top of RegisterAllHotstrings when DeferHeavy=false, since that path registers emoji/symbols whole) cancel the deferred pass before re-registering — `global _HS_DeferredQueue` then `_HS_DeferredQueue := []` and `try SetTimer(_HsDeferredChunkTick, 0)`. Symmetrically, have RegisterEmojisSymbolsDeferred() guard against a concurrent live rebuild having already registered the sections (e.g. skip arming if HSE already holds the emoji/symbol groups). Disarming the timer (SetTimer fn,0) is the established idiom in this codebase (tooltip.ahk, prefix watcher).
- **Test** : headless-unit (tests/test_hotstrings_cache.ahk or a new test_hs_deferred_cancel.ahk): seed _HS_CACHE_ROWS with a synthetic emoji-style section and push a Job onto _HS_DeferredQueue with Cursor mid-section; call the cancellation helper that RebuildHotstringsLive will invoke; assert _HS_DeferredQueue.Length == 0 afterwards, then call _HsDeferredChunkTick() once and assert the _HotstringRegistrar recorder records ZERO further registrations (no double-register). Also a meta-static test asserting RebuildHotstringsLive's body contains a `_HS_DeferredQueue` reset so the cancellation can never be silently dropped.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : The finding's STATED mechanism is fabricated and does not exist in the source, but a simpler bug of the same family is REAL.

WRONG IN THE FINDING (verified by reading + grep across the whole windows/ tree):
- There is NO `_HS_DeferredQueue`, NO `_HsDeferredChunkTick`, NO 150-row slicing, NO `Job.Cursor`, NO self-re-arming chunk loop. Grep returns zero hits for every one of those identifiers.
- The real RegisterEmojisSymbolsDeferred() (modules/hotstrings.ahk:953-964) is a SINGLE synchronous call to _RegisterEmojisSymbolsSections(), armed by a ONE-SHOT negative SetTimer(RegisterEmojisSymbolsDeferred, -HS_DEFERRED_REGISTRATION_DELAY_MS) where HS_DEFERRED_REGISTRATION_DELAY_MS := 1500 (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2245, hotstrings.ahk:923). No drain, no cursor, no re-arm.
- The cited line numbers are also wrong: hotstrings.ahk:997 and :1025 fall inside CreateDeadkeyHotstring / ShouldActivateDeadkey, not any deferred-queue code.

REAL BUG (simpler than described, same root family): RebuildHotstringsLive() (ui/tray_menu.ahk:2798) does HSE_RegistryClear() + HSE_HardReset() + RegisterAllHotstrings() (DeferHeavy=false, so _RegisterEmojisSymbolsSections runs WHOLE at hotstrings.ahk:684-686) but never disarms the still-pending one-shot RegisterEmojisSymbolsDeferred timer. _RegisterEmojisSymbolsSections / RegisterEmojisSymbolsDeferred have NO idempotency guard, and HSE_Register (lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:356/371) Pushes Specs with no dedup. hotstring_live_toggle.ahk confirms essentially every section toggle is live-eligible by default and routes through RebuildHotstringsLive. So: if the user toggles a section within the ~1500ms window after 'Driver fully initialised', the live rebuild registers emoji/symbols, then the pending one-shot timer fires at the next rest point and registers them a SECOND time -> duplicate Specs in HSE_RegistryByLastChar / HSE_StarSpecs and a doubled prefix-watcher index.

AHK concurrency does NOT save this. The timer is one-shot and pending; the menu callback runs to completion (no mid-callback interruption needed), then the deferred thread runs afterward. They never interleave, but nothing cancels the pending fire, so the double-registration is deterministic given the window — not a torn-state race at all.

Verdict = uncertain (not 'confirmed') because the finding as written describes a bug that does not exist (chunked stale-cursor drain). The duplication symptom is real but via a different, narrower mechanism: a single re-fire, NOT the cumulative 'worsens with each toggle' / 'continues from stale Job.Cursor' behavior claimed. The impact section ('doubled prefix-watcher index', 'duplicate Specs', 'extra per-keystroke scan') is directionally correct; the magnitude and 'worsens with each toggle during the window' claim is overstated (a single pending one-shot fires once).

<a id="f-winorder-unbounded-and-cross-thread"></a>
#### `winorder-unbounded-and-cross-thread` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : gestures (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:1166`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:1176`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:1182`
- **Description** : _GestureOnForeground (out-of-context WinEvent callback, runs as its own pseudo-thread on every foreground change) rebuilds _GestureWinOrder by allocating NewOrder=[HWnd], appending all others, then assigning _GestureWinOrder := NewOrder. The list is never pruned of dead HWNDs at insert time (only filtered at read time in _GestureOrderedWindows), so it grows by one entry per distinct window ever focused for the life of the process. The callback can also interrupt GestureCycleWindows mid-iteration (which reads _GestureWinOrder via _GestureOrderedWindows); because AHK assigns the new array reference atomically the reader sees either the old or new whole array (no torn array), so this is a slow leak rather than a crash.
- **Cause racine** : WinEvent OUTOFCONTEXT callbacks run as interrupting pseudo-threads. Mutation is via full-array replacement (reference swap is atomic in AHK), so no torn read, but there is no upper bound and no dead-HWND eviction, so memory and the per-cycle prune cost grow without limit on long-running sessions.
- **Scénario** : A machine left running for days opening/closing thousands of transient windows accumulates thousands of stale HWNDs in _GestureWinOrder; each win_next/win_prev gesture then pays an O(n) prune over that ever-growing list, and memory slowly climbs.
- **Impact** : Slow unbounded growth and gradually rising per-gesture cost; no correctness break thanks to read-time filtering. Low severity.
- **Correctif** : Cap _GestureWinOrder length (e.g. keep the most-recent N=64) when prepending in _GestureOnForeground, and/or prune entries whose HWND no longer exists at insert time. Add an A_IsSuspended early-return so the tracker does not churn while paused.
- **Test** : headless-unit: call _GestureOnForeground repeatedly with many distinct fake HWNDs (IdObject=0) and assert _GestureWinOrder.Length stays <= the cap. Also assert that passing IdObject!=0 leaves the list unchanged (existing guard).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-password-cache-torn-write"></a>
#### `password-cache-torn-write` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : keylogger-core (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1413`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1433`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1451`
- **Description** : KL_AsyncPasswordDetect (off-thread one-shot timer) sets KLPasswordCache.last_hwnd, then last_at, then last_val in three separate statements (lines 1415-1417). The keystroke-thread reader KL_IsFocusedFieldPassword tests `KLPasswordCache.last_hwnd = hwnd` then returns `KLPasswordCache.last_val` (lines 1433-1436). The async timer can be interrupted by an incoming keystroke at the rest point AFTER writing last_hwnd but BEFORE writing last_val.
- **Cause racine** : Multi-field cache update without Critical, read on a different pseudo-thread. If interrupted after `last_hwnd := hwnd` but before `last_val := Result`, the keystroke reader sees the new hwnd matched but the STALE last_val from the previous control's verdict. The synchronous reader path (1451-1453) has the same shape but is safe there because writer and reader are the same thread; the async writer is the exposure.
- **Scénario** : User tabs from a password field into a normal field; the async UIA detector for the new field is mid-write when the next keystroke fires. The keystroke sees last_hwnd already = new field but last_val still = true (old field's verdict), suppressing that keystroke — or in reverse ordering a not-yet-confirmed password field briefly reads stale 'false' and a single password char is logged before the verdict lands.
- **Impact** : Rare single-keystroke privacy leak (password char logged) or single-keystroke metric suppression around field transitions. Low frequency but privacy-relevant (PII), hence medium.
- **Correctif** : Write the cache fields under Critical in KL_AsyncPasswordDetect, or publish-after-fill: write last_val first and last_hwnd LAST so a reader's hwnd-match implies last_val already corresponds to that hwnd (hwnd is the commit flag, written last).
- **Test** : headless-unit: refactor the cache commit into KL_CommitPwCache(hwnd, at, val); assert it writes last_hwnd last (or under Critical). Simulate: start at (oldHwnd, old_at, true), commit (newHwnd, t, false), and assert no observable intermediate state has (last_hwnd==newHwnd AND last_val==true).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source. Writer KL_AsyncPasswordDetect (keylogger.ahk:1413) runs on a one-shot SetTimer(-1) thread and writes last_hwnd (1415), last_at (1416), last_val (1417) in three separate plain static-property assignments — no Critical, no setter, no rest point between them. Reader KL_IsFocusedFieldPassword (1422) runs on the keystroke thread: it is reached from MF_ShouldFilter -> KL_AppendLog and from the InputHook callbacks KL_Hook_OnChar/OnKeyDown (keylogger_hook.ahk:210/293), and tests last_hwnd=hwnd (1433) then returns last_val (1436). Two different pseudo-threads share KLPasswordCache with no synchronization. The finding's mechanism is sound under the AHK v2 model, BUT it is heavily gated by the Thread "Interrupt" grace window. No Thread "Interrupt" override exists anywhere in the tree (grep found only Critical usages), so the defaults apply: a freshly launched thread is uninterruptible until it has run 17 ms OR 1000 lines. The writer's line 1414 (KL_DetectPasswordFor) does the UIA COM round-trip the module documents as "5-15 ms". If that finishes under 17 ms (the documented typical case), the writer is STILL in its uninterruptible grace window when it executes the 3-line commit, so a keystroke cannot interrupt between 1415 and 1417 — the torn read cannot occur. The race is only reachable when the UIA call exceeds ~17 ms (cold COM init, contended desktop, slow Electron/WPF UIA trees — plausible but not the common path), expiring the grace window during the COM call; then a keystroke landing exactly between line 1415 and 1417 sees (last_hwnd=new, last_val=stale). So the bug is REAL but narrow and timing-dependent, not the unconditional race the description implies. Harm is also smaller than stated: the privacy-leak direction (logging a password char) is NOT reachable on first focus, because a brand-new unknown control is fail-safe-classified as password and committed synchronously on the keystroke thread (1445-1453) before any async timer writes — so first-touch password fields never leak. The async torn-read can only flip a verdict for an hwnd ALREADY cached as last_val=false, i.e. a re-validation flip from not-password to password on the same hwnd within TTL — rare. The finding's headline scenario (tab from password into a normal field) is actually the benign metric-LOSS direction (one suppressed keystroke), not a leak. Net: a real but rare, at-most-single-character exposure under an uncommon timing+state combination.

<a id="f-klpf-writeatomic-delete-window"></a>
#### `klpf-writeatomic-delete-window` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : keylogger-core (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_prefetch.ahk:147`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:205`
- **Description** : KLPF_WriteAtomic does `FileDelete(path)` then `FileMove(tmp, path)` (lines 153-155) — the non-atomic delete-then-rename pattern KL_WriteAtomic (keylogger.ahk:205) was rewritten to AVOID via MoveFileExW(MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING). The module docstring claims 'Atomic write: ... renamed so a half-flushed file can never reach the page mid-fetch', which the implementation does not guarantee.
- **Cause racine** : Inconsistent atomic-rename discipline between the two writers. FileDelete + FileMove leaves a gap during which `path` does not exist, and the FileMove can fail if an AV/indexer transiently holds tmp or path — the very failure KL_WriteAtomic added a retry + MoveFileExW for.
- **Scénario** : The dashboard page does fetch('./prefetch.json') exactly between the FileDelete and FileMove, getting a 404 / empty read and rendering 'no data'; or an AV scanner holds the file and FileMove returns false, leaving the page on the old blob with no retry.
- **Impact** : Occasional empty/failed prefetch fetch flicker on the dashboard; not a keystroke issue. Low.
- **Correctif** : Make KLPF_WriteAtomic use the same MoveFileExW(MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING | MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH) + single-retry primitive as KL_WriteAtomic (factor it into a shared helper) so the rename is a true atomic directory-entry swap with no absent-file window.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert KLPF_WriteAtomic does not contain a FileDelete(path) followed by FileMove on the destination (grep the delete-then-move antipattern), and/or that it calls the shared MoveFileExW helper.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-synthetic-flag-global-race"></a>
#### `synthetic-flag-global-race` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : llm-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:480-509`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:168-178`
- **Description** : LLM_Bridge_OnAccept sets a process-global synthetic flag (KL_MarkSynthetic) and schedules KL_ClearSynthetic after a fixed 80 ms. The flag is a single boolean shared across all accept/expansion sources, with no nesting/refcount. Two accepts within 80 ms, or a slow TextSend whose synthetic keystrokes arrive after the 80 ms clear, break the pairing.
- **Cause racine** : synth_active is a single static boolean (not a counter or generation token). The clear is an unconditional SetTimer((*) => KL_ClearSynthetic(), -80). If accept A schedules clear-at-T+80 and accept B (or a hotstring expansion that also uses KL_MarkSynthetic) fires at T+30 and schedules its own clear-at-T+110, the first timer at T+80 clears the flag while B's synthetic burst is still being captured -> B's auto-typed chars get logged as manual. Conversely, if a long TextSend burst is still draining at T+80 (slow target app / large prediction), the tail of the burst is captured after the flag was cleared and is mis-attributed to the user.
- **Scénario** : User rapidly accepts two predictions with Tab (or accepts a prediction immediately after a hotstring expansion). Within the 80 ms window the flag is cleared by the earlier timer, so the second auto-typed prediction's characters are recorded as human keystrokes in the keylogger — polluting WPM/n-gram/PII stats again.
- **Impact** : Intermittent keylogger PII/metrics corruption that is hard to reproduce and silent (no error). Severity bounded because it only affects logging accuracy, not typed output.
- **Correctif** : Replace the boolean with a refcount or a generation id: KL_MarkSynthetic increments a counter and returns a token; KL_ClearSynthetic decrements only when the token matches the latest, so overlapping bursts keep the flag set until the LAST one clears. Alternatively clear synchronously after TextSend returns (TextSend with SendMode Input is largely synchronous) rather than on a fixed-delay timer.
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub TextSend, call LLM_Bridge_OnAccept("a") then immediately LLM_Bridge_OnAccept("b"); fire the first deferred KL_ClearSynthetic callback manually; assert Keylogger.synth_active is still true (because the second accept's window has not elapsed). Fails today (single boolean cleared by first timer).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code and the full surrounding system. The finding holds up under the AHK v2 cooperative model.

FACTS FROM SOURCE:
- keylogger.ahk:130-131 — synth_active/synth_type are a single static boolean + string. KL_MarkSynthetic (168-171) sets them unconditionally; KL_ClearSynthetic (175-178) clears them unconditionally. No refcount, no generation token, no nesting. Confirmed exactly as described.
- The flag is SHARED across FOUR producers, not one: llm_bridge.ahk:488/490 (LLM accept, 80 ms clear) AND three hotstring sites (hotstring_engine.ahk:502/530 at 60 ms; hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1162/1187 and 1275/1388 at HSE_SUPPRESS_RELEASE_DELAY_MS = 60). Every site does its OWN independent SetTimer((*) => KL_ClearSynthetic(), -DELAY).
- Each negative-period SetTimer is a run-once timer keyed on a DISTINCT closure instance, so scheduling B's clear does NOT cancel A's. Both timers survive and both fire, each as its own pseudo-thread, each calling the same unconditional clear. That is the mechanism that breaks pairing.
- Consumer: keylogger_hook.ahk:242 and :312 read synth_active per-char as the synthetic burst drains through the InputHook.
- Drain is genuinely asynchronous: ErgoptiPlus.ahk:114 sets SendMode("Event") globally, and TextSend (text_sender.ahk:104) routes the LLM payload (<1000 chars) through SendText, i.e. Event mode, which posts characters one at a time and returns before the hook has seen them all. This is precisely why the clear is deferred 80 ms rather than inline — and precisely why an EARLY clear by a foreign timer mis-attributes the tail.

WHY IT IS REAL (not a false positive):
- No serialization saves it. LLM_Bridge_OnAccept runs no Critical. The HSE atomic send holds Critical only around SendInput and releases it (finally block) BEFORE scheduling its clear timer, so the clear timers are free-running and can interleave.
- Cross-source overlap is the strong, reachable case: a hotstring expansion (schedules clear at T+60) immediately followed by a Tab-accept of an already-visible prediction (schedules clear at T+110). The T+60 timer fires and clears the flag while the LLM Event-mode burst is still draining through OnChar, so the prediction tail is stamped s=absent → logged as manual and folded into chars/WPM/n-gram/PII stats by keylogger_walker.ahk. The symmetric case (LLM clear firing into a hotstring burst) is equally possible during fast typing. The two-LLM-accepts case is rarer because LLM_Tooltip_Hide(true) hides the tooltip on accept (TryAcceptTab returns false when not visible), so a second accept needs a fresh prediction within 80 ms — but the cross-subsystem case does not need that.
- It is exactly the AHK shared-mutable-state-without-guard hazard from the project invariants: one global flag mutated by multiple independent timer threads with no refcount.

SEVERITY: the description's own impact line is right — this only corrupts keylogger statistics (a handful of synthetic chars counted as human), never the typed output and never a dropped user keystroke. It is intermittent and silent. That is real but bounded; medium is a touch generous, low is more honest.

<a id="f-wpm-ring-buffer-cross-thread-race"></a>
#### `wpm-ring-buffer-cross-thread-race` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tooltip-widget (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/wpm_widget.ahk:213`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/wpm_widget.ahk:222`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/wpm_widget.ahk:256`
- **Description** : WPMWidget_Push runs on the keyboard OnChar thread (keylogger_hook.ahk:252) and on the hotstring-engine thread; it Push()es into or overwrites WPMWidget._ring and advances _ring_head. WPMWidget_Calc, running in the 500 ms WPMWidget_Tick timer, enumerates 'for _, ev in WPMWidget._ring' with no Critical on either side.
- **Cause racine** : Cooperative interruption: the keyboard thread can interrupt the tick timer between for-loop iterations and grow the array (Array.Push) or overwrite a slot (_ring[head+1] := entry). The enumerator can then read a newly-written or partially-updated entry, or observe a length change mid-walk.
- **Scénario** : User types rapidly while the widget is visible; a keystroke lands exactly while WPMWidget_Calc is iterating the ring. The computed WPM reflects a slightly inconsistent slice of the buffer.
- **Impact** : WPM is an approximate, self-correcting metric, so the practical effect is a momentarily slightly-off number — no crash (AHK array index assignment to an existing slot and Push are individually safe; the enumerator does not crash on concurrent length growth in practice). Cosmetic; included for completeness since it is a real shared-state race the brief asked to hunt.
- **Correctif** : If exactness ever matters, wrap the Push tail (the few lines that mutate _ring/_ring_head) and the Calc enumeration in 'Critical "On"/Critical "Off"'. The regions contain no Send/Sleep/blocking call so Critical will not starve the hook. Lower-cost alternative: have Calc snapshot the array length once and iterate by index up to that length.
- **Test** : headless-unit: not easily race-reproducible in the cooperative model; encode as an invariant test that WPMWidget_Calc tolerates a buffer whose length changes by constructing a ring at exactly RING_CAP and asserting Calc returns a finite Map without throwing for both the growing and overwriting branches.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-dispatcher-map-never-cleared-stale-misfire"></a>
#### `dispatcher-map-never-cleared-stale-misfire` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : tray-menu (module) · **Fichiers** : `lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:88`, `lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:93`, `lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:157`, `lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:280`, `ui/tray_menu.ahk:2815`
- **Description** : `_MenuDispatchCallbacks` and `_MenuDispatchLastFire` are process-global Maps keyed by Win32 menu-item ID. They are populated by RegisterMenuItem at build time but are NEVER cleared. RebuildTrayMenu() (ui/tray_menu.ahk:2815) does `A_TrayMenu.Delete()` + fresh `Menu()` objects + InitSubMenus() + initMenu(), yet leaves both Maps intact. AHK recycles freed menu command IDs across rebuilds, so the same ItemId is reused for a structurally DIFFERENT item on the next build (counts/labels/visible sections change with master gates, personal-file edits, the deferred language submenu, etc.).
- **Cause racine** : AHK assigns menu command IDs from an internal pool and reuses freed IDs after Menu.Delete(). The bypass keys callbacks by that reused ID. On a live rebuild, _DispatchIfMissed's double-fire guard compares ExpectedLastFire (snapshotted at click time, often 0 for a first click) against _MenuDispatchLastFire[ItemId], which RegisterMenuItem resets to 0 for the reused ID during the rebuild. When both are 0 the guard `CurrentLastFire != ExpectedLastFire` is FALSE, so _DispatchIfMissed proceeds and calls `_MenuDispatchCallbacks[ItemId]` — which after the rebuild points at a DIFFERENT item's callback. The leak (entries for IDs the shrunken new menu never re-registers) is the secondary effect; the wrong-callback fire is the dangerous one.
- **Scénario** : User clicks a live-eligible hotstring section toggle. AHK's native dispatch drops it (the ~30-50% drop this whole module exists to fix). WM_COMMAND schedules _DispatchIfMissed(ItemId_X, 0) for +60 ms. Meanwhile the click DID partially run (or another rebuild path runs) RebuildHotstringsLive()->RebuildTrayMenu(), which deletes the tray and rebuilds with a now-different item count; ItemId_X is reused for an unrelated entry Y and its LastFire reset to 0. The +60 ms timer fires, sees 0==0, and calls Y's callback — e.g. it flips a DIFFERENT feature, opens a picker, or triggers a Reload the user never asked for. Symptom: 'I clicked one toggle and a different setting changed / the script reloaded.'
- **Impact** : Silent wrong-action dispatch on the exact reliability mechanism that was added to stop dropped clicks. Hard to reproduce, easy to misattribute to AHK. Also an unbounded slow memory growth of both Maps over a long session of toggling (every rebuild that shrinks the menu leaks the dropped IDs).
- **Correctif** : Clear both Maps at the very start of RebuildTrayMenu() (and BuildTrayMenuDeferred()) BEFORE A_TrayMenu.Delete()/InitSubMenus()/initMenu() repopulate them: `global _MenuDispatchCallbacks, _MenuDispatchLastFire` then `_MenuDispatchCallbacks := Map()` / `_MenuDispatchLastFire := Map()`. Expose a `MenuDispatcher_Reset()` in menu_dispatcher.ahk and call it from the rebuild entry point so the ownership stays in the dispatcher module. This guarantees no stale ID survives a rebuild, so a reused ID always maps to the current item or is absent (and _DispatchIfMissed's `Has(ItemId)` guard then no-ops correctly).
- **Test** : headless-unit (include lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk in a dedicated runner — _MenuDispatcherInit's OnMessage does not keep the script alive): simulate the lifecycle by manually populating _MenuDispatchCallbacks[42] := () => Fired1++, call the new MenuDispatcher_Reset(), assert _MenuDispatchCallbacks.Count == 0 and _MenuDispatchLastFire.Count == 0. Plus a meta-static test asserting RebuildTrayMenu()'s body in ui/tray_menu.ahk contains a MenuDispatcher_Reset() (or the two Map() resets) so a future refactor cannot drop the clear.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code and empirically tested the load-bearing premise on the installed AHK v2 (v2.0.19).

STRUCTURAL CLAIM — TRUE. `_MenuDispatchCallbacks` and `_MenuDispatchLastFire` (menu_dispatcher.ahk:88,93) are initialized once at module load and cleared NOWHERE (grep confirms only the two `:= Map()` initializers exist). `RebuildTrayMenu()` (ui/tray_menu.ahk:2815-2821) does `A_TrayMenu.Delete()` + `SubMenus := Map()` + `InitSubMenus()` + `initMenu()` and never touches the dispatch Maps. The same is true of the real chokepoint `initMenu()` (ui/tray_menu.ahk:2421, `A_TrayMenu.Delete()` at 2431) and `BuildTrayMenuDeferred()` (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2031-2050), plus the updater's `SetTimer((*) => initMenu(), -50)` (lib/updater.ahk:207, etc.). So both Maps grow unbounded across a session — the memory-leak secondary effect is REAL and confirmed.

LOAD-BEARING PREMISE — EMPIRICALLY FALSE in the normal case. The whole "wrong callback fires" danger rests on "AHK recycles freed menu command IDs across rebuilds, so ItemId_X is reused for a structurally different item Y." I tested this directly with AutoHotkey64.exe v2: (a) `A_TrayMenu.Delete()`+rebuild 50 times -> 100 DISTINCT ids, zero collisions; (b) 100 fresh GC'd `Menu()` objects (2 items each) -> 200 distinct ids (11003..11202), zero collisions; (c) explicit `Menu.Delete()` + drop reference + new menu -> new ids 11006/11007, did NOT reuse the just-freed 11003/11004/11005. AHK 2.0's menu-command-id allocator is a monotonically increasing counter that does NOT recycle freed ids on `Menu.Delete()`. Therefore, on an ordinary rebuild, a freed ItemId is never reassigned to a different item, `_MenuDispatchCallbacks[ItemId]` after a rebuild still maps the SAME id to the SAME (re-registered) callback, and the `_DispatchIfMissed` `0==0` argument is moot because the reused-for-a-different-item id simply never exists. The finding's headline mechanism and its severity-justifying scenario do not occur on a normal toggle->rebuild.

WHY NOT A CLEAN FALSE-POSITIVE — the 16-bit counter DOES wrap. WM_COMMAND ids are the LOWORD (`wParam & 0xFFFF`), max ~65535. I observed the counter wrap: after ~54,000 ids consumed (build 453 of a 120-items-per-build loop) the highest id dropped 65242 -> 11065, i.e. it reset to the ~11003 base. ONLY after such a wrap can a freshly-allocated id collide with a still-present stale entry from ~450 builds earlier (the Maps retain every historical id because they are never cleared). Post-wrap, re-registration sets `_MenuDispatchLastFire[reusedId] := 0` (line 158), which exactly satisfies the `CurrentLastFire(0) == ExpectedLastFire(0)` guard the finding describes — so once wrap territory is reached, the wrong-callback misfire becomes genuinely possible. Reaching wrap needs roughly 180-360 heavy rebuilds (each real build registers ~150-300 items across 127 RegisterMenuItem call sites, many in loops) in ONE uninterrupted process session. That is high but not impossible for a long-lived driver whose live toggles, log-level changes and updater-interval changes each call a full rebuild.

CONCURRENCY — the interleave is plausible under AHK's cooperative model (no true race needed): rebuilds are genuinely scheduled on independent timer threads (updater `SetTimer initMenu -50`), so an independent rebuild can fire inside the +60ms `_DispatchIfMissed` window between a dropped click and its retry. `_DispatchIfMissed` takes `Critical` only at its OWN top (line 282), which does not protect against a rebuild that already ran earlier in the window.

NET: the bug is NOT the high-severity, normal-operation wrong-action dispatch the finding claims (that path is refuted by the monotonic, non-recycling id allocator). It IS a real, lower-severity latent defect: (1) a definite unbounded growth of two process-global Maps over a long session, and (2) a remote wrong-callback misfire that only manifests after the 16-bit id counter wraps (~tens-of-thousands of ids / hundreds of heavy rebuilds). I land on `uncertain` rather than `confirmed` because the dangerous behavior is real only past an extreme rebuild count, and rather than `false-positive` because the leak is concrete and the misfire is genuinely reachable (not impossible) given the never-cleared Maps + 16-bit wrap.

<a id="f-remap-emit-critical-uneven"></a>
#### `remap-emit-critical-uneven` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : e2e-keystroke-pipeline (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:376-380`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:519-538`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:575-580`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:599-608`
- **Description** : _RemapEmit() does `Critical("On")` then SendEvent to make each remapped letter's emission atomic vs. the next key's remap thread (per the detailed comment). But the parallel layout hotkeys that emit characters do NOT enter Critical: the direct-access digit-row handlers (`SC002:: SendEvent('{1 Down}')` etc., lines 519-538), `_DigitShiftSend` (575-580), and the dead-key bindings (599-608) all SendEvent/SendNewResult without Critical.
- **Cause racine** : SendMode is global 'Event' (interruptible). Critical only protects the handler that sets it; a non-Critical sibling handler firing for the next key CAN start its SendEvent while the OS input queue still holds events, re-introducing the exact interleave/out-of-order emission (`comme`->`cmooe`) that _RemapEmit's Critical was added to prevent — only now the unprotected key is the boundary. The two emitters use different atomicity guarantees for the same key stream.
- **Scénario** : Fast alternation between a remapped letter and a digit (e.g. typing an alphanumeric token like `a1b2`): the letter goes through Critical _RemapEmit, the digit through the non-Critical SC002 handler; their SendEvent injections can overlap in the single OS queue and emit out of order, mangling the token.
- **Impact** : Rare reordered/garbled output when remapped letters and digit-row/dead keys are interleaved at high speed; same class of bug the layout comment claims to have fixed for letters only.
- **Correctif** : Route the digit-row and _DigitShiftSend emissions through the same serialized emit primitive (wrap their SendEvent in Critical("On") like _RemapEmit, since there is no Sleep on those paths), or convert them to a shared `_LayoutEmit` helper so every layout-layer emission shares the one atomicity contract. Leave the dead-key path as-is only if it is made non-blocking (see deadkey finding).
- **Test** : headless-unit: with _SendHook installed as a recorder, drive a sequence that alternates a RemapKey-registered char and a digit handler and assert the recorded send order matches the typed order. meta-static: assert every char-emitting layout hotkey body in layout.ahk Section 3 either calls the shared emit helper or contains `Critical` so a new non-serialized emitter fails the test.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : The cited code is accurate: in modules/layout.ahk only `_RemapEmit` (376-380) enters `Critical("On")` before its SendEvent; the digit-row handlers (519-538), `_DigitShiftSend` (575-580) and the dead-key bindings (599-608, which call SendNewResult / DeadKey, both bare SendEvent) do NOT. Globals: A_MaxThreads=64, SendMode("Event"). The maintainer's own commit 127caa8e4 added the Critical to fix the observed `comme -> cmooe` letter-vs-letter reorder. So the structural premise of the finding is verified.

But the asserted IMPACT is largely refuted by the ASYMMETRY of Critical, reasoned from the AHK cooperative model:

1. letter->digit ("a1"): `a` runs `_RemapEmit`, sets Critical("On") and SendEvents `{Blind}a` to completion. Critical blocks ALL new threads, so the SC002 digit handler CANNOT start until `a` has fully drained. Ordering a-then-1 is already protected. No hole.

2. digit->letter ("1a"): the SC002 handler is `SendEvent("{1 Down}") UpdateLastSentCharacter("1")`. `{1 Down}` is a SINGLE key event. Event-mode SendEvent only yields to a waiting thread BETWEEN discrete events; with one event the realistic interruption point is AFTER `{1 Down}` is already posted to the OS queue (the rest point between the two statements). When `a`'s thread interrupts there, `1 Down` is already ahead of `a` in the queue -> correct order `1a`. The letter->letter reorder the comment fixed required a MULTI-event SendEvent (char Down + char Up = 2 events, with a yield between them); the single-Down digit send lacks that internal window. So the finding's headline scenario (`a1b2` "mangling") is mostly a false alarm.

3. The down/up split (SC002:: vs SC002 Up::) does not reorder either: the digit is produced by the OS on key-down, which fires first; the deferred non-Critical Up is harmless.

The genuinely valid kernel is narrower and lower-severity: (a) the codebase has an INCONSISTENT atomicity contract — sibling emitters on the same key stream don't share the one serialization primitive, which is a real maintainability/robustness defect; and (b) the dead-key path is the one place a MULTI-codepoint emission (e.g. a multi-char DeadkeyMapping value via SendNewResult) could present an internal Event-mode yield of the same class the Critical fix addressed — but the dead-key path is already gated behind a blocking InputHook ih.Wait() (the user is mid-sequence), making concurrent fast typing into it improbable. I cannot statically prove a reproducible digit-row reorder, and cannot disprove a rare multi-codepoint dead-key splice; hence uncertain rather than confirmed or false-positive.

<a id="f-textsend-restore-timer-race"></a>
#### `textsend-restore-timer-race` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-send-clipboard (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:96-100`
- **Description** : TextSend's clipboard branch arms a SetTimer(() => CB_Restore(SavedForTimer), -150) to restore the clipboard 150 ms after the paste. If a second clipboard-mode TextSend (or a SendInstant) fires within that 150 ms window, the first timer can fire AFTER the second write, restoring the OLD saved value over the content the second operation just placed — and/or the second operation's restore races the first. There is no generation counter or cancellation of the prior restore timer.
- **Cause racine** : Two independent deferred restores mutate the same shared resource (the OS clipboard) with overlapping windows and no serialization/generation guard. AHK timers are cooperatively scheduled but the ordering of overlapping negative timers vs. intervening writes is not coordinated here.
- **Scénario** : User accepts two long predictions in quick succession (<150 ms apart), or accepts a long prediction then immediately triggers a selection-wrap. The first restore timer fires after the second write, leaving the clipboard holding the first operation's saved snapshot instead of the user's real content — or pasting the wrong thing if a manual paste lands in the gap.
- **Impact** : Intermittent wrong clipboard contents after rapid clipboard-mode injections. Low frequency (needs >1000-char payloads or rapid wraps) but the failure is silent and confusing.
- **Correctif** : Serialize clipboard injection: bump a module generation counter on each clipboard-mode TextSend, capture it in the restore closure, and have the restore no-op if the generation has advanced (another injection took over). Or cancel the previous pending restore timer before arming a new one. Sharing one clipboard-injection helper between TextSend and SendInstant would also remove the duplicated, uncoordinated logic.
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub CB_Save/CB_Write/CB_Restore and the timer seam; fire two clipboard-mode TextSend calls back-to-back with the same fake clock, run pending timers, and assert the final restored value matches the EARLIER saved snapshot exactly once (no double/overwritten restore). Asserts the generation-guard behaviour.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Read the cited code (adapters/text_sender.ahk:90-100), the Clipboard port (adapters/clipboard.ahk), and all callers. The mechanism is REAL under the AHK cooperative model. TextSend's clipboard branch does Saved:=CB_Save(); CB_Write(Text); ClipWait(1); SendInput("^v"); then arms SetTimer(()=>CB_Restore(SavedForTimer), -150) and RETURNS. AHK negative-interval timers are one-shot but each SetTimer call with a fresh closure is a DISTINCT timer; they do not coalesce, and nothing cancels a prior pending restore. Two clipboard-mode TextSend calls <150ms apart (each on its own pseudo-thread) produce a torn sequence: Call A saves the user's real clipboard (Saved_A) and arms timer A; Call B, firing before timer A, calls CB_Save() while the clipboard still holds text_A, so Saved_B==text_A (NOT the user's content), writes text_B, arms timer B(restore text_A); timer A fires restoring Saved_A over text_B, then timer B fires restoring text_A. Final clipboard = text_A — neither the user's snapshot nor text_B. Confirmed clobber. The CB_* adapter is a plain synchronous A_Clipboard wrapper, so no internal serialization saves it.

Three findings refine the severity DOWNWARD to low:
(1) Both production callers (modules/llm/prediction_engine.ahk:1125 and modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:489) pass Opts=0 -> "auto", so the clipboard branch executes ONLY when StrLen(text) > TEXT_CLIPBOARD_THRESHOLD (1000). LLM inline/accept predictions are short completions; a single >1000-char prediction is rare-to-nonexistent. The finding's own impact note concedes this ("needs >1000-char payloads").
(2) LLM_Engine_OnResults auto-types only on is_final and returns immediately (one burst per request); LLM_Bridge_OnAccept fires on explicit user Tab. Two >1000-char clipboard sends within 150ms is an extreme edge.
(3) The finding's "or a SendInstant" overlap claim is a FALSE POSITIVE: SendInstant (lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:285-293) restores the clipboard SYNCHRONOUSLY inside try/finally within the same pseudo-thread (OldClipboard:=ClipboardAll(); ... A_Clipboard:=OldClipboard) — no deferred timer, so it cannot leave a pending restore to collide. GetSelection (same file, 308-318) is likewise synchronous. ONLY TextSend defers via a timer. So the duplicated/uncoordinated-logic part of the proposed fix is half-right: there is duplication, but only TextSend introduces the race; SendInstant is already correct.

Net: real bug, narrow trigger, one bogus collision partner. Confirmed but low.

<a id="f-hse-register-torn-read-vs-onchar"></a>
#### `hse-register-torn-read-vs-onchar` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : gap-hse-registry-mutation-vs-onchar-reader (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:253`, `lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:356`, `lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:359`, `lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:363`, `lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:821`, `modules/hotstrings.ahk:997`, `modules/hotstrings.ahk:1012`
- **Description** : HSE_Register inserts each new Spec into FOUR parallel global indices in sequence, with no Critical: HSE_RegistryByLastChar[LookupKey].Push(Spec) (line 356), then for star specs HSE_StarSpecs.Push (line 360), _HSE_IndexStarPrefixes (line 361, mutates HSE_StarPrefixSetCI/CS), and _HSE_IndexStarTrigger (line 362, mutates HSE_StarByTriggerCI/CS + HSE_MaxStarTriggerLen). The boot deferred-chunk driver _HsDeferredChunkTick (modules/hotstrings.ahk:997) calls LoadHotstringsSection -> _HsCacheRegisterSection -> CreateHotstring -> HSE_Register ~150 times per chunk, registering ~3000 emoji/symbol star triggers AFTER the InputHook + keylogger hooks are live. The chunk tick runs as a default (interruptible) SetTimer thread and is NOT Critical.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 is cooperatively threaded: a default SetTimer callback can be interrupted at any inter-line rest point (and during the file I/O in HotstringsCacheEnsure) by a newly-arriving InputHook OnChar message, because A_MaxThreads is 64 and nothing pins the timer Critical. _OnPrefixChar's Critical("On") (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:723) makes the READER uninterruptible but does nothing for the WRITER: when the chunk tick is the running thread, an OnChar can preempt it BETWEEN the bucket Push (line 356) and the star-index updates (lines 360-362). HSE_FindMatchAtEnd's STAR path (lines 856-879) keys off HSE_StarByTriggerCI/CS — updated LAST — so a star trigger already in HSE_StarSpecs/the bucket but not yet in the by-trigger index is invisible to the star match; conversely its end-char-suppression sibling _HSE_StarTriggerCoversBody reads HSE_StarPrefixSetCI/CS which may be partially populated, so suppression decisions key off a half-built prefix set. The four indices are momentarily inconsistent.
