- **Scénario** : During the first ~couple of seconds after the tray icon appears (the deferred emoji/symbol pass, boot+1500ms onward) the user is already typing. A physical magic-key press fires OnChar exactly while _HsDeferredChunkTick is mid-HSE_Register: the star trigger the user just completed is in the last-char bucket but not yet in HSE_StarByTriggerCI, so HSE_FindMatchAtEnd returns no match and the emoji/symbol expansion silently no-fires (the user sees the raw trigger text), or an end-char trigger is wrongly suppressed/allowed because the star prefix set is half-built. It is intermittent and unreproducible from the user's side — exactly the class the project treats as critical.
- **Impact** : Intermittent missed or mis-resolved hotstring expansions for the first seconds of every session, on the single hottest function. Silent (no log, no crash). Affects the heaviest/most-numerous category (emoji/symbol, ~3000 triggers) precisely when the warm-up pass is running.
- **Correctif** : Make each HSE_Register call atomic with respect to the OnChar reader by wrapping the index-mutation block (the body from HSE_SeqCounter++ through the group-index Push, i.e. lines 259-371, or at minimum the live-index block lines 350-363) in Critical("On")/Critical(restore). There is no Sleep or blocking call in that block, so holding Critical is safe and cannot starve the hook. Save/restore the prior Critical state (PrevCrit := Critical("On") ... Critical(PrevCrit)) so it nests cleanly if a future caller is already Critical. This guarantees an OnChar either sees the Spec fully indexed in all four maps or not at all. (Alternative, lighter: reorder so the by-trigger/prefix indices are updated BEFORE the last-char bucket push, AND publish via a single atomic swap — but Critical is simpler and matches the reader-side pattern already in the codebase.)
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/test_hotstring_engine_main.ahk: register a star trigger via HSE_Register, then assert the post-condition that the Spec is present in ALL of HSE_RegistryByLastChar (under TailChar), HSE_StarSpecs, HSE_StarByTriggerCI/CS, and HSE_StarPrefixSetCI/CS — i.e. the indices never diverge after a single register (codifies the invariant the torn read violates). PLUS a meta-static test in tests/meta/ (mirroring test_input_serialization.ahk) asserting HSE_Register's source contains Critical("On") guarding the bucket+star-index mutations, so a future edit that drops it fails loudly. The interleave itself cannot be reproduced purely headlessly (AHK cannot preempt a tight synchronous loop without a real message-pump point), so the meta-static guard is the durable regression.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code. The MECHANISM the finding describes is real, but its IMPACT is grossly overstated, so I land on "uncertain" leaning false-positive-on-severity.

WHAT IS TRUE (verified against source):
- The writer is genuinely interruptible. HSE_Register (hotstring_engine_main.ahk:253) runs from _HsDeferredChunkTick (modules/hotstrings.ahk:997), which is a default SetTimer thread re-armed via SetTimer(_HsDeferredChunkTick, -1) (line 1025). There is NO Critical anywhere in the chunk path. Confirmed.
- The reader is an interrupting thread. _OnPrefixChar is bound as the InputHook OnChar callback (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:630, via _OnPrefixCharProfiled). OnChar callbacks ARE interrupting pseudo-threads in AHK v2.
- The reader's Critical does NOT protect the writer. Critical("On") is acquired at line 723, i.e. AFTER the A_IsSuspended/suppress guards and AFTER the UIA-wrap branch — it serializes the reader against the NEXT key, it cannot retroactively make the writer atomic. The finding is correct that "reader Critical does nothing for the writer."
- The index ordering matches the claim. In HSE_Register the live block is: bucket Push (356), then for star specs HSE_StarSpecs.Push (360), _HSE_IndexStarPrefixes (361), _HSE_IndexStarTrigger (362). HSE_FindMatchAtEnd's star path (852-880) keys off HSE_StarByTriggerCI/CS, populated LAST by _HSE_IndexStarTrigger (1023-1041). So between line 356 and line 362 a Spec is in the bucket + HSE_StarSpecs but invisible to the star match. A genuine inter-line torn read.

WHY THE IMPACT IS NEGLIGIBLE (the refutation):
1. The torn window is ~3 inter-line gaps (lines 360-362) of pure in-memory work — no Sleep, no blocking call. The finding's claim that "the file I/O in HotstringsCacheEnsure" widens the window is wrong: HotstringsCacheEnsure (hotstrings_cache.ahk:309) is gated by `if _HS_CACHE_LOADED return`, so its FileRead runs exactly ONCE (first chunk), never per-chunk.
2. For the user to lose an expansion, the OnChar must fire AND its just-completed trigger must be the ONE specific Spec being registered in its own 3-line window — one trigger out of ~3000, in a 3-instruction window, inside a ~20ms chunk. The coincidence is effectively unobservable; the "intermittent every session" framing in the impact section is not supported.
3. Self-healing, no persistent corruption. The instant _HSE_IndexStarTrigger returns, all four indices are consistent. HSE_StarByTriggerCI/CS is append-only (lines 1033/1039), so a concurrent reader can never see a SHRUNK or corrupted prior entry — at worst it misses the brand-new Spec for one keystroke; the very next press works. There is no torn read of OTHER triggers (the user is far more likely typing a normal already-registered word, which is fully indexed and unaffected).
4. The _HSE_StarTriggerCoversBody / prefix-set angle is even weaker: a "half-built prefix set" for the Spec currently registering only mis-suppresses an end-char match for that same one trigger in the same 3-line window — same negligible coincidence, and it self-heals identically.

So: the bug is mechanically real (not a hard false positive — the path truly is unserialized and the reader's Critical genuinely doesn't cover it), but the severity "high" with "every session" impact is unjustified. This is a benign, self-healing, vanishingly-rare torn read worth fixing only as cheap defense-in-depth.

<a id="f-rebuild-hotstrings-live-clear-rebuild-window"></a>
#### `rebuild-hotstrings-live-clear-rebuild-window` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : gap-hse-registry-mutation-vs-onchar-reader (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `ui/tray_menu.ahk:2798`, `ui/tray_menu.ahk:2800`, `ui/tray_menu.ahk:2802`, `lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:379`
- **Description** : RebuildHotstringsLive (tray_menu.ahk:2798), invoked from the menu-callback path (_HS_ApplyLiveToggle tray_menu.ahk:438 and ToggleMenuVariableByPath ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1538), runs synchronously with NO Critical: HSE_RegistryClear() (resets every registry Map to empty, engine_main:379), HSE_HardReset(), then RegisterAllHotstrings() which re-registers ~5400 specs over hundreds of ms. The first invocation also calls HotstringsCacheEnsure (a file read of the .tsv). Throughout this clear+rebuild window the OnChar reader can run and see a CLEARED or partially-rebuilt registry.
- **Cause racine** : The menu callback is an interruptible thread (no Critical) and RegisterAllHotstrings contains many rest points: file I/O (HotstringsCacheEnsure / TOML reads), HotstringsResolve, BootProfile_Mark, and thousands of inter-line points. At any of these an OnChar message can preempt the rebuild. Between HSE_RegistryClear() (line 2800) and the completion of RegisterAllHotstrings() (line 2802) the four HSE indices are empty or partial, so HSE_FindMatchAtEnd matches nothing (or matches against a subset). The tray-menu unit flagged the unbalanced-Critical dispatch bypass but NOT this clear+rebuild visibility gap.
- **Scénario** : User toggles a hotstring section on/off from the tray while continuing to type (common: flip a section, keep working). For the few-hundred-ms rebuild, any expansion the user triggers silently fails to fire because HSE_FindMatchAtEnd reads the half-rebuilt registry. On the FIRST toggle of the session the window is longer (HotstringsCacheEnsure reads the .tsv). Intermittent, user sees raw trigger text instead of the expansion.
- **Impact** : Transient total/partial loss of all hotstring expansions during each live toggle, on the hot path. Bounded in frequency (live toggles are user-initiated and rare) but the failure is silent. Also the menu thread holds no Critical while doing file I/O, so a slow disk lengthens the dead window.
- **Correctif** : Serialize the swap against the reader. Two options: (a) wrap the HSE_RegistryClear()+HSE_HardReset()+RegisterAllHotstrings() trio in Critical("On")/restore — but RegisterAllHotstrings does file I/O on the first call, and Critical must not span blocking I/O, so first call HotstringsCacheEnsure() OUTSIDE Critical, then take Critical only around the clear+register. Better: (b) build the new registry into FRESH local Maps and publish them with a single atomic pointer swap under a brief Critical, so the reader never sees an empty registry — the old registry stays live and matchable until the instant the new one is ready. Option (b) eliminates the dead window entirely. At minimum, gate HSE_FindMatchAtEnd via a module-level `HSE_RebuildInProgress` generation flag set true across the rebuild so the reader can fall back gracefully instead of silently no-matching.
- **Test** : headless-unit: register a known star + end-char trigger, capture HSE_Size(); then simulate the swap by calling HSE_RegistryClear() and asserting that a documented atomicity helper (the new build-then-swap function, or a HSE_RebuildInProgress flag) is what production uses — i.e. assert HSE_FindMatchAtEnd returns the OLD match until the swap completes, never "" mid-rebuild. PLUS a meta-static test asserting RebuildHotstringsLive does NOT call HSE_RegistryClear() directly on the live registry without the atomic-swap/Critical guard (source-introspection on tray_menu.ahk). The live interleave needs live hardware to observe directly; the unit + meta tests pin the invariant.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read the real code and the finding holds up against the AHK v2 cooperative-threading model.

WRITER (the bug surface): RebuildHotstringsLive (ui/tray_menu.ahk:2798-2808) runs HSE_RegistryClear() -> HSE_HardReset() -> RegisterAllHotstrings() with NO Critical anywhere in the function, nor in either caller (_HS_ApplyLiveToggle tray_menu.ahk:438; ToggleMenuVariableByPath). I grepped tray_menu.ahk for Critical/Thread on this path and found none. HSE_RegistryClear (hotstring_engine_main.ahk:379-393) replaces all eight live indices (HSE_RegistryByLastChar, HSE_StarSpecs, HSE_StarPrefixSetCI/CS, HSE_StarByTriggerCI/CS, HSE_MaxStarTriggerLen=0, HSE_RegistryByGroup) with empty Maps/arrays in one go, then RegisterAllHotstrings (modules/hotstrings.ahk:29+) repopulates them over a very long synchronous function: thousands of inter-line rest points, HotstringsResolve TOML lookups, and on the first session toggle a .tsv file read via the cache layer. The menu callback is a normal interruptible pseudo-thread at priority 0.

READER: _OnPrefixChar (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:654) -> HSE_FeedChar (engine_main:575) -> HSE_FindMatchAtEnd (engine_main:821) reads HSE_RegistryByLastChar / HSE_StarByTriggerCS etc. directly from globals (engine_main:822, 856...). I confirmed an empty registry yields a clean "" no-match, not a crash. This OnChar fires from a dedicated InputHook (priority 0).

CONCURRENCY VERDICT: This is exactly the asymmetry that defeats the usual "AHK is serialized" refutation. The reader's Critical("On") (watcher:723) is set INSIDE _OnPrefixChar AFTER it begins, and only protects the reader from being interrupted during its own match->fire->sync. It does nothing to stop OnChar from being LAUNCHED while the menu thread sits at one of its many rest points mid-rebuild. With equal priority (0/0) a new InputHook thread can interrupt the menu thread, and the menu thread holds no Critical, so the clear+rebuild window is genuinely visible to the reader. During it, HSE_FindMatchAtEnd reads empty/partial indices and silently no-fires. So the path is NOT already gated/serialized/Critical-protected on the writer side — the finding's core mechanism is correct, not a false positive.

Two refinements to the finding's narrative: (1) errors thrown inside the menu callback are not the issue here — there is no throw; it is a pure visibility race. (2) The impact is self-healing: HSE_Buffer is a separate global fed from the watcher buffer, and after RegisterAllHotstrings completes the next keystroke matches normally, so the dead window is bounded to one rebuild, not persistent.

SEVERITY: I nudge to low. The mechanism is real but the real-world trigger probability is heavily gated: the user has just moved to and clicked a tray menu item, i.e. is interacting with the menu rather than typing a trigger into the target app at that instant. A trigger only no-fires if a terminator/star char is delivered in the few-hundred-ms rebuild window. It is possible (menu dismissed and typing resumed immediately, key auto-repeat, or a queued char), but it is not the routine "I flipped a section and kept working" certainty the description implies, and the failure self-corrects on the next keystroke. Silent + on the hot path keeps it above info, but bounded + rare + self-healing pulls it below medium.

<a id="f-disable-enable-group-unguarded-but-dead-path"></a>
#### `disable-enable-group-unguarded-but-dead-path` — **info** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : gap-hse-registry-mutation-vs-onchar-reader (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:450`, `lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:484`, `lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:493`
- **Description** : HSE_DisableGroup (engine_main:450) splices out a group's specs, then rebuilds HSE_StarPrefixSetCI/CS from scratch (HSE_StarPrefixSetCI := Map() at line 484, then re-index all remaining star specs) and rebuilds the by-trigger index (_HSE_RebuildStarTriggerIndex). For the duration of that rebuild the star prefix set and by-trigger index are empty/partial — a far WIDER torn-read window than a single HSE_Register. Neither HSE_DisableGroup nor HSE_EnableGroup takes Critical.
- **Cause racine** : Same cooperative-threading mechanism: an OnChar could preempt the rebuild and read empty star indices. However, grep shows HSE_DisableGroup/HSE_EnableGroup are referenced ONLY in hotstring_engine_main.ahk and the test files — the production live-toggle path uses RebuildHotstringsLive's clear+rebuild instead, NOT group enable/disable. So today this is a latent race on a dead code path, not a live user-facing bug.
- **Scénario** : No current production trigger (the menu live-toggle path does not call these). It would become a live high-severity race if a future change wires group toggling to the menu (the original splice-based live toggle the comments describe as removed). Reported so that re-introducing group toggling does not reintroduce an unguarded wide-window rebuild.
- **Impact** : None today (dead path). Latent: if re-wired to a hot path, an OnChar mid-rebuild would see an empty star prefix set / by-trigger index and drop every star expansion for the rebuild duration.
- **Correctif** : When (and only when) these are re-wired to a live path, apply the same Critical-wrapped build-then-swap as proposed for HSE_Register/RebuildHotstringsLive: build replacement HSE_StarPrefixSetCI/CS and HSE_StarByTriggerCI/CS into locals and assign each in one statement, under a brief Critical, so the reader never sees an empty index. For now, leave a code comment flagging the requirement.
- **Test** : headless-unit (already partially covered by test_hotstring_engine_main.ahk): after HSE_DisableGroup(g), assert the surviving specs' star indices are complete and consistent (no empty HSE_StarByTriggerCI when star specs remain). Add a meta/comment guard if these are ever exposed to a live menu path.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below threshold.

### Catégorie : Blocage du thread clavier (hot-path) (29)

<a id="f-changelog-infinite-resolve-timeout"></a>
#### `changelog-infinite-resolve-timeout` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : boot-main (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/changelog_window.ahk:300`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/changelog_window.ahk:282`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/changelog_window.ahk:287`
- **Description** : _CLW_DoFetch opens a SYNCHRONOUS WinHttpRequest (Req.Open("GET", Url, false)) and calls Req.SetTimeouts(0, 10000, 20000, 20000). The first parameter is the DNS-resolve timeout, and WinHTTP treats 0 as INFINITE. _CLW_FetchAndInject defers the fetch via SetTimer(() => _CLW_DoFetch(Channel), -1), but a SetTimer(-1) callback runs on the single AHK pseudo-thread — it interrupts whatever is running and executes Req.Send() synchronously, blocking the message pump and the low-level keyboard hook for the duration of the call.
- **Cause racine** : WinHttpRequest.SetTimeouts interprets a 0 in any slot as 'no timeout'. A synchronous Req.Send() blocks the calling thread until the phase completes; on the AHK main thread that starves the message loop and the LowLevelKeyboardProc, so Windows drops keystrokes (LowLevelHooksTimeout) or input lags/interleaves. On a stalled DNS resolver (connecting VPN, captive portal, dead resolver) the resolve phase never times out, so the freeze is unbounded.
- **Scénario** : User opens the changelog window (tray menu) on a flaky/captive-portal network, then keeps typing. The deferred _CLW_DoFetch fires on the next idle tick and blocks in DNS resolution; every key the user presses while it hangs is dropped or delayed until the network recovers — potentially indefinitely.
- **Impact** : Indefinite keyboard freeze / dropped keystrokes triggered by a user-initiated, network-dependent action. This is a regression of the exact bug the updater module already fixed and guards with a regression test (tests/test_updater.ahk asserts no SetTimeouts(0,...) in updater.ahk) — but the test scopes only updater.ahk, so changelog_window.ahk slipped through.
- **Correctif** : Replace SetTimeouts(0, 10000, 20000, 20000) with finite values for every phase, reusing the updater's constants (UPDATER_HTTP_RESOLVE_TIMEOUT_MS, …CONNECT…, …SEND…, …RECEIVE…) so the resolve phase is bounded (e.g. 5000 ms). Longer term, move the changelog fetch to the same async WinHTTP + poll-timer pattern used by _Updater_FetchLatestJsonAsync so it never runs synchronously on the main thread at all.
- **Test** : meta-static: extend the existing tests/test_updater.ahk regex guard (or add tests/test_changelog.ahk) that FileReads lib/changelog_window.ahk and asserts RegExMatch(Source, "SetTimeouts\(\s*0\s*,") is false — mirroring _UpdaterTest_NoZeroResolveTimeout — so any 0 resolve timeout anywhere in the changelog/webview fetch path fails the suite forever.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against source, not the finding's prose. lib/changelog_window.ahk:300 is exactly `Req.SetTimeouts(0, 10000, 20000, 20000)`; line 297 opens the request synchronously (`Req.Open("GET", Url, false)`) and line 301 calls `Req.Send()`. The first SetTimeouts slot is the DNS-resolve phase, and WinHttpRequest treats 0 as infinite there (the project's own updater.ahk:48-54 and test_updater.ahk:124-130 document precisely this: "a 0 resolve phase is infinite in WinHttp and freezes the main thread").

AHK concurrency model confirms the freeze is real, not a false race. The fetch is deferred via SetTimer(() => _CLW_DoFetch(Channel), -1) at line 284. A SetTimer(-1) callback is a new pseudo-thread on the single cooperatively-scheduled AHK thread; when it runs it executes the synchronous COM Req.Send() inline. A synchronous WinHttp Send is a blocking built-in: while it blocks, no other pseudo-thread can run, so the message pump and the LowLevelKeyboardProc are starved. Windows then drops keystrokes via LowLevelHooksTimeout or input lags/interleaves. With an infinite resolve timeout and a stalled resolver (connecting VPN, captive portal, dead DNS), the resolve phase never returns, so the keyboard freeze is unbounded — the exact mechanism the updater module already fixed.

Trigger path is genuine and user-initiated: tray menu -> Updater_ShowChangelog (updater.ahk:869) -> Changelog_Open (changelog_window.ahk:53) -> _CLW_FetchAndInject -> deferred _CLW_DoFetch. It also re-fires on the singleton-reuse branch (line 61) every time the already-open window is reopened.

Test-gap claim is also verified: the existing guard _UpdaterTest_NoZeroResolveTimeout (test_updater.ahk:147-159) FileReads ONLY lib\updater.ahk and applies RegExMatch(Source, "SetTimeouts\(\s*0\s*,"). changelog_window.ahk is not in its scope, so the 0 resolve timeout legitimately slipped through.

Not a false positive: the path is not Critical-protected, not A_IsSuspended-relevant (it is a network call, not a remap), and the blocking Send is the textbook hot-path-blocking-on-main-thread defect. The only mitigating nuance affects severity, not validity (see below).

<a id="f-uia-selection-query-on-hot-path"></a>
#### `uia-selection-query-on-hot-path` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : hotstrings-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:684-712`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:437-459`
- **Description** : Inside _OnPrefixChar, when Features["shortcuts"]["wrap_text_if_selected"] is on (it defaults to true on Windows — manifest.toml:1145) and the typed char is one of the wrap-symbol keys, the code calls GetUIASelection() synchronously. GetUIASelection() performs a chain of UI Automation COM calls: UIA.GetFocusedElement(), el.IsTextPatternAvailable, el.GetPattern("Text"), tp.GetSelection(), ranges[1].GetText(-1). This happens BEFORE Critical("On") and on EVERY such keystroke regardless of whether any text is actually selected. The wrap-symbol set is large and includes extremely common keys: ( ) [ ] { } < > " ' * _ ~ | / @ # % $ & ! ? + = ; :.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 runs the InputHook OnChar callback on the keyboard thread (the same pseudo-thread that must return promptly so the low-level keyboard hook can keep up). A cross-process UIA call is a blocking RPC into the focused app's UI thread; if that app is busy/unresponsive (Electron, browsers, Office, RDP, a hung window) the call can take tens to hundreds of ms. While it blocks, Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout can silently DROP the in-flight keystroke, and subsequent keys lag/interleave.
- **Scénario** : User has a slightly busy Electron app (Slack/Discord/VS Code-like) focused, no text selected, and types a ratio like "a/b" or a smiley ":" or an opening paren mid-sentence. The "/" or ":" or "(" keystroke triggers a UIA round-trip into the busy app; the app does not answer for ~150 ms; the very next character the user types is dropped by the OS hook timeout. The user sees "a/b" come out as "ab" or "a/" with the next letter missing — a classic intermittent lost-keystroke that is impossible to reproduce on a fast/idle app.
- **Impact** : Intermittent dropped/lagged keystrokes whenever a wrap-symbol key is pressed while a slow or unresponsive app is focused — and it fires on the common symbols, not rare ones. This is the single most realistic remaining 'lost keystroke' vector in this unit because the blocking work is unbounded (depends on the foreground app) and sits directly on the synchronous per-keystroke path.
- **Correctif** : Gate GetUIASelection() so the UIA round-trip only runs when a selection plausibly exists. Cheapest: skip the UIA query entirely unless GetKeyState modifiers or a cheap heuristic indicate selection; better, check selection state without a full TextPattern walk, or move the UIA query off the synchronous path (e.g. only attempt the wrap on an explicit gesture, or cache 'this app never exposes TextPattern' results per-process so repeated misses cost nothing). At minimum, wrap GetUIASelection in a hard timeout (run it via a short-lived worker / ComCall with a deadline) and treat a timeout as 'no selection', and extend the WinActive("Code") fast-bail into a small allowlist/denylist of process names known to be slow so the query is never even attempted there.
- **Test** : Headless-unit: refactor the selection probe behind an injectable seam (like _SendHook) — a test sets a fake selection-provider that records how many times it was invoked and can simulate a slow/throwing provider. Assert _OnPrefixChar does NOT invoke the provider when wrap_text_if_selected is off, and that a throwing/timed-out provider is caught and yields the no-wrap fall-through (char still delivered) rather than aborting the callback. Add a meta/static test asserting _OnPrefixChar's UIA-wrap branch sits before Critical("On") (so a future edit cannot pull a blocking UIA call inside Critical) AND that GetUIASelection is only reached behind the active-pairs membership guard.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I tried to refute this and could not — the finding is real and is corroborated by the maintainer's own prior commit.

VERIFIED AGAINST SOURCE:
- hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:684-712: inside _OnPrefixChar (the InputHook OnChar callback), once the typed Char is a member of WrapSymbols_GetActivePairs() (line 692, `_ActivePairsSnap.Has(Char)`), the code calls GetUIASelection() SYNCHRONOUSLY at line 694, and this is BEFORE Critical("On") at line 723. Confirmed.
- GetUIASelection() (layout.ahk:437-459) is a chain of raw COM calls: UIA.GetFocusedElement() = ComCall(8) (UIA.ahk:1086), el.IsTextPatternAvailable, el.GetPattern("Text"), tp.GetSelection(), ranges[1].GetText(-1) = ComCall(12) (UIA.ahk:6848). UI Automation is cross-process RPC; GetFocusedElement and the TextPattern walk marshal into the focused app's UIA provider/UI thread, so latency is bounded by the foreground app, not by ErgoptiPlus. The only fast-bail is `WinActive("Code")` and `!isSet(UIA)` — no selection-state pre-check, no timeout, no per-process denylist/cache.
- The active wrap-symbol set (shared/wrap_symbols.json) includes extremely common keys: ( ) [ ] { } < > " ' ` * _ ~ | / \ @ # % $ & ! ? + = ; :. So the UIA round-trip fires on essentially any sentence with punctuation, regardless of whether anything is selected (the membership check passes first; the UIA query is what determines selection).
- Default is ON: manifest.toml:1144 / config_template.toml:314 / features_manifest.ahk:162 all set wrap_text_if_selected default=true.

AHK CONCURRENCY MODEL — why this is a true hot-path stall, not a phantom race: the InputHook OnChar callback runs on the script's pseudo-thread. The maintainer's own comment at layout.ahk:370-373 establishes that while a keystroke callback runs (and is later made Critical), AHK cannot start the NEXT physical key's remap hotkey nor the watcher's next OnChar until it drains. The layout remap is implemented as hook-based hotkeys (`Hotkey("*"ScanCode, ..., "I2")`, layout.ahk:386-388) that force the WH_KEYBOARD_LL hook. So a 50-200 ms blocking UIA call sitting on this single serialized path stalls subsequent key processing; at the upper latency bound this approaches Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout territory where keystrokes can be dropped/passed-through unprocessed, and below that it is severe interleave/lag. The whole _OnPrefixChar is wrapped by the QPC hot-path profiler (_OnPrefixCharProfiled, lines 642-646) whose WARNING threshold is 5 ms (hotpath_profiler.ahk:30) — i.e. the maintainer already treats >5 ms in this exact region as a keystroke-loss-class defect; the UIA call lives inside that profiled region and routinely exceeds it.

DECISIVE CORROBORATION (refutation failed): git commit 136bbeeac "fix(ahk): prevent massive lag by scoping UIA selection-wrap checks" — the maintainer's own message states verbatim: "Since UIA calls can block for 50-200ms depending on the target app, this caused severe typing lag for users on the compiled and local AHK versions." That earlier fix only SCOPED the UIA call to wrapper symbols; it did NOT remove the per-keystroke UIA call for those symbols, and the symbol set is dominated by common punctuation. So the remaining behavior is the residue of a real, empirically observed lag bug, not a theoretical one. This is the single most realistic remaining hot-path-blocking vector in the unit because the blocking work is unbounded (foreground-app dependent) and sits on the synchronous per-keystroke path.

MINOR INACCURACY IN THE FINDING (does not change the verdict): it cites two files as if both are independent hot-path triggers. Only the prefix_watcher OnChar path (684-712) fires on every plain symbol keystroke. The layout.ahk WrapTextIfSelected (461-481) is the shared helper, invoked only from explicit AltGr/Shift+symbol hotkeys (layout_altgr.ahk, layout_shift_caps.ahk) — a deliberate gesture, which is actually the finding's own recommended posture for UIA. The real fix target is the OnChar path.

<a id="f-uia-selection-blocks-keyboard-thread"></a>
#### `uia-selection-blocks-keyboard-thread` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : layout (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:437`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:461`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/layout/layout_altgr.ahk:115`
- **Description** : WrapTextIfSelected (called as the Plain action for AltGr+`<` `>` `(` `)` `[` `]` `{` `}` `"` `:` `|` `;` `~` `@` `*` `%` `#` `&` `$` `=` `+` `?` `^` `/` `\` `_` and Shift+Space) calls GetUIASelection() BEFORE it knows whether anything is selected. GetUIASelection() does a full synchronous UIA/COM chain on the keyboard hotkey thread: UIA.GetFocusedElement(), el.GetPattern("Text"), tp.GetSelection(), ranges[1].GetText(-1). These are cross-process automation calls with no timeout. The master gate Features["shortcuts"]["wrap_text_if_selected"] defaults to true (config_template.toml:314, features_manifest.ahk:162), so on a stock install this fires for the large majority of AltGr symbol keys and for Shift+Space.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 is cooperatively single-threaded; a hotkey callback runs on the same thread that services the low-level keyboard hook. UIA GetFocusedElement/GetPattern/GetSelection/GetText are blocking out-of-process COM round-trips. When the focused app is busy, hung, elevated/remote, or a slow Electron/Office surface, the call can take tens-to-hundreds of ms (or stall on the 2 s+ COM call timeout). While the callback blocks, the next physical keystroke must be handled within Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout (default ~300 ms) or it is silently dropped/passed through unprocessed.
- **Scénario** : User has wrap-on-selection on (default). With nothing selected they type a fast burst that includes AltGr+`(` (a very common French/programming key). The `(` callback issues a UIA query against, say, a momentarily-busy Office/Electron window; the query takes 250 ms. The very next character the user typed during that window is dropped or leaks through as the raw OS-layout key, producing missing/garbled output exactly when typing fast.
- **Impact** : Intermittent dropped or mis-ordered keystrokes during fast typing, scaling with how slow/hung the focused app is. This is the user's #1 concern (lost keystrokes) and it is on a default-on, very frequently used key set.
- **Correctif** : Do not block the keyboard thread on UIA. Options, in order of preference: (a) Only attempt the UIA selection probe when a selection is plausible — gate it behind a cheap heuristic (e.g. require a recent Shift-drag / Ctrl+A, or skip the probe entirely when the active app is known-slow such as Electron, which is already partially done via WinActive("Code")). (b) Wrap the entire GetUIASelection() in a hard timeout using a worker (e.g. a short-lived ComCall on a separate AHK process, or a SetTimer-driven async probe that falls back to plain SendNewResult if it has not answered within ~30 ms). (c) Add ComObjFlags / IUIAutomation timeout configuration and a defensive A_TickCount budget so the function returns "" after a small cap. At minimum, document and measure the worst-case latency and bound it well under LowLevelHooksTimeout.
- **Test** : headless-unit is hard because modules/layout.ahk is not #Included by run_all (WrapTextIfSelected is stubbed in test_stubs.ahk). Add a meta-static test in tests/meta/ that asserts GetUIASelection (or its call site) is bounded: e.g. source-scan layout.ahk to assert the UIA chain is either guarded by an A_TickCount/timeout budget or only reached after a cheap pre-gate. For a behavioural guarantee, refactor GetUIASelection to take an injectable probe function and add a headless-unit test that feeds a probe which sleeps > budget and asserts the function returns "" within the budget and falls back to SendNewResult.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against source, not the description. Registration is plain `Hotkey("SC138 & " SC, AltGrShiftDispatch.Bind(...), "I2")` (lib/layout/layout_altgr.ahk:298/307/316), so the callback runs on the single AHK pseudo-thread that services the low-level keyboard hook. Dispatch is fully synchronous: AltGrShiftDispatch -> Cb() -> WrapTextIfSelected -> GetUIASelection() (layout_altgr.ahk:273-276; modules/layout.ahk:461-481). There is NO Critical, NO SetTimer, NO async indirection on this path.

GetUIASelection() (modules/layout.ahk:437-459) is uncapped synchronous COM: UIA.GetFocusedElement() is ComCall 8 (vendor/UIA.ahk:1086), el.GetPattern("Text")/tp.GetSelection() (vendor/UIA.ahk:5800)/ranges[1].GetText(-1) are all raw cross-process ComCalls with no cache request and no A_TickCount budget. The library exposes ConnectionTimeout/TransactionTimeout statics (vendor/UIA.ahk:408-416) but the call site never sets them, so the default multi-second IUIAutomation timeout is the worst case. Crucially the probe runs BEFORE knowing anything is selected (layout.ahk:470-471), so it fires on EVERY press of these keys.

Default-on confirmed: wrap_text_if_selected = true in config_template.toml:314, features_manifest.ahk:162, test_stubs.ahk:102; bound to a large AltGr symbol set plus Shift+Space (SC039) across lib/layout/layout_altgr.ahk and modules/layout.ahk.

Under the AHK cooperative model this is a genuine hot-path block: while the single thread sits inside a blocking out-of-process COM round-trip, it cannot service the next physical keystroke, and Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout drops/leaks keys whose hook callback exceeds the limit. The project itself already classifies this exact path as Sleep-ing: tests/meta/test_input_serialization.ahk:18-20 calls "the UIA wrap" a Sleep-ing path deliberately kept OUT of Critical. Being out of Critical only avoids pump starvation via buffering; it does nothing to stop the synchronous COM call from monopolizing the one thread, so the lost-keystroke concern stands and is unaddressed by any existing guard. The only mitigation present is WinActive("Code") to skip Electron (layout.ahk:438), which narrows but does not bound the worst case — any non-Electron busy/hung/elevated/remote provider still blocks unbounded. This is not a generic-threading false alarm; it is a real synchronous block on the keyboard thread.

<a id="f-ingest-tick-blocks-keyboard-thread"></a>
#### `ingest-tick-blocks-keyboard-thread` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : keylogger-core (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1285`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1557`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:363`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:371`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_reader.ahk:110`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_reader.ahk:214`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_webview.ahk:506`
- **Description** : KL_IngestOnce (SetTimer 5000 ms) ends with `try KLWV_NotifyIngest()`, and KL_Hook_LivePush (armed off the 200 ms flush tick as a -1500 ms one-shot) calls `KLWV_NotifyIngest("manifest")`. When any dashboard window is open (first_paint_done set), KLWV_NotifyIngest calls KLPF_BuildAndWrite -> KLR_BuildDatabase, which performs synchronous SQLite work (KLR_ApplyIncremental Seek+Read of the appended data.sql tail; KLR_ExecLargeFile reading data.sql in 4 MB chunks and calling SQLite_Exec/SQLite_ExecReturnCarry on each; KLR_RebuildAggregates running ~8 GROUP BY statements; plus repeated FileAppend to prefetch.log). All executes as an ordinary SetTimer pseudo-thread on the process main thread — the SAME thread that services the HookDispatcher InputHook OnChar/OnKeyDown callbacks. None holds Critical, but each individual SQLite_Exec / sqlite3_step / FileAppend / fh.Read is a single blocking DllCall/built-in that cannot be interrupted mid-call.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 is cooperatively single-threaded: SetTimer callbacks and InputHook callbacks are pseudo-threads multiplexed onto one OS thread. A blocking built-in/DllCall (SQLite_Exec on a 4 MB chunk, a GROUP BY over a large events table, a FileAppend of a large SQL body) holds the thread for its full duration; during that window the low-level keyboard hook callback cannot run, so Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout (~300 ms) silently drops the keystroke or the OS stops delivering hook events.
- **Scénario** : User opens the typing-metrics dashboard then keeps typing fast. After months of capture data.sql is tens-to-hundreds of MB. Every 5 s the ingest tick (and, while bursting, the 1.5 s-debounced live push) freezes the keyboard thread for the SQLite rebuild duration — characters are dropped or arrive in a burst after the freeze. KL_Hook_Tick:346 acknowledges this exact hazard for FileAppend yet the live-push path it arms re-introduces the same blocking work via KLR_BuildDatabase.
- **Impact** : Lost / laggy / reordered keystrokes whenever a metrics dashboard is open — the user's #1 concern. Worsens with dataset size; even a warm incremental + RebuildAggregates is 150 ms-1 s, already over LowLevelHooksTimeout.
- **Correctif** : Never run KLR_BuildDatabase / KLPF_BuildAndWrite synchronously on the timer sharing the keystroke thread when it can exceed a few ms. Either move the SQLite rebuild + projection to a worker process/child AHK script over a file/pipe, or chunk it so each timer slice does bounded work and yields (one 4 MB chunk per tick; time-budget RebuildAggregates) and gate the live-push so it only fires when the keystroke thread has been idle (A_TickCount - KLHook.last_tick > threshold). At minimum drop the per-call FileAppend debug logging in KLR_BuildDatabase from the hot cadence and gate KL_Hook_LivePush behind 'no dashboard open'.
- **Test** : headless-unit: extract KL_LivePushShouldDefer(last_tick, now, idle_threshold_ms) and assert it returns true while typing is active so the heavy rebuild defers. meta-static: assert KL_Hook_LivePush / KL_IngestOnce do not call KLR_BuildDatabase without an idle/defer guard (grep KLWV_NotifyIngest call sites). live: measure max inter-keystroke stall on a synthetic 100 MB data.sql with an open dashboard.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against real source, not the finding's prose. The blocking-on-the-keystroke-thread mechanism is real under the AHK v2 cooperative model.

THREAD IDENTITY (the load-bearing fact): HookDispatcher (lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:284) installs ONE in-process InputHook("V L0"); its OnChar/OnKeyDown fire as pseudo-threads on the process MAIN thread (_OnChar->Dispatch, line 210-216). The keylogger's KL_IngestOnce (SetTimer 5000 ms, keylogger.ahk:1557) and the one-shot KL_Hook_LivePush (SetTimer -1500 ms armed off the 200 ms flush tick, keylogger_hook.ahk:363) ALSO run on that same single OS thread. AHK runs exactly one pseudo-thread at a time and can only switch at rest points (between lines, Sleep, blocking built-ins) — NOT in the middle of a DllCall. So while a timer thread is inside SQLite_Exec/sqlite3_step or fh.Read, the InputHook OnChar physically cannot dispatch.

HEAVY WORK IS SYNCHRONOUS + INLINE + UNGUARDED: KL_IngestOnce ends with `try KLWV_NotifyIngest()` (default mode "live", keylogger.ahk:1285); KL_Hook_LivePush calls KLWV_NotifyIngest("manifest") (keylogger_hook.ahk:371). KLWV_NotifyIngest (keylogger_webview.ahk:506-526) iterates KLWV.windows and, for each with first_paint_done, calls KLPF_BuildAndWrite SYNCHRONOUSLY (line 520). KLPF_BuildAndWrite (keylogger_prefetch.ahk:78-138) calls KLR_BuildDatabase inline (line 87) — no Run, no worker, no pipe — then projects+JSON-encodes+writes, even timing each stage (t_db,t_proj,t_json,t_write), which shows the authors know it is multi-stage heavy work. KLR_BuildDatabase (keylogger_reader.ahk:110) on the warm path does KLR_ApplyIncremental (Seek+Read+SQLite_Exec of the appended tail), KLR_ClearAggregates, then KLR_RebuildAggregates — eight full GROUP BY INSERTs over events_* (lines 380-417), several using per-row json_each subqueries (380/401/405) that scale ~quadratically with events_typing size — plus repeated FileAppend to prefetch.log per call (116-205). No Critical (wouldn't help; would worsen starvation), no idle/last-tick guard, no "dashboard closed" gate.

INTERNAL CONTRADICTION CONFIRMS THE HAZARD: KL_Hook_Tick (keylogger_hook.ahk:346-351) deliberately does NOT call KL_IngestOnce from the 200 ms flush tick because its FileAppend "would block incoming keystroke callbacks, causing perceptible input lag at high typing speed" — yet the live-push it arms re-introduces far heavier blocking (full SQLite rebuild) on that very thread. The codebase already acknowledges the exact mechanism the finding raises.

WHY NOT A FALSE POSITIVE: This is not a "data race" claim that AHK's single-threading refutes — it is a thread-starvation/hook-timeout claim, which the single-threaded model AMPLIFIES rather than excuses. A_IsSuspended gating (Dispatch line 191) is irrelevant: it silences fan-out under pause, it does not stop the timer from blocking the thread.

CALIBRATION (why high, not critical): The expensive path only runs while a metrics dashboard window is open AND first_paint_done is set (the for-loop body at webview.ahk:517-521 is skipped, n stays 0, otherwise). A user typing without the dashboard open is unaffected — matching the finding's own scenario. The incremental SQL exec is bounded by 1.5-5 s of new bytes, so the dominant unbounded cost is RebuildAggregates' 8 GROUP BY scans over the full in-memory events_* (grows with history, independent of delta). Whether a single tick exceeds the ~300 ms LowLevelHooksTimeout — i.e. an actually DROPPED key vs merely a stall/burst — depends on dataset size and CPU and cannot be proven by static reading; the finding's "150 ms-1 s even warm" is directionally credible but unverified. Net: real, user-visible stalls/reorder are essentially certain when a dashboard is open on a large dataset; outright key DROP is plausible but magnitude needs measurement. High is right (the user's #1 concern, but conditional on dashboard-open).

PROPOSED FIX is sound. The minimal correct fix: gate KL_Hook_LivePush (and the live mode of KL_IngestOnce's notify) behind both (a) no heavy work unless A_TickCount-KLHook.last_tick exceeds an idle threshold, deferring while the user is actively typing, and (b) ideally offload KLR_BuildDatabase to a worker. The cheap immediate wins: drop the per-call FileAppend debug logging in KLR_BuildDatabase from the hot cadence, and time-budget/yield RebuildAggregates (one bounded slice per tick).

<a id="f-bypass-dispatch-critical-starves-keyboard-hook"></a>
#### `bypass-dispatch-critical-starves-keyboard-hook` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : tray-menu (module) · **Fichiers** : `lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:282`, `lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:295`, `ui/tray_menu.ahk:2798`, `ui/tray_menu.ahk:438`
- **Description** : _DispatchIfMissed (menu_dispatcher.ahk:280) does `Critical` (no arg = Critical On) at line 282 and never turns it off, then calls the user callback at line 295. For a live hotstring toggle the callback chain is ToggleMenuVariableByPath -> _HS_ApplyLiveToggle (tray_menu.ahk:438) -> RebuildHotstringsLive (tray_menu.ahk:2798) -> HSE_RegistryClear() + HSE_HardReset() + RegisterAllHotstrings() + RebuildTrayMenu(). RegisterAllHotstrings re-creates the project's ~5400 hotstrings; this is the documented heavy boot phase. Running it with Critical ON disables thread interruption AND buffers messages, so the low-level keyboard hook cannot be serviced for the full rebuild duration.
- **Cause racine** : Per the AHK concurrency model, `Critical "On"` held across a long synchronous operation starves the message pump and the LL keyboard hook (LowLevelHooksTimeout can then DROP physical keystrokes). The Critical here was presumably added to make the timestamp read/write atomic, but it is never scoped down — it remains in force across the arbitrarily long user callback. The native (non-bypass) dispatch path runs the same callback WITHOUT Critical, so the engine rebuild is only dangerous on the ~30-50% of clicks that get the bypass.
- **Scénario** : User toggles a hotstring category/section while typing (or immediately resumes typing). AHK drops the native dispatch; 60 ms later _DispatchIfMissed fires it under Critical and re-registers 5400 hotstrings. During that window every physical keystroke can be dropped or arrive reordered because the LL hook is starved and messages are buffered. The user sees missing/garbled characters right after a menu toggle — the unit's #1 concern.
- **Impact** : Dropped/laggy/reordered keystrokes for the duration of an in-process hotstring rebuild, on a significant fraction of toggle clicks. Worse than the native path because Critical converts an interruptible rebuild into a hook-blocking one.
- **Correctif** : Scope Critical to only the timestamp compare-and-set, then release it BEFORE invoking the user callback: read CurrentLastFire and write `_MenuDispatchLastFire[ItemId] := A_TickCount` under `Critical "On"`, then `Critical "Off"`, THEN `Callback.Call(...)`. The callback must run interruptibly exactly like AHK's native dispatch path. (Optionally also move RebuildHotstringsLive's heavy work off the click thread, but at minimum do not hold Critical across it.)
- **Test** : meta-static: assert that in _DispatchIfMissed the `Critical "Off"` (or a Critical(0)) appears textually BEFORE the `Callback.Call(` line — i.e. Critical is released before the user callback runs. Encodes the root cause (Critical must not span the callback). A live test would require hardware to observe dropped keys, so the static guard is the reliable regression encoding.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code directly and the finding is accurate end to end.

CODE: lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:282 is a bare `Critical` (no arg == Critical "On"). It is never turned off; the next/only off-ramp is the function returning. The user callback runs at line 295 (`Callback.Call("bypass_dispatch")`) still under that Critical. Lines 283-291 are the legitimate atomic compare-and-set on _MenuDispatchLastFire (read at 283, compare at 284, write at 291) that needs the guard against a concurrent _TrackedDispatch; lines 292-295 (logging + the call) do NOT.

CHAIN: Hotstring section toggles ARE registered through RegisterMenuItem (tray_menu.ahk:38/70/108 -> `(*) => ToggleMenuVariableByPath(FullPath)`), so they participate in the bypass. ToggleMenuVariableByPath (356) -> _HS_TryLiveToggle (362) -> _HS_ApplyLiveToggle (433) -> RebuildHotstringsLive (2798) -> HSE_RegistryClear() + HSE_HardReset() + RegisterAllHotstrings() + RebuildTrayMenu(). All verified.

HEAVINESS: modules/hotstrings.ahk's own header documents the emojis+symbols categories as "~3000 registrations / ~410 ms" and defers them at boot specifically because they "should not sit on the critical boot path"; a live rebuild calls RegisterAllHotstrings(DeferHeavy := false), i.e. registers the FULL ~5400-entry set synchronously. I grepped the whole registration path (RegisterAllHotstrings, HSE_RegistryClear at hotstring_engine_main.ahk:379, HSE_HardReset at 650) and there is NO Sleep/yield in it — it runs straight through. So Critical's message buffering + no-interruption holds for the entire multi-hundred-ms rebuild.

AHK MODEL: The keystroke path is a single InputHook in pass-through V mode (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:21, _PrefixInputHook), backed by WH_KEYBOARD_LL. AHK is cooperatively single-threaded; with Critical "On" and no rest point, the one pseudo-thread cannot service the LL hook callback during the rebuild. Windows LowLevelHooksTimeout (default 300 ms) then drops/passes-through events the hook can't process in time, and the InputHook OnChar/OnKeyDown threads are deferred by Critical. The native (non-bypass) dispatch runs the IDENTICAL callback WITHOUT Critical, so the hazard is unique to the ~30-50% of clicks that take the bypass — confirming the finding's "worse than native" claim, not a generic concern.

This is not a false positive on serialization grounds: Critical here is the CAUSE of the starvation, not a protection. The project already enforces the inverse rule everywhere else — tests/meta/test_input_serialization.ahk asserts the Sleep-ing send paths stay OUT of Critical ("Critical must not span a Sleep"), and hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1302-1307 explicitly drops Critical around a Sleep for exactly this reason. _DispatchIfMissed is the lone violator of that doctrine.

<a id="f-ingest-prefetch-blocks-keyboard-thread"></a>
#### `ingest-prefetch-blocks-keyboard-thread` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-async-background (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1285`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1557`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_webview.ahk:506`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_webview.ahk:520`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_prefetch.ahk:78`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_prefetch.ahk:87`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:366`
- **Description** : KL_IngestOnce is armed as a periodic SetTimer at INGEST_TICK_MS=5000 (keylogger.ahk:1557). On every tick it calls KLWV_NotifyIngest() (keylogger.ahk:1285), which for each open dashboard window runs KLPF_BuildAndWrite(which, dir, , "live") (keylogger_webview.ahk:520). That function (keylogger_prefetch.ahk:78) calls KLR_BuildDatabase (materialises an in-memory SQLite DB from data.sql, line 87), runs SQLite_Query + the today n-gram projection (KLR_BuildTodayIdxJson), KL_JsonEncode of the blob, and an atomic file write — the module's own comments rate this at ~150-300 ms for "live" and ~2-3 s for "full". All of it executes inline on the main AHK pseudo-thread.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 is cooperatively single-threaded: a SetTimer callback runs to completion on the same pseudo-thread as the low-level keyboard hook / PrefixWatcher InputHook. While KLPF_BuildAndWrite is executing (hundreds of ms of SQLite + JSON work, with no Sleep to yield), no other thread — including the InputHook OnChar that feeds both the app and the LLM buffer — can run. If that wall-clock window exceeds Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout (~300 ms default), the OS silently evicts the hook for that event and the keystroke is DROPPED; below that it manifests as visible input lag / interleaving. KL_Hook_Tick was carefully written to keep KL_IngestOnce OFF the flush thread for exactly this reason (keylogger_hook.ahk:346-351 comment), but the 5 s ingest timer itself still calls the heavy fan-out synchronously, and KL_Hook_LivePush (keylogger_hook.ahk:366) additionally fires KLWV_NotifyIngest("manifest") on a debounce right after a typing burst.
- **Scénario** : User opens the typing-metrics dashboard (WebView2 window stays open in the background) and keeps typing in their editor. Every 5 s the ingest tick rebuilds the live prefetch blob (~200 ms, longer on a large data.sql or a slow disk); on a machine that just woke, or with a multi-hundred-MB data.sql, the first "full" build can take seconds. During each build the user's keystrokes lag or are dropped — the classic "characters eaten at random" symptom. It only reproduces with a dashboard open, which is why it is intermittent and hard to attribute.
- **Impact** : Lost/laggy keystrokes during normal typing whenever a metrics dashboard window is open — the user's #1 concern, triggered by the metrics feature observing its own typing.
- **Correctif** : Take the heavy build off the keyboard thread. Either (a) spawn the SQLite projection in a curl-style detached child / separate AHK process (mirroring how the LLM dispatch moved to a curl child) and harvest the result via a poll timer, or (b) chunk the work and yield with periodic `Sleep -1` between phases so the message pump and InputHook can interleave, or at minimum (c) gate the live/full rebuild behind a coalescing one-shot that runs at most every N seconds AND skips entirely when KL_Hook recorded a keystroke within the last ~500 ms (defer until the user pauses), so a build never starts mid-burst. The fast manifest-only path (~20 ms) can stay inline; the n-gram/full projection must not.
- **Test** : meta-static: a test in tests/ that reads keylogger_prefetch.ahk + keylogger_webview.ahk source and asserts the live/full projection path is fenced — e.g. KLPF_BuildAndWrite's "live"/"full" branches are only reachable from a deferred/off-thread dispatcher (assert KL_IngestOnce's body does NOT call KLWV_NotifyIngest with the default "live" mode directly, mirroring the existing test_updater.ahk _UpdaterTest_BackgroundTickIsAsync brace-walk pattern). Plus a headless-unit timing assertion is impractical without a DB, so pair it with a meta-test asserting a 'recent-keystroke defer' guard string exists in KLWV_NotifyIngest.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read all cited code. The mechanism is real under the AHK v2 cooperative-threading model.

CHAIN VERIFIED:
- keylogger.ahk:1557 arms KL_IngestOnce as a periodic SetTimer at INGEST_TICK_MS=5000 (constant confirmed at keylogger.ahk:73).
- keylogger.ahk:1285: every tick ends with `try KLWV_NotifyIngest()` — no argument.
- keylogger_webview.ahk:506: `KLWV_NotifyIngest(mode := "live")` — the default is "live", NOT "manifest". For each open dashboard with first_paint_done it calls `KLPF_BuildAndWrite(which, KLWV.metrics_dir, , mode)` synchronously (line 520) then PushPrefetch.
- keylogger_prefetch.ahk:78-141: KLPF_BuildAndWrite runs KLR_BuildDatabase (line 87), SQLite_Query, the today n-gram projection via KLR_BuildTodayIdxJson (line 233 in KLPF_BuildTyping), KL_JsonEncode, and KLPF_WriteAtomic — all inline, no Sleep/yield. The module's own header comments rate "live" at ~150-300 ms.

WHY IT IS A REAL LOST-KEYSTROKE BUG (not a false positive):
- AHK v2 runs exactly one pseudo-thread. A SetTimer body runs to completion before any other thread can start. The InputHook OnChar/OnKeyDown handlers (keylogger_hook.ahk:205-334) carry NO Critical and are dispatched through the shared HookDispatcher; they cannot preempt a running SetTimer body. So while KLPF_BuildAndWrite executes (hundreds of ms of synchronous SQLite + JSON + file IO with zero yield), the keystroke callback for a key the user just pressed is stalled. If the stall exceeds Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout (~300 ms default) the OS evicts the hook for that event and the key is dropped; below that it is visible lag/interleave. The "live" 150-300 ms window straddles that timeout exactly, so drops occur at the margin (slow disk, large data.sql, post-wake cold rebuild) and lag occurs the rest of the time — matching the "characters eaten at random, only with a dashboard open" symptom.
- The author already KNEW this hazard: KL_Hook_Tick (keylogger_hook.ahk:346-351) explicitly keeps KL_IngestOnce off the flush thread because its FileAppend "would block incoming keystroke callbacks, causing perceptible input lag," and KL_Hook_LivePush (366-371) deliberately uses the cheap "manifest" (~20 ms) path, deferring the heavy "live" rebuild to "the 5 s ingest cycle so it never runs on the flush thread." But the 5 s ingest cycle IS the same single pseudo-thread — moving the heavy build there does not make it off-thread, it just moves the stall to a different timer. The fence is incomplete.
- This is the exact failure class the repo already guards against elsewhere: tests/test_updater.ahk:200 (_UpdaterTest_BackgroundTickIsAsync) enforces that a background SetTimer must not run a blocking call on the main thread because "a synchronous WinHttp call on the main thread freezes ALL keyboard remapping." The ingest tick violates the same invariant for SQLite+JSON instead of HTTP.
- Correctly scoped: KLWV_NotifyIngest returns early when KLWV.metrics_dir is empty and only fans out to open windows, so it is a true no-op with no dashboard open. No A_IsSuspended concern (no-op when closed). The bug is genuinely dashboard-open-only and intermittent, as claimed.

ONE INACCURACY IN THE FINDING (does not change the verdict, lowers it from the implied worst case): the PERIODIC 5 s tick calls "live" (~150-300 ms), not "full" (~2-3 s). The only "full" build is the one-shot KLWV_DelayedFullBuild fired ~2 s after a dashboard opens (keylogger_webview.ahk:482-492) — a single multi-second stall at open, not every 5 s. So the steady-state cost is the recurring 150-300 ms "live" build every 5 s plus one ~2-3 s "full" stall at open. That is solidly high (recurring, user's #1 concern) but not the catastrophic 2-3s-every-5s the description implies.

<a id="f-remote-poll-no-deadline-cap"></a>
#### `remote-poll-no-deadline-cap` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-async-background (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_remote.ahk:169`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_remote.ahk:181`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_remote.ahk:182`
- **Description** : _LLMRemote_PollRequest (api_remote.ahk:169) checks WaitForResponse(0); when not ready it unconditionally re-arms `SetTimer(() => _LLMRemote_PollRequest(req_id), -LLM_REMOTE_POLL_MS)` (line 182) with LLM_REMOTE_POLL_MS = 50. There is NO absolute deadline, no poll-count budget, and no check that WinHTTP's own timeout fired. The only exits are: WaitForResponse(0) flips true, the COM call throws, or the entry's `cancelled` flag is set.
- **Cause racine** : This is the identical unbounded-poll bug that the Ollama side explicitly documents and fixed: see api_ollama.ahk:629-635 (_LLM_Ollama_PollGeneric) — 'a WinHTTP request whose WaitForResponse(0) never flips true (the COM proxy silently breaks under heavy load, or the server is unreachable AND WinHTTP fails to honour its own timeout) would re-arm SetTimer every 50 ms forever ... saturated the AHK message loop and caused the InputHook to drop keystrokes'. The Ollama generic poll added a hard `deadline` argument; the updater async poll added UPDATER_ASYNC_MAX_POLLS. The remote poll received neither — it trusts WinHTTP's SetTimeouts(LLM_REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS...) to always surface, which the project's own comments declare unreliable.
- **Scénario** : User selects the remote 'api' backend (OpenAI/Anthropic/Gemini/etc.). A request is dispatched on a flaky network or to an endpoint that accepts the TCP connection then stalls (captive portal, half-open proxy, provider hang). WinHTTP fails to honour its receive timeout (the documented COM-proxy failure mode); WaitForResponse(0) never flips true and never throws. _LLMRemote_PollRequest now fires every 50 ms indefinitely. The engine's request_id moves on as the user keeps typing, but the timer entry is never `cancelled` (LLM_RemoteCancelAllAsync only flags entries it knows about, and a new fire creates NEW entries while this one keeps polling). The 20 Hz timer storm contends with the InputHook → keystrokes lag/drop.
- **Impact** : On the paid-API backend, a single wedged request degrades typing for the rest of the session (or until reload). Same lost-keystroke mechanism the Ollama/updater fixes were written to prevent, left unguarded on the remote path.
- **Correctif** : Mirror api_ollama.ahk: thread an absolute `deadline := A_TickCount + LLM_REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS + slack` (and/or a max-polls budget like the updater's UPDATER_ASYNC_MAX_POLLS) through _LLMRemote_PollRequest. On `A_TickCount >= deadline`, call `try entry["http"].Abort()`, delete the registry entry, fire on_fail() once, and stop re-arming. Also have LLM_RemoteCancelAllAsync call `.Abort()` on the http object (it currently only sets cancelled := true, leaving the WinHTTP request live).
- **Test** : meta-static (mirrors test_updater.ahk): brace-walk _LLMRemote_PollRequest's body in api_remote.ahk and assert it references a deadline/max-polls cap and an Abort/Delete-on-timeout path (e.g. AssertTrue(InStr(body,"deadline") || InStr(body,"MAX_POLLS"))). Optionally a headless-unit test that drives a fake http stub whose WaitForResponse always returns false and asserts the poll function stops re-arming after the budget.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Read the real code at api_remote.ahk:169-213 and api_ollama.ahk:496-668. The finding is accurate and, on one point, understated.

WHAT THE FINDING GETS RIGHT (verified):
- _LLMRemote_PollRequest (api_remote.ahk:169) has NO absolute deadline, NO poll-count budget, and NO WinHTTP-timeout check. When `ready` is false it unconditionally re-arms `SetTimer(() => _LLMRemote_PollRequest(req_id), -LLM_REMOTE_POLL_MS)` (line 182, 50 ms = 20 Hz). The twin _LLM_Ollama_PollGeneric (api_ollama.ahk:636-668) AND the main _LLM_Ollama_PollRequest (line 500) both carry an explicit `deadline` and a documented comment (lines 626-635) describing this EXACT failure: a WaitForResponse(0) that never flips true saturates the AHK message loop and makes the InputHook drop keystrokes. The remote path was never given that guard. This is a genuine asymmetry, not a misread.

THE FINDING IS ACTUALLY TOO GENEROUS TO THE CODE: it lists "the COM call throws" as a loop exit. It is NOT. Line 180 is `try ready := http.WaitForResponse(0)` — a thrown COM exception is swallowed and `ready` stays false (initialised line 179), so the timer re-arms. The documented COM-proxy-breaks-under-load mode therefore does NOT self-terminate the loop; it perpetuates it. The bug is more robust against self-healing than the report claims.

ADVERSARIAL ANGLE I CHECKED (a real partial backstop the finding omitted): LLM_REMOTE_MAX_INFLIGHT = 16 + _LLMRemote_TrimAsyncRegistry (line 268-276). On each NEW dispatch, if the registry is at 16, the OLDEST entry is Deleted from the Map; that entry's next tick hits `if !_LLM_Remote_Async.Has(req_id) return` (line 171) and its timer chain stops. So a single wedged request does eventually die — but only after 16 further requests are dispatched. With the "api" rate floor (LLM_BACKEND_MIN_REQUEST_INTERVAL_MS["api"] = 500, prediction_engine.ahk:474-485), that is >= 8 s of continued typing. Worse: a genuinely wedged endpoint (captive portal / half-open proxy) wedges EVERY new request too, so up to 16 pollers run concurrently at 20 Hz = up to ~320 timer callbacks/sec contending with the InputHook before eviction even begins. And the trim only fires on dispatch — if the user pauses typing with 16 entries wedged, NOTHING evicts them; all 16 poll at 20 Hz until the engine is disabled.

WHY THE OTHER SUPPOSED OFF-RAMP DOESN'T SAVE IT: the `cancelled` flag is only set by LLM_RemoteCancelAllAsync, called solely from LLM_Engine_StopGeneration (prediction_engine.ahk:298), which fires on explicit stop events (engine disable / llm_bridge.ahk:364 teardown) — NOT on the per-keystroke debounce. LLM_Engine_OnKeystroke (line 201) and the rate-limited re-fire path call only LLM_Engine_CancelTimer, which never touches the async registry. So normal continued typing — the exact scenario where keystrokes matter — never cancels the wedged pollers. The finding's causal chain holds.

NET: the cap bounds the storm's MAGNITUDE (<=16 pollers, <=~320 Hz) but not its DURATION on a paused buffer, and even the bounded magnitude is precisely the 20+ Hz-timer-vs-InputHook contention the Ollama fix was written to eliminate. The bug is real.

<a id="f-spotlight-blocks-keyboard-thread"></a>
#### `spotlight-blocks-keyboard-thread` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : shortcuts (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/spotlight.ahk:162-177`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/shortcuts/win.ahk:478`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/shortcuts/win.ahk:484`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:514`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:721`
- **Description** : SpotlightMouseAt ends with `loop { Sleep(DISMISS_POLL=100); ... if Elapsed>=DurationMs ... break }`, i.e. it busy-waits up to DurationMs (3000 ms for teleport, 5000 ms for Win+' and the gesture) on the very pseudo-thread that fired the hotkey/gesture. That call is invoked directly from the Win+' hotkey lambda, the Win+T TeleportMouse handler, and two gesture dispatch lambdas — never via SetTimer.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 is cooperatively threaded. The Win+' / Win+T hotkey thread (and the gesture thread) does not return until SpotlightMouseAt returns, which is after the poll loop. Although Sleep is a rest point that pumps messages and lets the low-level keyboard hook deliver other keys, the *physical Win key* that triggered the combo is still logically held for the whole 3–5 s, and the entire current thread stays pinned. Any code path that assumes the shortcut completes promptly (modifier release, subsequent Win combos, gesture re-entry guard) is stalled. On a multi-monitor machine GR_CreateWindow/GR_DrawBitmap also do GDI work synchronously before the loop even starts.
- **Scénario** : User presses Win+' to flash the spotlight, then immediately starts typing or presses another Win combo. For up to 5 s the Win key is still considered down by AHK's combo bookkeeping and the firing thread is pinned in the Sleep loop; the next Win-based shortcut can mis-fire or feel laggy, and if the user lifts/re-presses Win during the loop the modifier state is inconsistent. With the gesture binding the held mouse button is similarly tied up.
- **Impact** : Multi-second perceived lock-up of the Win layer and the gesture engine after every spotlight trigger; degraded typing/shortcut responsiveness during the overlay; modifier-state confusion if Win is released mid-loop.
- **Correctif** : Make the dismiss watcher non-blocking: create the overlay windows, store the hwnds + StartX/StartY + deadline in module-level state, and drive dismissal from a `SetTimer(SpotlightTick, DISMISS_POLL)` that checks elapsed/mouse-move and tears the windows down + shuts GDI+ down when done. Return from SpotlightMouseAt immediately so the hotkey thread ends. Guard the tick timer with `if A_IsSuspended` (stop + dismiss) so pause hides the overlay too.
- **Test** : headless-unit: with the existing _Stub_SpotlightCalls stub, blocking can't be measured. Add a meta-static test asserting lib/spotlight.ahk no longer contains a `Sleep(` call inside a `loop {` (regex over source) AND that a SetTimer-driven dismiss helper exists; plus a headless-unit test that calls a refactored SpotlightArm()/SpotlightTick() pair and asserts SpotlightTick clears the stored hwnds once Elapsed>=DurationMs without ever Sleeping.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Structural claims verified against source. lib/spotlight.ahk:164-170 ends SpotlightMouseAt with `loop { Sleep(DISMISS_POLL=100); Elapsed += 100; MouseGetPos(...); if (Elapsed >= DurationMs or moved>5px) break }`, polling up to DurationMs (3000 ms teleport, 5000 ms Win+' and gesture). All three call sites invoke it SYNCHRONOUSLY on the firing pseudo-thread, never via SetTimer: win.ahk:478 (Win+T TeleportMouse handler), win.ahk:484 (Win+' lambda registered via AddShortcut -> plain Hotkey(), utils.ahk:26-28), and the gesture lambda gestures.ahk:514 / GestureTeleportMouse gestures.ahk:721, both reached from GestureDispatch line 1702 (`try GESTURE_ACTIONS[ActionName].Fn()`), which is itself driven by the plain hotkeys ^#+F1..F10 at gestures.ahk:1726-1735. spotlight.ahk has no Critical, no A_IsSuspended, no SetTimer (grep confirmed). So the firing thread is genuinely pinned for 3-5 s and cannot end until the loop breaks. THE BUG IS REAL.

BUT the finding's framing is overstated and I am downgrading it. Reasoning from the AHK v2 cooperative model: Sleep(100) is a rest point — during it AHK pumps messages and the low-level keyboard hook runs, so OTHER keystrokes are delivered and hotkeys/hotstrings/InputHook callbacks can interrupt. There is NO synchronous WinHTTP/sqlite/ClipWait/file-I/O blocking the message pump inside the loop, so the user's #1 "LowLevelHooksTimeout silently drops the keystroke" mechanism does NOT fire here. The finding itself concedes Sleep pumps messages. The "physical Win key still logically held for 3-5 s" and "modifier-state confusion" claims are speculative and largely wrong: AHK tracks physical modifier state via the hook independently of whether the callback has returned, and the #' combo already fired; a physical Win release during the loop is seen by the hook. The GR_CreateWindow/GR_DrawBitmap GDI work runs synchronously before the first Sleep but is one-time window setup (tens of ms), not the multi-second concern.

The genuinely valid consequences, narrower than claimed: (1) the pinned thread blocks gesture re-entry — while GestureDispatch sits in the spotlight loop, AHK's default #MaxThreadsPerHotkey=1 means a subsequent ^#+Fn gesture is dropped/buffered, so the gesture engine feels locked for 3-5 s; (2) perceived latency of the Win/gesture layer; (3) the overlay cannot be paused — there is no A_IsSuspended guard, so an already-running overlay won't dismiss when the user pauses, brushing against the project invariant "Pause must silence ALL features" (native Suspend disarms the hotkey but not an in-flight callback). This is a real responsiveness + architecture defect that the proposed SetTimer refactor fixes correctly, but it is not a lost-keystroke bug. Severity high -> medium.

<a id="f-sync-winhttp-blocks-keyboard-on-user-check"></a>
#### `sync-winhttp-blocks-keyboard-on-user-check` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : updater-infra (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:380`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:402`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:827`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:1440`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:1526`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:1535`
- **Description** : Updater_FetchLatestJson, Updater_FetchReleasesListJson, the one-click slow path (Updater_OneClickUpdate), Updater_ShowAvailableUpdate's cold path, and the entire Updater_DownloadAndInstall (GET + ADODB.Stream.SaveToFile) all use Req.Open(GET, url, false) + Req.Send() — synchronous WinHttp. The background poller was correctly moved to async, but these user-triggered paths were left synchronous on the rationale 'the user is actively waiting on the click.'
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 is cooperatively single-threaded. A synchronous WinHttpRequest.Send() blocks the pseudo-thread (the menu/hotkey callback) until the response or timeout. While it blocks, the AHK message pump and the low-level keyboard hook cannot run, so Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout silently DROPS keystrokes that arrive during the stall. The per-phase finite timeouts (resolve 5s + connect 15s + send 30s + receive 30s) cap the freeze at ~80s worst case on a flaky network or a silent CDN mid-transfer — but even a 2-5s stall on a captive portal drops the keys typed during it.
- **Scénario** : User clicks 'Vérifier les mises à jour' (or 'Install this version' in the changelog, or the asset download in Updater_DownloadAndInstall) while on hotel/airport Wi-Fi behind a captive portal. The synchronous resolve/connect stalls for seconds; the user keeps typing into their editor; those keystrokes are dropped or arrive reordered after the call returns. For Updater_DownloadAndInstall the asset is multi-MB, so even a healthy connection blocks the keyboard for the full download.
- **Impact** : Dropped/laggy keystrokes during any user-initiated update operation — the user's #1 concern. Worst on slow networks; guaranteed multi-second freeze during the actual binary download.
- **Correctif** : Route the user-initiated check and the asset download through the same WinHTTP-async + SetTimer(WaitForResponse(0)) poll pattern already implemented in _Updater_FetchLatestJsonAsync/_Updater_PollAsync. Show a non-blocking 'Vérification…'/'Téléchargement…' state in the menu/dialog and complete via the async callback. For the download, stream to disk from the async request once WaitForResponse(0) signals completion. This makes every updater network call non-blocking, matching the background path.
- **Test** : headless-unit: in tests/ assert that Updater_FetchLatestJson and Updater_DownloadAndInstall no longer call Open with the synchronous 'false' flag — a meta/static test over the source text (Grep the function bodies for '\.Open("GET", [^,]+, false)') that fails if any user-facing fetch is synchronous. The true behavioral assertion needs live hardware (a stalled-DNS network) so a static guard on the async-only invariant is the durable regression.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code and it matches the finding. Updater_FetchLatestJson (updater.ahk:388) and Updater_DownloadAndInstall (1519) both use Req.Open("GET", url, false) + Req.Send() — synchronous WinHttp. Updater_OneClickUpdate's slow path calls Updater_FetchLatestJson directly on a menu-callback thread (827), Updater_ShowAvailableUpdate calls it on its cold path (1440), Updater_FetchReleasesListJson is synchronous (592) and feeds the changelog path (897), and Updater_DownloadAndInstall is reachable from menu callbacks (816, 862) and the changelog GUI install button (1382). All five user-facing entry points fire on the single AHK pseudo-thread (RegisterMenuItem -> OnMessage callback, e.g. tray_menu.ahk:2596).

AHK concurrency reasoning (why it is real, not a generic-threading false positive): AHK is cooperatively single-threaded and services its WH_KEYBOARD_LL low-level hook on the main thread's message pump. A new thread can only interrupt the running one at a rest point — between lines, during Sleep, or inside specific blocking built-ins. A synchronous COM WinHttpRequest.Send() is NOT a rest point: the resolve and connect phases are pure blocking waits with no AHK message pumping, so while Send() blocks, the message pump is starved and the hook callback cannot run. Windows then enforces LowLevelHooksTimeout (~300ms default): if the hook does not return in time, Windows silently bypasses it and delivers the keystroke unremapped (drop/reorder). So keys typed during the stall are lost. The worst case is exactly the finding's 80s = 5000+15000+30000+30000 (the four UPDATER_HTTP_*_TIMEOUT_MS constants at updater.ahk:51-54). The binary download (Updater_DownloadAndInstall) blocks for the full multi-MB receive even on a healthy network.

The codebase itself confirms the mechanism: comments at updater.ahk:44-50 and the existing regression tests in tests/test_updater.ahk state that a synchronous WinHttp call "blocks the synchronous call — and therefore the AHK main thread, and therefore ALL keyboard remapping." The team already hit this as an unprompted startup freeze and fixed the BACKGROUND path by moving it to async (_Updater_FetchLatestJsonAsync + _Updater_PollAsync, 449-499), while deliberately leaving the USER paths synchronous (explicit rationale at 446-447: "User-initiated paths keep the synchronous fetch ... the user is actively waiting on the click"). So this is a conscious, tested design decision, not an oversight — but it is still wrong with respect to the maintainer's #1 invariant that nothing on the keyboard thread may block while the user types: a bounded-but-synchronous fetch still drops the keys typed into another window during the bounded stall.

Severity adjustment to medium (finding says high): unlike the already-fixed startup freeze, this path is gated behind a deliberate user click that shows a visible "Vérification…/Téléchargement…" wait UI (Updater_OneClickUpdate sets _UpdaterCheckInProgress and rebuilds the menu; Updater_ShowAvailableUpdate pops a "checking" MsgBox). On a healthy network the JSON fetch returns fast; keystroke loss only materializes on a stalled/captive-portal network or during the binary download. That is real and matches the user's top concern, but materially less severe than an unprompted freeze.

Design tension to flag for the fix author: the existing tests _UpdaterTest_FetchLatestJsonHasTimeout and _UpdaterTest_DownloadHasTimeout codify "user paths may be synchronous as long as timeouts are bounded"; only _UpdaterTest_BackgroundTickIsAsync requires async. Making the user paths async (the proposed fix) is a strengthening of the invariant and would require updating those two tests' intent, not just adding a new one.

<a id="f-sqlite-wrapper-no-bounded-blocking-on-main-thread"></a>
#### `sqlite-wrapper-no-bounded-blocking-on-main-thread` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : updater-infra (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/sqlite3.ahk:145`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/sqlite3.ahk:210`
- **Description** : SQLite_Exec and SQLite_Query drive prepare/step/finalize in tight DllCall loops with no progress callback, no interrupt, and no chunked yielding. The keylogger prefetch (KLPF_BuildAndWrite, scheduled off SetTimer) documents that the projection costs ~2-3s and the JSON encode ~1s; that entire cost runs synchronously inside these wrapper loops on the main thread. While the scheduling decision belongs to the keylogger unit, the wrapper provides no mechanism to make long queries cooperative.
- **Cause racine** : The step loop 'Loop { step_rc := sqlite3_step(...); if != ROW break }' and the Query while-loop block the AHK pseudo-thread for the full duration of the query. AHK cannot run the keyboard hook or message pump during a synchronous DllCall sequence, so a multi-second projection on the main thread is a multi-second keyboard freeze (LowLevelHooksTimeout drops keys).
- **Scénario** : The metrics dashboard prefetch timer fires while the user is typing. SQLite_Exec/SQLite_Query churn for 2-3 seconds rebuilding aggregates; during that window the keyboard hook cannot run and keystrokes are dropped or lag. The wrapper has no sqlite3_progress_handler hook or row-budget to break out and let the pump run.
- **Impact** : Potential multi-second keystroke loss whenever a heavy projection runs on the main thread. The wrapper is the chokepoint that could make this cooperative but currently cannot.
- **Correctif** : Expose an optional sqlite3_progress_handler-backed callback (or a row/step budget) in SQLite_Query/SQLite_Exec that lets the caller insert a periodic 'Sleep(-1)' yield so the message pump and keyboard hook can run between batches. Strategically, the keylogger projection should run off-thread (a separate process or a worker) — but at minimum the wrapper should support cooperative yielding so a long query cannot monopolize the keyboard thread.
- **Test** : headless-unit: run SQLite_Query against an in-memory DB with a large generated table and assert that, when a progress/yield callback is supplied, it is invoked at least once during a long scan (proving the wrapper can yield).
