The keystroke-loss behavior itself needs live timing measurement, so flag the cooperative-yield hook as the unit-testable surface.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the full cited file. The factual claims hold: SQLite_Exec drives a tight uninterruptible step loop (sqlite3.ahk:183-188: `Loop { step_rc := DllCall(...sqlite3_step...); if (step_rc != ROW) break }`) and SQLite_Query does the same in its `while (true)` row loop (242-268). Both are pure synchronous DllCall sequences with no progress_handler, no row budget, no yield. I confirmed the wrapper exposes nothing cooperative anywhere in the file.

The AHK concurrency model makes the blocking mechanism real, not hypothetical. AHK v2 is a single cooperative pseudo-thread; rest points only occur between lines / during Sleep / during certain blocking built-ins. A back-to-back DllCall step loop offers no rest point, so the low-level keyboard hook and message pump cannot run for the loop's full duration. PROJECT_MEMORY (project-updater-nonblocking-http, lines 1136/1144) independently confirms the maintainer treats exactly this — a multi-second synchronous call on the main thread — as freezing ALL keyboard remapping (hotkey subs and Send() run on the main thread) and as a critical class of bug. So a 2-3s projection on the main thread is a 2-3s keyboard freeze, and LowLevelHooksTimeout drops keys.

The heavy path genuinely reaches these loops from a SetTimer on the main thread, NOT just from a user click: keystrokes -> InputHook flush (KL_Hook_Tick) and the 5s ingest timer (keylogger.ahk:1285 `KLWV_NotifyIngest()`) and the -2000 deferred full build (keylogger_webview.ahk:482) all call KLPF_BuildAndWrite, which calls SQLite_Query/Exec. AHK timers always run on the main pseudo-thread.

I tried hard to refute this via the project's own gating invariants, and the gating only NARROWS it, does not kill it:
1. KLPF_BuildAndWrite is only ever invoked inside `for which, entry in KLWV.windows` (KLWV_NotifyIngest, webview.ahk:513) AND only when entry["first_paint_done"] is true. With no metrics dashboard open, KLWV.windows is empty, so during ordinary typing zero SQLite work hits the keyboard thread. This is the strongest mitigation.
2. The per-keystroke-burst debounced live-push (KL_Hook_LivePush, hook.ahk:366-372) deliberately uses ONLY the fast "manifest" mode (~20-50ms cache-warm); the comment explicitly states the heavy ~150-300ms "live" rebuild is "left to the 5 s ingest cycle so it never runs on the flush thread." So the worst case is staged off the hottest path.

But the residual exposure is a genuine defect: when the user IS typing with the dashboard open (the dashboard's whole purpose is live tracking while you type), a 5s ingest tick or the -2000 full build runs the ~2-3s projection + ~1s JSON encode synchronously and freezes all remapping for that window, dropping keys. The wrapper is correctly identified as the chokepoint that cannot be made cooperative — the finding even concedes the scheduling decision belongs to the keylogger unit and scopes itself to the wrapper's missing yield surface, which is exactly what I verified is absent. This is not a false positive: the path is not Critical-protected, not serialized away, and not A_IsSuspended-gated in a way that prevents the freeze; A_IsSuspended would not even help since timers bypass Suspend.

<a id="f-logger-sync-double-write-on-warning"></a>
#### `logger-sync-double-write-on-warning` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : core-utils (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/logger.ahk:427-451`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/logger.ahk:188-218`
- **Description** : _LoggerEmit, for any level >= WARNING, calls `_LoggerFlush(true)` (a synchronous FileOpen 'a' → Write → Close on the main log) AND then does a second independent synchronous FileOpen → Write → Close on the errors-only file. So a single LoggerWarn performs two blocking open/close cycles. These run on whatever pseudo-thread emitted the line; warnings fire from the prediction/HTTP completion path (modules/llm/prediction_engine.ahk:926/945 'Variant failed') and other timer/OnMessage callbacks that interrupt the foreground at rest points while the user is typing.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 is cooperatively single-threaded: a SetTimer/HTTP-callback pseudo-thread that fires while the user types runs to completion on the only thread, and a synchronous FileOpen/Close on a log file that AV or OneDrive has briefly locked can block for tens to hundreds of ms. While that blocks, the low-level keyboard hook callback cannot run; Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout can then silently drop the in-flight keystroke. Two open/close cycles per warning doubles the worst-case stall, and a repeated warning (e.g. each failing LLM variant) repeats it.
- **Scénario** : User is typing while a background LLM prediction request is in flight. The request fails or all variants fail (a realistic, repeatable condition with a flaky local Ollama). LoggerWarn fires on the HTTP/timer thread and performs two synchronous file open/close cycles against a log file momentarily locked by antivirus/OneDrive. The keyboard hook is starved for the duration and a keystroke is dropped or input lags/interleaves — exactly the user's #1 concern.
- **Impact** : Intermittent dropped/laggy keystrokes correlated with warning bursts (failing predictions, repeated recoverable errors). Low frequency but directly in the lost-keystroke failure class, and amplified by the double write.
- **Correctif** : Do not force a synchronous flush for WARNING — reserve synchronous, file-handle-closed writes for ERROR only (the level that actually precedes a crash). For WARNING, push to the pending queue and let the 500ms batched _LoggerFlush handle it like INFO. Also collapse the errors-only-file write into the same single forced flush rather than a second independent open/close (e.g. append the errors-file write to the same ForceFlush routine, or only force it for ERROR). This removes the per-warning double blocking write from any thread that can interrupt typing.
- **Test** : headless-unit via the existing test sink: register LoggerSetTestSink, then spy on file writes is hard headlessly, so instead assert the policy statically (meta-static test over logger.ahk source): assert that the synchronous ForceFlush / FileOpen-Write-Close branch in _LoggerEmit is gated on `LOGGER_SEVERITY[Level] >= LOGGER_SEVERITY["ERROR"]` (not WARNING). Fails on the current `>= WARNING` threshold, passes after narrowing to ERROR. Complement with a live test logging an ERROR then asserting the line is on disk immediately (survives the unbuffered path).  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : The cited code is accurate. _LoggerEmit (logger.ahk:427-451) performs TWO synchronous blocking I/O cycles for any level >= WARNING (severity 30): (a) _LoggerFlush(true) does FileOpen("a")->Write->Close on the main log (lines 207-215, invoked at 433-435), and (b) an independent FileOpen->Write->Close on the errors-only file (442-450). One LoggerWarn = two open/close round-trips. The non-WARNING/ERROR path correctly uses the batched 500ms FileAppend via the _LoggerFlush SetTimer.

The interrupting-thread premise holds under the AHK v2 cooperative model. The LLM async path is SetTimer-polled at 50ms: _LLM_Ollama_PollRequest/_LLM_Ollama_PollCurl re-arm via SetTimer (api_ollama.ahk:509, 568) and invoke the on_fail closures () => _LLM_Engine_OnVariantFail(state) (prediction_engine.ahk:824, 834). _LLM_Engine_OnVariantFail->LoggerWarn (926) and _LLM_Engine_FinalizeRequest->LoggerWarn (945) therefore execute on a SetTimer pseudo-thread, which interrupts the foreground at a rest point while the user types. Many more LoggerWarn calls sit directly on that polling path in api_ollama.ahk (372, 388, 502, 532, 559, 583, 612, 907, 955). Crucially, SetTimer callbacks BYPASS A_IsSuspended, and there is no pause/typing guard around these warning emits — so the blocking write can land mid-keystroke. While the only thread is blocked inside FileOpen/Close, the low-level keyboard hook callback cannot run, so Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout can drop the in-flight keystroke. This is squarely in the lost-keystroke failure class.

The blocking-on-lock root cause is not hypothetical: the test suite itself documents it. test_logger.ahk:17-19 redirects logger output to A_Temp specifically because 'the CI antivirus (Windows Defender real-time scan) does not hold a file lock on a path inside the repo checkout and block FileOpen calls from LoggerWarn/LoggerError'. The repo has already been bitten by exactly this lock-blocks-FileOpen behavior. On a healthy local SSD the writes are sub-millisecond, so the worst case requires the rare conjunction of an AV/sync lock window overlapping a keystroke — but that conjunction is precisely what the double write amplifies and what warning bursts (flaky local Ollama, each failing variant) repeat.

Where I push back on the finding: the double-write claim is correct but the typical-case cost is tiny, so this is a worst-case/probabilistic stall, not a deterministic drop. 'medium' is on the generous side for the common case yet defensible given it is the user's #1 concern, the fix is cheap, and warnings genuinely should not carry a crash-survival guarantee (WARNING is recoverable by definition per CLAUDE.md §4.1; only ERROR precedes a crash). So narrowing the forced sync write to ERROR is both correct and removes the hot-path double write from every interrupting thread. This is a real, fixable hot-path-blocking bug, not a false positive.

<a id="f-kl-synchronous-disk-write-on-keystroke"></a>
#### `kl-synchronous-disk-write-on-keystroke` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-lost-keystroke (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:618`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:650`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:273`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:173`
- **Description** : KL_AppendLog ends with a synchronous fh.Write(line . "`n") to today.log. It is reached directly from the keystroke-thread InputHook callbacks: KL_Hook_OnKeyDown -> KL_LogShortcut (fires on every Ctrl+S / Ctrl+C / Ctrl+V etc. via KL_Watchers_DetectShortcut), and KL_Hook_RefreshContext -> KL_LogAppSwitch / KL_LogWindowSwitch -> KL_AppendLog. The design comment in KL_Hook_Tick correctly keeps the heavy data.sql ingest OFF the keystroke path, but the per-event JSONL append to today.log is NOT deferred — it runs inline on the key event.
- **Cause racine** : AHK is single-threaded and cooperative; a blocking built-in (fh.Write hitting NTFS through an antivirus minifilter, or a cold file handle) does not yield to the low-level keyboard hook. If the write stalls longer than the OS LowLevelHooksTimeout (default ~300 ms, registry-tunable), Windows silently removes the hook's chance to process that input and DROPS the keystroke (or delivers it late, reordered relative to a SendInput burst).
- **Scénario** : User is typing fast in an editor and hits Ctrl+S to save while a real-time AV scanner is busy (or today.log lives on a slow/network drive). KL_LogShortcut fires inline and fh.Write blocks for a few hundred ms behind the AV filter; the next physical character the user types during that window is eaten by the hook timeout, producing the classic "I pressed the key and nothing came out" intermittently around save/copy actions or right after switching apps (where KL_FlushBuffer + two KL_AppendLog writes also run inline — see kl-refresh-context-blocks).
- **Impact** : Intermittent dropped keystrokes correlated with shortcut presses, app switches and window-title changes — exactly the user's #1 reported symptom, but rare and machine-dependent (AV/disk-speed dependent), which makes it maddening to reproduce.
- **Correctif** : Make ALL keystroke-thread log writes deferred, mirroring the existing _HSE_QueueFireLog pattern in the prefix watcher: KL_AppendLog should push the entry to Keylogger._pending_entries (it already does) and the actual fh.Write should happen ONLY on a SetTimer drain (the flush/ingest tick), never inline. Shortcut/app-switch/window-switch logging from the InputHook should enqueue an O(1) record and arm a short one-shot drain timer, so the disk touch always lands off the keystroke. At minimum, gate the inline fh.Write behind a check that it is not being called from within an InputHook callback.
- **Test** : headless-unit: install a recording FileObject stub for KL_OpenTodayFh that asserts it is never .Write()-called synchronously within a simulated InputHook callback (set a module flag _KL_InHookCallback := true around a KL_Hook_OnKeyDown(shortcut) drive and assert the write recorder saw zero writes during that window, only a queued entry). meta-static fallback: assert KL_AppendLog's write path is reached only from a *_Tick/*_Drain timer function and that KL_LogShortcut/KL_LogAppSwitch/KL_LogWindowSwitch enqueue rather than write.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : The finding is accurate against the real source. KL_AppendLog ends with a synchronous fh.Write(line . "`n") at keylogger.ahk:650 (today.log is the durable WAL; _pending_entries.Push at :643 is only an in-RAM fast path the ingest tick also drains at :1217). KL_LogShortcut (:751), KL_LogAppSwitch (:732) and KL_LogWindowSwitch (:741) each call KL_AppendLog with no deferral. These are reached directly from the keystroke-path InputHook callbacks: KL_Hook_OnKeyDown -> KL_Watchers_DetectShortcut -> KL_LogShortcut (keylogger_hook.ahk:273-285), and KL_Hook_RefreshContext -> KL_FlushBuffer + KL_LogAppSwitch/KL_LogWindowSwitch (keylogger_hook.ahk:173-182), invoked from both OnChar (:216) and OnKeyDown (:299). So the inline disk write on the keystroke thread is genuinely present.

On the AHK concurrency model: the keylogger subscribes to the shared HookDispatcher InputHook("V L0") (keylogger_hook.ahk:396-399, hook_dispatcher.ahk:284), a *visible/passive* hook. Its OnChar/OnKeyDown are AHK pseudo-threads dispatched from the message queue (Dispatch at hook_dispatcher.ahk:187 has no Critical), not code running inside the WH_KEYBOARD_LL proc, and they do not gate OS key delivery or the consuming layout remap (_RemapEmit, layout.ahk:376, on separate *ScanCode hotkeys). BUT all of these — the WH_KEYBOARD_LL hook proc's queued work, the remap hotkey threads, and the passive InputHook callbacks — are serviced by AHK's single OS thread. If a passive callback blocks for hundreds of ms inside fh.Write behind an AV minifilter or a cold/slow handle, that single thread cannot service the low-level hook in time and Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout (~300 ms default) drops the key from the hook chain — a dropped/late keystroke. This is not a generic-threading false alarm: the project itself asserts this exact mechanism. hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:765-768 deferred KL_LogHotstring (another KL_AppendLog caller) via _HSE_QueueFireLog precisely "so a disk/lookup spike can never stall the fire keystroke and stretch the suppress window into a key-swallow", and KL_Hook_Tick (:346-351) keeps KL_IngestOnce's FileAppend off the keystroke thread for the same stated reason ("would block incoming keystroke callbacks, causing perceptible input lag"). The shortcut/app-switch/window-switch log paths were simply not given the same deferral — a real, consistent gap.

Severity medium is right, not higher: it is a passive-observer stall, so the most common outcome is input lag/late delivery rather than a guaranteed swallow, and it only bites when fh.Write actually exceeds the OS timeout (AV/network/slow-disk dependent), matching the "rare, machine-dependent, maddening to reproduce" framing. It is strictly less acute than the prefix-watcher case (there the stall straddles the active suppress/send window, where a swallow is more direct), which is why I do not raise it to high.

<a id="f-deadkey-inputhook-blocks-thread"></a>
#### `deadkey-inputhook-blocks-thread` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-keystroke-pipeline (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:315-331`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/layout/layout_altgr.ahk:150-176`
- **Description** : DeadKey(Mapping) creates an InputHook("L1", ...) and calls ih.Wait() inside the hotkey thread that fired for the dead-key (^ / ¨ base keys and many AltGr-shifted keys: superscript, greek, currency, R, subscript, diaeresis, circumflex). ih.Wait() has no timeout argument, so it blocks until exactly one matching key is produced.
- **Cause racine** : InputHook.Wait() is a blocking built-in that pumps messages but suspends the current pseudo-thread until the input completes. While DeadKey is waiting, the firing hotkey thread is still 'active' (occupying an A_MaxThreads slot) and the global InDeadKeySequence latch is true. Because there is no timeout, if the next event is one that the EndKeys/length-1 spec never resolves cleanly (or focus moves away so no char ever arrives), the thread can sit blocked; meanwhile any key the user taps is consumed/translated by THIS InputHook rather than reaching the app or the watcher. Holding a live InputHook that intercepts the very next keystroke is precisely the 'two consumers claim the same key' hazard.
- **Scénario** : User presses the circumflex dead key, then gets distracted / clicks into another window before pressing the second key. The driver's DeadKey InputHook is still armed; the next keystroke they type anywhere is swallowed into the dead-key resolution (and InDeadKeySequence stays latched), producing a dropped/mis-mapped character and a stuck modal feel.
- **Impact** : Occasional swallowed keystroke after a dead key when the sequence is abandoned; the dead-key state machine can desync from what the user sees.
- **Correctif** : Add a timeout to the InputHook (e.g. `InputHook("L1 T2", ...)`) and handle the timeout branch by sending the bare dead-key char and clearing InDeadKeySequence, so an abandoned sequence resolves deterministically and releases the captured key stream. Verify InDeadKeySequence is reset in a finally so an exception in SendNewResult cannot leave it latched.
- **Test** : headless-unit: refactor the resolve step into `_DeadKeyResolve(Mapping, PressedKey)` returning the string to send, and Test() the mapping/fallback logic plus that the InDeadKeySequence flag is cleared on every path. meta-static: assert the DeadKey InputHook spec string contains a `T` (timeout) flag so a regression to the no-timeout Wait() fails.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code. layout.ahk:315-331 confirms DeadKey(Mapping) sets the global latch InDeadKeySequence := true, builds InputHook("L1", "{F1}...{Delete}") with NO timeout, calls ih.Start()/ih.Wait(), then resolves Mapping[ih.Input]. layout_altgr.ahk:150-176 and layout.ahk:599-608 / layout_shift_caps.ahk:97-99 confirm DeadKey is bound to the ^/diaeresis base keys and many AltGr-shifted keys.

The finding's PRIMARY framing ("blocks the keyboard thread -> LowLevelHooksTimeout drops keys", category hot-path-blocking) is largely WRONG against the AHK model. InputHook.Wait() is a blocking built-in that PUMPS the message queue, so the low-level keyboard hook keeps being serviced -- it does not freeze input the way Critical + a tight loop would. And capturing/suppressing the very next key is the INTENDED behavior of a dead key (deadkey + letter -> accented letter). So there is no general "dropped keystrokes while waiting" defect.

But the finding's SCENARIO field identifies a REAL defect. With no timeout: if the user presses a dead key then switches focus / gets distracted and never presses a resolving key, (a) the global InputHook stays armed indefinitely and will capture+suppress the user's NEXT keystroke typed anywhere (default non-V InputHook is process-global, not window-scoped), mis-resolving it through the dead-key Mapping (swallowed/mistranslated char), and (b) InDeadKeySequence stays latched true. This is the genuine "abandoned sequence desyncs the state machine / stuck modal feel" bug.

This is corroborated by the project's OWN convention: every sibling capture-next-key InputHook uses a timeout and a Timeout branch -- SpaceTapHold uses InputHook("L1 T3") (space.ahk:51), OneShotShift uses InputHook("L1 T"...) with explicit `if (ihvText.EndReason == "Timeout") return` (one_shot_shift.ahk:27,51-52), enter.ahk mirrors it. DeadKey is the lone outlier with a bare Wait(). The suite never catches it because tests/test_stubs.ahk:444 stubs DeadKey to merely record the mapping, so the live no-timeout Wait() is never exercised.

Secondary real point in proposed_fix: InDeadKeySequence := false sits between Wait() and SendNewResult with no try/finally; SendNewResult -> SendEvent (hotstring_engine.ahk:248-259) can throw, and a throw before line 325 would leave the latch set. The fix should clear the latch in a finally.

Net: bug confirmed, but the dominant mechanism is the missing timeout + latched global hook on an abandoned sequence, NOT hot-path thread-blocking. Severity overstated.

<a id="f-wraptext-sendinstant-sleep-hotpath"></a>
#### `wraptext-sendinstant-sleep-hotpath` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-send-clipboard (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:461-481`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:692-707`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:277-294`
- **Description** : WrapTextIfSelected (bound to symbol keys like %, #) and the prefix-watcher UIA-wrap branch call SendInstant(Left . Selection . Right). SendInstant does ClipboardAll() snapshot, A_Clipboard := Text, SendInput('^v'), then Sleep(200) (SEND_INSTANT_PASTE_DELAY_MS), then restore. The Sleep(200) runs on the keyboard thread synchronously every time the user wraps a selection. GetUIASelection() additionally makes a synchronous UIA COM call (UIA.GetFocusedElement / GetPattern('Text') / GetSelection) on the keyboard thread, which can stall for tens-to-hundreds of ms in some apps before the wrap even starts.
- **Cause racine** : Sleep(200) and synchronous UIA ComCall both block the active pseudo-thread; while blocked the low-level keyboard hook is not serviced, so fast follow-up keystrokes can be dropped (LowLevelHooksTimeout) or arrive after the paste, reordered relative to the wrapped output.
- **Scénario** : User selects a word, presses % to wrap it in %...%, and immediately starts typing the next characters. The 200 ms paste-settle Sleep plus a slow UIA query stalls the hook; the first key(s) of the next word are dropped or land after the pasted wrapper. In a UIA-heavy app (some Electron/Java apps) the GetUIASelection call alone can lag perceptibly.
- **Impact** : Occasional dropped/reordered keystrokes right after a selection-wrap, and a perceptible input hitch. Lower severity than TextSend because the user just issued a deliberate wrap action, but still on the hot path.
- **Correctif** : Keep the clipboard write synchronous but defer the restore via a negative SetTimer (as TextSend already does) instead of an inline Sleep, so the keyboard thread returns immediately after the paste. For UIA, cap/timebox the GetUIASelection call and/or move it behind the existing 100 ms active-app cache so it cannot stall the hook. Document that no Critical may be held across SendInstant (the prefix watcher already releases Critical before this branch — good).
- **Test** : headless-unit: assert via _SendHook that SendInstant's restore is scheduled (no inline Sleep recorded) after the fix, or a meta-static test asserting SendInstant uses a deferred timer rather than Sleep. For the UIA stall, a meta test asserting GetUIASelection is wrapped by the active-app cache / a timeout guard.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read all three cited sites and the dispatch chain. The finding holds.

DISPATCH PATH (verified): SC138 & SCxxx hotkey -> AltGrShiftDispatch (layout_altgr.ahk:252-276) -> Cb() (line 275) -> WrapTextIfSelected (layout.ahk:461) -> SendInstant (hotstring_engine.ahk:277-294). SendInstant does ClipboardAll() snapshot, A_Clipboard := Text, SendInput("^v"), then inline Sleep(SEND_INSTANT_PASTE_DELAY_MS) where SEND_INSTANT_PASTE_DELAY_MS := 200 (hotstring_engine.ahk:48), then restores in finally. Confirmed exactly as described. The prefix-watcher branch (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:694-704) calls the same SendInstant.

NOT CRITICAL-PROTECTED (the load-bearing point): AltGrShiftDispatch invokes Cb() with NO Critical("On"). This is in direct contrast to the SIBLING base-remap path _RemapEmit (layout.ahk:376-380) which deliberately holds Critical("On") around its SendEvent, with a maintainer comment (layout.ahk:367-375) stating the exact hazard this finding describes: overlapping remap threads "interleave in the single OS input queue and emit out of order (comme -> cmooe)" and "can likewise splice into an in-flight hotstring expansion", and explicitly "There is NO Sleep on this path, so Critical's guarantee holds (a Sleep would yield it)." With A_MaxThreads := 64 (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:111), a follow-up physical key pressed during the 200 ms Sleep fires its own _RemapEmit/SendEvent thread, whose output races the in-flight ^v paste and the clipboard restore. So the one place a Sleep sits on the remap-dispatch hot path is exactly the place Critical is absent.

UIA STALL (verified, uncapped): GetUIASelection (layout.ahk:437-459) makes synchronous COM calls (UIA.GetFocusedElement, GetPattern("Text"), GetSelection, GetText(-1)) on the calling thread with NO timebox and NO active-app cache (it only short-circuits WinActive("Code")). The repo HAS an active_app_cache.ahk with ACTIVE_APP_CACHE_TTL_MS := 100 used elsewhere, so the proposed UIA mitigation has precedent too.

WHERE I PUSHED BACK: the finding's "dropped via LowLevelHooksTimeout during Sleep(200)" is the weaker mechanism — AHK's Sleep pumps messages, so the LL keyboard hook is generally still serviced during the Sleep and keys are usually not dropped by LL timeout in that window. The robust, statically-defensible mechanism is REORDERING/INTERLEAVE (follow-up _RemapEmit output vs the ^v paste, on an uncovered-by-Critical path) plus the uncapped synchronous UIA COM call before the Sleep, which in a UIA-heavy Electron/Java app can stall the thread for hundreds of ms before the hook resumes. The actual keystroke-drop/reorder symptom is timing- and app-dependent, hence needs-live confirmation, but the underlying defect (inline Sleep + synchronous unbounded COM on the remap hot path, no Critical) is real from static reading.

FIX PRECEDENT IS REAL: the finding cites TextSend as the precedent and it checks out — TextSend (text_sender.ahk:80-105) does CB_Write -> SendInput("^v") -> SetTimer(() => CB_Restore(Saved), -TEXT_CLIPBOARD_RESTORE_DELAY_MS) (150 ms) and returns immediately. SendInstant should mirror this: write clipboard, paste, then SetTimer a negative deferred restore instead of Sleep(200), returning the thread to the hook at once.

<a id="f-deferred-menu-critical-file-io"></a>
#### `deferred-menu-critical-file-io` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : boot-main (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2031`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2033`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2043`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_menu.ahk:1733`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_menu.ahk:1735`
- **Description** : BuildTrayMenuDeferred (armed at -MENU_BUILD_DEFER_MS = 16 ms after 'ready') does Critical("On"), then InitSubMenus() + initMenu(), then Critical("Off"). InitSubMenus calls _HS_PreScanPersonal() and iterates categories doing FileExist + ReadTomlFile + _ParseExtTomlSections (which itself ReadTomlFile + regex-parses) on the SHARED hotstrings TOMLs and personal_hotstrings.toml. Those files live under _ConfigDir / ScriptInformation["PersonalTomlPath"] — a path the onboarding wizard explicitly encourages pointing at Dropbox/OneDrive/synced volumes. Holding Critical("On") buffers messages and disables interruption, so the low-level keyboard hook cannot run for the entire duration of this file I/O.
- **Cause racine** : Critical("On") starves the message pump and the LL keyboard hook until Critical("Off"). Synchronous file reads inside that window can stall well beyond the ~157 ms steady-state build estimate if the config dir is on a cold/sleeping/network-backed volume (OneDrive 'Files On-Demand' fetch, spun-down drive), turning a one-time menu build into a multi-second keyboard freeze the moment the user starts typing after launch.
- **Scénario** : User configures _ConfigDir on a OneDrive folder during onboarding (an officially-suggested option). On the next launch they start typing immediately after the tray icon appears; ~16 ms after 'ready' BuildTrayMenuDeferred fires, takes Critical, and blocks while OneDrive hydrates the hotstrings TOMLs on demand — the first second of typing is dropped/laggy.
- **Impact** : Time-to-ready window keystroke loss/lag on cloud-synced config dirs. The Critical was added to fix a half-built-menu paint bug, but the cure introduces a hook-starvation window whose length is unbounded by external storage latency.
- **Correctif** : Do the file I/O OUTSIDE Critical: pre-read/parse all TOMLs into in-memory structures first (no Critical), then take Critical only around the pure Win32 Menu.Add/RegisterMenuItem calls that must be one uninterrupted pass. Alternatively warm the personal/section caches on a normal (non-Critical) deferred tick before BuildTrayMenuDeferred so InitSubMenus hits only caches under Critical. _HS_PreScanPersonal already has a cache flag (_HS_PreScanPersonalCacheLoaded) — populate it off-Critical first.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert that BuildTrayMenuDeferred's body does not call file-reading helpers (FileRead/ReadTomlFile/_ParseExtTomlSections/_HS_PreScanPersonal) between Critical("On") and Critical("Off") — i.e. parse the function text and fail if any I/O token appears inside the Critical span. Complement with a headless timing assertion that _HS_PreScanPersonal is idempotent and a no-op once cached, so the under-Critical call is cache-only.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : The structural facts in the finding are correct, but its worst-case impact is largely refuted by the boot ordering.

VERIFIED MECHANISM (real): BuildTrayMenuDeferred (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2031) does Critical("On") -> InitSubMenus() (2034) + initMenu() (2043) -> Critical("Off") (2046) with no Sleep. Critical disables interruption and buffers messages, so the LL keyboard hook cannot dispatch for the whole span -- this is the documented half-built-menu fix (comment 2026-2030) and is genuinely a hook-starvation window. It is armed at -16ms (MENU_BUILD_DEFER_MS, line 2232) right after _DriverReady:=true (2210), at which point RegisterAllHotstrings(true) (2205) has already registered hotstrings, so the user CAN type and the window lands at a plausible typing moment. InitSubMenus (tray_menu.ahk:1733) calls _HS_PreScanPersonal (1735), which Loop-Files-walks PersonalHotstringsDir and calls _ParseExtTomlSections -> ReadTomlFile (toml_loader.ahk:70 = synchronous FileRead). ReadTomlSectionsOrder (1761) also calls ReadTomlFile. So file-touching code does run under Critical -- the finding is not fabricated.

WHY THE SCENARIO (unbounded multi-second OneDrive freeze) IS REFUTED: The expensive part of cloud storage is "Files On-Demand" CONTENT hydration, not menu Win32 calls. But that content hydration is ALREADY PAID AT BOOT, before the deferred timer is armed: RegisterAllHotstrings(true) (line 2205, before _DriverReady:=true at 2210) runs the personal block at modules/hotstrings.ahk:884-905, whose _LoadPersonalExtRecursive walks PersonalHotstringsDir and calls LoadExtTomlFile -> ReadTomlFile (toml_loader.ahk:375) for every personal .toml. That warms _TomlFileCache (keyed by absolute path, never invalidated except on explicit user reload via _HS_InvalidatePersonalCache, which is not called at boot). So when the under-Critical _ParseExtTomlSections later calls ReadTomlFile, it is a CACHE HIT -- no FileRead, no disk, no OneDrive fetch. The finding's premise that the bundled hotstrings TOMLs "live under _ConfigDir" is also wrong: InitSubMenus reads _SharedDir\hotstrings\*.toml, and _SharedDir = _StaticDir\ergopti_plus\shared (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:51) is the INSTALL dir, not the cloud _ConfigDir; those are warmed by boot registration and the new .tsv cache. Only PersonalTomlPath/PersonalHotstringsDir (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:424-425) are under the user-chosen possibly-cloud _ConfigDir.

RESIDUAL REAL RISK (smaller, why not a clean false-positive): _HS_PreScanPersonalCacheLoaded starts false and is NOT warmed before the deferred build, so the FIRST _HS_PreScanPersonal under Critical genuinely re-runs the recursive Loop Files directory enumeration (tray_menu.ahk:1224-1244) plus FileExist checks (1745). That is directory-METADATA I/O against the possibly cloud/spun-down volume executing inside Critical. Content is cached, but on a spun-down physical disk or high-latency network mount, directory enumeration alone can take hundreds of ms, bounded by the (usually small) number of personal TOMLs -- a real but bounded hook stall, not the multi-second content-hydration freeze the finding claims. So the design does violate the good practice "do I/O outside Critical," but the catastrophic impact is overstated. I cannot fully bound the metadata-enumeration latency from static reading alone, hence uncertain rather than confirmed/false-positive.

<a id="f-error-handler-heavy-diagnostics"></a>
#### `error-handler-heavy-diagnostics` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : boot-main (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:89`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/crash_reporter.ahk:97`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/crash_reporter.ahk:140`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/crash_reporter.ahk:341`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/healthcheck.ahk:500`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/healthcheck.ahk:551`
- **Description** : ErgoptiGlobalErrorHandler → CrashReport_Build calls HealthCheck_Run() (line 140) AND _CrashReport_SysInfo() (line 97); CrashReport_Build also calls HealthCheck_Run() a second time at line 100. Each HealthCheck_Run / SysInfo does a WMI ComObject('WbemScripting.SWbemLocator').ConnectServer() + ExecQuery for CPU info and a synchronous RunWait(A_ComSpec ' /c git ... rev-parse') for the git hash. So a single caught error spawns up to ~3 WMI connections and ~3 cmd.exe+git RunWaits synchronously on the main thread before the user even sees the error dialog.
- **Cause racine** : Synchronous WMI and RunWait(cmd/git) block the AHK main thread. They run inside the OnError handler, which fires at an arbitrary point — potentially mid-keystroke — and the handler has just force-released modifiers because the keyboard may be in a bad state. Piling multi-hundred-ms diagnostics on top extends the window during which input is unresponsive.
- **Scénario** : A callback throws while the user is typing; the error handler force-releases modifiers, then spends several hundred ms collecting WMI/git diagnostics (longer if git is on PATH but the repo/exe dir is slow, or WMI is degraded) before showing the MsgBox — input is dead for that whole stretch, on top of whatever caused the error.
- **Impact** : Extended input-dead window after any uncaught error; redundant work (HealthCheck_Run invoked twice, SysInfo collected by both healthcheck and crash_reporter). Low severity because errors are rare and the keyboard is already degraded, but it makes a bad moment worse and noticeably slow.
- **Correctif** : Collect the heavy diagnostics (WMI CPU, git hash) ONCE and cache them at boot (they don't change during a session), so the error handler reuses cached values instead of re-querying. Call HealthCheck_Run() at most once per crash and pass the result into CrashReport_Build. Consider dropping the git RunWait entirely in compiled mode (no repo) and giving every RunWait a finite path / timeout.
- **Test** : headless-unit: refactor SysInfo collection behind a cached accessor and assert the WMI/git collection function is invoked exactly once across a CrashReport_Build call (use a counter spy / injectable collector). Assert CrashReport_Build calls HealthCheck_Run at most once.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-textsend-clipwait-blocks-input-thread"></a>
#### `textsend-clipwait-blocks-input-thread` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : input-dispatch (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:95`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:96`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:108`
- **Description** : For payloads > TEXT_CLIPBOARD_THRESHOLD (1000) or explicit mode="clipboard", TextSend does CB_Write then `ClipWait(1)` then SendInput("^v"). ClipWait blocks the current pseudo-thread for up to 1 second. TextSend is invoked from user-input-driven threads (LLM accept on Tab via LLM_Bridge_OnAccept, inline auto-type). While the thread blocks in ClipWait, the low-level keyboard hook and message pump are starved. Additionally Callback() is invoked immediately after arming the deferred restore timer — i.e. before the paste is actually processed by the target app — so 'completion' is reported prematurely.
- **Cause racine** : ClipWait is a blocking built-in; running it on a thread that is gating input means physical keys typed during that window can hit LowLevelHooksTimeout and be dropped or lag. The clipboard restore is also a fixed 150ms SetTimer, decoupled from the actual paste completion that the callback claims.
- **Scénario** : User accepts a very long LLM prediction (>1000 chars) or any mode=clipboard send into a slow/unfocused target; the clipboard does not settle, ClipWait blocks ~1s, and keys the user types in that second can be dropped or arrive reordered after the burst.
- **Impact** : Rare but real keystroke-loss / lag window tied to large clipboard sends; premature completion callback can mislead callers that sequence work after 'done'. Severity bounded because LLM predictions are usually short (direct path), so this bites only large/explicit clipboard sends.
- **Correctif** : Reduce the ClipWait timeout to a small value (e.g. ClipWait(0.2)) and proceed/log on timeout rather than blocking a full second; or move the clipboard write+paste fully onto a SetTimer(-0) so it never runs synchronously on the input-gating thread. Fire Callback only after the paste has been issued, and document that completion is best-effort for the async clipboard path.
- **Test** : headless-unit with CB_* and _AHK_SendInput stubbed: call TextSend with mode="clipboard" and assert the ClipWait timeout constant used is small (meta/static grep of the source for the literal), and that Callback is invoked. A true blocking-window assertion needs live hardware; flag the timing assertion as static.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited source (adapters/text_sender.ahk:80-109) and the call graph. The code matches the finding line-for-line: ClipWait(1) at :95, SendInput("^v") at :96, and Callback() at :108 fired immediately after arming the -150ms CB_Restore timer.

Reachability on an input thread is REAL. TextSend's clipboard branch is reached two ways: (a) via the physical Tab hotkey -> LLM_Tooltip_FireTabOrAccept -> LLM_Tooltip_TryAcceptTab (ui/tooltip_llm.ahk:179) -> LLM_Bridge_OnAccept (llm_bridge.ahk:489) -> TextSend; a hotkey is an input-driven pseudo-thread. (b) via inline autotype LLM_Engine_OnResults (prediction_engine.ahk:1125), which runs on SetTimer/async-completion threads (e.g. _LLM_Ollama_StreamPoll re-armed via SetTimer at api_ollama.ahk:927). So a blocking call here can defer processing of subsequent input threads.

But the finding's central premise -- "ClipWait blocks ~1s" as the normal case -- is WRONG against the actual code. CB_Write (clipboard.ahk:51-58) does a SYNCHRONOUS `A_Clipboard := Text`; on success the clipboard already holds the text BEFORE ClipWait(1) runs. ClipWait returns the instant the clipboard contains data, i.e. ~microseconds in the common path. The full ~1s only occurs in the degenerate edge where CB_Write's try/catch silently swallowed a failure (locked/restricted clipboard) and left it empty -- and in that edge the subsequent ^v pastes nothing anyway.

No Critical is held anywhere in the chain (neither TextSend nor LLM_Bridge_OnAccept/FireTabOrAccept set Critical "On"), so there is no message-buffering hook starvation. AHK's low-level keyboard hook runs in its OWN thread and keeps collecting physical keys during ClipWait; the slow work sits on the SCRIPT thread, not inside the hook callback, so LowLevelHooksTimeout (which bounds the hook callback's own runtime) is not the mechanism here. The true symptom is deferred processing / lag / possible interleave on the rare blocking edge -- not the guaranteed keystroke DROP the finding asserts.

The premature-callback claim is technically true but currently dead code: both real callers pass Callback=0 (llm_bridge.ahk:489, prediction_engine.ahk:1125), so no caller sequences work after "done". And the clipboard branch only triggers for accepted text > TEXT_CLIPBOARD_THRESHOLD (1000) while single predictions are budgeted at ~150-256 tokens (~600-1024 chars), so it is an uncommon edge -- consistent with the finding's own "severity bounded" note.

Verdict is uncertain rather than confirmed/false-positive: a real but narrow latency smell exists (full-1s block possible only on the clipboard-failure edge; callback fires before paste), but the finding materially overstates both the blocking window and the drop mechanism, and the premature-callback issue has no live consumer. I cannot prove the lag is user-perceptible from static reading -- it requires live timing -- but I can prove the "normal-case 1s block" framing is incorrect.

<a id="f-tooltip-hide-inside-critical-finally"></a>
#### `tooltip-hide-inside-critical-finally` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : hotstrings-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1373-1389`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:971-986`
- **Description** : HSE_DispatchMatch's finally block calls _ResetPrefixBuffer(true) → TooltipHide("ResetBuf", true), and on the no-end-char path _OnPrefixChar also schedules renders. When dispatched from _OnPrefixChar, the OUTER Critical("On") set at line 723 is still in effect during this finally (HSE_DispatchMatch restores only its own inner Critical around the atomic SendInput). TooltipHide destroys/hides layered GUIs and updates DWM state. The atomic-send meta-test pins Critical around the SendInput, but nothing pins that the post-send GUI teardown stays cheap and non-blocking.
- **Cause racine** : Holding Critical across a GUI operation that pumps the message loop or blocks (DWM, layered-window hide, a future heavier teardown) starves the low-level keyboard hook for that duration. Today TooltipHide is believed not to Sleep, but the invariant is undocumented and unguarded — a future change to TooltipHide (e.g. adding an animation/await) would silently reintroduce keystroke loss under the held Critical.
- **Scénario** : A maintainer later adds a fade-out or a WinWait/SetTimer-driven animation to TooltipHide. Because TooltipHide is called from HSE_DispatchMatch's finally while _OnPrefixChar's Critical is held, every hotstring fire now holds Critical across that animation, freezing the keyboard hook for its duration and dropping keys typed immediately after an expansion — with no test catching the regression.
- **Impact** : No bug today, but a latent trap: the keystroke-loss guarantee depends on TooltipHide never blocking, which is neither documented at the call site nor tested. Low severity because current TooltipHide is synchronous and fast.
- **Correctif** : Either move the tooltip hide out of the Critical region (defer it via the same SetTimer that releases suppression, accepting a 1-frame later hide) OR add an explicit invariant comment + a meta/static assertion that TooltipHide contains no Sleep/WinWait/blocking-await, mirroring the existing input-serialization meta-test that pins the Notepad branch releasing Critical before its Sleep.
- **Test** : Meta/static: assert the body of TooltipHide in lib/tooltip.ahk contains no Sleep(/WinWait(/ClipWait(/MsgSleep token (so a future blocking call in the teardown is rejected), and document the 'TooltipHide must stay non-blocking because it is called under Critical from the fire path' invariant next to the assertion.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-send-instant-sleep-clipboard-on-keyboard-thread"></a>
#### `send-instant-sleep-clipboard-on-keyboard-thread` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : layout (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:474`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:277`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:289`
- **Description** : When a selection IS found, WrapTextIfSelected calls SendInstant(LeftSymbol Selection RightSymbol). SendInstant saves ClipboardAll(), overwrites A_Clipboard, SendInput("^v"), then Sleep(SEND_INSTANT_PASTE_DELAY_MS = 200), then restores the clipboard. All of this runs on the keyboard hotkey thread, right after the (already slow) UIA query.
- **Cause racine** : ClipboardAll()/A_Clipboard assignments are synchronous, and the explicit Sleep(200) yields the thread but holds the wrap callback open for at least 200 ms; SendInput("^v") also blocks other input during the send. Because the Sleep yields, a second remap thread can start, mutate A_Clipboard, or fire its own SendInstant in the gap, racing the not-yet-restored clipboard (the OldClipboard restore happens only after the 200 ms).
- **Scénario** : User selects a word and presses AltGr+`(` to wrap it in parentheses, then immediately presses another key. During the 200 ms paste settle, the second key's handler runs, and if it also touches the clipboard (another wrap, or a hotstring SendInstant), the two clipboard dances interleave and either the wrong text is pasted or the user's original clipboard is not correctly restored.
- **Impact** : Occasional wrong-paste / clipboard-corruption and a 200 ms+ stall per wrap-with-selection; combined with the UIA latency above, a single wrap keypress can hold the thread for several hundred ms.
- **Correctif** : Serialise clipboard-using sends (a process-wide reentrancy guard / Critical-bracketed clipboard section that still yields for the paste but blocks a second clipboard dance from starting), and/or replace the fixed 200 ms Sleep with a ClipWait-style confirmation bounded by a small timeout. Prefer sending the wrapped text via SendEvent("{Text}") when the payload is small enough that the clipboard route is unnecessary (the clipboard route is only needed for large payloads per the module header).
- **Test** : headless-unit via the existing _SendHook indirection: the engine already routes SendInstant through Hook when _SendHook is set (hotstring_engine.ahk:280). Add a test that sets _SendHook, fires two overlapping SendInstant calls, and asserts the clipboard save/restore is reentrancy-guarded (e.g. the second call observes the guard and defers, or both restores leave A_Clipboard equal to the original).  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read the real code at all three cited sites plus every other SendInstant caller. The finding is part-real, part-wrong; the wrong parts are load-bearing, so I cannot confirm it as stated.

WHAT IS REAL: SendInstant (hotstring_engine.ahk:277-294) does save ClipboardAll(), set A_Clipboard, SendInput("^v"), then Sleep(SEND_INSTANT_PASTE_DELAY_MS=200), restoring in finally. WrapTextIfSelected (layout.ahk:461-481) calls it on a hotkey thread with NO Critical. A_MaxThreads:=64 (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:111) and #MaxThreadsPerHotkey defaults to 1 (blocks only the SAME hotkey), so during the 200ms Sleep a buffered physical key CAN start a different clipboard-touching handler: another AltGr-symbol WrapTextIfSelected, a Win+U/W case-conversion (win.ahk:417-438, gestures.ahk:724-741, all SendInstant), or the InputHook OnChar wrap (prefix_watcher.ahk:701-704). None share a process-wide reentrancy guard. So an unguarded interleave window genuinely exists. That much stands.

WHAT IS WRONG (refuted): (1) "a second remap thread can mutate A_Clipboard" — FALSE. The only per-key remap is _RemapEmit (layout.ahk:376-380); it does SendEvent and never touches the clipboard, and it is itself Critical("On"). It cannot corrupt the clipboard. (2) "the user's original clipboard is not correctly restored" — largely FALSE. Every SendInstant is try/finally-balanced and nests LIFO: if A is sleeping (A_Clipboard=TextA) and B interrupts, B saves TextA, pastes TextB, restores TextA; then A restores the true Orig. The outermost restore wins, so the user's original survives. The realistic harm is a mis-timed/wrong PASTE and a cumulative stall (nested 200ms sleeps), not a permanently corrupted clipboard. The 200ms is documented as deliberately tuned (engine.ahk:45-48) for slow paste targets.

WHAT IS BROKEN IN THE PROPOSED TEST: SendInstant returns through _SendHook at lines 280-284 BEFORE the clipboard/Sleep block. With _SendHook installed (the harness seam), lines 285-293 — exactly the clipboard save/restore the test wants to assert on — never run. So "set _SendHook, fire two overlapping SendInstant, assert the save/restore is reentrancy-guarded" exercises nothing real. The harness is also synchronous/single-threaded (test_input_serialization.ahk:25 states concurrency cannot be reproduced headless).

The maintainers are demonstrably aware of this exact bug class: HSE_DispatchMatch and _OnPrefixChar deliberately keep the SendInstant/Sleep paths OUT of Critical (engine_main.ahk:1302-1313, prefix_watcher.ahk:713-723), and a meta-test pins it. The wrap-via-clipboard path simply was not given a cross-handler reentrancy guard. The interleave is real but rare and self-healing for the clipboard contents; the worst realistic outcome is an occasional wrong paste under fast overlapping wrap/case-convert presses.

<a id="f-activate-hotstrings-sleep-on-keyboard-thread"></a>
#### `activate-hotstrings-sleep-on-keyboard-thread` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : layout (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/layout/layout_shift_caps.ahk:80`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:297`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:300`
- **Description** : SHIFT_SYMBOLS binds Shift+`:` `;` `!` `?`, Shift+euro and Shift+`%` to closures that call ActivateHotstrings() before emitting the nbsp-prefixed symbol. ActivateHotstrings does SendNewResult(" ") then Sleep(ACTIVATE_HOTSTRINGS_DELAY_MS = 50) then SendNewResult("{BackSpace}"), all on the keyboard hotkey thread (the Sleep is only skipped under the test _SendHook).
- **Cause racine** : The 50 ms Sleep is needed to let the hotstring engine see the injected space, but it parks the dispatch thread for 50 ms on every one of these very common French-typography keys. The Sleep yields, so a fast follow-up keystroke can interleave with the space/backspace pair, and a long enough chain of such keys serialises 50 ms stalls.
- **Scénario** : User types a sentence ending in « phrase ? » quickly: Shift+`?` triggers a 50 ms space/backspace dance; if the user is already pressing the next word's first letter, that letter can land between the injected space and the backspace, or be perceived as lag/stutter on the punctuation key.
- **Impact** : Perceptible per-key latency and occasional interleave on the most common French punctuation under Shift. Lower severity than the UIA path because the delay is bounded and small, but it is on a hot, default key set.
- **Correctif** : Avoid the synchronous space/backspace+Sleep poke for layout punctuation: have the hotstring engine commit the pending buffer via a direct API call (a 'flush pending hotstring now' function) instead of the Sleep-based space dance, or reduce/parameterise the delay. If the dance is unavoidable, gate it so it only runs when there is actually a pending hotstring abbreviation in the buffer (cheap to check) rather than on every Shift-punctuation press.
- **Test** : headless-unit via _SendHook: assert that for the SHIFT_SYMBOLS punctuation entries the engine commits the pending buffer without a blocking Sleep on the dispatch thread (e.g. assert a 'flush' API is invoked rather than a literal space+backspace pair), or measure that the dispatch path issues no Sleep when no abbreviation is pending.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : The cited code is accurate. lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:297-303 ActivateHotstrings() does SendNewResult(" ") -> Sleep(ACTIVATE_HOTSTRINGS_DELAY_MS=50, skipped only under _SendHook) -> SendNewResult("{BackSpace}", False). lib/layout/layout_shift_caps.ahk:80-87 binds Shift+euro/%/:/!/;/? to closures that run ActivateHotstrings() before emitting the nbsp-prefixed symbol. These are common French-typography keys.

Why the Sleep exists (verified against the real engine, not the finding's text): hotstring matching is now driven by the prefix watcher's InputHook (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk _OnPrefixChar -> HSE_FeedChar). End-char-gated triggers (the bulk of autocorrect/expansion) only fire when the InputHook OBSERVES a word terminator. ActivateHotstrings injects a space so the hook feeds " " to HSE (committing any pending trigger), then deletes it. The injected space reaches the InputHook only when the thread yields the OS message loop -- i.e. during the Sleep. So the 50 ms is load-bearing for THIS design: remove it naively and the {BackSpace} is sent before the space is delivered to the hook, scrambling order. The finding's root_cause is correct.

AHK concurrency assessment of harm:
1. Hook-starvation / dropped key (mechanism #1): FALSE here. ActivateHotstrings is NOT inside Critical -- the Shift symbol hotkeys bind LayerDispatch.Bind(...), and LayerDispatch (layout_shift_caps.ahk:120-129) never calls Critical("On"); only _RemapEmit (modules/layout.ahk:376-380) does, and these punctuation keys do not route through _RemapEmit. So the Sleep YIELDS and does not starve the low-level keyboard hook. No LowLevelHooksTimeout drop. The finding does not claim hook starvation, correctly.
2. Interleave (mechanisms #2/#4): REAL but bounded. Because the thread is not Critical and Sleep(50) yields, a sub-50ms follow-up physical key starts its own _RemapEmit thread (SendEvent) during the gap between the injected space and the backspace. tests/meta/test_input_serialization.ahk exists precisely to pin _RemapEmit, _OnPrefixChar and HSE_DispatchMatch as Critical to forbid exactly this reorder/interleave class -- ActivateHotstrings is an unguarded gap in that scheme. The user's letter is not consumed (it still reaches the app via its own SendEvent; the backspace only removes the injected space), so this is perceptible lag/stutter and possible transient visual reordering, not a permanently lost keystroke.

Decisive corroboration that the fix is real and already solved in-repo: modules/tap_holds/space.ahk:97-116 (_SpaceTap) is the canonical pattern -- it calls HSE_FeedChar(" ") DIRECTLY (synchronous, in-process), and on a returned match calls HSE_DispatchMatch. Zero injected space, zero backspace, zero Sleep. The Shift-punctuation closures can do the same. (HSE_FindMatchAtEnd already handles NNBSP/NBSP + :/; typographic end-chars natively via its IsTypoEndChar path, so the layout's own SendNewResult(NNBSP+sym) is even partly self-committing.) This makes the proposed_fix concrete and low-risk.

<a id="f-simulateactivity-timer-sleep-blocks-and-leaks"></a>
#### `simulateactivity-timer-sleep-blocks-and-leaks` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : gestures (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:654`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:664`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:666`
- **Description** : SetTimer(GestureSimulateActivity, Random(1000,5000)) creates a RECURRING timer (positive period). Each firing runs 'loop Random(3,8) { MCSetPos(...); Sleep(Random(200,800)) }' — up to 8 iterations of up to 800ms = ~6.4s of Sleep held inside a single timer pseudo-thread, then SendFinalResult. On toggle-off the flag is set False but SetTimer(GestureSimulateActivity, 0) is never called, so the timer keeps re-firing forever (each firing cheaply early-returns, but it is a leaked periodic wakeup).
- **Cause racine** : A SetTimer callback that performs long Sleeps monopolises the single AHK pseudo-thread for seconds. During that window the message pump / lower-priority threads are deferred; the keyboard low-level hook still runs (separate OS thread) but any AHK-side processing the hook needs (InputHook OnKeyDown, hotstring engine continuation) is starved until the Sleep loop yields. Combined with the missing SetTimer(...,0) disable, the periodic firing also leaks.
- **Scénario** : User enables the gesture's activity simulation, then starts typing. A jiggle tick fires and begins its Sleep loop; for up to several seconds, AHK-side keystroke post-processing (hotstring expansion, tap-hold resolution) is delayed because the timer thread is sleeping, producing laggy/bursty text. After disabling, the timer keeps waking the process indefinitely.
- **Impact** : Multi-second input-processing stalls while the feature is on, and a permanently leaked periodic timer after it is turned off. Lower severity than the click-hold issue because the user opted into a deliberately-disruptive mode, but the leak persists silently.
- **Correctif** : Superseded by the activitysim-global-collision fix (delete this function and delegate to win.ahk, which already uses a self-rescheduling single tick without a 6s Sleep loop). If kept standalone: replace the Sleep loop with a single MCSetPos per tick, re-arm via SetTimer(GestureSimulateActivity, -Random(...)) (negative = one-shot, self-rescheduled), and call SetTimer(GestureSimulateActivity, 0) in the toggle-off branch.
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub SetTimer to record (callback, period) pairs and MCSetPos/Sleep to no-ops. Call GestureToggleActivitySimulation() to enable then again to disable; assert the disable path issues SetTimer(GestureSimulateActivity, 0) (period 0). Assert no single timer callback contains a Sleep > a small bound (meta/static grep that GestureSimulateActivity has no 'Sleep(' once refactored).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code. The three factual claims hold:
- gestures.ahk:654 `SetTimer(GestureSimulateActivity, Random(1000, 5000))` — positive period => RECURRING timer. Confirmed.
- gestures.ahk:664-667 `loop Random(3, 8) { MCSetPos(...); Sleep(Random(200, 800)) }` — up to 8 x 800ms = ~6.4s of Sleep per tick. Confirmed (MCSetPos itself is a non-blocking `DllCall("SetCursorPos")`; the time is all in Sleep).
- gestures.ahk:650-655 `GestureToggleActivitySimulation` only flips the flag; there is NO `else`/toggle-off branch and NO `SetTimer(GestureSimulateActivity, 0)`. After toggle-off the periodic timer keeps firing forever, each firing early-returning at lines 659-660. Confirmed leaked periodic wakeup.

Reachability is real, not dead code: GESTURE_ACTIONS["activity_simulation"].Fn (line 496) -> GestureToggleActivitySimulation(), invoked by GestureDispatch (line 1702) from a touchpad-gesture hotkey thread, and also via the keyboard-shortcut picker (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1751). It also collides with a SECOND, better implementation of the same feature in modules/shortcuts/win.ahk (StartActivitySimulation/SimulateActivity) that shares the same global `ActivitySimulation` and is the self-rescheduling, properly-disarmed version. So the gestures.ahk copy is a redundant inferior duplicate.

CRITICAL correction to the finding's root_cause: the AHK concurrency reasoning is overstated. There is NO `Critical "On"` in GestureSimulateActivity. AHK v2 `Sleep` yields to the message pump, so during every `Sleep(Random(200,800))` other pseudo-threads (hotkeys, the InputHook OnKeyDown/OnChar, the hotstring-engine continuation) CAN and DO interrupt the sleeping timer thread; the low-level keyboard hook runs on its own OS thread regardless. Therefore the dramatic "multi-second input-processing stalls / laggy, bursty text / hotstring + tap-hold starvation" scenario does NOT hold — keystroke post-processing is not blocked for seconds. The only true thread effect is that the tick itself doesn't reach SendFinalResult("{VKFF}") until ~6.4s and AHK won't re-enter the same timer while it's mid-Sleep, so periodic jitter ticks merely coalesce — no lost keystrokes.

What remains genuinely valid: (1) the leaked periodic timer (never disarmed -> permanent wakeups + CPU/battery drain, and it can never be cleanly handed to the good win.ahk path because both fight over the same `ActivitySimulation` global), and (2) holding a process pseudo-thread busy/asleep for several seconds per tick is sloppy and pointlessly delays the feature's own activity signal. The A_IsSuspended guard at line 662 does correctly honor the pause invariant. The proposed fix is correct.

<a id="f-tooltip-teardown-on-keyboard-thread"></a>
#### `tooltip-teardown-on-keyboard-thread` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : llm-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:252-285`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/tooltip.ahk:457-543`
- **Description** : LLM_Bridge_OnChar (called from the PrefixWatcher InputHook OnChar for every printable key) calls LLM_Tooltip_Hide(true) when a prediction is shown and the grace window has elapsed. LLM_Tooltip_Hide -> LLM_TooltipHide -> TooltipHide performs GR_Hide, BeginDeferWindowPos/DeferWindowPos-per-row/EndDeferWindowPos and Gui Destroy() — synchronous Win32/DWM calls — on the keyboard hook thread while the user is typing.
- **Cause racine** : The dismiss path is invoked inline from the InputHook callback, not deferred to a timer. Although the LLM feed in _OnPrefixChar runs before Critical("On") (so it does not starve the hook via Critical), DWM window operations and Gui destruction still execute synchronously on the same thread that must return promptly for the next physical key. Under DWM contention or a slow compositor these calls can take long enough that Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout drops the in-flight keystroke or the next one lags.
- **Scénario** : User is typing quickly while a multi-slot prediction tooltip (several row Guis + border layered window) is on screen. The keystroke that dismisses it triggers a multi-window DeferWindowPos batch + Destroy on the keyboard thread; on a busy machine this adds perceptible latency or, worst case, a dropped character right after a suggestion is dismissed.
- **Impact** : Occasional keystroke lag or loss exactly in the common 'type past a suggestion' flow — the user's #1 concern. Bounded because the work is bounded (a handful of windows) and not under Critical.
- **Correctif** : Defer the hide off the hook: in OnChar, set the visibility flags to hidden immediately (so logic is consistent) but schedule the actual window teardown via SetTimer(..., -1) so the GDI/DWM work runs on a fresh thread after the hook returns. Mirror what the auto-hide TimerFn already does (it runs off-thread). Keep the buffer update + LLM_Engine_OnKeystroke synchronous (cheap), only defer the expensive window destruction.
- **Test** : headless-unit: instrument TooltipHide to record the call thread/critical state is not held and that it can be invoked via a deferred timer; assert LLM_Bridge_OnChar schedules a deferred hide rather than calling DeferWindowPos inline (verify via a stubbed LLM_Tooltip_Hide counter that fires from a timer, not synchronously within OnChar). Practically this is a meta/static + behavioral test; full latency proof needs live hardware.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : The cited path is real and reads exactly as described. _OnPrefixChar (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:654) is the InputHook OnChar callback (bound at :630 via _OnPrefixCharProfiled). At :674-675 it calls LLM_Bridge_FeedCharIfActive(Char) BEFORE Critical("On") at :723 — so the finding is right that the LLM feed does NOT starve the hook via Critical, but it DOES run synchronously on the OnChar pseudo-thread. From there LLM_Bridge_OnChar (llm_bridge.ahk:252) appends to the buffer, and if a prediction is shown AND past the grace window (:271-276) calls LLM_Tooltip_Hide(true) -> LLM_TooltipHide (tooltip.ahk:1582) -> TooltipHide("LLM", true) (:1593). TooltipHide (:457-571) performs synchronous Win32/DWM teardown: GR_Hide on the layered border (= User32\ShowWindow(SW_HIDE), graphics_renderer.ahk:234), a BeginDeferWindowPos/per-row DeferWindowPos/EndDeferWindowPos batch (:528-543), per-row Gui.Destroy() (:551-553), and a defensive DestroyWindow sweep (= User32\DestroyWindow, :563-567). All synchronous, all on the keyboard pseudo-thread.

Why this is a genuine hazard by the project's OWN standard, not generic threading FUD: AHK is cooperatively single-threaded, so while _OnPrefixChar spends this time tearing down windows, the message pump is not pumping and the next physical key cannot be serviced. The project's HotPath profiler already measured this exact class of work — test_prefix_render_deferred.ahk documents ~60 ms for a full Gui rebuild and ~16 ms per no-match HIDE — and the team deemed it severe enough to defer the heavier RENDER half off the OnChar path behind a regression meta-test. The LLM-dismiss HIDE (~16 ms-class) is the one teardown still executed inline on _OnPrefixChar. So the finding correctly identifies a recognized latency hazard the codebase has elsewhere already engineered away.

Why I downgrade to low rather than medium: the dismiss fires AT MOST ONCE per prediction display — only the first keystroke after the grace window while _LLM_Tooltip_Visible is true; after it the flag is false and subsequent OnChars skip the branch (:266). It is not a per-keystroke recurring cost. ~16 ms is well under the 300 ms default LowLevelHooksTimeout, so an outright DROPPED keystroke is unlikely on a healthy machine — the honest impact is a one-off ~16 ms input-latency/jitter spike on that single dismiss keystroke, degrading to a possible drop only under heavy DWM/compositor contention. The work is also bounded (a handful of windows) and is NOT under Critical. "Occasional keystroke lag, rare drop" => low.

Important correction to the proposed fix: it is wrong as written. It says to defer via SetTimer(..., -1) and to "mirror what the auto-hide TimerFn already does (it runs off-thread)". But the auto-hide TimerFn is ARMED from LLM_TooltipShow (tooltip.ahk:1556), which runs from the engine/HTTP timer (main-thread context), not from OnChar — that is why it fires reliably. tooltip.ahk:79-82 documents the exact gotcha: "one-shot timers armed from an InputHook OnChar thread never fire", which is precisely why the dequeue uses a repeating poll timer registered from the main body. A bare SetTimer((*)=>LLM_Tooltip_Hide(true), -1) armed inside LLM_Bridge_OnChar would be armed from the OnChar pseudo-thread and could never fire — stranding a stale prediction on screen, a visible-ghost regression this file is heavily defended against.

<a id="f-stream-final-flush-sleep-blocks-thread"></a>
#### `stream-final-flush-sleep-blocks-thread` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : llm-api (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:932`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:948`
- **Description** : _LLM_Ollama_StreamPoll's post-exit flush runs `loop 5 { ... Sleep(40) }` (up to 200 ms of blocking) inside a SetTimer callback to wait for the curl redirect to flush its last bytes. While a SetTimer thread is running, the message pump and low-level keyboard hook are starved for the duration of the Sleep chain. This is the exact 'work that blocks the keyboard thread during typing' the unit warns about.
- **Cause racine** : Synchronous Sleep inside a timer callback to paper over a child-process stdout flush lag, rather than re-arming the timer for another non-blocking tick.
- **Scénario** : Currently NOT reachable in production because streaming is hard-disabled for Ollama (prediction_engine.ahk:542) and there is no remote streaming dispatcher — so _LLM_Ollama_StreamPoll never runs from the engine. It WOULD bite the moment streaming is re-enabled (the comments suggest that is a goal): at end-of-stream the user could feel up to 200 ms of input lag / dropped keys per prediction.
- **Impact** : Dormant lost-keystroke / input-lag bug gated behind a currently-off feature flag; harmless today, latent landmine when streaming is turned back on.
- **Correctif** : Replace the in-callback Sleep loop with a re-armed one-shot timer: if acc is still empty and the file is non-empty, schedule another _LLM_Ollama_StreamPoll tick (e.g. -40 ms) instead of Sleeping. This keeps the message pump live between reads.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert _LLM_Ollama_StreamPoll's body contains no `Sleep(` call (regression guard so the blocking flush can't be reintroduced once streaming is re-enabled). Optionally a headless-unit driving the consume/flush logic without the Sleep.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-remote-api-validate-blocks-message-pump"></a>
#### `remote-api-validate-blocks-message-pump` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tray-llm (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/menu_api_entries.ahk:204-212`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_remote.ahk:334-343`
- **Description** : After persisting a new/edited API entry, _LLM_Tray_PromptApiEntry calls LLM_RemoteIsReady(new_entry), which does Http.Open("GET", PingUrl, false) — a SYNCHRONOUS WinHttpRequest with SetTimeouts(2000,2000,2000,2000) — and Http.Send() on the calling (main) pseudo-thread. While this blocks, the AHK message pump and the low-level keyboard hook cannot run.
- **Cause racine** : Synchronous WinHTTP (the async=false flag) on the main thread, exactly the documented 'never do synchronous WinHttp on the main thread' anti-pattern, here reachable from a tray dialog.
- **Scénario** : User adds an API entry with an unreachable or slow BaseUrl. On clicking OK through the last InputBox, the whole script freezes for up to ~2s (DNS + connect + send + receive each capped at 2s, but a hung connect alone burns the full 2s). If the user immediately starts typing into another window during that window, LowLevelHooksTimeout can drop those first keystrokes. Lower severity than a per-keystroke block because it is a one-shot, user-initiated action and the user just dismissed a modal dialog.
- **Impact** : A brief full-script stall (and possible loss of the first keystrokes typed right after) when validating an unreachable API endpoint. Bounded to one stall per save.
- **Correctif** : Use an async WinHTTP probe (Http.Open(..., true) + poll, like LLM_OllamaIsRunning_Async) and surface the success/failure TrayTip from the async callback, so the menu/save path returns immediately and the message pump keeps running. At minimum drop the per-phase timeout (e.g. 1500ms total) to shrink the worst-case stall.
- **Test** : headless-unit: refactor the validation into a function taking an injected http-sender; assert the save path does not call the sender synchronously (i.e. the post-save code path returns before the probe result is known) by having the stub record call order. live: not required.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-textsend-clipboard-blocks-and-races"></a>
#### `textsend-clipboard-blocks-and-races` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : adapters-os (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:90`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:95`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:100`
- **Description** : When TextSend resolves to clipboard mode (payload > TEXT_CLIPBOARD_THRESHOLD = 1000 chars, or Opts.mode = 'clipboard'), it runs on the calling thread: CB_Save() -> CB_Write(Text) -> ClipWait(1) -> SendInput('^v') -> SetTimer restore after 150 ms. TextSend is invoked from LLM_Bridge_OnAccept (llm_bridge.ahk:489) and prediction_engine.ahk:1125, both reached from the tooltip Tab hotkey — i.e. the keyboard thread. ClipWait(1) can block that thread for up to one full second while the user is typing, during which Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout can drop subsequent physical keystrokes.
- **Cause racine** : ClipWait is a blocking wait on the keyboard thread; holding the thread there starves the low-level keyboard hook. Separately, the 150 ms SetTimer restore (line 100) writes the saved clipboard back asynchronously — if the user issues their own copy within that window, or a second TextSend fires, the restore clobbers/loses the in-between clipboard content (a classic save/restore race).
- **Scénario** : User triggers an LLM prediction long enough to exceed 1000 chars (or a profile forces clipboard mode) and presses Tab to accept while continuing to type. The accept path blocks on ClipWait, the next one or two physical keystrokes are dropped by the OS hook timeout, and if the user copied something in the same 150 ms the paste-restore overwrites their clipboard.
- **Impact** : Dropped keystrokes during large/forced-clipboard expansions and occasional clipboard clobber. Bounded in practice because LLM predictions are usually short (direct SendText path, no ClipWait), so the blocking branch is rarely hit — hence medium, not high.
- **Correctif** : Do the clipboard round-trip off the keyboard thread: schedule the CB_Write/ClipWait/^v sequence on a one-shot SetTimer(-1) so the hotkey thread returns immediately; or lower/abort the ClipWait timeout and guard restore with a generation counter so a newer clipboard write is never overwritten by a stale restore. Match the hotstring engine pattern (snapshot via ClipboardAll, restore in finally).
- **Test** : headless-unit: with the _AHK_SendInput/_SendHook seam and a stub CB_Save/CB_Write/CB_Restore + a stub ClipWait, drive TextSend(longText, Map('mode','clipboard'), cb) and assert (a) the heavy work is deferred (e.g. ClipWait/SendInput recorded only after a simulated timer tick, not inline) and (b) a second TextSend before the restore timer does not lose the second payload's saved value.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against source. The clipboard branch in TextSend (text_sender.ahk:90-100) exists exactly as described and is reached from the keyboard thread: LLM_Bridge_OnAccept (llm_bridge.ahk:489) and LLM_Engine_OnResults inline-autotype (prediction_engine.ahk:1125). The accept path fans in from the Tab hotkey (ui/tray_llm/tab_accept.ahk:42), the prefix-watcher InputHook OnKeyDown (lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:925), tap-holds (rctrl.ahk:100, lalt.ahk:127) and gestures (gestures.ahk:209) — all keyboard-thread contexts. No Critical anywhere in modules/llm, so the path is interruptible and runs synchronously on the calling thread. So far the finding holds.

But two of its core claims are overstated, which is why I downgrade rather than confirm:

(1) "ClipWait(1) blocks the thread for up to one full second" is the worst case, not the normal case. CB_Write(Text) runs on the SAME thread immediately before ClipWait(1) and synchronously assigns A_Clipboard := Text (clipboard.ahk:51-58). By the time ClipWait(1) runs, the clipboard already contains data, so ClipWait returns in microseconds-to-ms in the common case; the full-second block only materializes under genuine OS clipboard contention (another app holding the clipboard open). The finding presents the rare contention case as typical. Furthermore the identical synchronous-ClipWait-on-keyboard-thread pattern is already accepted in the codebase: hotstring_engine.ahk:313 GetSelection() does A_Clipboard:=""; SendEvent("^c"); ClipWait(GET_SELECTION_TIMEOUT_SEC) on a hotstring (keyboard) thread. So the blocking hazard is neither novel nor rejected by project convention.

(2) The branch is essentially never hit. Both callers pass Opts=0, so Mode resolves to "auto" (text_sender.ahk:82-88), which only becomes "clipboard" at StrLen(Text) > TEXT_CLIPBOARD_THRESHOLD = 1000. No caller and no profile ever forces mode:"clipboard" (grep confirms the only "clipboard" string uses are gestures screenshots and the spec doc). LLM max_tokens defaults are 150 (Ollama, api_ollama.ahk:333) and 256 (remote, api_remote.ahk:65), i.e. ~600 chars typical and ~1024 chars absolute upper bound. So the clipboard branch is reachable only at the extreme top edge of the remote path and never deterministically. The finding itself concedes this.

The genuinely valid, novel part is the 150 ms async-restore race: line 100 schedules SetTimer(() => CB_Restore(SavedForTimer), -150) with no generation guard, so if the user copies (or a second TextSend fires) within that 150 ms window, the stale pre-paste snapshot clobbers the newer clipboard content. The hotstring engine avoids exactly this by restoring synchronously in a try/finally with a ClipboardAll() binary snapshot (hotstring_engine.ahk:308-318); TextSend's deferred, unguarded restore is the real (if low-probability) defect. The race is doubly gated though: needs the >1000-char branch AND a copy within 150 ms.

Net: a real but very-low-likelihood correctness defect (the restore race) plus an overstated blocking claim. Not a false positive, but medium is too high — the dropped-keystroke scenario is largely theoretical and the path is almost never taken. Adjusted to low.

<a id="f-kl-refresh-context-blocks-on-keystroke"></a>
#### `kl-refresh-context-blocks-on-keystroke` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-lost-keystroke (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:149`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:155`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:173`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:216`
- **Description** : KL_Hook_OnChar and KL_Hook_OnKeyDown call KL_Hook_RefreshContext() inline. Although it is throttled by CONTEXT_TTL_MS (1000 ms), the throttle means that exactly ONE keystroke per second pays the full cost: WinGetTitle("A") + WinGetProcessName("A"), and if the focused app/title changed, KL_FlushBuffer() (which itself calls KL_AppendLog -> synchronous fh.Write) plus KL_LogAppSwitch and KL_LogWindowSwitch (two more synchronous writes). Separately, MF_ShouldFilter() -> MF_RefreshFocus() runs WinGetID/WinGetProcessName/WinGetTitle/WinGetClass on every keystroke, also throttled to 250 ms.
- **Cause racine** : WinGetTitle/WinGetProcessName/WinGetClass send messages to the target window's thread (WM_GETTEXT etc.) and can block when that thread is busy or hung — a known AHK gotcha. On the cooperative single thread, a hung foreground window (e.g. a not-responding app the user just alt-tabbed away from) makes the WinGet* call stall, starving the keyboard hook past LowLevelHooksTimeout and dropping the in-flight keystroke.
