The once-per-second flush+3-writes amplifies the kl-synchronous-disk-write risk at the same instant.
- **Scénario** : User switches focus to an application that is momentarily Not Responding (a common Electron/Office cold-start state) and starts typing. On the first keystroke past the 1 s context window, WinGetTitle/WinGetProcessName block on the hung window's message queue; the keystroke that arrives during the stall is dropped by the hook timeout. The user sees the first character or two after an app switch occasionally vanish.
- **Impact** : Rare but real dropped keystrokes clustered around app switches / focus changes to busy windows — the worst possible moment because that is when the user is starting to type fresh content.
- **Correctif** : Move context refresh off the keystroke thread entirely: run KL_Hook_RefreshContext (and MF_RefreshFocus) from a dedicated low-frequency SetTimer (e.g. 250-500 ms) rather than lazily from inside OnChar/OnKeyDown. The hook callbacks then read the cached Keylogger.session_app/title with zero Win32 cost. Ensure the app/title-switch flush and its KL_AppendLog writes also run on that timer (ties into kl-synchronous-disk-write). Where a synchronous WinGet* is unavoidable, the title/process queries already tolerate failure (wrapped in try) but should also be bounded — prefer DllCall(GetWindowThreadProcessId)+QueryFullProcessImageName style probes that do not pump the target window's message queue.
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub WinGetTitle/WinGetProcessName with a recorder and drive 200 simulated OnChar calls within one second; assert the Win32 probes were invoked at most once (the cache holds) and, critically, assert that during a simulated InputHook callback no WinGet* probe runs at all if the refresh is moved to a timer. meta-static: assert KL_Hook_OnChar/OnKeyDown no longer call KL_Hook_RefreshContext directly (it is armed by a SetTimer) once the fix lands, so a regression that re-inlines the Win32 probe fails.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : The cited code is accurate. KL_Hook_RefreshContext (keylogger_hook.ahk:149-191) calls WinGetTitle("A") (155) and WinGetProcessName("A") (158) inline, throttled by CONTEXT_TTL_MS=1000; on a detected app change it runs KL_FlushBuffer (173) -> KL_AppendLog -> fh.Write (keylogger.ahk:650/710) plus KL_LogAppSwitch/KL_LogWindowSwitch (two more synchronous fh.Write). Both KL_Hook_OnChar (216) and KL_Hook_OnKeyDown (299) call it inline on the keystroke thread, and MF_ShouldFilter() -> MF_RefreshFocus() (metrics_filters.ahk:101-118) additionally runs WinGetID/WinGetProcessName/WinGetTitle/WinGetClass on EVERY keystroke (210, 293), throttled to 250 ms.

The AHK concurrency premise holds. The keylogger InputHook callbacks are passive observers fanned out by HookDispatcher.Dispatch (hook_dispatcher.ahk:187-198); the actual key OUTPUT comes from separate layout remap hotkeys (modules/layout.ahk: SC029::, RemapKey -> Hotkey(...)). Both the InputHook callbacks and the remap hotkeys run on AHK's single cooperative main thread. If the keylogger callback blocks synchronously inside WinGetTitle, AHK's thread is busy, its WH_KEYBOARD_LL hook proc cannot be serviced, and past Windows' LowLevelHooksTimeout the OS drops the in-flight key. No LowLevelHooksTimeout registry override is set by the app. This is NOT Critical-gated away: Dispatch does not wrap callbacks in Critical, and even if it did, Critical does not stop a synchronous blocking Win32 call from holding the thread.

The mechanism is real but the finding over-attributes it. Only WinGetTitle (GetWindowText -> WM_GETTEXT to the target's UI thread) blocks on a hung foreground window. WinGetProcessName/WinGetClass/WinGetID use GetWindowThreadProcessId / GetClassName / process-handle queries that do NOT pump the target's message queue and do not block on a Not-Responding app. So the root_cause's claim that WinGetProcessName blocks is wrong; the single genuine offender is WinGetTitle (line 155, and metrics_filters.ahk:115). The flush+3-writes amplification is real but minor: the file handle is cached (KL_OpenTodayFh) and writes are unflushed/buffered, so disk cost is small next to a multi-100ms WinGetTitle stall.

Not a false positive, but scoped: the keylogger is opt-in (MetricsShortcuts.enabled defaults false, metrics_shortcuts.ahk:61), so only metrics users are exposed; the WinGet calls are try-wrapped and throttled (>=250 ms / >=1000 ms), so the stall is at most ~once/250ms; and the trigger requires a genuinely hung foreground window (intermittent), making this a rare drop, not a frequent one. Notably the repo ALREADY applies the recommended off-thread pattern to the comparable UIA password path (KL_SchedulePasswordDetect / KL_AsyncPasswordDetect, keylogger.ahk:1403-1420 move the UIA round-trip to a -1 SetTimer) and the hot hotstring path's GetActiveApp (active_app_cache.ahk:79-97) deliberately omits WinGetTitle entirely — so the safe pattern is established in-tree and this code is an inconsistency, not an unknown.

<a id="f-textsend-clipwait-blocks-kbd-thread"></a>
#### `textsend-clipwait-blocks-kbd-thread` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : e2e-send-clipboard (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:93-100`
- **Description** : TextSend's clipboard branch does CB_Write(Text) then ClipWait(1) then SendInput('^v'). ClipWait(1) blocks the current thread for up to 1000 ms waiting for the clipboard to contain data. This runs on the keyboard thread when reached from the Tab-accept hotkey (LLM_Bridge_OnAccept -> TextSend) or any synchronous caller. While ClipWait blocks, the low-level keyboard hook cannot be serviced, so keystrokes the user types during that window can hit LowLevelHooksTimeout and be dropped. The ClipWait return value is also discarded: if it times out, '^v' still fires and pastes whatever stale content is on the clipboard (or nothing), with no error logged.
- **Cause racine** : ClipWait is a blocking built-in that yields the message pump but stalls the active pseudo-thread up to its timeout; placing it on the keyboard thread during a user-triggered accept starves the hook. Ignoring its boolean return means a timeout is treated as success (fail-fast violation per project rule 5.3).
- **Scénario** : User accepts a long LLM prediction with Tab while continuing to type the next word. The clipboard is briefly contended (another app polling it, or a clipboard manager); ClipWait stalls ~hundreds of ms; the user's next 1-2 keystrokes are dropped or arrive after the paste. Or ClipWait times out and Ctrl+V pastes the previous clipboard content instead of the prediction.
- **Impact** : Dropped/laggy keystrokes during accept of large payloads; possible wrong paste (stale clipboard) on timeout with no log. Directly the user's #1 lost-keystroke concern.
- **Correctif** : Check ClipWait's return and bail loudly on timeout (LoggerError + abort the paste / fall back to SendText) instead of pasting blindly. Better: avoid the blocking wait on the keyboard thread entirely — write the clipboard, then schedule the paste via a negative SetTimer so the keyboard thread returns immediately, or cap to a much smaller timeout. At minimum, never SendInput('^v') when ClipWait returned false.
- **Test** : headless-unit: drive TextSend with mode='clipboard' through the _AHK_SendInput/_SendHook stubs and a CB_* stub whose write never settles; assert that on a simulated ClipWait failure no '^v' is recorded and an error is surfaced. Plus a meta-static test asserting the clipboard branch consults the ClipWait result (grep that ClipWait's value is captured into a variable / used in an if).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code (adapters/text_sender.ahk:90-100) and traced every caller. The clipboard branch is real and is reachable on the keyboard thread: TextSend is called from LLM_Bridge_OnAccept (llm_bridge.ahk:489), which fires from LLM_Tooltip_TryAcceptTab via the prefix-watcher InputHook OnKeyDown (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:925 — a keyboard-thread callback), via tap-hold hotkey threads (rctrl.ahk:100, lalt.ahk:127), and via gestures. So the "runs on the keyboard thread" premise holds.

However, the finding's headline mechanism — ClipWait(1) blocking the thread up to 1000 ms and dropping keystrokes via LowLevelHooksTimeout — is largely WRONG under the actual AHK semantics and the actual code order:

1. CB_Write(Text) (clipboard.ahk:51) does `A_Clipboard := Text`, a SYNCHRONOUS blocking assignment that does not return until the clipboard already holds Text. By the time ClipWait(1) runs on line 95, the clipboard is already non-empty, so ClipWait's wait condition is ALREADY satisfied and it returns essentially immediately. It does not spend "up to 1000 ms"; the only path to a real timeout is the clipboard write failing/being cleared in a sub-ms race, which is rare.

2. Even if ClipWait did wait, it is a message-pumping wait (like Sleep), not a busy-spin. In AHK's cooperative model it is an interruption point and it keeps the message pump alive, so the low-level keyboard hook continues to be serviced during it. The finding conflates "the active pseudo-thread is paused" with "the LL hook cannot run" — those are different. ClipWait does not starve the hook the way Critical-across-Send or a synchronous WinHTTP call would. So the LowLevelHooksTimeout dropped-keystroke claim does not follow from this code.

3. The branch is also RARE: both real callers pass Opts=0 → Mode resolves to "auto" → clipboard only when StrLen(Text) > 1000 (text_sender.ahk:88). No caller passes mode="clipboard" explicitly (verified by grep). An accepted prediction over 1000 chars is an edge case, not the typical Tab-accept the scenario describes.

What IS a genuine (but minor) defect: the ClipWait(1) return value is discarded, so in the rare genuine-failure case (CB_Write's try/catch swallowed a failure, or another app cleared the clipboard in the race window) `^v` still fires and pastes stale/empty content with NO log — a real fail-fast violation (project rule 5.3). The proposed fix (capture ClipWait's result, LoggerError + abort/fall back to SendText on false) is correct and worth doing. But it should be framed as a correctness/fail-fast hardening of a rare path, not as a fix for the user's #1 lost-keystroke concern. I mark this uncertain rather than false-positive because the discarded-return fail-fast issue is real, but I reject the high severity and the dropped-keystroke causal story.

<a id="f-getselection-blocks-and-eats-keys"></a>
#### `getselection-blocks-and-eats-keys` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : e2e-send-clipboard (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:305-320`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/shortcuts/win.ahk:403-441`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:724-741`
- **Description** : GetSelection() clears A_Clipboard, SendEvent('^c'), then ClipWait(GET_SELECTION_TIMEOUT_SEC = 2) and reads A_Clipboard. ClipWait blocks the keyboard thread for up to 2 seconds. It is reached from the Win+E/Win+U/Win+W case-conversion shortcuts and the gesture case-conversion/web-search handlers — all keyboard-thread triggered. The ClipWait return is ignored, so on timeout (nothing selected, or app didn't respond to Ctrl+C) GetSelection returns whatever stale/empty A_Clipboard holds and the caller proceeds to SendInstant it, potentially typing garbage.
- **Cause racine** : ClipWait is blocking; a 2 s ceiling on the keyboard thread is a large LowLevelHooksTimeout exposure window. Ignoring the return value violates fail-fast (rule 5.3): an empty/timed-out capture is treated as a valid empty selection.
- **Scénario** : User presses Win+U (uppercase) with nothing selected, in an app that doesn't respond to Ctrl+C. ClipWait blocks ~2 s; any keys typed in that window can be dropped; then SendInstant pastes whatever was on the clipboard, possibly duplicating old content. Even with a selection, a slow app makes the chord feel frozen.
- **Impact** : Multi-hundred-ms-to-2s input stall and possible dropped keys on every case-conversion / web-search chord that fails or is slow; wrong output (stale clipboard) on timeout.
- **Correctif** : Check ClipWait's return; on false, log and return '' so callers no-op instead of pasting stale clipboard. Lower the timeout for an interactive chord (e.g. 0.3-0.5 s) since a real selection copies near-instantly. Optionally short-circuit when the active-app cache indicates an app known to be slow/unresponsive to programmatic copy.
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub the clipboard seam so ClipWait 'times out' (clipboard stays empty); call GetSelection and assert it returns '' and that the case-conversion callers do not invoke SendInstant on empty text. Encodes that a failed copy must be a no-op, not a stale paste.  _(faisabilité : hard)_
- **Verdict** : I read all three cited sites and they match the code: GetSelection() (hotstring_engine.ahk:305-320) does `A_Clipboard:=""; SendEvent("^c"); ClipWait(GET_SELECTION_TIMEOUT_SEC); Text:=A_Clipboard` and ignores ClipWait's return. GET_SELECTION_TIMEOUT_SEC := 2 (line 53). The four case-conversion callers (win.ahk:403 ConvertToTitleCase, win.ahk:432 ConvertToUppercase, gestures.ahk:724/732) all call GetSelection() then unconditionally SendInstant(Format(...)) with no empty guard, and all are keyboard/gesture-thread triggered. So the *mechanism* (ignored return, 2 s ClipWait on the chord thread) is real.

But two of the finding's headline claims are WRONG under careful reading:

1. STALE PASTE / "duplicating old content" is incorrect. GetSelection clears the clipboard (`A_Clipboard := ""`) BEFORE the copy. On a genuine timeout the copy produced nothing, so the read yields "" (not stale content), and the user's original clipboard is restored in the `finally`. The caller then does SendInstant(Format("{:U}","")) = SendInstant(""), which sets `A_Clipboard:=""` and pastes empty — a no-op empty paste, NOT a paste of old/duplicate content. There is no data corruption as described. (The only non-empty-but-wrong read would require a foreign app populating the clipboard between the clear and the read — an exotic edge case, not the scenario stated.)

2. DROPPED KEYSTROKES via LowLevelHooksTimeout is overstated. ClipWait is a message-pumping blocking wait, not a busy-loop inside the low-level hook callback; the keyboard hook keeps running during it. The classic LLHooksTimeout drop requires the hook callback itself to stall (e.g. heavy synchronous work *inside* InputHook OnKeyDown), which is not what happens here. So the user's #1 "lost keystroke" concern does not cleanly apply — this is AHK's cooperative model: the chord's own pseudo-thread is parked in ClipWait, but other threads (the hook) still get their rest points.

What survives: (a) a real fail-fast violation (rule 5.3) — the ignored ClipWait return conflates "copy timed out / nothing selected" with "empty selection"; (b) a real UX issue — in an app unresponsive to Ctrl+C the chord visibly freezes for the full 2 s before returning. That is sluggish but not data loss. Hence the bug is partially real (latency + missing guard) but the severity-justifying impact (lost keys, stale paste) is not substantiated. I mark uncertain rather than confirmed because the most damaging claims are refuted, and rather than false-positive because the ignored-return / 2 s-stall / unguarded-empty-paste defects are genuine.

<a id="f-roi-full-map-prune-scan-on-hot-path"></a>
#### `roi-full-map-prune-scan-on-hot-path` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : gap-keylogger-roi-onchar-race-and-pause (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:201`
- **Description** : When KLRoi.word_counts.Count > MAX_TRACKED_WORDS (500), the prune (lines 201-210) iterates the ENTIRE map building a `prune` array of count==1 keys, then iterates that to Delete each — all on the keystroke thread inside the OnChar callback. Worse, the threshold is checked with `>` and the prune only removes count==1 entries: once the long tail of count>=2 words exceeds 500, the map can stay permanently above 500 and the full O(n) scan + allocation then runs on EVERY subsequent word boundary (every space the user types), not just once. That is a sustained per-keystroke-boundary cost, plus per-scan it can be interrupted mid-Delete by another OnChar (see roi-map-mutation-during-enumeration-race).
- **Cause racine** : Unbounded-growth guard implemented as a full-map scan on the hot path with a pruning predicate (only count==1) that does not guarantee the map drops back below the cap — so the guard degrades from amortised-O(1) to O(n)-every-word.
- **Scénario** : A power user with a long session types hundreds of distinct repeated words (code, prose). word_counts saturates above 500 with mostly count>=2 entries; from then on every word boundary triggers a 500+-entry scan + array build on the keystroke thread, adding latency to ordinary typing.
- **Impact** : Sustained per-word CPU + allocation on the keyboard thread for long sessions, compounding the dropped-keystroke risk. Degrades over the life of a session rather than at a single moment.
- **Correctif** : Make the prune bounded and guaranteed-shrinking: when over cap, evict to a target (e.g. keep the top MAX_TRACKED_WORDS/2 by count, or evict the lowest-count half) so the map is always brought strictly below the cap and the scan amortises. Better, move the prune off the hot path entirely — run it from the (off-thread) ingest tick or a low-frequency SetTimer that snapshots under Critical. At minimum, gate the prune behind a counter so it runs at most once per N words, not on every boundary while saturated.
- **Test** : headless-unit: insert 600 distinct words each with count>=2 into KLRoi.word_counts, call the prune helper, and assert KLRoi.word_counts.Count is strictly < MAX_TRACKED_WORDS afterward (proves the map actually shrinks below the cap, so the next word does not re-trigger a full scan). Also assert a single additional word after pruning does NOT trigger another full scan (e.g. via an instrumented scan-counter).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code (keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:175-211) plus its call chain (keylogger_hook.ahk:203-253 KL_Hook_OnChar -> KL_Roi_OnChar -> KL_Roi_ProcessWord) and the dispatcher (lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:187-198).

PATH IS ON THE KEYSTROKE THREAD, UN-Critical: KL_Roi_OnChar is registered as a HookDispatcher EVT_KB_CHAR subscriber (keylogger_hook.ahk:396-398) and runs synchronously inside the single process InputHook's OnChar fan-out. HookDispatcher.Dispatch (lines 187-198) does NOT hold Critical, and the keylogger module contains zero Critical/Thread calls. So KL_Roi_ProcessWord genuinely executes on the InputHook OnChar pseudo-thread, on every printable character (the prune only at word boundaries). The finding's threading premise is accurate, not a false positive.

ALGORITHM DEFECT IS REAL: The prune (lines 201-210) fires when word_counts.Count > 500, scans the ENTIRE map building a `prune` array of count==1 keys, then Deletes them. Because the predicate is strictly `v = 1`, it can only remove never-repeated noise words. I traced the steady state: once the population of count>=2 words alone reaches 501, the prune removes zero entries, Count stays >=501, and EVERY subsequent word boundary re-enters the `> 500` branch and re-runs the full O(n) enumeration + array allocation, removing nothing. The guard provably cannot bring the map below the cap in that regime, so it degrades from amortised-O(1) to O(n)-per-word-boundary for the rest of the session. Reaching 500+ distinct words each typed twice is realistic for a long prose/code session (active vocabulary is in the thousands; triggers excluded via _TriggerSet at line 182 are a small set). So the described degradation is genuinely reachable and the proposed fix (evict to a target below the cap so the map always shrinks, and/or move the prune off the hot path / gate it behind a per-N-words counter) is correct and necessary.

WHY I DOWNGRADE SEVERITY (the finding's impact framing is overstated): the work in lines 204-209 is a pure in-memory Map enumeration with integer comparisons plus a few Deletes — no Send, Sleep, file/sqlite I/O, ClipWait, COM/UIA or GDI. A ~500-entry integer-compare loop costs on the order of tens of microseconds, far under Windows' ~300 ms LowLevelHooksTimeout, and it runs only at word boundaries (on space/punctuation), not per keystroke. So this is NOT a dropped-keystroke / hook-starvation hazard; the finding's claim that it is "compounding the dropped-keystroke risk" is not supported by the AHK model. It is a sustained, session-degrading CPU/allocation inefficiency on the keystroke thread — real and worth fixing, but low severity, not medium. (The separate mid-Delete interruption / torn-enumeration concern is explicitly the cross-referenced finding roi-map-mutation-during-enumeration-race and is out of scope here.)

### Catégorie : Fuites de pause / suspend (22)

<a id="f-sensors-bypass-pause-suspend"></a>
#### `sensors-bypass-pause-suspend` — **critical** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : keylogger-sensors (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_sensors.ahk:90`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_network.ahk:94`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_av_state.ahk:116`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_window_topology.ahk:117`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_mouse.ahk:364`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_clipboard.ahk:89`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:626`
- **Description** : Every background sensor entry point (KL_Sensors_Tick, KL_Net_WifiTick/ReachTick/VpnTick, KL_AV_FastTick/SlowTick, KL_Topo_Tick, KL_Mouse_ParkTick, KL_Mouse_FlushScroll, KL_Clip_OnChange) checks only `Keylogger.initialized` and `MF_ShouldFilter()`, never `A_IsSuspended`. KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:626-638) likewise gates only on `initialized` + `MF_ShouldFilter()`. None of these clear `initialized` on pause, and MF_ShouldFilter has no suspend check (lib/metrics/metrics_filters.ahk:161). So when the user pauses the driver, these timers keep firing and keep writing events to today.log / data.sql.
- **Cause racine** : Native AHK Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings. SetTimer, OnClipboardChange and OnMessage callbacks BYPASS Suspend (project invariant). The shared keyboard fan-out is gated once in HookDispatcher.Dispatch (`if A_IsSuspended return`, hook_dispatcher.ahk:191) and the keylogger's own InputHook re-checks it (keylogger_hook.ahk:206,256 — comment: 'its InputHook is separate from HookDispatcher, so it needs its own guard'). The peripheral sensors are NOT routed through either gate: they are standalone SetTimers / an OnClipboardChange handler, so they inherit no suspend guard, and Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter() (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006) does not stop them.
- **Scénario** : User pauses ErgoptiPlus (tray 'Suspend' / shortcut) before a private meeting or to stop all tracking. The keyboard hook correctly goes silent, but every ~250 ms-60 s the mouse-park, network, audio/capture, window-topology, sensor and clipboard timers keep emitting events (mouse_idle_park, network_change with SSID hash, screen_recording_start, volume_change, clipboard_copy with char counts, window geometry, CPU/RAM) into the metrics log. The user's expectation 'pause = tout éteint' is violated for the entire sensor surface.
- **Impact** : Privacy/trust breach: the metrics keep recording while the user explicitly disabled the driver. Directly contradicts the stated invariant 'Pause must silence ALL features.' Also wastes CPU on a paused process.
- **Correctif** : Add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` as the first line of every standalone sensor entry point: KL_Sensors_Tick, KL_Net_WifiTick/ReachTick/VpnTick, KL_AV_FastTick/SlowTick, KL_Topo_Tick, KL_Mouse_ParkTick, KL_Mouse_FlushScroll, KL_Clip_OnChange, KL_Clip_OnPaste (and KL_Roi_HalflifeTick). The cleanest single-chokepoint fix is to add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` to KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk, right after the `initialized` check) and KL_LogSystemEvent so EVERY non-hook event source inherits the pause gate for free, mirroring how MF_ShouldFilter is the single privacy chokepoint. Keep the per-callback guards too for callbacks that mutate state before logging (mouse park distance bump).
- **Test** : meta-static: a tests/meta/ test that reads each keylogger_*.ahk sensor file and asserts the tick/handler function bodies contain an `A_IsSuspended` guard (mirror of test_require_state_pattern.ahk's source-text scan). headless-unit: load keylogger.ahk's KL_AppendLog logic under a stub where Keylogger.initialized=true and A_IsSuspended is forced true via a seam, push an entry, and AssertEqual that Keylogger._pending_entries.Length stayed 0 (fails before the chokepoint guard, passes after).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read every cited entry point and the supporting code; the finding holds in full under the AHK v2 cooperative model.

ASYMMETRY IS REAL AND DELIBERATE ELSEWHERE. The keyboard path is gated on A_IsSuspended in two places: HookDispatcher.Dispatch (hook_dispatcher.ahk:191, comment "pause = tout eteint en one place") and the keylogger's own InputHook (keylogger_hook.ahk:206 KL_Hook_OnChar, :256 OnKeyDown — comment "records nothing while the script is paused — its InputHook is separate from HookDispatcher, so it needs its own guard"). This establishes the project invariant that pause must silence ALL features, and proves the maintainer knows SetTimer/InputHook/OnClipboardChange bypass native Suspend.

SENSORS ARE NOT ON EITHER GATE. Each standalone sensor entry point checks only Keylogger.initialized (+ optionally MF_ShouldFilter), never A_IsSuspended:
- KL_Sensors_Tick (keylogger_sensors.ahk:90-96)
- KL_Net_WifiTick/ReachTick/VpnTick (keylogger_network.ahk:94, :145, :172)
- KL_AV_FastTick/SlowTick (keylogger_av_state.ahk:116, :196)
- KL_Topo_Tick (keylogger_window_topology.ahk:117-123)
- KL_Mouse_ParkTick (keylogger_mouse.ahk:364 — NO initialized/MF guard at the TOP; it reads MouseGetPos and calls KL_BumpMouseDistance at :374 before any gate, then emits mouse_idle_park via KL_AppendLog at :415 behind only MF_ShouldFilter + initialized at :413)
- KL_Clip_OnChange (keylogger_clipboard.ahk:89-95) and KL_Clip_OnPaste (:129-135)
- KL_Roi_HalflifeTick (keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:223)
These are armed as standalone SetTimer / OnClipboardChange (e.g. keylogger_network.ahk:220-224, keylogger_mouse.ahk:484, keylogger_av_state.ahk:319-321), so they inherit no suspend guard.

CHOKEPOINT HAS NO GUARD. KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618-654) gates only on !Keylogger.initialized (:626) and MF_ShouldFilter (:636) — no A_IsSuspended. KL_LogSystemEvent (:761-768) routes straight through KL_AppendLog. MF_ShouldFilter (lib/metrics/metrics_filters.ahk:161-189) is purely a focused-window privacy filter; it has no A_IsSuspended check, so it does NOT cover pause.

SUSPEND HANDLER DOES NOT STOP THEM. Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006-2010) only hides tooltips and cancels the LLM timer; it stops no keylogger sensor timer and does not clear initialized. Keylogger.initialized is set false ONLY in KL_Stop full teardown (keylogger.ahk:1589), never on suspend — so the initialized gate cannot mask the pause case.

CONCURRENCY MODEL CONFIRMS, NOT REFUTES. These are SetTimers/OnClipboardChange, exactly the callback classes the project invariant says BYPASS native Suspend. There is no Critical, A_IsSuspended branch, or serialization upstream that would stop them while paused. So when the user pauses, every ~250 ms-60 s the park, network (SSID hash), AV (screen_recording/volume), topology and clipboard (char_count) events keep writing to today.log and growing _pending_entries (ingested to data.sql). The user's "pause = tout eteint" expectation is violated for the whole sensor surface.

One partial mitigation worth noting (does not save the finding): mouse SCROLL deltas are accumulated via HookDispatcher subscribers, and Dispatch gates on A_IsSuspended, so while paused no scroll ticks accumulate and KL_Mouse_FlushScroll early-returns at :320. But the park timer, sensors, network, AV, topology and clipboard paths are fully independent of the dispatcher and leak.

Severity: critical is justified — it is a privacy/trust breach directly contradicting the stated invariant, on by default, on the very feature the user toggled off.

<a id="f-keylogger-telemetry-ignores-suspend"></a>
#### `keylogger-telemetry-ignores-suspend` — **critical** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-suspend-pause (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:618`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:626`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_network.ahk:94`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_sensors.ahk:168`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_av_state.ahk:270`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_window_topology.ahk:117`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_watchers.ahk:200`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_mouse.ahk:364`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:223`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006`
- **Description** : The project invariant is « pause = tout éteint » — Suspend must silence ALL features, and the docs explicitly note that InputHooks/SetTimers/OnMessage handlers BYPASS native Suspend and need explicit A_IsSuspended guards. HookDispatcher.Dispatch is gated and the keyboard callbacks are gated, but the keylogger's ENVIRONMENTAL telemetry layer is driven by ~10 independent recurring SetTimer callbacks armed once at boot (KL_Sensors_Tick, KL_Net_WifiTick/ReachTick/VpnTick, KL_AV_FastTick/SlowTick, KL_Topo_Tick, KL_Watchers_IdleTick, KL_Mouse_ParkTick, KL_Roi_HalflifeTick, KL_IngestOnce, KL_MidnightCheck). NONE of these checks A_IsSuspended — they gate only on Keylogger.initialized and MF_ShouldFilter() (a privacy/window filter that knows nothing about pause). They all funnel their writes through KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618), which also has no A_IsSuspended check. The suspend reactor Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006) only hides tooltips and cancels the LLM debounce timer; it does not stop or gate any keylogger timer. Result: while the user believes the driver is fully paused, the keylogger keeps writing window-movement events, idle/session-start/end transitions, mouse-idle-park coordinates, CPU/RAM/battery samples, SHA-256 Wi-Fi SSID hashes, VPN/Focus-Assist transitions, and trigger half-life events into today.log, and the 5 s ingest keeps committing them to data.sql.
- **Cause racine** : AHK native Suspend only disarms Hotkey/Hotstring threads. SetTimer callbacks are a separate pseudo-thread class and continue firing across Suspend. The keylogger's environmental timers were written to gate only on Keylogger.initialized + MF_ShouldFilter(), and the single write funnel KL_AppendLog never consults A_IsSuspended — so suspend has zero effect on this subsystem.
- **Scénario** : User opens a banking site or a private document, hits AltGr+Enter (or the tray 'Suspendre' item) expecting the driver to be fully off, and keeps working. The keylogger silently records every window move/resize, every idle/active session boundary, mouse-park positions, the hashed SSID of the network they switched to, VPN connect/disconnect, and CPU/battery telemetry to today.log + data.sql for the entire 'paused' period, and the metrics dashboard later shows activity during a window the user explicitly silenced.
- **Impact** : Direct violation of the headline « pause = tout éteint » invariant and a privacy breach: pausing does not stop personal data collection. The user has no way to truly silence telemetry short of quitting the driver. Also wastes CPU (netsh WLAN polls, CreateToolhelp32Snapshot AV scan, registry enumeration, MouseGetPos) and grows data.sql while supposedly idle.
- **Correctif** : Add a single early-return guard `if A_IsSuspended return` at the very top of KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618, right after the `if !Keylogger.initialized return`). This mirrors the HookDispatcher.Dispatch single-gate pattern and silences EVERY telemetry source's disk write in one canonical place, since all of them funnel through KL_AppendLog. Additionally (defence in depth + to stop wasted polling), add `if A_IsSuspended return` at the top of each recurring tick (KL_Sensors_Tick, KL_Net_*Tick, KL_AV_*Tick, KL_Topo_Tick, KL_Watchers_IdleTick, KL_Mouse_ParkTick, KL_Roi_HalflifeTick) and skip the FileAppend in KL_IngestOnce while suspended so no data.sql commit happens during pause. Keeping the guard in KL_AppendLog as the authoritative gate ensures any future telemetry source is covered automatically.
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/ (e.g. test_keylogger.ahk): stub Keylogger.initialized:=true and a fake today FileObject / capture buffer, set a test shim for A_IsSuspended (the suite already uses script_control/A_IsSuspended simulation per test_logger.ahk:384). Call KL_AppendLog(Map('type','window_move',...)) with suspend simulated true and assert NOTHING was written (pending_entries length unchanged, no fh.Write); then with suspend false assert the entry IS recorded. Plus a meta/static test asserting `A_IsSuspended` appears in the body of KL_AppendLog so the guard cannot silently regress. The unit test fails before the fix (entry recorded while paused) and passes after.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Read the actual cited code; every load-bearing claim holds.

KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618-654) is the single write funnel and gates ONLY on `if !Keylogger.initialized` (626) and `MF_ShouldFilter()` (636-638). No A_IsSuspended. KL_LogSystemEvent (761-768) wraps KL_AppendLog, so every system_event flows through it. Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006-2010) only calls TooltipHide + LLM_Tooltip_Hide + LLM_Engine_CancelTimer; it never stops or gates a keylogger timer, and keylogger.ahk contains ZERO references to A_IsSuspended / is_paused / a suspend reactor (verified by grep — no matches).

Telemetry ticks confirmed unguarded: KL_Sensors_Tick (90-96 gates init+filter only), KL_Net_WifiTick (94-96 init only), KL_Topo_Tick (117-123 init+filter), KL_Watchers_IdleTick (191-195 init only), KL_Roi_HalflifeTick (223-225 init only), KL_AV_* and KL_Mouse_ParkTick (364: no init guard at all, runs MouseGetPos + KL_AppendLog unconditionally). All ultimately write via KL_AppendLog.

Why the AHK concurrency model CONFIRMS rather than refutes: native Suspend only disarms Hotkey/Hotstring pseudo-threads. SetTimer callbacks are a distinct thread class and keep firing across Suspend — exactly the documented project invariant ("InputHooks/SetTimers/OnMessage BYPASS native Suspend, need explicit A_IsSuspended guards"). The timers are armed once at boot (KL_Sensors_Start 187-188, KL_Net_Start 220-225, etc.) and are torn down only by the per-module *_Stop() functions, which are called solely from Keylogger.Stop() (keylogger.ahk:1570-1586) — never from the suspend path. So during Suspend they continue to run and write. This is NOT a race that requires interruption; it is a plain missing gate on a recurring thread class, and it is deterministic.

The asymmetry is the clincher: the keyboard-capture InputHook callbacks DO gate (keylogger_hook.ahk:206 and 256 both `if A_IsSuspended return`, with a comment noting the hook is separate from HookDispatcher and needs its own guard). The same invariant was simply never propagated to the environmental-telemetry timer layer. So this is a genuine, plausible regression gap, not a misread.

Mitigating nuance (kept critical, not downgraded): MF_ShouldFilter() still runs in most ticks, so private-browsing / exclusion-list / auth-dialog windows are filtered even while paused, and the keystroke TEXT path is gated. What leaks during pause is behavioral/environmental metadata (window topology, idle/session boundaries, hashed SSID, VPN/Focus transitions, CPU/RAM/battery, mouse-park coords), committed to today.log and data.sql by the 5 s ingest. KL_Mouse_ParkTick is the worst offender (no init guard, no filter for the distance/park write). This is a real privacy + invariant violation, consistent with the project treating this invariant as critical.

<a id="f-layout-poll-reload-unpauses"></a>
#### `layout-poll-reload-unpauses` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : boot-main (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2251`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2256`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2259`
- **Description** : CheckKeyboardLayoutChange runs every 1000 ms and calls Reload() whenever GetForegroundKeyboardLayout() changes. It is not gated by A_IsSuspended. After Reload, AHK starts a fresh process and A_IsSuspended resets to false; nothing in the boot path restores a previously-suspended state (grep confirms no Suspend('On')/persisted-suspend logic anywhere in windows/). So a layout switch while the user has the driver PAUSED silently re-enables every feature.
- **Cause racine** : AHK Reload restarts the script from scratch, discarding the runtime-only A_IsSuspended flag. The layout-poll timer issues an unprompted Reload on an external event (HKL change) without checking pause, and the project never persists/re-applies suspend on boot. This directly violates the documented invariant 'Pause must silence ALL features.'
- **Scénario** : User pauses ErgoptiPlus (e.g. to play a game or type in a remapping-hostile app), then Windows/another app switches the active keyboard layout (common with multi-language input or RDP/VM focus changes). Within 1 s the driver reloads and comes back UNPAUSED, re-injecting remaps/hotstrings into the app the user deliberately silenced.
- **Impact** : Pause is not durable: an external, automatic event re-arms the entire driver behind the user's back — the precise class of suspend-leak the project flags as critical. All the other Reload call sites are user-initiated, but this one fires on a timer.
- **Correctif** : Persist the suspend state before any automatic Reload and restore it on boot: e.g. write a one-shot marker (a temp flag file or a [script] runtime key) when A_IsSuspended is true and re-apply Suspend('On') early in the auto-execute section if the marker is present (clearing it after). Minimally, guard CheckKeyboardLayoutChange with `if A_IsSuspended return` so a paused driver never auto-reloads on layout change. The marker approach is preferable because user-triggered Reloads (menu) also drop pause today.
- **Test** : headless-unit: a logic test that simulates the suspend-persist/restore helpers — write the marker with a 'suspended=true' state, run the restore function, assert it would call Suspend('On') and then clear the marker; assert the no-op path when the marker is absent. Plus a meta-static test asserting CheckKeyboardLayoutChange's body contains an A_IsSuspended guard (or that a restore-suspend call exists in the early boot layer).  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against real source. ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2251-2259: CheckKeyboardLayoutChange() is armed unconditionally via SetTimer(CheckKeyboardLayoutChange, 1000) (line 2259), polls GetForegroundKeyboardLayout() every 1000ms, and calls Reload() (line 2256) on any HKL change, with NO A_IsSuspended guard.

AHK concurrency model: native Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; SetTimer callbacks bypass it. This is a documented project invariant — every OTHER timer/hook handler explicitly guards itself: lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:191, modules/llm/prediction_engine.ahk:345, modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:206/256, lib/metrics/wpm_widget.ahk:857, lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:659/861 all carry `if A_IsSuspended return`. CheckKeyboardLayoutChange is the conspicuous exception. So while paused, the timer still fires.

No suspend persistence exists. Grep across all of windows/ confirms there is no Suspend("On")/Suspend(1)/marker-file/[script] runtime key. The only suspend activations are Suspend(-1) toggles in ToggleSuspend (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1998) and a gesture binding (gestures.ahk:452). After Reload() the fresh process boots with A_IsSuspended=false; line 372 `global _LastSuspendState := A_IsSuspended` just captures false; healthcheck.ahk:568 reads A_IsSuspended directly, so there is no secondary persisted pause source. Nothing in the boot auto-execute re-applies Suspend.

Scenario is realistic and reachable. GetForegroundKeyboardLayout() (hotstring_engine.ahk:83-93) returns the HKL of the FOREGROUND window's thread. Switching focus to an app whose thread carries a different active input language (multi-language input, RDP/VM focus, a game) changes the HKL -> Reload within 1s -> the driver comes back UNPAUSED and re-injects into the very app the user silenced. The design comment at ErgoptiPlus.ahk:587-589 frames this Reload purely as 're-adapt to the new layout' and never considers pause. All other Reload sites (1985, 2069, tray_menu 2285/2294) are user/config-initiated; this is the only timer-driven one.

This directly violates the 'Pause must silence ALL features' invariant. Real bug. One nuance: it requires an actual foreground-HKL CHANGE (not merely remaining paused), so it is conditional rather than continuous — but the trigger is common and entirely outside the user's control, so high (not lowered to medium) is correct. The finding's description, root_cause, scenario and impact all match the code.

<a id="f-dispatcher-unregister-never-removes"></a>
#### `dispatcher-unregister-never-removes` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : input-dispatch (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:163`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/keyboard_hook.ahk:80`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/keyboard_hook.ahk:81`
- **Description** : Unregister() finds the target by `arr[idx].Ptr = callback_fn.Ptr`. Because subscribers are BoundFuncs with no .Ptr, the comparison throws on the first element, the surrounding `try {}` swallows it, RemoveAt never runs, and the callback stays registered forever. So KHStop(), KL_Hook_Stop(), KL_Mouse_Stop() and LLM_Bridge_Stop() all silently fail to detach their keyboard/mouse subscribers from the shared hook. Reproduced: Register(fn) then Unregister(SAME fn ref) leaves the subscriber list unchanged (count stays 1).
- **Cause racine** : Same BoundFunc-has-no-.Ptr mechanism as the Register defect. `arr[idx].Ptr` throws, the `try {}` eats it, the `break`/`RemoveAt` is never reached. keyboard_hook.ahk compounds this by calling `_KH_DispatchChar.Bind()` afresh in BOTH Register and Unregister, producing a different object each time — so even with a working comparator the KH adapter could not match (it must store the bound reference once, like KLHook/LLM do).
- **Scénario** : User toggles the keylogger off, or pauses a feature that calls its *_Stop(): the dispatcher keeps invoking the supposedly-stopped char/down handlers on every keystroke. The keylogger keeps recording typed characters (PII) after the user disabled it; the LLM fallback keeps consuming chars after Stop. Only A_IsSuspended (global pause) in Dispatch and per-handler guards mask it — a per-feature disable does not.
- **Impact** : Per-feature disable/stop is non-functional for all hook subscribers: privacy regression (keylogger keeps logging after 'off'), stale callbacks fire indefinitely, and the leaked subscribers accumulate across non-Reload Stop/Start cycles.
- **Correctif** : In Unregister replace `arr[idx].Ptr = callback_fn.Ptr` with `arr[idx] == callback_fn`. Separately, fix keyboard_hook.ahk to bind once and store the reference (e.g. global _KH_CHAR_FN := _KH_DispatchChar.Bind() created in KHStart, reused in KHStop) so Register and Unregister pass the identical object, matching the KLHook.cb_char / _LLM_Bridge_DispatcherCharFn pattern.
- **Test** : headless-unit: register A.Bind() stored in a variable for an event, assert Length 1, call Unregister with the SAME stored reference, assert Length 0. Add a meta/static test asserting keyboard_hook.ahk does not call `.Bind()` inside both KHStart and KHStop on the same symbol (grep: Register and Unregister must reference a stored global, not an inline .Bind()).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code and confirmed it empirically against the real AHK v2.0.19 runtime (AutoHotkey64.exe on this machine), reproducing each causal link with three throwaway scripts.

CORE MECHANISM (all verified):
1. hook_dispatcher.ahk:163 — Unregister finds the target via `arr[idx].Ptr = callback_fn.Ptr`. The repo already contains tests/_verify_boundfunc.ahk probing exactly this. Running it: `b1.Ptr THREW: This value of type "BoundFunc" has no property named "Ptr"`. Even a PLAIN Func reference throws: `A.Ptr THREW: ... "Func" has no property named "Ptr"`. So `.Ptr` does not exist on either Func or BoundFunc in v2 — the finding is right (and broader: not just BoundFuncs).
2. The `.Ptr` access throws on the FIRST array element; that element exists during Stop because something was registered at Start. The throw is caught by the surrounding `try {}` (line 154), so RemoveAt (164) and break (166) never run. My faithful copy of the Unregister body confirmed: with the SAME stored reference (the KLHook/LLM best case), removed=0, count after=1. The subscriber is NEVER detached. This is a logic bug, not a race — Critical("On") around it is irrelevant, and the AHK cooperative model offers no protection.
3. Consumers confirmed: LLM_Bridge_Stop (llm_bridge.ahk:147→124→127/128), KL_Hook_Stop (keylogger_hook.ahk:409→417/418), KL_Mouse_Stop (keylogger_mouse.ahk:512), and KHStop (keyboard_hook.ahk:76→80/81) all route through this dead Unregister. So per-feature Stop genuinely fails to detach.
4. A_IsSuspended in Dispatch (line 191) only gates GLOBAL pause; a per-feature disable does not set A_IsSuspended, so the stuck char/down handlers keep firing. The keylogger keeps recording typed characters after the user turns it off — real privacy regression. Confirmed exactly as the finding states.

PROPOSED FIX VALIDATED: replacing the comparator with `arr[idx] == callback_fn` works for the same-reference case (my test: removed=1, count after=0). T3a showed `b1 == b1` = 1 and T3b showed `b1 == b2` (distinct binds of the same func) = 0, so == is correct identity semantics.

SECONDARY DEFECT CONFIRMED: keyboard_hook.ahk calls `_KH_DispatchChar.Bind()` fresh in BOTH KHStart (line 68) and KHStop (line 80) — distinct objects. My test with `==` and two distinct binds: removed=0, count after=1. So even after fixing the comparator, the KH adapter still cannot detach unless it stores the bound reference once (the pattern KLHook uses: cb_char/cb_down stored on the class; LLM uses _LLM_Bridge_DispatcherCharFn cached as a global). Both prongs of the proposed fix are necessary.

ONE OVERSTATEMENT in the finding: "leaked subscribers accumulate across Stop/Start cycles." They do NOT accumulate. The same `.Ptr` throw also breaks Register: the dedup loop body throws on the first existing element, and because the `.Push` (line 141) sits INSIDE the same `try` (line 130), it is skipped too. Verified: first Register into empty array pushes (count 1); every subsequent Register throws-and-skips (count stays 1). So you get exactly ONE permanently-stuck subscriber per (event,fn), never unbounded growth. This also explains why the bug survived: normal startup registers once and works; the defect only surfaces on Stop. The privacy / stale-callback core harm is fully real regardless.

<a id="f-warmup-retry-bypasses-suspend"></a>
#### `warmup-retry-bypasses-suspend` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : llm-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:778-793`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/prediction_engine.ahk:369-375`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006-2010`
- **Description** : LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryTick (a SetTimer callback re-armed every interval) has no A_IsSuspended guard. FirePrediction can schedule warmup retries via LLM_OllamaScheduleWarmupRetry. Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter cancels only the debounce timer (LLM_Engine_CancelTimer) and hides the tooltip — it does NOT call LLM_OllamaCancelWarmupRetry nor LLM_Engine_StopGeneration. SetTimer callbacks bypass native Suspend, so the warmup loop keeps firing while paused.
- **Cause racine** : Native AHK Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; SetTimer callbacks fire regardless of A_IsSuspended unless they explicitly check it. The warmup retry tick only guards on LLM_OllamaIsReady, in-flight count, and the tray-enabled flag — none of which reflect the paused state. So during pause the driver continues issuing /api/chat warmup requests to localhost:11434.
- **Scénario** : User starts the driver on a cold machine (Ollama still loading the model), then presses the pause hotkey while the model warms up. Despite the explicit pause, the script keeps hammering Ollama with warmup POSTs every 5–60 s (and could fire a fresh inference if a deferred FirePrediction re-arm survives), keeping the GPU/CPU busy and the model resident — exactly the background activity pause is supposed to silence.
- **Impact** : Violates the project's hard 'pause silences ALL features' invariant; wastes CPU/GPU and battery; keeps the local server churning when the user explicitly disabled the feature.
- **Correctif** : Add `if A_IsSuspended { LLM_OllamaCancelWarmupRetry(); return }` at the top of LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryTick (or, better, cancel it from Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter via try LLM_OllamaCancelWarmupRetry() and try LLM_Engine_StopGeneration() so in-flight generation and warmup both stop on pause, then let Ergopti_OnSuspendResume re-arm warmup if needed).
- **Test** : meta-static: assert LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryTick's body contains an A_IsSuspended early-return (grep the source text in tests/meta/, same pattern as the existing suspend-invariant meta tests). Plus headless-unit: stub LLM_OllamaWarmup to count calls, set A_IsSuspended via the harness shim, invoke LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryTick, assert zero warmup POSTs.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source. The warmup retry loop genuinely bypasses pause.

1. LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryTick (api_ollama.ahk:778-793) has NO A_IsSuspended guard. Its only exits are LLM_OllamaIsReady() (model-loaded flag, api_ollama.ahk:161-163 = returns _LLM_Ollama_IsReady, unrelated to pause), the in-flight async count, and !_LLM_Tray["enabled"]. None reflect Suspend state.

2. It is armed via a real SetTimer (api_ollama.ahk:775, 792), not a hotkey/hotstring. Per the AHK v2 model, SetTimer callbacks fire regardless of A_IsSuspended — native Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings. The tick re-arms itself by calling LLM_OllamaScheduleWarmupRetry (line 792), which re-issues LLM_OllamaWarmup — a real async /api/chat POST to localhost:11434 (api_ollama.ahk:704-730, line 716) plus an async poll timer. So the loop self-perpetuates (5s → 60s backoff cap) entirely off the typing path.

3. _LLM_Tray["enabled"] is the LLM feature toggle (set in LLM_Tray_OnToggle actions.ahk:81-100, cleared on deps failure actions.ahk:463). It is NOT touched by ToggleSuspend or Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter, so pausing the driver does not flip it — the tick's enabled guard never catches pause.

4. Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006-2010) calls only TooltipHide, LLM_Tooltip_Hide, and LLM_Engine_CancelTimer. LLM_Engine_CancelTimer (prediction_engine.ahk:250-260) cancels ONLY the debounce pending_timer; it does not touch _LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryFn. It does NOT call LLM_OllamaCancelWarmupRetry nor LLM_Engine_StopGeneration. Confirmed by grep: the only suspend-side cancel sites for warmup are in tray actions (toggle-off / model change), never in the suspend handlers.

5. Realistic trigger: the warmup loop is armed at LLM-enable / deps-ready (actions.ahk:449) and on model change (actions.ahk:186), independent of typing. User enables LLM on a cold machine → Ollama loads model → loop ticking → user presses pause → loop keeps POSTing. Exactly the described scenario.

One finding inaccuracy, which does NOT rescue it: the secondary claim that a deferred FirePrediction re-arm could fire a fresh inference while paused is wrong — LLM_Engine_FirePrediction has an A_IsSuspended early-return at prediction_engine.ahk:345 that bails before the warmup-schedule / HTTP path (lines 369-373). So FirePrediction won't fire inference or schedule warmup during pause. But the warmup retry loop never routes through FirePrediction's guard; it self-re-arms via its own tick, so the leak stands.

Related gap supporting the fix: Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter also never calls LLM_Engine_StopGeneration, so an already-in-flight streaming prediction continues to completion across pause (request_id is only bumped by StopGeneration / new FirePrediction, prediction_engine.ahk:291-299). This is a second pause-leak in the same handler.

This violates the hard "pause = tout éteint" invariant the maintainer treats as critical.

<a id="f-halflife-tick-no-suspend-guard"></a>
#### `halflife-tick-no-suspend-guard` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : gap-keylogger-roi-onchar-race-and-pause (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:223`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:256`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:618`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:191`
- **Description** : KL_Roi_HalflifeTick is registered as a standalone SetTimer (KL_Roi_Start, line 256-261, default 1 h cadence). Unlike the per-char ROI path — which is silenced under pause because HookDispatcher.Dispatch returns early on A_IsSuspended (hook_dispatcher.ahk:191) and KL_Hook_OnChar re-checks (keylogger_hook.ahk:206) — the half-life tick bypasses the dispatcher entirely and has NO A_IsSuspended guard. On each fire it iterates KLRoi.trigger_last_use and calls KL_AppendLog with trigger names and usage timestamps. KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618) only guards on Keylogger.initialized and MF_ShouldFilter() — and MF_ShouldFilter (metrics_filters.ahk:161) does NOT consult A_IsSuspended (it only checks disabled-apps / password / system-auth / private-browsing). So a trigger_halflife event naming what the user typed lands in today.log even after the user explicitly paused the keylogger.
- **Cause racine** : AHK Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; SetTimer callbacks bypass it. The project invariant 'pause must silence ALL features' requires every timer/OnMessage handler that writes metrics to carry its own `if A_IsSuspended return` guard. KL_Roi_HalflifeTick (and KL_AppendLog) lack it, and the privacy filter MF_ShouldFilter is orthogonal to pause, so neither layer stops it.
- **Scénario** : User pauses the script (tray Pause / suspend hotkey) for privacy — e.g. before typing in a sensitive app the filters don't cover. The half-life timer fires while paused and appends a trigger_halflife JSONL line (trigger text + days_since_use) to today.log, which the 5 s ingest tick then commits to data.sql. The user believes pause stopped all recording; it didn't.
- **Impact** : Privacy-invariant violation: metrics are persisted while the user has paused the keylogger. Same class of bug the project treats as critical for InputHooks/Timers/OnMessage. Low frequency (1 h cadence) but it writes during exactly the window the user expected silence.
- **Correctif** : Add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` as the first line of KL_Roi_HalflifeTick (after the RequireInit guard). For defence-in-depth and to cover any future timer-driven caller, also add an `if A_IsSuspended return` guard at the top of KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618) so no background timer can ever persist a metric while paused — this is the single choke-point all 20+ KL_Log* entry points funnel through.
- **Test** : headless-unit: load keylogger.ahk in run_all.ahk, set Keylogger.initialized := true, stub MF_ShouldFilter to return false, point today_log_path at a temp file, then set A_IsSuspended via an actual Suspend On in the harness (or refactor the guard into a testable pure predicate KL_ShouldPersist() that ANDs !A_IsSuspended) and assert KL_AppendLog(Map('type','trigger_halflife',...)) writes nothing while suspended and writes one line while not. Plus a meta/static test grepping keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk asserting KL_Roi_HalflifeTick body contains 'A_IsSuspended' (regression pin).  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read every cited path and the finding holds up under the AHK cooperative-threading model.

VERIFIED FACTS:
1. KL_Roi_HalflifeTick (keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:223-244) is armed as a standalone SetTimer by KL_Roi_Start (line 256-261) and contains NO A_IsSuspended guard — only the KL_Roi_RequireInit (initialized) check. On each fire it loops KLRoi.trigger_last_use and calls KL_AppendLog with a "trigger_halflife" Map carrying the trigger text (line 236-242).
2. KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618-654) gates ONLY on Keylogger.initialized (626) and MF_ShouldFilter() (636). No A_IsSuspended check anywhere in the chokepoint.
3. MF_ShouldFilter (metrics_filters.ahk:161-198) is orthogonal to pause — it inspects disabled-apps, password field (UIA), system-auth processes/classes, and private-browsing titles. It never reads A_IsSuspended. So pause cannot be caught by the privacy filter.
4. The pause reactor Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006-2010) does ONLY: TooltipHide, LLM_Tooltip_Hide, LLM_Engine_CancelTimer. It does NOT stop any keylogger SetTimer. Native Suspend(-1) (line 1998) only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings, not SetTimer callbacks. So the half-life timer keeps firing while paused.
5. KL_Roi_Start() is called unconditionally at startup (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1046), so the timer is genuinely armed in production.

CONCURRENCY CHECK: AHK SetTimer callbacks bypass Suspend entirely; the timer fires at a rest point of the main pseudo-thread regardless of A_IsSuspended. There is no Critical/serialization gate that would prevent it. fh.Write at KL_AppendLog:650 commits the JSONL line to today.log; the 5 s ingest tick then persists it to data.sql. The chain to disk is real and unconditional under pause.

CONTRAST CONFIRMED: The per-char ROI path (KL_Roi_OnChar) is reached only from KL_Hook_OnChar (keylogger_hook.ahk:250), which is AFTER the explicit "if A_IsSuspended return" guard at line 206 — so it IS silenced under pause, exactly as the finding claims. The half-life timer is the divergent, unguarded path. This is the same class of bug the project documents as critical (InputHooks/Timers/OnMessage bypass Suspend and need explicit guards — the codebase already applies this to KL_Hook_OnChar at 206 and 256, and HookDispatcher.Dispatch at 191).

SCOPE IS BROADER THAN THE FINDING STATES (strengthens, not weakens, the verdict): The half-life tick is NOT a lone outlier. I grepped all KL_AppendLog callers and read the timer-driven ones — KL_AV_PollVolume / KL_AV_ScanCapture (keylogger_av_state.ahk:142,152,215,222), KL_Net_ReachTick / KL_Net_VpnTick / wifi tick (keylogger_network.ahk:128,155,158,184,191), plus sensors/window_topology/mouse-park timers — ALL guard only on Keylogger.initialized and ALL funnel into the same unguarded KL_AppendLog. Within the entire modules/keylogger tree, A_IsSuspended appears ONLY in keylogger_hook.ahk. So multiple background timers (volume changes, screen-recording detection, network/VPN transitions, sensor readings) ALSO persist metrics while paused. This makes the proposed defence-in-depth fix (guard at KL_AppendLog) the correct PRIMARY fix, not merely belt-and-suspenders: a per-timer guard on KL_Roi_HalflifeTick alone leaves the privacy invariant violated by 5+ other timers.

This is a genuine privacy-invariant violation ("pause = tout éteint" / pause must silence ALL features), not a false positive. The path is not gated, not serialized away, and not Critical-protected against the timer firing.

<a id="f-keepawake-bypasses-pause"></a>
#### `keepawake-bypasses-pause` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : shortcuts (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/shortcuts/win.ahk:136-147`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/shortcuts/win.ahk:215-257`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/shortcuts/win.ahk:185-213`
- **Description** : StartActivitySimulation arms `SetTimer(SimulateActivity,…)`, `SetTimer(AwakeCheckMouseMoved,150)`, three `~*$LButton/RButton/MButton` cancel hotkeys, and an `InputHook("L0 I")` keypress detector. SimulateActivity sends `{VKFF}` and moves the cursor (MCSetPos) on each tick. None of SimulateActivity, AwakeCheckMouseMoved, AwakeReturnToOrigin, AwakeCancelOnKeypress check A_IsSuspended.
- **Cause racine** : Project invariant: native Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; SetTimers and InputHooks BYPASS it and need explicit `if A_IsSuspended` guards. Keep-awake is started via the Win+M hotkey (correctly Suspend-gated to start), but once running it lives entirely on timers + an InputHook, so pausing the driver does NOT stop the cursor jiggle, the synthetic {VKFF} sends, or the keypress/mouse cancel hooks. The InputHook even keeps observing keystrokes while paused.
- **Scénario** : User enables keep-awake (Win+M), then pauses the driver (tray Pause / Suspend) expecting ALL driver behaviour to stop. The cursor keeps teleporting around the origin every 1–5 s and {VKFF} keeps firing; the user perceives the paused driver as still 'doing things', and the mouse jumps unexpectedly during a presentation/pause.
- **Impact** : Violates the 'Pause must silence ALL features' invariant: visible cursor movement and synthetic input continue during pause; the keep-awake InputHook also continues consuming keyboard events while the user believes the driver is inert.
- **Correctif** : Add `if A_IsSuspended` early-returns at the top of SimulateActivity, AwakeCheckMouseMoved, AwakeReturnToOrigin, and AwakeCancelOnKeypress/AwakeCancelOnMouse (return without acting). Better: hook the pause transition to call StopActivitySimulation() so the InputHook and timers are fully released on pause and re-armed only if the user re-triggers Win+M after resume.
- **Test** : headless-unit: set `global ActivitySimulation := true` and a fake A_IsSuspended-equivalent flag the functions read (or stub via a wrapper), invoke SimulateActivity, and assert the MCSetPos/SendFinalResult stubs recorded zero calls while suspended. meta-static: assert win.ahk's SimulateActivity body contains an `A_IsSuspended` guard (regression for the suspend-bypass class).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source. In modules/shortcuts/win.ahk (the `if Features["shortcuts"]["move"]` block, lines 113-258) none of SimulateActivity (215), AwakeReturnToOrigin (178), AwakeCheckMouseMoved (185), AwakeCancelOnKeypress (202), AwakeCancelOnMouse (195) check A_IsSuspended. They run entirely on SetTimer(SimulateActivity), SetTimer(AwakeCheckMouseMoved,150), SetTimer(AwakeReturnToOrigin,-200) and an InputHook("L0 I"). Per the documented project invariant — confirmed by the fact that 20+ other handlers in this codebase explicitly gate with `if A_IsSuspended` (hook_dispatcher.ahk:191, keylogger_hook.ahk:206/256, prediction_engine.ahk:345, gestures.ahk:662, tooltip.ahk, etc.) — SetTimer callbacks and InputHook callbacks BYPASS native Suspend. Suspend only disarms hotkey/hotstring TRIGGERS; it does not block synthetic input emitted by already-running code.

The emitted effects are genuinely unguarded: SendFinalResult (lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:264) resolves to a raw SendInput with no A_IsSuspended check, and MCSetPos (adapters/mouse_control.ahk:65) is a raw DllCall("SetCursorPos") with no check. So once keep-awake is armed (Win+M, which is itself correctly Suspend-gated to START because it is a #-hotkey), pausing the driver leaves the SimulateActivity timer firing every 1-5s: MCSetPos teleports the cursor +-80px around the origin, AwakeReturnToOrigin moves it back 200ms later, and SendFinalResult("{VKFF}") injects a synthetic key — all while the user believes the driver is inert. The InputHook also keeps observing keystrokes. This is a textbook violation of the "Pause must silence ALL features" invariant via the exact timer/InputHook-bypass mechanism the invariant warns about.

Crucially, the project ALREADY has the correct seam for the fix but never wired keep-awake into it: Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter() (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006), called from both ToggleSuspend (2002) and the out-of-band _SuspendStateWatchdog (2022), already tears down LLM timers/tooltips on pause but does NOT call StopActivitySimulation(). The existing "pause silences Win* dispatchers" test (test_shortcuts.ahk:481) is a vacuous `AssertTrue(true, …)` placeholder and does not exercise this path, so nothing catches it today.

One nuance on the cancel hotkeys: the three `~*$LButton/RButton/MButton` Hotkey() registrations DO obey Suspend by default (they are real hotkeys, not timers), so those specific handlers would likely be disarmed during pause — the finding slightly over-claims that the mouse-cancel hotkeys keep running. But that does not change the verdict: the cursor jiggle and {VKFF} (timers) and the keypress detector (InputHook) all definitively continue, which is the user-visible harm.

<a id="f-mouse-park-distance-no-gate"></a>
#### `mouse-park-distance-no-gate` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : keylogger-sensors (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_mouse.ahk:369`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1636`
- **Description** : KL_Mouse_ParkTick (a raw SetTimer at PARK_CHECK_MS=250 ms) calls KL_BumpMouseDistance(Round(d)) (keylogger_mouse.ahk:374) on every tick BEFORE any privacy/suspend check and unconditionally — KL_BumpMouseDistance (keylogger.ahk:1636) just does `Keylogger.mouse_distance += px` with no guard at all. The park-event emission lower down does check `Keylogger.initialized` and MF_ShouldFilter, but the distance accumulation does not.
- **Cause racine** : The distance bump sits above the privacy gate in the tick body and the bump helper has no guards. The park timer is a standalone SetTimer that bypasses Suspend; even on a paused or filtered (password field / private-browsing / disabled-app) window the cursor travel is still summed into Keylogger.mouse_distance, which the next KL_FlushBuffer writes as mouse_distance_px attributed to that window/session.
- **Scénario** : User pauses the driver, or works in a password manager / InPrivate window that is on the privacy exclusion list. They believe nothing is recorded. The next typing flush still reports a non-zero mouse_distance_px gathered during the paused/excluded period, leaking activity that the privacy filter was meant to drop.
- **Impact** : Privacy filter and pause are silently incomplete for the mouse-distance metric. The distance figure is also corrupted by movement during pause, skewing the ergonomics dashboard.
- **Correctif** : Guard the distance accumulation: at the top of KL_Mouse_ParkTick add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return`, and move the KL_BumpMouseDistance call to AFTER the `if !filtered and Keylogger.initialized` gate (or evaluate MF_ShouldFilter once at the top of the tick and skip both distance and park work when filtered). Still update prev_x/prev_y so the next live tick computes a correct delta rather than a huge jump.
- **Test** : headless-unit: extract the distance-accumulation predicate into a pure helper `_KL_Mouse_ShouldAccumDistance(initialized, suspended, filtered)` and assert it returns false whenever suspended or filtered or !initialized; assert true only for (true,false,false). This pins the gate without needing a live cursor.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Read the cited code and the surrounding data flow; the finding's core mechanism holds under the AHK cooperative model.

CODE CONFIRMED:
- keylogger_mouse.ahk:364-428: KL_Mouse_ParkTick calls KL_BumpMouseDistance(Round(d)) at line 374, BEFORE the `if !filtered and Keylogger.initialized` gate at line 413. The bump is unconditional.
- keylogger.ahk:1636-1638: KL_BumpMouseDistance is `Keylogger.mouse_distance += px` with no guard at all.
- The park timer is a RAW SetTimer (keylogger_mouse.ahk:484, PARK_CHECK_MS=250), not routed through HookDispatcher. By contrast the click/scroll handlers go through HookDispatcher, which gates on A_IsSuspended (lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:191) — so under pause those are already silent, but the park-distance timer is the one mouse source that escapes Suspend. This is exactly the documented project invariant: "Native Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; SetTimers bypass it and need explicit A_IsSuspended guards."

SUSPEND LEAK — FULLY REAL. Pause in this driver is ToggleSuspend()->Suspend(-1) (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1994-2001); it does NOT call KL_Mouse_Stop()/KL_Stop() (those are full shutdowns, KL_Stop at keylogger.ahk:1565). So during pause Keylogger.initialized stays true and the park timer keeps firing every 250ms, summing cursor travel into mouse_distance. The counter is reset ONLY in KL_FlushBuffer (keylogger.ahk:718); mouse_distance is cross-time. The keystroke InputHook is gated (KL_Hook_OnChar `if A_IsSuspended return`, keylogger_hook.ahk:206), so no typing buffers and no app-switch flush runs during pause (KL_Hook_RefreshContext is only reachable from the suspended OnChar path) — the pause-accumulated distance therefore survives untouched. On un-pause, the first keystroke eventually triggers KL_FlushBuffer, which reads mouse_distance into mouse_distance_px (keylogger.ahk:705). KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618) has NO A_IsSuspended check, and at un-pause time we are no longer suspended anyway, so the entry is written. Pause leak confirmed.

PRIVACY LEAK — REAL BUT PARTIALLY MITIGATED / NARROWER than the finding states. MF_ShouldFilter (lib/metrics/metrics_filters.ahk:161) checks only the focused window (disabled apps, password field, system-auth, private browsing); it does NOT fold in suspend. Two existing flush-time gates blunt the simple case: (1) KL_AppendLog re-evaluates MF_ShouldFilter at flush time (line 636) and drops the whole typing entry if the currently focused window is filtered; (2) app-switch flushes the buffer (keylogger_hook.ahk:173). So distance gathered over a filtered window and flushed while STILL in that filtered window does NOT leak — contrary to the finding's blanket claim. The genuine residual leak is cross-window/intra-app contamination: travel summed while the cursor is over a filtered window — or while a secure field gains focus WITHIN the same app (no app-switch flush fires on field-role change) — is later attributed to a subsequent non-filtered typing flush. So the finding's mechanism is real but the privacy half is weaker than written.

SEVERITY: the finding says high; adjusting to medium. What leaks is a single coarse aggregate integer (cumulative pixel travel), never keystroke content; the privacy half is substantially mitigated by the two flush-time gates, leaving mainly cross-window contamination; the dominant concrete defect is the suspend case corrupting/contaminating one numeric column. That is a data-accuracy plus minor-privacy issue, not a high-severity content leak.

<a id="f-warmup-retry-ignores-suspend"></a>
#### `warmup-retry-ignores-suspend` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : llm-api (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:778`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:757`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/ollama_deps_checker.ahk:333`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/ollama_deps_checker.ahk:351`
- **Description** : LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryTick re-arms itself on a 5 s..60 s backoff and calls LLM_OllamaWarmup -> WinHTTP POST /api/chat. It gates on LLM_OllamaIsReady, the in-flight async count, and the tray 'enabled' flag, but NOT on A_IsSuspended. Likewise LLM_Deps_PollServerReady fires LLM_OllamaIsRunning_Async every 3 s with no suspend guard. The project invariant is explicit: 'Native Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; InputHooks, SetTimers and OnMessage handlers BYPASS it and need explicit if A_IsSuspended guards.' FirePrediction correctly guards (prediction_engine.ahk:345) but these background timers do not, and LLM_OllamaCancelWarmupRetry is only invoked on full LLM_Bridge_Stop (feature disabled), not on pause.
- **Cause racine** : SetTimer callbacks run regardless of A_IsSuspended; the warmup/deps timers were written to stop only on explicit feature-disable, not on pause, so a paused-but-enabled driver keeps generating background network traffic and timer churn.
- **Scénario** : User pauses Ergopti (Suspend) while the Ollama model has not yet warmed up (cold boot, model still loading). The warmup retry loop keeps POSTing to localhost:11434 every few seconds for the entire pause, and the deps poll (if an install is in flight) keeps probing every 3 s. The user expected pause to silence the feature entirely.
- **Impact** : Violates the hard-won 'pause = tout eteint' invariant: background HTTP, CPU wakeups, and log spam continue during pause. Not a lost-keystroke (async, off-thread) but a clear policy regression and minor battery/privacy cost.
- **Correctif** : Add 'if A_IsSuspended { re-arm a short check-back timer and return }' at the top of LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryTick and _LLM_Deps_OnPollProbeResult/LLM_Deps_PollServerReady, mirroring the FirePrediction guard. Re-arm rather than cancel so warmup resumes when unpaused.
- **Test** : headless-unit not feasible for A_IsSuspended toggling; use a meta/static test asserting LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryTick and LLM_Deps_PollServerReady source bodies contain an 'A_IsSuspended' guard (same pattern as the existing FirePrediction suspend-guard regression test).  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the actual source. The two leaked timers are real and off-thread (not a lost-keystroke). (1) LLM_Ollama_WarmupRetryTick (api_ollama.ahk:778) gates on LLM_OllamaIsReady(), the in-flight async count (_LLM_Ollama_Async.Count), and the tray "enabled" flag (785-788) — but NOT A_IsSuspended. It re-arms by calling LLM_OllamaScheduleWarmupRetry (757), which (also un-suspend-gated) calls LLM_OllamaWarmup -> a live WinHttp.WinHttpRequest POST to LLM_OLLAMA_BASE_URL "/api/chat" (716). (2) LLM_Deps_PollServerReady (ollama_deps_checker.ahk:333) fires LLM_OllamaIsRunning_Async every 3 s with no suspend guard, and _LLM_Deps_OnPollProbeResult (351) has none either. The decisive confirmation is the suspend reactor: Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006-2010) cancels ONLY the prediction debounce timer (LLM_Engine_CancelTimer) plus hides tooltips — it does not call LLM_OllamaCancelWarmupRetry() nor stop _LLM_Deps_PollTimer. LLM_OllamaCancelWarmupRetry is invoked only on full feature-disable (LLM_Bridge_Stop, llm_bridge.ahk:156), warmup success (api_ollama.ahk:746), and deps user-cancel — never on pause. So a paused-but-enabled driver with a cold/loading model keeps POSTing to localhost:11434 on the 5..60 s backoff for the whole pause, and a paused-during-install driver keeps async-probing every 3 s. This is exactly the documented "Native Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; SetTimers BYPASS it" trap. FirePrediction (prediction_engine.ahk:345) correctly guards with `if A_IsSuspended return`, proving the intended invariant these two timers violate. AHK concurrency model does not save this path: the timers fire regardless of A_IsSuspended (Suspend does not pause SetTimer), and nothing serializes or gates them on pause. Not a false positive.

<a id="f-timer-scheduler-no-pause-guard"></a>
#### `timer-scheduler-no-pause-guard` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : adapters-os (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/timer_scheduler.ahk:159`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/timer_scheduler.ahk:160`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/timer_scheduler.ahk:143`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/tests/test_timer_scheduler.ahk:233`
- **Description** : The repeating wrapper _Repeating (timer_scheduler.ahk:159-167) and the one-shot wrapper _OneShot (143-156) invoke the user callback with only a `Fired` check — neither consults `A_IsSuspended`. SetTimer callbacks bypass native Suspend, so a TimerEvery(...) registered through this adapter keeps invoking its callback at full cadence after the user pauses the driver. This is the exact failure mode the project invariants single out: 'Pause must silence ALL features. Native Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; ... SetTimers ... BYPASS it and need explicit if A_IsSuspended guards.'
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 `Suspend`/`A_IsSuspended` only disarms hotkeys and hotstrings. A SetTimer keeps firing its bound function regardless of suspend state. The adapter's wrappers gate only on the per-handle `Fired` flag, never on `A_IsSuspended`, so paused-state work (network ticks, ingest, polling) would continue.
- **Scénario** : A future (or refactored) domain module schedules background work via the TimerScheduler port — e.g. the keylogger ingest tick or LLM debounce migrated to TimerEvery. The user pauses Ergopti+ (expecting total silence, e.g. while gaming or in a secure app). The repeating callback keeps running: still flushing keystroke buffers to disk, still polling, still mutating shared state — exactly what pause is supposed to stop.
- **Impact** : Silent violation of the strongest project invariant. In the keylogger/LLM case it means PII capture and disk writes continue during a pause the user believes is total. Today it is LATENT (production uses raw SetTimer with its own guards, e.g. prediction_engine.ahk:345), but the adapter is the sanctioned API and the test suite already declares the invariant required — the first migration to the port reintroduces the bug with no warning.
- **Correctif** : Add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` at the top of both `_Repeating` (before the `Fired` check) and `_OneShot` (before invoking BoundFn), mirroring HookDispatcher.Dispatch and prediction_engine's guard. For one-shot timers, prefer re-arming or dropping per the port's documented semantics rather than silently consuming the firing; at minimum repeating timers must not invoke the callback under suspend.
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/test_timer_scheduler.ahk: replace the placeholder `_TSTest_EveryUnderPauseNoFire` (currently AssertTrue(true)) with a real test that sets a test seam for A_IsSuspended (or stubs a `_TimerAdapterIsSuspended()` indirection), arms TimerEvery, invokes H["Fn"]() and AssertEquals the callback counter stays 0 while suspended and increments once suspend clears. Also a meta-static test asserting the source of timer_scheduler.ahk contains `A_IsSuspended` inside both wrapper functions.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Read the actual source. The finding is accurate, not hallucinated. _Repeating (timer_scheduler.ahk:160-167) gates the user callback only on BoundHandle["Fired"]; _OneShot (143-156) does bookkeeping then calls BoundFn() unconditionally. Neither consults A_IsSuspended.
