Under the AHK v2 model this is a genuine pause leak: SetTimer pseudo-threads are scheduled by the timer subsystem independently of the hotkey/hotstring suspend state, so A_IsSuspended (set by Suspend) does NOT stop a SetTimer-bound function from firing. The project invariant ("Pause must silence ALL features... SetTimers BYPASS native Suspend and need explicit if A_IsSuspended guards") is exactly this. Two production precedents confirm the intended pattern: lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:187-192 (HookDispatcher.Dispatch starts with `if A_IsSuspended return`, comment "pause = tout eteint") and modules/llm/prediction_engine.ahk:345-346 (LLM_Engine_FirePrediction, a SetTimer callback, returns early on A_IsSuspended). The adapter is the only timer path that omits the guard.

However, the impact is LATENT today, which the finding itself concedes. grep across the whole windows tree shows the only non-test references to TimerEvery/TimerAfter are lib/healthcheck.ahk:65 (an existence spec) and the test files. No production domain module schedules through the port — production timers (e.g. the LLM debounce) still use raw SetTimer with their own A_IsSuspended guard. The adapter IS #Include'd in the live entrypoint (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:131), so the API ships and is sanctioned, but nothing live can leak through it yet. The declared regression tests are placebos: _TSTest_EveryUnderPauseNoFire (line 232-237), _TSTest_PcallCallbackEmitsToErrorsSinkUnderPause, and _TSTest_HighVolumePauseReinitDiagnostic all just AssertTrue(true) — the invariant is asserted in a comment but never actually exercised, so a real caller would reintroduce the leak with a fully green suite.

Verdict: real bug, real invariant violation in the sanctioned API, but currently unreachable in production. Not a false positive (the path is genuinely unguarded and SetTimer genuinely bypasses Suspend), but not "high" either since no user-facing harm exists in the shipped build.

<a id="f-keylogger-watchers-bypass-pause"></a>
#### `keylogger-watchers-bypass-pause` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-lifecycle-leaks (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_watchers.ahk:323`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_watchers.ahk:342-343`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:618-654`
- **Description** : The project invariant is explicit: native Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; SetTimer and OnMessage handlers BYPASS it and need explicit `if A_IsSuspended` guards. The keylogger HOOK callbacks (keylogger_hook.ahk:206/256) and HookDispatcher.Dispatch are correctly gated. But the watcher's idle_check_timer (keylogger_watchers.ahk:323), and its WM_WTSSESSION_CHANGE / WM_POWERBROADCAST OnMessage handlers (lines 342-343), have NO A_IsSuspended guard, and the sink KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618) has none either (it checks Keylogger.initialized and MF_ShouldFilter, never A_IsSuspended). So session_end / idle_start / idle_end / lock / sleep events are still appended to today.log + data.sql while the user has paused the driver.
- **Cause racine** : Suspend toggles A_IsSuspended and disarms registered hotkeys/hotstrings, but a repeating SetTimer and OnMessage callbacks keep firing. KL_AppendLog is the common write sink and has no pause gate, so any keylogger event source that is not itself pause-gated leaks events into the metrics store during pause.
- **Scénario** : User pauses the driver (AltGr+Enter / tray Suspend) intending « tout éteint », then locks the workstation or lets it idle. WM_WTSSESSION_CHANGE / the idle timer fire and KL_LogSession('idle_start'/'session_end') / lock events get written to data.sql even though metrics should be silent. The pause did not actually silence the metrics feature.
- **Impact** : Privacy/correctness regression against the documented invariant: paused state still records activity transitions to the on-disk metrics store. Lower severity than keystroke capture (no typed text is logged from these handlers), but it is exactly the class of pause-bypass the project memory flags as critical-when-regressed.
- **Correctif** : Add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` at the top of the idle_check_timer callback and both OnMessage handlers in keylogger_watchers.ahk, OR — more robustly and in one place — add an `if A_IsSuspended` guard at the top of KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:618) so EVERY keylogger event source is silenced under pause uniformly (matching how HookDispatcher.Dispatch centralises the guard). The central guard is preferable; document that KL_Stop's final flush path must run BEFORE/independent of it so a pause does not block shutdown flushing.
- **Test** : headless-unit: KL_Init on a temp dir, set A_IsSuspended via Suspend(On) (or stub the guard), call KL_LogSession('idle_start'), KL_FlushBuffer/KL_IngestOnce, and assert no idle_start row was written; then Suspend(Off) and assert a subsequent KL_LogSession('idle_start') IS written. Encodes the invariant 'paused = no keylogger writes' at the sink level.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against source. The asymmetry the finding describes is real and unintentional. keylogger_hook.ahk explicitly guards both InputHook callbacks with `if A_IsSuspended\n return` (lines 206, 256) and HookDispatcher.Dispatch gates at line 191; the comment at hook line 204-205 states the keylogger "records nothing while the script is paused." But the watcher producers have NO such guard: KL_Watchers_IdleTick (timer body), KL_Watchers_OnSessionChange (line 285) and KL_Watchers_OnPowerBroadcast (line 298). A grep proves A_IsSuspended appears ONLY in keylogger_hook.ahk across the entire keylogger subsystem.

The watchers stay armed under pause. KL_Watchers_Start arms the SetTimer (keylogger_watchers.ahk:323) and the two OnMessage handlers (lines 342-343) once, at init (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1039). They are torn down only by KL_Watchers_Stop, which is called only from KL_Stop (keylogger.ahk:1573) — the metrics-OFF/shutdown toggle, never from ToggleSuspend. Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006-2010) only hides tooltips and cancels LLM timers; it does nothing to the keylogger. Per the AHK model and the project invariant, native Suspend disarms only hotkeys/hotstrings — SetTimer and OnMessage threads keep firing. These are exactly the two bypass sources the project memory flags.

The writes reach disk. KL_LogSession (keylogger.ahk:898) and KL_LogSystemEvent (line 761) both route through KL_AppendLog (line 618), which gates only on Keylogger.initialized and MF_ShouldFilter() and then pushes to _pending_entries + writes today.log. I read MF_ShouldFilter (metrics_filters.ahk:161) — it checks disabled-apps, password fields, system-auth dialogs, and private browsing only; it does NOT consult A_IsSuspended. So there is no upstream gate that could make this a false positive: idle_start/idle_end/session_end/lock/unlock/sleep/wake events genuinely land in today.log and the SQL ingest queue while the driver is paused.

Nothing serializes or Critical-protects this away — there is no concurrency-model escape hatch. The bug is real.

<a id="f-oneshotshift-lalt-lshift-stuck"></a>
#### `oneshotshift-lalt-lshift-stuck` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : tap-holds (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/lalt.ahk:98`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/lalt.ahk:101`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/rctrl.ahk:126`
- **Description** : In lalt.ahk block 4.1 (tap=one_shot_shift) the handler does TextPressKey('LAlt','Up'); OneShotShift(); TextPressKey('LShift','Down'); KeyWait('SC038'); TextPressKey('LShift','Up'). The LShift Down is held for the entire duration of the physical LAlt press via an unbounded KeyWait('SC038') (no timeout). If that KeyWait never returns (key-up event lost to LowLevelHooksTimeout under load, Suspend toggled while LAlt is physically held so the up-event is consumed natively, or the thread interrupted), LShift remains Down. rctrl.ahk 7.3 has the analogous pattern but at least uses KeyWait('SC11D','U').
- **Cause racine** : A modifier (LShift) is pressed Down with no try/finally guarantee that the paired Up runs, and the release is gated on an unbounded KeyWait of a *different* physical key (SC038). AHK custom-combination prefix and Suspend cannot clear a synthetically-pressed modifier; only the explicit TextPressKey('LShift','Up') clears it, and that line is skippable.
- **Scénario** : User taps LAlt (one_shot_shift) intending to capitalise the next letter, but mid-press the OS steals focus (UAC prompt) or the user hits the global Suspend hotkey while still physically holding LAlt. The SC038 up-event is processed while the handler is suspended/blocked, KeyWait sees the key already up or never fires its return path cleanly, and {LShift Up} is skipped — LShift is now stuck Down and every key is shifted.
- **Impact** : Stuck Shift after a one-shot-shift press near a focus change or suspend toggle; user must manually tap Shift to recover. Particularly bad because one_shot_shift is a high-frequency tap.
- **Correctif** : Guard the LShift Down/Up pair with try/finally: TextPressKey('LShift','Down'); try { KeyWait('SC038') } finally { TextPressKey('LShift','Up') }. Add a timeout to the KeyWait so a lost up-event cannot block forever (e.g. KeyWait('SC038','U T5')) and still release LShift. Apply the same finally guard to rctrl.ahk 7.3.
- **Test** : headless-unit: extract the LShift-arm/release sequence into a small testable helper that takes a 'keyUp arrived?' predicate and asserts that the release call is made in BOTH the normal-return and the timeout/exception paths (stub TextPressKey to record Down/Up counts; assert Down count == Up count after a simulated interruption).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code. lalt.ahk 4.1 (lines 86-103) is a BARE `SC038::` (not `$SC038::`) under `#HotIf TapHoldTapAction==one_shot_shift`. The vulnerable window is lines 100-102: `TextPressKey("LShift","Down"); KeyWait("SC038"); TextPressKey("LShift","Up")`. For LShift to latch, line 102 must be skipped, which requires the thread parked at line 101 to never resume — i.e. `KeyWait("SC038")` must block forever, OR an exception/abort to occur between 100 and 102.

Evaluating the finding's three scenarios against the AHK cooperative-thread model:

1) "Up-event lost to LowLevelHooksTimeout under load" — REAL but narrow. If AHK's hook never observes SC038 up, the no-option `KeyWait` (default = wait for logical up) blocks indefinitely and LShift stays down. Note that by line 101, `OneShotShift()` (one_shot_shift.ahk:24) has already blocked up to 2s in `InputHook.Wait()` while the user types the next char, so in the common case LAlt is already physically up and `KeyWait` returns immediately. The stuck case needs the up dropped at exactly the wrong instant with no later up arriving — a genuine but load/hardware-dependent edge.

2) "Suspend toggled while LAlt held" (the finding's headline scenario) — LARGELY A FALSE ALARM. `ToggleSuspend` (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1994) calls `Suspend(-1)`. `KeyWait` is a rest point, so the Suspend hotkey CAN interrupt the parked `SC038::` thread — but `Suspend` does NOT kill a running pseudo-thread; it only disarms FUTURE hotkey activations. After the Suspend thread ends, control returns to the still-parked `KeyWait("SC038")`; when LAlt is physically released the wait returns and line 102 executes. The `{LShift Up}` is therefore NOT skipped — it merely runs later. The documented prefix-latch class (PROJECT_MEMORY feedback-ahk-suspend-prefix-latch) is explicitly unique to AHK's custom-combination PREFIX-DOWN FLAG, which is separate from GetKeyState and cannot be cleared synthetically; an ordinary synthetic `{LShift Down}` is NOT a prefix flag and IS cleared by a normal `{LShift Up}`. So the finding conflates two different mechanisms.

3) "Thread interrupted / exception between Down and Up" — essentially impossible: no code that can throw runs between the bare `KeyWait` and the next `TextPressKey`. And the global `OnError(ErgoptiGlobalErrorHandler)` (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:74-99) releases stuck modifiers on any uncaught error — though note it checks `GetKeyState(ModKey,"P")` (PHYSICAL), so it would NOT clean a synthetically-held LShift; that is a real (separate) gap but not reachable here.

Two of the finding's supporting claims are wrong: (a) the relative comparison "rctrl at least uses U" is not meaningful — `KeyWait("SC038")` and `KeyWait("SC11D","U")` BOTH wait for key-up; the no-option default already means "wait for release". (b) rctrl.ahk 7.3 (lines 119-129) is actually SAFER than lalt 4.1, not "analogous": it arms LShift only AFTER the tap/hold discrimination (line 120 `KeyWait(...,"T...")`), so LShift is held only on a confirmed long-press, and it waits on the SAME physical key (SC11D), not a different one.

Net: a real but narrow latch risk exists ONLY via mechanism (1) — an unbounded `KeyWait` with no timeout holding a synthetic modifier. The finding's framing overstates it (Suspend scenario refuted; severity inflated). It is worth fixing defensively and for consistency with the team's own convention (space.ahk uses `U T2`; rctrl uses `U`), but it is not a high-likelihood stuck-Shift bug.

<a id="f-deadkey-inputhook-no-timeout-no-suspend-guard"></a>
#### `deadkey-inputhook-no-timeout-no-suspend-guard` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : layout (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:315`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:323`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2011`
- **Description** : DeadKey(Mapping) sets InDeadKeySequence := true, creates a suppressing InputHook (InputHook("L1", endkeys) — no "V" flag, so collected keys are consumed) and calls ih.Wait() with no timeout argument and no ih.Timeout set. There is no A_IsSuspended check anywhere in DeadKey, and Ergopti_OnSuspendResume() resets the prefix buffer but never InDeadKeySequence nor any pending dead-key hook.
- **Cause racine** : AHK Suspend disarms hotkeys/hotstrings but does NOT stop a live InputHook (per the project invariant about hooks bypassing Suspend). ih.Wait() with no timeout blocks the dead-key pseudo-thread indefinitely; the global InDeadKeySequence stays true until a follow-up key arrives. The InputHook keeps suppressing the next non-end key regardless of suspend state.
- **Scénario** : (a) Latch: user presses the circumflex dead key (SC02B) then gets distracted and never presses a follow-up. Minutes later they return and press a letter — that letter is silently swallowed by the still-waiting InputHook and emitted as a dead-key combination instead of the intended character (a lost/transformed keystroke). (b) Suspend leak: user starts a dead-key sequence, then pauses the driver via the tray icon (mouse, so no key cancels the wait). The driver is now 'paused' but the next physical key the user types is still captured and remapped by the dead-key InputHook, violating the 'pause silences ALL features' invariant.
- **Impact** : A keystroke after an abandoned dead key is lost/transformed; and the dead-key path can act while the user believes the driver is fully paused. Narrow but real, and it matches two of the explicitly-called-out invariant classes (lost keystroke + pause leak).
- **Correctif** : Set a bounded ih.Timeout (e.g. a few seconds) or pass a timeout to ih.Wait(); on timeout, set InDeadKeySequence := false and emit the bare dead-key glyph (or nothing). Add an A_IsSuspended check at the top of DeadKey() to no-op when suspended, and stop/cancel any in-flight dead-key InputHook in Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter() while clearing InDeadKeySequence in Ergopti_OnSuspendResume(). Keep a reference to the live ih so resume can call ih.Stop().
- **Test** : headless-unit: refactor DeadKey to accept an injected input source (or expose ih creation through a seam) so a test can simulate 'no follow-up + timeout' and assert InDeadKeySequence returns to false and the bare glyph is emitted. A complementary meta-static test can assert DeadKey's source contains an ih.Timeout / Wait(timeout) and an A_IsSuspended guard, so a regression that removes the bound fails the suite.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code. The finding is PARTLY right and one of its two proposed fixes is dead code.

ENTRY PATH (refutes part of the claim): DeadKey() is ONLY ever reached from Hotkey() callbacks — the base SC01B/SC02B handlers (modules/layout.ahk:599-608), the caps/shift symbol maps (lib/layout/layout_shift_caps.ahk:97,99), and the AltGr layer registered via Hotkey("SC138 & "...) with AltGrShiftDispatch -> DeadKey.Bind (lib/layout/layout_altgr.ahk:78,150-176,286-299). Hotkeys ARE disarmed by Suspend(-1). Therefore DeadKey() can never be NEWLY ENTERED while suspended, and the finding's proposed "A_IsSuspended check at the top of DeadKey()" is dead code that can never fire. That sub-claim is a false positive.

SCENARIO (a) latch/lost-keystroke — REAL but largely inherent: layout.ahk:318-323 builds InputHook("L1", endkeys) with no "V" (so the collected key is consumed/suppressed) and calls ih.Wait() with no timeout and no ih.Timeout. With no follow-up key, the dead-key pseudo-thread parks in ih.Wait() indefinitely, InDeadKeySequence stays true, and the next character key (minutes later) is swallowed and re-emitted as a dead-key combination. This is a genuine transformed keystroke — but it is exactly how a dead key is contractually defined (a circumflex on AZERTY also waits indefinitely and transforms the next key). So (a) is a UX papercut (no abandonment timeout), not a data-loss bug; the keystroke is transformed, not dropped, and one keystroke unparks it.

SCENARIO (b) suspend leak — REAL and correctly identified, this is the load-bearing finding. ih.Wait() pumps messages, so while the dead-key thread is parked the 500 ms _SuspendStateWatchdog (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:373) and a tray ToggleSuspend (2-2005) CAN still fire and interrupt it. They run Suspend(-1) + Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter() (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006-2010), which hides tooltips and cancels the LLM timer but NEVER stops the live local ih nor clears InDeadKeySequence. Per the AHK model and the explicit project invariant, an active InputHook BYPASSES Suspend — it keeps collecting. So: press circumflex (thread parks, ih live) -> click tray to pause via mouse (no key cancels the wait) -> type a letter: that physical key is captured & suppressed by the still-live dead-key ih, which un-parks the thread and emits the transformed glyph while the driver is believed paused. That is a true violation of "pause silences ALL features." The hook is a local with no stored reference (layout.ahk:318) and there is no global live-InputHook registry, confirming nothing in the suspend path can reach it.

Net: the bug is real (scenario b), but narrow: it needs the uncommon sequence start-deadkey -> pause-by-mouse-without-cancel -> type, recovery is one keystroke, and no data is destroyed. Scenario (a) is mostly inherent dead-key semantics. Hence I adjust severity down from medium to low.

<a id="f-pointer-watch-timer-bypasses-suspend"></a>
#### `pointer-watch-timer-bypasses-suspend` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : llm-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:436-473`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006-2014`
- **Description** : _LLM_PointerWatch_OnMoveTick (SetTimer every 50 ms) and the ~LButton/~Wheel pass-through hotkeys have no A_IsSuspended guard. _LLM_PointerWatch_Stop is only called from LLM_Bridge_Stop, never from Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter. So while the driver is paused the 50 ms MouseGetPos poll keeps firing.
- **Cause racine** : SetTimer and Hotkey callbacks bypass native Suspend. The move-tick relies on _LLM_Bridge_PredictionShown() returning false during pause (because Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter hid the tooltip) to avoid actually dismissing, but the timer body itself (MouseGetPos + branch) still executes 20x/second indefinitely while paused.
- **Scénario** : User pauses the driver and leaves it paused for hours. A MouseGetPos poll continues firing every 50 ms the whole time, contending — however slightly — for the message pump that also serves the keyboard hook when the user unpauses or uses other apps.
- **Impact** : Minor wasted CPU and message-pump contention during pause; no functional dismissal occurs (guarded by PredictionShown), so impact is low. Still a clean violation of 'pause = tout éteint' in spirit.
- **Correctif** : Add `if A_IsSuspended return` at the top of _LLM_PointerWatch_OnMoveTick, OR call _LLM_PointerWatch_Stop() from Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter and _LLM_PointerWatch_Start() from Ergopti_OnSuspendResume (only when the bridge is active).
- **Test** : meta-static: assert _LLM_PointerWatch_OnMoveTick body contains an A_IsSuspended early-return (tests/meta/), consistent with the other suspend-invariant meta tests; or headless-unit stubbing MouseGetPos to count calls and asserting it is not invoked while the harness sets A_IsSuspended.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-dequeue-poll-no-suspend-guard"></a>
#### `dequeue-poll-no-suspend-guard` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : tooltip-widget (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/tooltip.ahk:83`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/tooltip.ahk:131`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/tooltip.ahk:718`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:371`
- **Description** : _TooltipDequeuePollFn runs as a fixed 100 ms repeating SetTimer registered at boot (TooltipDequeueInit). It checks _TooltipDequeueItems and the generation counters but never checks A_IsSuspended. When an expired row is found it calls _TooltipDequeueRebuild, which calls _TooltipPresentStack -> Row.Gui.Show(...) and _TooltipRevealSurfaces() -> GR_Show — i.e. it builds and reveals windows. Neither _TooltipDequeueRebuild nor _TooltipPresentStack guards A_IsSuspended.
- **Cause racine** : SetTimer callbacks BYPASS native Suspend in AHK v2 (Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings). The project explicitly requires explicit 'if A_IsSuspended' guards on every SetTimer/OnMessage/InputHook. The driver's own ToggleSuspend tears the dequeue down synchronously via Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter -> TooltipHide, but suspend toggled OUTSIDE ToggleSuspend (the exact scenario the 500 ms _SuspendStateWatchdog exists for, per ErgoptiPlus.ahk:368-373) is only reacted to after the watchdog interval. The 100 ms poll therefore fires up to ~5 times inside the watchdog gap and can rebuild/reveal a tooltip while A_IsSuspended is true.
- **Scénario** : User types a trigger that shows a multi-row hotstring preview with mixed per-row delays (the dequeue path is active). Before the rows expire, the user pauses the driver via a path other than the tray/gesture ToggleSuspend (e.g. a native/global Suspend binding or programmatic suspend). For up to half a second the expired-row destack still fires and a tooltip flashes/redraws over the editor even though the driver is supposed to be fully silent — 'pause = AHK éteint' is violated.
- **Impact** : Visible tooltip activity during pause; contradicts the critical 'pause silences ALL features' invariant. Cosmetic rather than data-losing, but it is a regression of a stated hard invariant and the fix is one line.
- **Correctif** : Add 'if A_IsSuspended { return }' at the very top of _TooltipDequeuePollFn (mirroring TooltipShow:277 and WPMWidget_Tick:857). Optionally also early-out in _TooltipDequeueRebuild for defence in depth. Leaving the items in place (rather than clearing them) is fine — the items will simply be re-evaluated on resume, or torn down by the watchdog's TooltipHide.
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub A_IsSuspended via a test flag (or assert statically). Best as a headless-unit test that sets _TooltipDequeueItems to an array with an already-expired ExpireMs, sets a global is-suspended flag the function consults, calls _TooltipDequeuePollFn, and asserts no rebuild side effect occurred (e.g. a spy counter on _TooltipDequeueRebuild stays 0). If A_IsSuspended cannot be forced in-process, add a meta/static test asserting _TooltipDequeuePollFn's source contains an 'A_IsSuspended' guard.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source. (1) `TooltipDequeueInit()` (tooltip.ahk:137-139) arms `SetTimer(_TooltipDequeuePollFn, 100)` unconditionally at boot (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:366) — it is a persistent process-lifetime timer, not armed per cycle. (2) `_TooltipDequeuePollFn` (83-132) checks `_TooltipDequeueItems`, `IsObject`, and the generation counters but has NO `A_IsSuspended` check; on an expired row it calls `_TooltipDequeueRebuild(RebuildItems)` (line 131). (3) `_TooltipDequeueRebuild` (718) → `_TooltipPresentStack` (744) → `Row.Gui.Show(...)` (702) and `_TooltipRevealSurfaces()` (713), which does `ShowWindow(...,4)` + `UpdateWindow` + `GR_Show` (652-660) — i.e. it actually paints windows to screen. A grep for `A_IsSuspended` in tooltip.ahk returns only lines 277, 1455, 1565; NONE are in the rebuild/present path. The rebuild path does not route through `TooltipShow`, so the line-277 guard and the line-290 `LLM_TooltipOwnsSurface()` bail never intercept it. Per the AHK v2 model, SetTimer callbacks run on the main pseudo-thread and BYPASS native Suspend (Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings) — the project codifies this with explicit guards at TooltipShow:277 and WPMWidget_Tick:857. The timing window is genuine: suspend entered via `ToggleSuspend` tears the tooltip down synchronously through `Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter → TooltipHide` (2006-2009), but suspend toggled OUTSIDE `ToggleSuspend` is only reacted to by `_SuspendStateWatchdog`, which polls every 500 ms (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:371-373). In that up-to-500 ms gap the 100 ms poll fires up to ~5 times and, if a row's `ExpireMs` passes, rebuilds and reveals a tooltip while `A_IsSuspended` is true. This is a real, reachable violation of the stated 'pause = AHK éteint' invariant. The only thing I dispute is severity weighting: the concrete effect is a bounded (≤500 ms) transient cosmetic flash with no data loss, in a narrow scenario (active multi-row dequeue cycle coinciding with an external suspend), so raw user impact is low even though it breaches a hard invariant and the mandatory 'guard every SetTimer callback' convention.

<a id="f-health-probe-timer-not-suspend-guarded"></a>
#### `health-probe-timer-not-suspend-guarded` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tray-llm (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/init.ahk:147`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/actions.ahk:203-224`
- **Description** : LLM_Tray_Init installs SetTimer(_LLM_Tray_FireHealthProbe, 10000). _LLM_Tray_FireHealthProbe gates on backend==ollama, _LLM_Tray["enabled"], and a 3s throttle, but has NO `if A_IsSuspended` guard. Per the project invariant, native Suspend disarms hotkeys/hotstrings but SetTimer callbacks bypass it; pause is supposed to silence ALL features. So while the user has paused the whole driver, this timer keeps firing an async WinHTTP /api/version probe to localhost every 10s and, on a status flip, calls LLM_Tray_Build() (a full menu rebuild) during pause.
- **Cause racine** : Missing A_IsSuspended guard on a SetTimer callback that performs network I/O and can trigger a menu rebuild; SetTimer is not affected by Suspend().
- **Scénario** : User pauses the driver (e.g. to play a game / use a conflicting app). Every 10s the paused driver still opens a WinHTTP connection to Ollama and, if the daemon's reachability flips, rebuilds the tray menu. Behaviour during pause should be inert. Low severity because the probe is async (no keystroke block) and harmless, but it violates the stated 'pause silences everything' invariant and wastes a connection + a rebuild during pause.
- **Impact** : Network activity and a possible full tray-menu rebuild occur while the user believes the driver is fully paused — minor resource use and an invariant violation that future audits/tests assert against.
- **Correctif** : Add `if A_IsSuspended return` at the top of _LLM_Tray_FireHealthProbe (and skip the LLM_Tray_Build() in _LLM_Tray_OnHealthProbeDone when A_IsSuspended), matching the pattern already used in hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk / tooltip.ahk / keylogger_hook.ahk.
- **Test** : headless-unit mirroring the existing pause-guard skeleton tests (e.g. test_hotstring_engine.ahk pattern): set a fake A_IsSuspended sentinel, call _LLM_Tray_FireHealthProbe, and assert no probe was dispatched (inject the async-probe function and record call count == 0). meta-static: assert actions.ahk's _LLM_Tray_FireHealthProbe body contains an A_IsSuspended early-return.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-no-suspend-guard-on-background-tick-and-focus-poll"></a>
#### `no-suspend-guard-on-background-tick-and-focus-poll` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : updater-infra (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:1272`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/process_lifecycle.ahk:163`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/process_lifecycle.ahk:128`
- **Description** : Updater_BackgroundTick (a SetTimer callback) and PLC_Poll (a SetTimer callback firing every 250ms) have no 'if A_IsSuspended return' guard. The project invariant is explicit: native Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; SetTimer callbacks BYPASS it and need an explicit A_IsSuspended guard. The codebase enforces this everywhere else (hook_dispatcher.ahk:191, prediction_engine.ahk:345, keylogger_hook.ahk:206, gestures.ahk:662, tooltip.ahk, wpm_widget.ahk) but these two timers were missed.
- **Cause racine** : SetTimer callbacks are interruptions scheduled by the AHK timer subsystem and are not gated by A_IsSuspended. When the user pauses the driver, PLC_Poll keeps firing every 250ms (WinGetProcessName/WinGetTitle + fanning out to focus callbacks), and Updater_BackgroundTick keeps dispatching network checks and can still pop a TrayTip and rebuild the tray menu via _Updater_HandleBackgroundResult.
- **Scénario** : User pauses ErgoptiPlus (Suspend) expecting ALL features silent — e.g. to use a conflicting app or to stop keystroke observation. PLC_Poll continues firing focus callbacks (which downstream feed the keylogger's app-switch events), and the updater can still surface a 'new version' TrayTip and mutate the tray menu while paused. The pause invariant ('Pause must silence ALL features') is violated.
- **Impact** : Privacy/behavioral leak under pause: focus changes keep being observed and fanned out; update notifications keep popping. Directly contradicts a documented critical invariant.
- **Correctif** : Add 'if A_IsSuspended return' at the top of PLC_Poll (after re-arm is not needed since SetTimer is periodic) and at the top of Updater_BackgroundTick AFTER re-arming the next tick (so the loop survives pause and resumes cleanly) — i.e. re-arm the timer, then 'if A_IsSuspended return' before the network dispatch. For PLC_Poll, simply early-return so no callbacks fire while suspended.
- **Test** : headless-unit: a test that sets a simulated suspended flag and asserts PLC_Poll fires zero focus callbacks and Updater_BackgroundTick performs no fetch/TrayTip. Since A_IsSuspended cannot be forced in-process, mirror the existing pause-skeleton tests (test_hotstrings_full.ahk:421 style) as a meta/static assertion that both functions contain an 'A_IsSuspended' guard in their body.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : The finding bundles two claims; one is a false positive and one is a real but over-rated gap. I read the actual cited code.

PLC HALF = FALSE POSITIVE. The finding's scenario ("PLC_Poll keeps firing every 250ms, fanning out to focus callbacks which feed the keylogger's app-switch events") cannot occur because the PLC_Poll timer is NEVER ARMED in production. PLC_Poll only runs after PLC_Start() calls SetTimer(PLC_Poll, 250) (process_lifecycle.ahk:128). I grepped the whole tree: PLC_Start is called nowhere in production. ErgoptiPlus.ahk has ZERO references to PLC_Start/PLC_OnFocusChange/PLC_Poll. The only non-test reference is healthcheck.ahk:61, which is merely a Map spec-string list (Specs["process_lifecycle"] := ["PLC_Start","PLC_Stop"]) used by the adapter-compliance check — it never invokes the function. Likewise PLC_OnFocusChange is never called in production, so PLC_FocusCallbacks (process_lifecycle.ahk:38) is permanently []; even if the timer fired, the for-loop at :171 would iterate over an empty array — zero side effects, zero callbacks. The downstream the finding worries about — the active-app cache feeding shortcuts/keylogger — is lib/active_app_cache.ahk, a wholly independent lazy on-demand cache (consulted per-keystroke, refreshed via WinGet on a 100ms TTL), NOT driven by PLC_Poll. So the privacy/keylogger-leak narrative for PLC has no basis in the actual wiring. Under the AHK model the bug is moot: an un-armed timer is not an interruption source.

UPDATER HALF = REAL but LOW severity. Updater_BackgroundTick (updater.ahk:1272) IS armed at boot (Updater_StartBackgroundChecks at ErgoptiPlus.ahk:599) and has no 'if A_IsSuspended' guard. It re-arms, then fires _Updater_FetchLatestJsonAsync (truly async WinHTTP via WinHttpRequest.Open(...,true) + WaitForResponse(0) poll loop at :449-489 — so NO main-thread block, NO lost-keystroke risk; the user's #1 concern is not implicated). On a genuinely-new, not-yet-notified release, _Updater_HandleBackgroundResult (:1291) rebuilds the tray menu (SetTimer((*) => initMenu(), -50) at :1318) and pops a TrayTip (:1321). That IS an observable side effect under pause, so it is a legitimate pause-invariant gap per PROJECT_MEMORY.md:880 ("every InputHook/timer/OnMessage callback that produces an observable side effect MUST early-return on A_IsSuspended"). But it is narrow: deduped by LAST_NOTIFIED_TAG, fires at most once per new release, and the memory's own scoping (line 880) explicitly leaves a NOT-yet-guarded set without flagging the updater as critical.

NET: the finding's 'high' severity rests on the fabricated PLC privacy/keylogger leak. The real residual (updater TrayTip/menu under pause) is low-to-medium, info/cosmetic, no keystroke loss. I mark uncertain (not false-positive) because half the finding is a genuine gap worth fixing, but the headline claim and severity are wrong.

<a id="f-llm-pointer-watch-not-stopped-on-suspend"></a>
#### `llm-pointer-watch-not-stopped-on-suspend` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : e2e-suspend-pause (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:390`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:407`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:436`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006`
- **Description** : _LLM_PointerWatch_Start arms a recurring SetTimer(_LLM_PointerWatch_MoveFn, _LLM_POINTER_POLL_MS) plus a set of ~9 pass-through mouse Hotkeys (~LButton etc.). On suspend, Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006) calls LLM_Tooltip_Hide(true) and LLM_Engine_CancelTimer() but never calls _LLM_PointerWatch_Stop(). LLM_Tooltip_Hide does not stop the watcher either (it only hides the GUI and emits a dismissed event). So the move-tick keeps running every poll interval while paused, calling MouseGetPos and the prediction-shown check. _LLM_PointerWatch_OnMoveTick early-returns because _LLM_Bridge_PredictionShown() is false once the tooltip is hidden, so it does not log or type anything — but it is a recurring timer that keeps executing during a state the user expects to be fully inert, contrary to « pause = tout éteint ».
- **Cause racine** : The pointer-watch lifecycle is tied to bridge Start/Stop, not to the suspend reactor. Suspend cancels the debounce timer and hides the tooltip but leaves the pointer poll armed; AHK SetTimer threads bypass native Suspend.
- **Scénario** : User pauses the driver while an LLM prediction tooltip was on screen. The tooltip is hidden, but the pointer-dismiss timer keeps polling the mouse position every poll interval (and the ~9 mouse hotkeys stay registered) for the entire pause, doing background work the user believes is off.
- **Impact** : Minor — wasted CPU (a MouseGetPos poll) and a still-armed set of pass-through mouse hotkeys during pause. No data write and no user-visible action because the early-return fires when no prediction is shown. Low severity but a genuine breach of the full-silence invariant.
- **Correctif** : In Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2009 region) add `try _LLM_PointerWatch_Stop()` alongside the existing LLM_Tooltip_Hide / LLM_Engine_CancelTimer calls, and re-arm it from Ergopti_OnSuspendResume only if the bridge is active (or simply add `if A_IsSuspended return` at the top of _LLM_PointerWatch_OnMoveTick so the poll is inert while paused). Stopping the timer is preferable since it also frees the recurring thread.
- **Test** : headless-unit (extend tests/run_llm_pointer_dismiss.ahk pattern): simulate suspend, drive _LLM_PointerWatch_OnMoveTick with a prediction notionally shown, and assert LLM_Bridge_OnPointerActivity / the hide signal is never invoked under pause; or a meta/static test asserting Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter's body references _LLM_PointerWatch_Stop (or that _LLM_PointerWatch_OnMoveTick contains an A_IsSuspended guard).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-no-onexit-keylogger-flush"></a>
#### `no-onexit-keylogger-flush` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : e2e-lifecycle-leaks (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1035-1047`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2256`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1565-1590`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:409-423`
- **Description** : KL_Stop() (keylogger.ahk:1565) performs the only durable-shutdown sequence: stop the hook, KL_FlushBuffer(), KL_IngestOnce(), KL_SaveState(), KL_CloseTodayFh(). It is defined and documented ('Called by KL_Stop()...') but a repo-wide search shows it is never actually invoked from anywhere. The only two OnExit registrations in the whole driver are the logger flush (lib/logger.ahk:177) and the gesture WinEvent unhook (modules/gestures.ahk:1990). There is no OnExit(KL_Stop) / OnExit flush in ErgoptiPlus.ahk.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 Reload and ExitApp tear the process down without running per-module destructors; only callbacks explicitly registered via OnExit run. The keylogger's hot path is intentionally RAM-buffered: KL_AppendLog pushes into Keylogger._pending_entries and writes today.log without per-keystroke flush, and KL_Hook_Tick only flushes buffer_events to today.log every 200 ms while KL_IngestOnce drains _pending_entries to data.sql every 5 s. On Reload/exit with no OnExit, up to ~200 ms of buffer_events plus up to ~5 s of _pending_entries (and the advanced today_log_offset / next_event_id state) never reach disk.
- **Scénario** : User types a sentence, then immediately toggles any tray-menu option, edits config, or simply switches OS keyboard layout (Win+Space) — the latter fires CheckKeyboardLayoutChange (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2251) which calls Reload() within 1 s. The process restarts; the last few seconds of typing metrics (the events the user just produced) are gone, and the same loss recurs on every reload. Over a day of normal menu use the metrics undercount real typing.
- **Impact** : Silent, recurring data loss in the metrics pipeline — the feature's entire reason to exist. Because Reload is the driver's standard 'apply settings' mechanism, the loss is frequent, not exceptional. No crash, no log line, so it is invisible until someone audits the numbers.
- **Correctif** : Register a single global shutdown handler in ErgoptiPlus.ahk's auto-execute section (after the keylogger is started), e.g. OnExit(Ergopti_OnShutdown) where Ergopti_OnShutdown(reason, code){ try KL_Stop(); try HotstringPrefixWatcherStop(); try HookDispatcher.Stop(); try KLWV_CloseAll(); try OllamaWV_Close(); return 0 }. KL_Stop is already idempotent (guards on Keylogger.initialized) and already flushes+ingests+saves, so wiring it to OnExit is sufficient for the data-loss part. Keep the handler fast and fully try-wrapped (an OnExit callback that throws is swallowed and can hang exit).
- **Test** : headless-unit (tests/): drive KL_Init on a temp metrics dir, KL_AppendLog a typing entry so _pending_entries.Length>0 and the FlushBuffer path has content, then call the new Ergopti_OnShutdown('Exit',0) (or KL_Stop directly) and assert today.log + data.sql on disk now contain the entry and _pending_entries is empty. Add a complementary meta-static test asserting ErgoptiPlus.ahk source contains an OnExit(...) registration that names KL_Stop (or the shutdown wrapper), so removing the wiring fails CI — this encodes the root cause (missing OnExit), not just the symptom.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the actual cited code. The structural facts of the finding are TRUE: KL_Stop() (keylogger.ahk:1565) is defined but never invoked anywhere (grep finds it only in its definition + comments/README), and the only two OnExit registrations in the driver are the logger flush (lib/logger.ahk:177) and the gesture unhook (modules/gestures.ahk:1990). Reload() is genuinely used as the "apply settings" mechanism (ui/tray_menu.ahk:2294 + the 2814 comment, plus 1985/2069 config-edit and 2256 layout-switch), so it fires reasonably often.

BUT the finding's IMPACT/data-loss model is largely wrong because it misreads the durability architecture, and AHK's cooperative single-threaded teardown does not lose what it claims:

1) today.log is the DURABLE SOURCE OF TRUTH, not a 200ms cache. KL_AppendLog (keylogger.ahk:643-650) pushes to _pending_entries AND immediately writes the entry to today.log via the persistent fh.Write(). There is no per-call fh.Flush() (line 651-653), but on a CLEAN Reload()/ExitApp the AHK runtime closes the FileObject and the C-runtime flushes its buffer to the OS cache — data survives. The repo itself documents exactly this contract: lib/logger.ahk:185-212 states "FileAppend provides no flush guarantee", "Close forces a flush of the underlying buffer", and the only danger is "a subsequent hard crash (OS kill, power loss)" — NOT a clean exit. The keylogger comment (651-653) makes the same assumption: a crash "would only lose a few hundred ms of in-flight events."

2) data.sql is a DERIVED projection of today.log, recovered on next boot. KL_IngestOnce (1207) reads today.log from the persisted today_log_offset via fh.Seek (KL_ReadNewTodayLog:1185), advances today_log_offset ONLY AFTER a successful data.sql FileAppend (1270 then 1278-1279), and bails leaving the offset unchanged on write failure (1276). On next boot, KL_Init → KL_LoadState() (1541) restores today_log_offset, then arms an initial ingest pass SetTimer(KL_IngestOnce.Bind(), -250) (1562) whose comment is literally "drain anything buffered while the script was not running." So the finding's "up to ~5s of _pending_entries never reach disk" is a FALSE POSITIVE: _pending_entries is an optimization (an in-RAM mirror to skip JSON decode for same-process entries, see 1210-1213), not the only write path. Its loss on Reload costs nothing because the identical entries are in today.log and get re-read via the offset on next boot. "next_event_id never reaches disk" → it is persisted on every KL_SaveState; worst case a couple IDs reuse, not metrics loss.

3) The ONLY genuine loss is the typing buffer_events RAM array (keylogger_hook.ahk:247/317) that hasn't hit the 200ms KL_FlushBuffer tick yet — those keystrokes never reached KL_AppendLog, so never reached today.log, and are lost on a clean Reload. That is at most ~200ms of typing per Reload (KLHookConst.FLUSH_PERIOD_MS = 200, keylogger_hook.ahk:66), not the "~5s + 200ms" the finding claims. AHK's cooperative model is irrelevant here: there's no race; it's purely "the 200ms RAM window between flushes is not drained on exit."

So this is NOT "high silent recurring data loss undercounting metrics over a day." The bulk of the claimed loss is recovered by the today.log + offset cold-replay. The residual real loss is a small, bounded ~200ms-of-typing window per Reload, plus a secondary correctness nit the finding undersells: with no KL_Stop on exit, the watchers' session_start/idle_start markers are left dangling (KL_Stop calls KL_Watchers_Stop precisely to avoid this — keylogger.ahk:1571-1573), which can subtly skew session/idle aggregation. The proposed fix (a single fast, fully try-wrapped OnExit(Ergopti_OnShutdown) calling the idempotent KL_Stop) is still a legitimate, cheap hardening and would capture that last ~200ms and close sessions cleanly — but it must be justified as low-impact polish, not as preventing seconds-of-metrics loss.

I mark UNCERTAIN rather than confirmed/false-positive because the load-bearing assumption — that a clean AHK Reload() flushes the FileObject's user-space buffer to the OS — is asserted by the repo's own logger code and the keylogger comments but I cannot prove it by static reading alone; it depends on AHK runtime teardown behavior. If (contrary to the repo's documented assumption) Reload() does NOT flush the FileObject, then today.log itself would lose its unflushed tail and the finding's data-loss impact would be much larger. That single question decides between low-severity polish and a real high-severity bug, and it needs a live process-restart test to settle.

<a id="f-metrics-timers-bypass-pause"></a>
#### `metrics-timers-bypass-pause` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-async-background (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_sensors.ahk:90`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1207`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1285`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:346`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:366`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/metrics_filters.ahk:161`
- **Description** : KL_Sensors_Tick (keylogger_sensors.ahk:90) guards only on Keylogger.initialized and MF_ShouldFilter(); KL_IngestOnce (keylogger.ahk:1207), KL_Hook_Tick (keylogger_hook.ahk:346) and KL_Hook_LivePush (keylogger_hook.ahk:366) guard on Keylogger.initialized only. None of them check A_IsSuspended, and MF_ShouldFilter (metrics_filters.ahk:161) is purely a focused-window/app privacy gate — it does NOT consult A_IsSuspended. So under Suspend the sensor snapshot keeps emitting system_load events every 60 s, the ingest tick keeps draining today.log into data.sql and rebuilding/pushing dashboard blobs every 5 s, and the flush/live-push fire.
- **Cause racine** : Project invariant (PROJECT_MEMORY / brief): 'Native Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; InputHooks, SetTimers and OnMessage handlers BYPASS it and need explicit if A_IsSuspended guards.' The keystroke-capture hooks were correctly retrofitted (keylogger_hook.ahk:206/256 check A_IsSuspended), but the background SetTimer callbacks that fan out from the same module were not. The pause path therefore silences capture of NEW keystrokes but not the periodic timers that process/persist/render what is already buffered, nor the system sensors.
- **Scénario** : User presses the pause hotkey expecting the whole driver to go quiet (the documented contract). The sensor timer still writes a system_load event a minute later; the ingest timer still churns data.sql and, if a dashboard is open, still runs the heavy SQLite build (compounding finding ingest-prefetch-blocks-keyboard-thread even while 'paused'); the WPM/live widgets still update. From the user's perspective the driver is not fully paused.
- **Impact** : Breaks the pause invariant for the entire metrics subsystem: background CPU/disk work and dashboard repaints continue while paused, and — combined with finding #1 — the dashboard SQLite build can still hitch the thread during a period the user believes is fully idle.
- **Correctif** : Add `if A_IsSuspended\n    return` at the top of KL_Sensors_Tick, KL_IngestOnce (after the initialized check, before any disk work), KL_Hook_Tick and KL_Hook_LivePush — or, cleaner, centralise it: have these ticks early-return when A_IsSuspended so they re-arm but do nothing (matching the TimerEvery contract asserted in tests/test_timer_scheduler.ahk:233). Note: KL_IngestOnce on Stop still needs a final drain, so guard the periodic-tick entry, not the explicit Stop-path call.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert each of KL_Sensors_Tick / KL_IngestOnce / KL_Hook_Tick / KL_Hook_LivePush source bodies contains an A_IsSuspended early-return (brace-walk + InStr, same style as the existing pause-invariant skeleton tests in test_hotstring_engine.ahk / test_timer_scheduler.ahk). This matches the project's project_suspend_pause_invariant test family and fails if a future edit drops the guard.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against source. Pause = ToggleSuspend -> Suspend(-1) (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1998). AHK native Suspend only disarms hotkeys/hotstrings; it never touches SetTimers. The pause reactor Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter() (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006-2010) only hides the two tooltips and cancels the LLM timer -- it does NOT stop KL_Sensors_Stop / KL_Hook_Stop / the ingest timer or any keylogger teardown. Grep confirms the ONLY two A_IsSuspended guards in the entire keylogger tree are the InputHook callbacks (keylogger_hook.ahk:206 OnChar, :256 OnKeyDown); every SetTimer callback (KL_Sensors_Tick, KL_IngestOnce, KL_Hook_Tick, KL_Hook_LivePush, plus KLNet/KLAVState/KLTopo/KLWatch/mouse-park) has no such guard. MF_ShouldFilter (metrics_filters.ahk:161-198) is purely a focused-window privacy gate and never consults A_IsSuspended. So the 'pause = tout eteint' invariant is genuinely broken for the metrics subsystem. The bug is real.\n\nBUT the finding overstates 3 of the 4 cited callbacks by ignoring self-quiescence (AHK is cooperatively single-threaded; under pause the dispatcher gates ALL keyboard+mouse events, so the shared buffers receive nothing new):\n- KL_Hook_Tick (keylogger_hook.ahk:352-355) early-returns on the empty-buffer guard once the pre-pause burst is flushed, and therefore never re-arms KL_Hook_LivePush. Under steady-state pause these two do nothing meaningful -- not an active leak.\n- KL_IngestOnce mostly self-quiesces: with no new keystrokes the backlog drains within a tick or two, then entries.Length=0 returns early (keylogger.ahk:1229), and the heavy KLWV_NotifyIngest() at :1285 is gated behind statements.Length>0 so it is NOT reached with an empty backlog. The finding's '5s churn of data.sql + heavy SQLite build while paused' is wrong in steady state.\n\nThe genuinely self-driving leak is KL_Sensors_Tick: it is buffer-independent, polls GetSystemTimes/GlobalMemoryStatusEx/GetSystemPowerStatus and UNCONDITIONALLY writes a system_load event every SENSOR_TICK_MS via KL_LogSystemEvent -> KL_AppendLog -> FileAppend to today.log (keylogger_sensors.ahk:168, keylogger.ahk:761/767/260). This runs forever under pause. Secondary effect: each appended system_load line gives KL_IngestOnce a non-empty backlog on its next tick, so the ingest path AND KLWV_NotifyIngest (heavy dashboard build, only if a dashboard window is actually open) do fire -- but at the ~60s sensor cadence, driven by the sensor tick, not the 5s churn the finding claims. So the core invariant violation is confirmed; the dominant offender is the sensor tick, and the broader (uncited) KLNet/KLAVState/KLTopo/KLWatch timers leak the same way.

<a id="f-prefix-index-rebuild-no-suspend-guard"></a>
#### `prefix-index-rebuild-no-suspend-guard` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : gap-hse-registry-mutation-vs-onchar-reader (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:335`, `ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2244`, `modules/hotstrings.ahk:1002`
- **Description** : HotstringPrefixWatcherRebuildIndex (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:335) sets _PrefixIndex := Map() and _TriggerSet := Map() then re-populates them via _RegisterCategoryTriggers for each category (a TOML rescan). It is armed as a bare SetTimer at boot+300ms (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2244), called again when the deferred chunk queue drains (modules/hotstrings.ahk:1002), and from RebuildHotstringsLive (tray_menu.ahk:2804). It has no `if A_IsSuspended` guard and no Critical. The index it rebuilds is exactly what _OnPrefixChar's preview-render path (_PrefixScheduleRender / _LookupAndRender) reads.
- **Cause racine** : Same two mechanisms as the registry findings: (1) a SetTimer callback bypasses Suspend and lacks the explicit A_IsSuspended guard the sibling InputHook callbacks have; (2) the clear (_PrefixIndex := Map()) followed by a multi-category TOML rescan is non-atomic, so an OnChar preview lookup during the rebuild reads an empty/partial _PrefixIndex. Impact is limited to the PREVIEW tooltip (not the expansion itself, which keys off HSE), hence lower severity.
- **Scénario** : User pauses during boot, or types during the boot+300ms index warm / the post-emoji index rebuild: the tooltip preview transiently shows nothing or a partial set (a freshly-built index momentarily missing the magic-key triggers). Cosmetic — the expansion still fires off the HSE registry — but the preview is briefly wrong, and during Pause the index keeps rebuilding when the user expects silence.
- **Impact** : Transient/incorrect preview tooltip during index rebuilds, plus a minor pause-invariant leak (index rebuild work runs while suspended). No lost keystroke and no wrong expansion (the HSE registry, not this index, drives firing). Low severity.
- **Correctif** : Add `if A_IsSuspended { return }` (or a short re-arm) at the top of HotstringPrefixWatcherRebuildIndex, mirroring _OnPrefixChar's guard, so the rebuild does not run during Pause. For the atomicity gap, build the new _PrefixIndex/_TriggerSet into locals and assign them in one statement each at the end (Map() clear last), so the reader never observes an empty index mid-rebuild.
- **Test** : headless-unit in the prefix-watcher test file: stub Features + TOML paths, call HotstringPrefixWatcherRebuildIndex and assert _PrefixIndex.Count > 0 afterward; assert (via a wrapper) that the index assignment is build-then-swap (no observable empty window). PLUS a meta-static test asserting the function source contains an A_IsSuspended guard. Fails before the guard is added, passes after.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below threshold.

<a id="f-suspend-watchdog-no-prefix-keywait"></a>
#### `suspend-watchdog-no-prefix-keywait` — **info** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : e2e-suspend-pause (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1994`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2015`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/layout/layout_altgr.ahk:263`
- **Description** : ToggleSuspend (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1994) correctly prevents the AltGr/Kana custom-combination prefix latch by KeyWait('SC138','T1') before suspending when the Kana fixup is active and SC138 is physically held. The _SuspendStateWatchdog (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2015, 500 ms) is the reactor for suspend toggled OUTSIDE ToggleSuspend, but it only observes the post-flip state — it runs Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter/Resume AFTER A_IsSuspended already changed, so it cannot perform the at-the-source KeyWait that prevents the latch. The AltGrShiftDispatch guard rail (layout_altgr.ahk:263) logs a WARNING if a spurious dispatch with SC138 not physically held ever occurs, which catches the symptom. In practice every user-facing pause path (tray 'Suspendre' via RegisterMenuItem -> ToggleSuspend, and the AltGr+Enter gesture -> GESTURE_ACTIONS script_pause_toggle -> ToggleSuspend) goes through ToggleSuspend, so this is currently latent; it would only bite if suspend were flipped by an external tool or a native Suspend hotkey not routed through ToggleSuspend.
- **Cause racine** : The watchdog is a state-change detector, not a pre-suspend hook; the KeyWait that clears the prefix latch must run BEFORE Suspend() flips, which only ToggleSuspend does. AHK custom-combination prefix flags latch across Suspend and cannot be cleared by synthetic events — they must be prevented at the source.
- **Scénario** : A user (or an external automation tool, or a hypothetical future native Suspend hotkey) toggles A_IsSuspended without going through ToggleSuspend while physically holding AltGr (SC138) on a Kana-remap layout. The watchdog reacts to the change but the prefix flag is already latched; the next AltGr-layer key could dispatch a stale combo on resume.
- **Impact** : Currently no user-facing impact because all real pause entry points route through ToggleSuspend. Documented as info so a future refactor that introduces another suspend path does not silently reintroduce the « AltGr bloqué » regression.
- **Correctif** : Either (a) route ALL suspend toggles through ToggleSuspend and document that nothing else may call Suspend() directly, or (b) factor the SC138 KeyWait into a small _SuspendDrainPrefix() helper and call it from any code path that can trigger suspend. Keep the existing AltGrShiftDispatch WARNING guard rail as the safety net.
- **Test** : meta/static test asserting that the only call sites of `Suspend(` in the driver source are inside ToggleSuspend (so no future code path bypasses the prefix drain), and that ToggleSuspend contains the `KeyWait("SC138"` call — encodes the invariant as source text the way the existing tests/test_shortcuts.ahk pause-latch documentation tests do.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

### Catégorie : Fuites de ressources (19)

<a id="f-textsend-clip-destroys-nontext"></a>
#### `textsend-clip-destroys-nontext` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-send-clipboard (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:90-100`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/clipboard.ahk:63-82`
- **Description** : When TextSend resolves to the clipboard strategy (payload > 1000 chars, or Opts.mode = 'clipboard'), it snapshots/restores the clipboard via CB_Save() / CB_Restore(). Those wrap A_Clipboard (a String), so the snapshot only captures plain text. If the user had an image, a file list, or rich/HTML content on the clipboard, CB_Save returns '' (A_Clipboard coerces non-text to empty, per the adapter's own docstring) and CB_Restore then writes '' back — silently wiping the user's clipboard.
- **Cause racine** : CB_Save/CB_Restore use A_Clipboard (text only) rather than ClipboardAll(), which is the only AHK v2 construct that round-trips ALL clipboard formats (CF_BITMAP, CF_HDROP, HTML, RTF). The sibling SendInstant() in hotstring_engine.ahk:285-292 correctly uses ClipboardAll(); TextSend does not, so the two clipboard injection paths are inconsistent and TextSend is lossy.
- **Scénario** : User copies an image (or a set of files in Explorer, or formatted text from Word), then accepts a long LLM prediction (>1000 chars) via Tab, or any caller passes mode:'clipboard'. The paste works, but afterwards their clipboard is empty/garbled — the image/files are gone. They paste expecting the image and get nothing.
- **Impact** : Silent data loss of the user's clipboard whenever a clipboard-mode TextSend fires while non-text content is held. Especially likely for long LLM completions accepted via Tab.
- **Correctif** : Make the Clipboard port snapshot/restore preserve all formats: add CB_SaveAll()/CB_RestoreAll() (or change CB_Save to return ClipboardAll() and CB_Restore to assign it back) and have TextSend's clipboard branch use them, mirroring SendInstant. Keep CB_Read/CB_Write text-only for the read/write contract, but the save/restore pair must use ClipboardAll so non-text content survives.
- **Test** : headless-unit: in tests/, stub A_Clipboard access via the existing CB_* injectable seam (or _SendHook) and assert that a clipboard-mode TextSend round-trips a non-text sentinel unchanged. Simpler meta-static test: assert text_sender.ahk's clipboard branch references ClipboardAll (grep the source for 'ClipboardAll' in the Mode='clipboard' block, fail if absent), encoding the root cause that save/restore must be format-complete.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the actual source — the finding is accurate (only the sibling path it cites has the wrong location: it's lib\hotstrings\hotstring_engine.ahk, not adapters\hotstring_engine.ahk, lines 285/308).

This is NOT a concurrency bug; the AHK cooperative-threading model is irrelevant. It is a deterministic data-loss bug from text-format coercion:

1. clipboard.ahk:63-69 — CB_Save() returns A_Clipboard, a String. In AHK v2 A_Clipboard coerces any non-text clipboard content (CF_BITMAP images, CF_HDROP file lists, HTML/RTF) to "". The adapter's own docstrings (lines 21, 37-39, 62) explicitly admit this. So when the user holds an image/files, CB_Save returns "".
2. text_sender.ahk:90-100 — the clipboard branch does Saved := CB_Save() (==""), CB_Write(Text), paste ^v, then SetTimer(()=>CB_Restore(SavedForTimer), -150). CB_Restore("") executes A_Clipboard := "" (clipboard.ahk:75-81), which clears the clipboard. The original image/files are gone — silent, unrecoverable.
3. Reachable in production. Both LLM acceptance callers pass Opts=0, so Mode stays "auto" and resolves to "clipboard" when StrLen(text) > TEXT_CLIPBOARD_THRESHOLD (1000): llm_bridge.ahk:489 LLM_Bridge_OnAccept (accept-via-Tab) and prediction_engine.ahk:1125 LLM_Engine_OnResults (inline auto-type). text_sender.ahk has no _SendHook rerouting (grep clean), so in production it hits the real SendInput, not a stub. The path is genuinely exercised.
4. Genuine inconsistency confirmed: SendInstant (hotstring_engine.ahk:285) and GetSelection (:308) both use OldClipboard := ClipboardAll(), which round-trips ALL formats, and wrap restore in try/finally plus null the buffer (OldClipboard := ""). TextSend is the lossy outlier.

No gating prevents this: no A_IsSuspended check, no Critical, no auto/direct fallback that avoids the clipboard branch for long text. The bug is real. Category label "resource-leak" is wrong — this is silent user-data loss; high severity is defensible, though it is conditional (requires non-text clipboard content AND a >1000-char payload or explicit mode:'clipboard'), not constant — but the failure is total and silent when triggered.

<a id="f-spotlight-gdiplus-token-leak-on-error"></a>
#### `spotlight-gdiplus-token-leak-on-error` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : shortcuts (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/spotlight.ahk:54-57`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/spotlight.ahk:110-159`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/spotlight.ahk:172-176`
- **Description** : SpotlightMouseAt calls LoadLibrary + GdiplusStartup at the top (line 54-57) and only calls GdiplusShutdown at the very bottom (line 176), with no try/finally. Every DllCall in between (GdipCreateFromHDC, GdipCreateSolidFill, GdipFillEllipse, MonitorGet, GR_CreateWindow, etc.) and the MonitorGet loop can throw; if anything between startup and shutdown raises, the function exits via the exception and the GDI+ token (and any already-created overlay hwnds) are never released.
- **Cause racine** : GdiplusStartup allocates a process-global GDI+ token that must be paired with GdiplusShutdown. AHK propagates a DllCall failure / a thrown error up past line 176, so the shutdown is skipped. Because the function is called fresh on every spotlight trigger, each failing invocation leaks one GDI+ token + GDI objects; the CircleHwnd window created at line 110 is also orphaned (GR_DestroyWindow at 172 never runs).
- **Scénario** : On a machine where GDI+ or monitor enumeration intermittently fails (driver glitch, RDP session change, monitor hot-plug between MonitorGetCount and MonitorGet), one spotlight press throws mid-draw. The token leaks. Repeated triggers accumulate GDI+ tokens and a phantom click-through topmost window per failure, eventually exhausting GDI handles / leaving a stuck overlay on screen.
- **Impact** : Cumulative GDI/GDI+ handle leak and orphaned always-on-top click-through windows across repeated failures; in the worst case a permanently visible stuck overlay and resource exhaustion requiring a Reload.
- **Correctif** : Wrap the body from after GdiplusStartup through the dismiss/destroy in `try { ... } finally { GR_DestroyWindow(CircleHwnd); for h in CrossHwnds GR_DestroyWindow(h); DllCall('gdiplus\GdiplusShutdown','ptr',pToken) }`. Initialise CircleHwnd:=0 and CrossHwnds:=[] before the try so the finally is always valid. (This also dovetails with the non-blocking refactor: shutdown happens in the final tick / on suspend.)
- **Test** : meta-static: assert lib/spotlight.ahk contains a `finally` block (or a single teardown helper) covering GdiplusShutdown so the pairing can never silently regress. headless-unit: stub GR_CreateWindow to return 0 / throw and a GDI+ DllCall wrapper to throw, call SpotlightMouseAt, assert a shutdown counter (incremented by the stubbed GdiplusShutdown) is exactly 1 even on the throwing path.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source (lib/spotlight.ahk:54-57, 110-176). The pairing is exactly as described: GdiplusStartup allocates pToken at line 57; GdiplusShutdown(pToken) runs only at line 176; there is no try/finally, and GR_DestroyWindow(CircleHwnd)/the CrossHwnds loop at 172-174 are equally unprotected.

Concurrency is NOT the issue (single-threaded; the Sleep in the dismiss loop at 165 is post-draw and harmless). This is a plain resource-pairing defect on an exception path, so the question is purely: can anything between line 57 and line 176 actually throw and propagate past 176?

Going through it with the real AHK v2 semantics:
- Raw DllCalls that return an HRESULT/status (GdipCreateFromHDC, GdipFillEllipse, etc.) do NOT throw on a bad status — they just return the code. So a GDI+ *operation* failing does not raise.
- The GfxDrawFn / CircleDraw / CrossDraw bodies (lines 72-78, 92-108, 129-156) run inside GR_DrawBitmap, which wraps the painter in `try DrawFn(MemDC, W, H)` (graphics_renderer.ahk:172). Any throw there — including a missing-export DllCall throw — is swallowed inside the adapter and never reaches spotlight. So the draw callbacks are NOT a propagating path.
- GR_CreateWindow returns 0 on failure (never throws), and CreateOverlayWindow handles 0 gracefully. Not a throw path.
- The genuinely propagating throw is MonitorGet at line 118: it is a built-in that raises OSError on an invalid index, and it runs OUTSIDE any try, AFTER CircleHwnd was already created at line 110. The finding's own monitor-hot-plug / RDP-session-change scenario (MonitorGetCount read at 116, a monitor removed before MonitorGet(A_Index,...) at 118) makes this throw real. When it fires, the function unwinds: CircleHwnd's layered, topmost, click-through window is orphaned and GdiplusShutdown is skipped — one leaked GDI+ token + one stuck always-on-top overlay per failure.
- Secondary propagating path: if LoadLibrary("gdiplus") at line 54 (return unchecked) fails, the very next `gdiplus\GdiplusStartup` DllCall throws "function not found", which is fine (nothing allocated yet) — but it confirms unguarded throws exist on this path.

So the bug is REAL: a token+window leak that recurs once per failing invocation, and the trigger is a user-pressable shortcut (Win+', gestures, monitor-teleport). It is correctly categorized as resource-leak; the AHK model does not gate or serialize it away.

Where I push back on the finding: "high" overstates it. The leak only occurs when MonitorGet/GDI+ genuinely throws (driver glitch, monitor hot-plug, RDP transition) — rare in practice — and the per-incident cost is bounded (one token + one overlay), not unbounded unless the user keeps triggering during an active fault. Per-incident impact is real and user-visible (a sticky topmost click-through window), so it is not info/false-positive, but the realistic risk is medium-leaning-low, not high.

<a id="f-toml-batchwrite-nonatomic-config-loss"></a>
#### `toml-batchwrite-nonatomic-config-loss` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : config-toml (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_helpers.ahk:342`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_helpers.ahk:344`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_helpers.ahk:237`
- **Description** : The writer claims to be 'Atomic via .tmp + rename' (line 237 comment) but is not. It writes Path.tmp, then `if FileExist(Path) FileDelete(Path)` (lines 342-343), THEN `FileMove(tmp, Path)` (line 344). The delete and the move are two separate syscalls; between them the canonical config.toml does not exist on disk.
- **Cause racine** : FileMove to an existing destination fails in AHK v2 unless the overwrite flag is passed, so the code deletes the target first — which opens a window where the only copy of the config is the .tmp orphan. If the process is killed (crash, Reload race, power loss, AV quarantine) between FileDelete and FileMove, config.toml is gone and only config.toml.tmp survives; the next boot's ParseTomlFile sees a missing file and silently falls back to all defaults (every per-feature toggle, shortcut, gesture, LLM and metrics setting reset).
- **Scénario** : User toggles a feature in the tray menu; this triggers SaveFullConfig -> TOML_BatchWrite. The driver is killed/Reloaded (or the machine loses power) in the millisecond after FileDelete but before FileMove. On next launch all of the user's customisation is silently wiped back to factory defaults, and a config.toml.tmp file is left orphaned in the config dir.
- **Impact** : Total, silent loss of all driver configuration on an ill-timed termination during any config save. Saves happen on every menu toggle and 500 ms after boot, so the exposure window recurs constantly.
- **Correctif** : Replace the delete-then-move with a single atomic replace: `FileMove(tmp, Path, true)` (overwrite=true) — one rename-with-replace call, no zero-file window. Remove the FileDelete(Path) lines. Update the comment to reflect that the temp+replace is what provides the atomicity, and on FileMove failure leave the original Path intact (do not delete it first).
- **Test** : headless-unit (test_toml_helpers / test_session_regressions): write a config to a temp path, call TOML_BatchWrite, and after the call assert the destination always exists and no `.tmp` orphan remains. Add a meta-static test asserting toml_helpers.ahk contains no `FileDelete(Path)` immediately preceding the `FileMove(tmp, Path` call (i.e. the overwrite-replace form is used), so a regression to delete-first fails.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : The cited code is real and matches the finding (actual lines: 342-343 `if FileExist(Path) try FileDelete(Path)`, then 344 `try FileMove(tmp, Path)` in TOML_BatchWrite at lib/toml/toml_helpers.ahk). The header comment at line 237 does claim "Atomic via .tmp + rename" — which is false: delete-then-move are two syscalls with a window in which the canonical config.toml does not exist on disk. The AHK concurrency model is irrelevant to this bug; it is not a thread race. It is a non-atomic on-disk sequence vulnerable to *external* termination (process kill, Reload race, power loss, AV quarantine of the temp/target) between the FileDelete and the FileMove. If terminated in that window, only config.toml.tmp survives and the next boot's ParseTomlFile sees a missing file. I confirmed the downstream impact is real: TOML_Read returns Default on a missing section/key (lines 227-228), so a wiped config silently degrades to all defaults — exactly the user's "lost settings" concern. The exposure is genuinely recurrent: TOML_BatchWrite is invoked by TOML_Write and by SaveFullConfig, which fire on tray toggles and shortly after boot (18 call-site files).\n\nThe strongest corroboration is in-repo: modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk KL_WriteAtomic (lines 205-241) documents this EXACT bug as already fixed — "The previous implementation did FileDelete(path) + FileMove which left a window where `path` did not exist; an antivirus scanner or file indexer holding a transient handle on the freshly-deleted name would then make FileMove fail" — and replaced it with DllCall Kernel32\\MoveFileExW using MOVEFILE_REPLACE_EXISTING|MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH plus a single retry. So the maintainers already recognized the pattern as a defect; toml_helpers.ahk simply was not migrated. Note this is a real (small) window, not merely theoretical, but a kill landing precisely in the sub-millisecond gap is rare, and TOML_RunStrictCanonicalization re-saving afterward does not widen or close the gap. Two other files share the same flaw and are worth flagging in the same fix: keylogger_prefetch.ahk KLPF_WriteAtomic (lines 153-155, identical delete-then-move) and config_shortcuts.ahk lines 189-191 (delete-then-bare-FileMove). config_shortcuts is actually worse — its FileMove has no try/catch, so on failure it throws and also has already deleted the original.

<a id="f-webview2-userdata-temp-dir-leak"></a>
#### `webview2-userdata-temp-dir-leak` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : updater-infra (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:1040`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:1394`
- **Description** : _Updater_OpenChangelogWindow uses 'udir := A_Temp . "\ergopti_changelog_wv_" . A_TickCount' and Updater_ShowUpdatePrompt uses 'udir := A_Temp . "\ergopti_update_wv_" . A_TickCount', each DirCreate'd and handed to WebView2.create as the user-data folder. Nothing ever deletes these directories — not on G.Destroy(), not on close, not on exit. WebView2 populates them with caches/IndexedDB, so each open leaves a multi-MB directory behind under %TEMP%.
- **Cause racine** : The A_TickCount suffix guarantees a fresh directory every time the window opens, and there is no OnExit/Close cleanup to remove the directory or the WebView2 controller's user-data tree. WebView2 also keeps a msedgewebview2.exe process tied to that user-data dir; without explicit controller teardown the process and the dir linger.
- **Scénario** : User opens the changelog or update prompt several times across a session (e.g. checking release notes, switching channels which calls _Updater_OpenChangelogWindow recursively on the local-source branch). Each open leaks a new %TEMP%\ergopti_*_wv_<tick> directory of WebView2 cache data; over weeks this accumulates to hundreds of MB of orphaned temp data.
- **Impact** : Disk leak in %TEMP% (and possibly lingering msedgewebview2.exe processes). Not a keystroke issue but a slow resource leak the user eventually pays for.
- **Correctif** : On G.OnEvent('Close')/G.Destroy(), explicitly tear down the WebView2 controller (close the CoreWebView2/controller so its process exits) and DirDelete(udir, 1) the user-data folder. Alternatively reuse a single stable user-data dir per window type instead of an A_TickCount-suffixed one, and clean it on script exit via OnExit.
- **Test** : headless-unit cannot spin WebView2, so use a meta/static test asserting that both changelog/update window builders pair their 'udir := A_Temp ...' creation with a DirDelete on close (Grep the function bodies for both DirCreate(udir) and a corresponding DirDelete(udir). Flag as needing live verification for the controller-process teardown.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Both cited lines verified verbatim. updater.ahk:1040 `udir := A_Temp . "\ergopti_changelog_wv_" . A_TickCount` and updater.ahk:1394 `udir := A_Temp . "\ergopti_update_wv_" . A_TickCount`, each `try DirCreate(udir)` then handed to `WebView2.create(...)`. I grepped the entire updater.ahk and the whole windows tree: there is NO `DirDelete` of either dir anywhere, and the close handlers are bare `G.OnEvent("Close", (*) => G.Destroy())` / `G.OnEvent("Escape", (*) => G.Destroy())` (lines 1032-1033 and 1386-1387). `ErgoptiPlus.ahk` has no OnExit glob-cleanup of `ergopti_*_wv_*`. So nothing ever removes these dirs.

This is not a concurrency claim, so the AHK cooperative-threading model is not a refutation lever; it is a plain resource leak and the static reading settles it. The A_TickCount suffix guarantees a brand-new multi-MB WebView2 profile (Code Cache, IndexedDB, etc.) per open, and the BtnSwitch handler at 1021-1026 indeed re-opens `_Updater_OpenChangelogWindow` repeatedly on the local-source branch, so the accumulation scenario is realistic.

The controller-process angle is also real and confirmed by an in-repo precedent. The WebView2 wrapper (vendor/WebView2.ahk:374) exposes `Close() => ComCall(24, this)` (ICoreWebView2Controller::Close, which exits msedgewebview2.exe), while `__Delete` (line 182) only does `ObjRelease` — releasing the COM ref does NOT close the controller or kill the browser process. In the updater, `WVC` is a function-local; when the builder returns it is merely ObjRelease'd, never Closed. By contrast modules/llm/ollama_webview.ahk:155 does `try _OllamaWV_Controller.Close()` on window close, and modules/keylogger/keylogger_webview.ahk:271 does `try entry["controller"].Close()` — so the codebase already knows the controller must be explicitly Closed. The updater (and changelog_window, healthcheck, llm_model_browser, wpm_widget) omit it.

One correction to the finding's impact framing: it is codebase-wide, not just the two updater sites. Six creation sites use the same A_TickCount pattern (changelog_window.ahk:120, healthcheck.ahk:313, llm_model_browser.ahk:385, wpm_widget.ahk:603, ollama_webview.ahk:234, plus the two in updater). Only ollama closes its controller; none of the six delete the dir.

Severity medium is appropriate (slow disk leak in %TEMP%, lingering msedgewebview2.exe; no keystroke/data-loss impact).

<a id="f-webview2-udir-temp-leak"></a>
#### `webview2-udir-temp-leak` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-lifecycle-leaks (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_webview.ahk:182-183`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_webview.ahk:267-274`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/ollama_webview.ahk:234-235`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/ollama_webview.ahk:150-161`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/wpm_widget.ahk:603-604`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/llm_model_browser.ahk:385-386`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/healthcheck.ahk:313-314`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/changelog_window.ahk:120-121`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:1394-1395`
- **Description** : Every WebView2 host creates udir := A_Temp . "\ergopti_<feature>_wv..." . A_TickCount and DirCreate(udir), but none of their Close paths delete the dir, and nothing sweeps stale siblings at startup. Confirmed: keylogger_webview KLWV_Close (line 267) closes the controller and destroys the Gui but never DirDelete(entry["udir"]) — the udir is even stored in the windows Map but only ever read for logging. OllamaWV_Close (line 150) likewise leaves its dir. The Edge --app= fallback in keylogger_ui.ahk:151-159 is the only path that does the right thing (it sweeps old ergopti_metrics_edge_* dirs before creating a new one) — the WebView2 paths copied the per-launch-tick pattern but not the cleanup.
