- **Cause racine** : WebView2's CoreWebView2Environment user-data-dir is a full Edge profile (Code Cache, GPUCache, etc., often several MB). Because the dir name is suffixed with A_TickCount it is unique per open, so closing the window orphans that dir. Reload() restarts the process and the WPM-widget webview re-attaches with a fresh tick-suffixed dir, so the leak compounds across the frequent reloads identified in finding no-onexit-keylogger-flush.
- **Scénario** : A user with the WPM graph widget enabled reloads the driver a dozen times a day (menu toggles, layout switches); each reload spawns a new ergopti_wpm_wv2_<tick> profile in %TEMP%. Opening the metrics dashboards, model browser, healthcheck, or changelog adds more. Over weeks %TEMP% fills with hundreds of multi-MB orphaned Edge profiles.
- **Impact** : Unbounded disk growth in %TEMP% (the OS only clears %TEMP% opportunistically, never the per-user TEMP automatically). Slow %TEMP% enumeration and, on constrained disks, eventual exhaustion. Pure leak, no functional symptom until disk pressure appears.
- **Correctif** : Mirror the keylogger_ui.ahk sweep in every WebView2 host: immediately before DirCreate(udir), do `loop files, A_Temp . "\ergopti_<feature>_wv*", "D" { try DirDelete(A_LoopFileFullPath, true) }` (best-effort — a dir still locked by a lingering Edge helper is retried next launch). Additionally, have each Close path attempt `try DirDelete(udir, true)` after controller.Close()/Gui.Destroy(). Factor the sweep into a shared helper (e.g. WebView_SweepStaleProfiles(prefix)) so all seven sites stay consistent.
- **Test** : headless-unit: create a few fake A_Temp\ergopti_test_wv_* dirs, call the new shared WebView_SweepStaleProfiles("ergopti_test_wv") helper, and assert the stale dirs are gone (skip any that DirDelete cannot remove). The actual WebView2 instantiation needs live hardware, so test the sweep helper in isolation; flag that the per-host wiring is covered only by a meta-static grep test asserting each udir := ... site is preceded by a sweep call.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Read all cited sites plus grepped the entire windows/ tree. The leak is real and structurally confirmed.

VERIFIED FACTS:
1. Nine (not seven) WebView2 host sites each do `udir := A_Temp . "\ergopti_<feature>_wv*_" . A_TickCount` followed by DirCreate(udir): keylogger_webview.ahk:182-183, ollama_webview.ahk:234-235, wpm_widget.ahk:603-604, llm_model_browser.ahk:385-386, healthcheck.ahk:313-314, changelog_window.ahk:120-121, updater.ahk:1394-1395, AND updater.ahk:1040-1041 (the changelog-in-updater site the finding missed; the finding listed lib/changelog_window.ahk:120 and lib/updater.ahk:1394 but not updater.ahk:1040). The per-launch A_TickCount suffix makes each dir unique, so closing the host orphans it.
2. `Grep DirDelete` over all of windows/ returns exactly two production hits touching browser temp dirs: keylogger_ui.ahk:158 (the Edge --app= fallback sweep — the ONE correct pattern, which does `loop files, udir_root . "_*", "D" { try DirDelete(A_LoopFileFullPath, true) }` before DirCreate) and bundle.ahk:183 (unrelated vendor-bundle extraction). No DirDelete ever names any ergopti_*_wv* dir.
3. KLWV_Close (keylogger_webview.ahk:267-273) calls controller.Close() + gui.Destroy() and Delete(which) but never deletes entry["udir"]; the udir is stored in the Map (line 248) and read only for logging (line 189). Confirmed by direct read.
4. OllamaWV_Close (ollama_webview.ahk:150-161) resets state, Closes controller, Destroys Gui, unsets globals — never deletes udir. Confirmed.
5. The Ollama orphan cleaner (api_ollama.ahk:1068-1073) sweeps only ergopti_ollama_*.json and *.out FILES (no "D" flag), so it does NOT cover ergopti_ollama_wv_* DIRECTORIES. Even the one feature with temp cleanup misses its WebView2 profile.

CONCURRENCY ANGLE: This is a pure resource-leak, not a race/lost-keystroke claim, so the AHK cooperative-threading model gives nothing to refute. The proposed fix runs only at window-open (a user action), not on the keyboard hot path / InputHook / hotkey, so it introduces no LowLevelHooksTimeout or interleaving risk. Nothing here is Critical-protected, suspend-gated, or otherwise mitigating the leak — there simply is no cleanup at all. Cannot be refuted.

<a id="f-webview-udir-temp-leak"></a>
#### `webview-udir-temp-leak` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : boot-main (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/changelog_window.ahk:120`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/changelog_window.ahk:395`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/healthcheck.ahk:313`
- **Description** : _CLW_BuildWindow creates udir := A_Temp . "\ergopti_changelog_wv_" . A_TickCount and DirCreate's it for the WebView2 user-data folder; _CLW_Reset / _CLW_OnClose never delete it. HealthCheck_ShowWindow does the same with ergopti_hc_wv_<tick> and never cleans up. Each open of either window leaves a WebView2 profile directory (cache, logs) behind in %TEMP%.
- **Cause racine** : WebView2.create requires a user-data folder; a fresh tick-suffixed path is used per window to avoid lock contention, but no teardown removes it. Reset only clears the AHK-side globals, not the on-disk profile.
- **Scénario** : A user who opens the changelog and/or diagnostic window repeatedly over weeks accumulates dozens of ergopti_*_wv_* folders (each potentially several MB of WebView2 cache) in %TEMP%, slowly consuming disk until Windows' own temp cleanup (if any) reclaims it.
- **Impact** : Unbounded slow disk growth in %TEMP%; not a keyboard/correctness issue. WebView2 may also keep file handles open on the dir while the window lives.
- **Correctif** : On window close (_CLW_OnClose/_CLW_Reset and the healthcheck Close handler), after Destroy()/Controller release, try DirDelete(udir, true) wrapped in try (the controller must be torn down first to release the handle). Or reuse a single stable per-feature user-data dir (e.g. A_Temp\ergopti_changelog_wv) and only fall back to a tick suffix if the stable dir is locked, so at most one dir persists.
- **Test** : live/integration (hard to unit-test WebView2 headless): a meta-static assertion that the close path references DirDelete on the udir variable, plus a manual/live check that opening+closing the changelog twice does not leave two ergopti_changelog_wv_* dirs.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-dispatcher-stop-not-wired-and-leaks"></a>
#### `dispatcher-stop-not-wired-and-leaks` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : input-dispatch (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:38`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:328`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:353`
- **Description** : The module docstring states Stop() is 'Called by the script's OnExit handler if cleanup is needed', but no OnExit(HookDispatcher.Stop) registration exists anywhere (grep finds none in ErgoptiPlus.ahk). Stop() also never clears _subscribers or resets the _hk_* bound references to unset, so a Stop()/Start() cycle without a Reload keeps stale subscribers and re-arms over leftover state.
- **Cause racine** : Documentation/implementation drift: the lifecycle contract claims an OnExit hook that was never wired. Most state changes go through Reload (which tears everything down), so on a plain exit the InputHook is only reclaimed by the OS, not by Stop().
- **Scénario** : Process exits without Reload (e.g. ExitApp from a menu): the InputHook is not explicitly Stopped by the driver. Or a future code path calls Stop() then Start() expecting a clean subscriber set and instead inherits the previous (and, given the Unregister bug, un-removable) subscribers.
- **Impact** : Mostly latent because Reload dominates and the OS reclaims hooks on exit; becomes real only if Stop/Start is used in-process. The doc claim is also misleading for future maintainers.
- **Correctif** : Either wire `OnExit((*) => HookDispatcher.Stop())` at startup (after Start) or correct the docstring to say cleanup relies on process teardown/Reload. In Stop(), clear `_subscribers := Map()` (or document deliberate persistence) and reset the _hk_* references to unset so Start() rebuilds cleanly.
- **Test** : meta/static: assert that if the docstring mentions an OnExit handler, ErgoptiPlus.ahk contains an OnExit registration referencing HookDispatcher.Stop (grep). headless-unit: call Stop() and assert _subscribers is empty (once the clear is added).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-altgr-shortcut-hotkeys-reregister-unguarded"></a>
#### `altgr-shortcut-hotkeys-reregister-unguarded` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : shortcuts (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/shortcuts/altgr.ahk:152-167`
- **Description** : _RegisterAltGrShortcutsHotkeys() is called unconditionally at module auto-execute (line 167) and registers `SC138 & SC038` and `SC138 & SC03A` via Hotkey() with a fresh HotIf closure each time. There is no guard preventing repeated registration, and each call installs a brand-new `(*) => IsAltGrLAltEnabled() and IsRealAltGrPress()` HotIf criterion.
- **Cause racine** : Reload re-runs the whole script in a fresh interpreter, so within one process the function only runs once — fine today. The concern is fragility: the AltGr layer already has an explicitly-called sibling (RegisterAltGrLayer) and onboarding toggles _OB_ALTGR_PASSTHROUGH, so an in-process re-arm path is plausible. Calling _RegisterAltGrShortcutsHotkeys twice silently replaces the variant with a new HotIf closure, orphaning the previous criterion, with no log to trace when/how often registration ran.
- **Scénario** : A maintainer adds a 'reconfigure AltGr shortcuts without full Reload' path. Calling _RegisterAltGrShortcutsHotkeys twice stacks HotIf closures and shifts SC138-as-prefix latch semantics; debugging a misbehaving combo is hard because there is no log of registration.
- **Impact** : Latent: no user-visible bug today (single call per process), but the unguarded, unlogged dynamic registration is a foot-gun for the in-process re-arm the AltGr layer already does elsewhere.
- **Correctif** : Add a module-level `_AltGrShortcutsRegistered := false` guard: warn+return if already true, else set true. Wrap registration in a LoggerStart/LoggerSuccess pair so any future double-call is visible in the log (errors thrown here would otherwise be swallowed).
- **Test** : headless-unit: call _RegisterAltGrShortcutsHotkeys() twice (modules/shortcuts/altgr.ahk is already #Included by test_shortcuts.ahk) and assert the second call is a logged no-op via a registration counter the function increments only once.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-kl-stop-dead-no-exit-flush"></a>
#### `kl-stop-dead-no-exit-flush` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : keylogger-core (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1565`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:1585`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:948`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:440`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1035`
- **Description** : KL_Stop() (the only orderly KL_FlushBuffer -> KL_IngestOnce -> KL_SaveState -> KL_CloseTodayFh sequence) is referenced nowhere except documentation/comments. There is no OnExit(KL_Stop) and the dashboard toggles (KLUI_ToggleTyping/Apps) only open/close windows. The script Reloads on config changes / updater runs. On Reload the process terminates without running KL_FlushBuffer (the last <200 ms of buffer_events are lost) or KL_SaveState (next_event_id / today_log_offset / KLW ngram_ctx not persisted since the last 5 s tick); the persistent _today_fh FileObject is not Close()d explicitly.
- **Cause racine** : Missing lifecycle wiring: KL_Init arms the timers and hook (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1035-1039) but no symmetric teardown is registered with OnExit. AHK only auto-runs OnExit handlers; it does not auto-call arbitrary cleanup. KL_SaveState normally runs every 5 s inside KL_IngestOnce, so steady-state is fine, but the shutdown window is unprotected.
- **Scénario** : The updater or a config change triggers Reload while the user was mid-burst. The final flush window's events vanish, and the day's accumulated walker ngram_ctx since the last tick is lost. On next launch KL_LoadState reads a next_event_id from a state.json that lagged the true max id; KL_AllocEventId re-mints ids already present in data.sql for different events.
- **Impact** : Two effects. (1) Data loss of events buffered in the final window + aggregation context since the last tick on every Reload. (2) Latent id-collision data loss: new events get stale lower ids that collide, and the schema's `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO events_* (device_id, id, ...)` silently drops them — permanent, invisible loss.
- **Correctif** : Register `OnExit(KL_OnExit)` in KL_Init where KL_OnExit(*) calls KL_Stop() (already idempotent, guarded by Keylogger.initialized) so flush+save+close run before teardown. Separately harden against id reuse: at startup derive next_event_id as MAX(persisted next_event_id, 1 + max id already present for this device) rather than trusting state.json alone. Remove the dead-code ambiguity by wiring KL_Stop rather than leaving it dangling.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert KL_Init contains an OnExit(...) call binding KL_Stop/KL_OnExit. headless-unit (id safety): seed state.json next_event_id=5 while data.sql already has ids 5..10 for the device; call refactored KL_ResolveStartId() and assert it returns >=11 so no INSERT OR IGNORE collision drops a new event.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : VERIFIED against source. The lifecycle gap is real: KL_Stop() (keylogger.ahk:1565) is called nowhere (grep: only the def, a README row, and a comment in keylogger_hook.ahk reference it), and KL_Init (lines 1535-1563) arms the hook + the 5s ingest timer + the midnight timer with no symmetric teardown and no OnExit. The codebase already establishes the missing idiom elsewhere — gestures.ahk:1990 OnExit(_GestureUnhook) and logger.ahk:177 OnExit(_LoggerOnExitFlush) (the logger even force-flushes on exit so crash diagnostics survive) — so the keylogger is the conspicuous exception. Reload is pervasive (config/layout/updater paths in ErgoptiPlus.ahk, tray_menu.ahk, gestures.ahk), and AHK runs OnExit handlers on Reload, so a registered handler WOULD fire; its absence means flush/save/close never run on the most common teardown.

BUT the finding's severity rests on TWO impacts, and the load-bearing one is a FALSE POSITIVE:

(1) Data loss — real but small. The durable on-disk boundary is today.log, written SYNCHRONOUSLY per flush in KL_AppendLog via fh.Write() (line 650). The only thing genuinely lost on Reload is the in-RAM buffer_events not yet flushed — bounded to <=200ms (KLHookConst.FLUSH_PERIOD_MS=200; the periodic KL_Hook_Tick flushes every 200ms, and app/window switches force a flush). _pending_entries is a harmless RAM mirror of lines already in today.log (re-read from today_log_offset next boot). Walker ngram_ctx since the last 5s KL_SaveState is also lost, but the walker rebuilds context on the next typing entry (comment at lines 454-455). KL_Hook_Stop's final KL_FlushBuffer (hook:422) would salvage the buffer, but it too is never called.

(2) Id collision — FALSE. KL_AllocEventId() (line 948) is invoked ONLY from KL_BuildInserts (line 1067), which runs ONLY inside KL_IngestOnce. The id is NOT written into the today.log JSONL — it is minted only when an entry is converted to a data.sql INSERT. In KL_IngestOnce, after FileAppend to data.sql succeeds, today_log_offset := new_offset AND KL_SaveState() run together (lines 1278-1279), and KL_SaveState persists next_event_id and today_log_offset in the SAME write (lines 466-467). They advance atomically as a pair. On Reload, KL_ReadNewTodayLog seeks to today_log_offset (line 1185) and only re-reads lines NOT yet ingested — which by construction have not consumed an id. So next_event_id from state.json is always consistent with data.sql; a stale-lower-id collision cannot arise from the Reload path. Even if FileAppend succeeded but the subsequent KL_SaveState failed, id and offset would be stale TOGETHER, so re-ingesting the same lines re-mints the SAME ids for the SAME events and INSERT OR IGNORE on PRIMARY KEY (device_id, id) dedupes identical rows — idempotent replay, exactly as the module header (lines 24-25) states. The finding's premise (new events get stale lower ids that collide and get silently dropped) requires id/offset desynchronization, which the code structurally prevents.

Verdict is 'uncertain' rather than 'confirmed' or 'false-positive' because the finding is half-right: the OnExit wiring gap is a genuine (minor) defect worth fixing, but the headline 'high / id-collision permanent invisible data loss' justification is incorrect, so the finding as written cannot be confirmed.

<a id="f-klw-ctx-unbounded-hist-growth"></a>
#### `klw-ctx-unbounded-hist-growth` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : keylogger-core (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_walker.ahk:600`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_walker.ahk:799`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:462`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger.ahk:469`
- **Description** : KLW_WalkTypingEntry pushes onto ctx["hist"] (backtrack) for every keystroke and never trims it (lines 600, 799) — only backspaces Pop. recent_typing IS capped at TRIGGER_LOOKBACK_LEN, but hist is unbounded until a MAX_KEYSTROKE_DELAY pause resets it to [] or day rollover wipes ctx. KL_SaveState serialises the entire KLW.ctx (KLW_SerializeCtx returns it by reference) into state.json every 5 s via full JSON re-encode.
- **Cause racine** : hist is only popped on backspace, so during a long burst with few corrections it grows ~linearly with characters typed since the last pause-break. Combined with re-encoding the whole ctx into state.json every 5 s, this is O(chars) memory plus O(chars) JSON work per tick that scales with the day's typing.
- **Scénario** : A user types a multi-thousand-character document in one app without long pauses; hist grows to thousands of Maps (up to 7 string fields each). Every 5 s KL_SaveState JSON-encodes the whole structure on the ingest timer (which per the high finding can share the keystroke thread).
- **Impact** : Gradually rising RAM and per-tick CPU over a heavy typing day, amplifying the ingest-tick cost. Low because a long pause resets hist and day-rollover wipes ctx, bounding it.
- **Correctif** : Cap ctx["hist"] to the deepest n-gram order the walker reads back (~8, matching the heptagram depth), trimming with RemoveAt(1) past the cap like recent_typing. Optionally prune KLW.ctx of apps not seen recently.
- **Test** : headless-unit: feed KLW_WalkTypingEntry a synthetic entry with 1000 non-separator chars and assert KLW.ctx[app]["hist"].Length stays <= the cap, while backspace error attribution still works at the cap boundary.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-webview-temp-dir-and-com-leak-on-reload"></a>
#### `webview-temp-dir-and-com-leak-on-reload` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : keylogger-sensors (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_webview.ahk:182`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_ui.ahk:151`
- **Description** : KLWV_Open creates a fresh `A_Temp\ergopti_webview2_<tick>` dir on every open (line 182-183) and stores a live WebView2 controller + Gui in KLWV.windows, but there is no sweep of stale ergopti_webview2_* dirs (unlike the Edge path which at least sweeps ergopti_metrics_edge_* siblings at keylogger_ui.ahk:156-159). On a script Reload, KLWV.windows is reset to an empty Map without KLWV_CloseAll() running, so the previous controller/Gui/COM objects and their locked temp dirs are orphaned.
- **Cause racine** : AHK Reload re-initialises static class state (KLWV.windows := Map()) but does not run destructors; the WebView2 COM controller and its CoreWebView2 host process keep the per-launch udir locked. Nothing calls KLWV_CloseAll on Reload/ExitApp, and KLWV_Open never garbage-collects old ergopti_webview2_* dirs the way the Edge launcher does for its own root.
- **Scénario** : User opens the metrics dashboard, then the driver auto-reloads (config change / update). The old WebView2 window's COM host lingers, its temp profile dir stays locked, and a new dir is created on the next open. Over many reloads/opens, %TEMP% accumulates dozens of multi-MB ergopti_webview2_* profile dirs and orphaned msedgewebview2.exe processes.
- **Impact** : Disk bloat in %TEMP% and leaked browser-host processes/COM objects over a long-running session with reloads. Not a keystroke hazard, but a steady resource leak.
- **Correctif** : Mirror the Edge sweep: before DirCreate(udir) in KLWV_Open, `loop files, A_Temp . '\ergopti_webview2_*', 'D'` and best-effort DirDelete each (skipping the just-allocated path, as the Edge path does). Register KLWV_CloseAll() via OnExit so Reload/ExitApp closes controllers and destroys Guis. Optionally delete entry['udir'] in KLWV_Close after the controller closes.
- **Test** : headless-unit/meta: assert via source scan that keylogger_webview.ahk contains an `ergopti_webview2_*` cleanup loop and an OnExit registration for KLWV_CloseAll (mirrors the documented Edge sweep). A pure-logic test can also assert KLWV_Close deletes the windows entry and that KLWV_CloseAll iterates a Clone() (already correct at line 277).  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source. keylogger_webview.ahk:182 creates A_Temp\ergopti_webview2_<A_TickCount> on every KLWV_Open and stores it as entry["udir"] (line 248). A repo-wide grep shows DirDelete is invoked nowhere for ergopti_webview2_* — the only sweep (keylogger_ui.ahk:157-158, "loop files, udir_root . '_*', 'D'" then DirDelete) targets the Edge ergopti_metrics_edge_* dirs, not the WebView2 ones. KLWV_Close (267-274) closes the controller (ComCall 24 via the vendored wrapper) and destroys the Gui but never deletes entry["udir"]. So the per-launch profile dir is orphaned even on an ordinary open/close cycle — the leak does not require a Reload at all.

The Reload/exit half is also real: KLWV_CloseAll (276) and KLUI_CloseAll (293) are defined but have ZERO callers and are NOT registered via OnExit (the only OnExit handlers are _GestureUnhook and _LoggerOnExitFlush). There are ~30 Reload sites (tray toggles, layout switch, shortcut/section changes, ErgoptiPlus.ahk:952), and "static KLWV.windows := Map()" re-initialises empty on the new instance with no destructor for the prior instance.

This is a resource-leak, not a concurrency bug, so the AHK cooperative-thread model (one pseudo-thread, Critical, A_IsSuspended gating) is irrelevant and does not refute it — nothing here runs on the keyboard hot path or drops keystrokes; the finding correctly classifies it as such.

Adversarial caveat on the COM/process sub-claim: AHK Reload terminates the old script process. On that exit, COM proxies are released by OS process teardown, and the WebView2 Runtime's parent-process-death watch normally reaps the child msedgewebview2.exe. So "dozens of orphaned host processes accumulate across reloads" is partly self-healing — the durable, unconditional harm is the on-disk temp dir (and a just-closed WebView2 profile dir can stay briefly file-locked, exactly the case the Edge code already documents and works around). The bug is real; one of its stated impacts (process accumulation) is weaker than the disk-bloat impact.

<a id="f-remote-async-no-timeout-deadline"></a>
#### `remote-async-no-timeout-deadline` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : llm-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_remote.ahk:169-184`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:636-668`
- **Description** : _LLMRemote_PollRequest re-arms SetTimer(..., -LLM_REMOTE_POLL_MS) every tick until WaitForResponse(0) flips true. Unlike _LLM_Ollama_PollRequest (which carries a deadline and bails) and _LLM_Ollama_PollGeneric (which has the explicit 3000 ms unbounded-poll fix documented in its comment), the remote poll has NO deadline. If the COM proxy's WaitForResponse never flips true (the exact failure mode the Ollama code added deadlines to fix), the remote poll re-arms a 50 ms timer indefinitely.
- **Cause racine** : The remote backend relies solely on WinHTTP's own SetTimeouts to fail the request, but the documented Ollama bug shows WaitForResponse(0) can fail to ever return true under load / proxy breakage, leaving the SetTimer chain alive forever with the COM object pinned. The remote path never received the same deadline hardening.
- **Scénario** : User configures a remote API (OpenAI/Anthropic) and the network or COM proxy stalls such that WaitForResponse(0) never returns true. A 50 ms timer chain then runs forever, pinning the WinHTTP COM object and contending with the keyboard hook's message pump — the same 'characters eaten at random' symptom the Ollama health-probe fix called out.
- **Impact** : Rare but unbounded: leaked COM object + perpetual 50 ms timer that can contend with the InputHook, only on the remote-API backend under a stalled proxy. The generation guard prevents a stale render but does not stop the poll chain.
- **Correctif** : Mirror the Ollama fix: thread a deadline (A_TickCount + LLM_REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS + slack) through _LLMRemote_PollRequest and, when A_TickCount >= deadline, try http.Abort(), delete the registry entry, and call on_fail() once. Bind the deadline on first entry exactly like _LLM_Ollama_PollGeneric.
- **Test** : headless-unit: build a fake http object whose WaitForResponse(0) always returns false; register it in _LLM_Remote_Async and drive _LLMRemote_PollRequest with the harness's tick shim past the deadline; assert on_fail fired exactly once and the registry entry was deleted (no infinite re-arm). Fails today (no deadline branch).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-winhttp-no-abort-on-poll-timeout"></a>
#### `winhttp-no-abort-on-poll-timeout` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : llm-api (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:653`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_remote.ahk:181`
- **Description** : _LLM_Ollama_PollGeneric on deadline expiry calls on_err() and returns without http.Abort(), and _LLMRemote_PollRequest has no deadline at all — it re-arms forever until WaitForResponse(0) flips or the entry is cancelled, relying solely on WinHTTP's own SetTimeouts to eventually error the COM object. The cancelled-flag path in _LLMRemote_PollRequest deletes the entry but never calls http.Abort() either, so a slow/stuck remote request keeps its socket and COM proxy alive in the background after the engine has moved on.
- **Cause racine** : The poll layer drops its reference to the WinHTTP COM object and trusts the underlying request to wind down on its own timeout; for remote there is no independent wall-clock deadline mirroring the Ollama curl path, so a provider that accepts the connection but never responds within SetTimeouts bounds keeps a poll chain or request alive longer than intended.
- **Scénario** : A flaky paid-API endpoint accepts the TCP connection but stalls past the receive timeout; or a warmup/health probe hits a half-open Ollama. The COM request object and its socket stay resident until WinHTTP's internal timeout fires. Under rapid typing many such stuck requests can accumulate briefly.
- **Impact** : Transient resource/handle pressure and slower cancellation, not a dropped keystroke (work is off-thread). Bounded by WinHTTP's own timeouts, so it self-heals, but cancellation is not immediate.
- **Correctif** : On poll timeout and on the cancelled path, call try http.Abort() before deleting the registry entry (the Ollama curl path already ProcessCloses its child symmetrically). Add an absolute deadline to _LLMRemote_PollRequest (A_TickCount + LLM_REMOTE_TIMEOUT_MS + slack) so a never-responding accept can't keep the poll chain re-arming indefinitely.
- **Test** : headless-unit: build a fake http object whose WaitForResponse always returns false, drive _LLMRemote_PollRequest with a tiny injected deadline, assert it eventually calls on_fail and Delete()s the entry rather than re-arming forever; assert Abort() was called on the fake.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-border-gdi-cleanup-broken-nesting"></a>
#### `border-gdi-cleanup-broken-nesting` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tooltip-widget (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/tooltip.ahk:1070`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/tooltip.ahk:1075`
- **Description** : The failure-handling block reads:
  if (!HBmp or !MemDC) {
      if HBmp DllCall("Gdi32\DeleteObject", "Ptr", HBmp)
          if MemDC DllCall("Gdi32\DeleteDC", "Ptr", MemDC)
              return
  }
The DeleteDC and the return are nested inside 'if HBmp', so they only run when HBmp is truthy. But the block is only entered when HBmp OR MemDC is falsy. In the case HBmp == 0 (CreateDIBSection failed) and MemDC valid, 'if HBmp' is false, so the entire inner block is skipped: MemDC is never deleted (leak) AND there is no return, so execution falls through to SelectObject(MemDC, HBmp=0), PatBlt, CreatePen, RoundRect and UpdateLayeredWindow with a null bitmap.
- **Cause racine** : Statement-without-braces nesting: AHK v2 treats each single-line 'if' as guarding only the next statement, so 'if HBmp X; if MemDC Y; return' chains such that Y and return are conditional on HBmp. The intended logic (delete whichever handle exists, then always return on failure) is not expressed. The border is destroyed and recreated on EVERY tooltip show (lines 1035-1038), so under GDI handle pressure this failure path is reached repeatedly and each pass leaks the surviving MemDC, accelerating exhaustion.
- **Scénario** : Under GDI/USER object pressure (the documented ~10k GDI object ceiling) or low memory during a long high-frequency typing session with many tooltip rebuilds, CreateDIBSection returns 0 while CreateCompatibleDC succeeds. The function leaks that MemDC and continues issuing GDI calls against a null bitmap, painting garbage / no-op into the layered border and compounding the leak on every subsequent show.
- **Impact** : Slow GDI handle leak plus operating on invalid handles on an already-degraded system; can turn a transient resource shortage into a persistent broken-border / further-leak spiral until reload.
- **Correctif** : Replace with explicit braces and unconditional cleanup+return:
  if (!HBmp or !MemDC) {
      if (HBmp)
          DllCall("Gdi32\DeleteObject", "Ptr", HBmp)
      if (MemDC)
          DllCall("Gdi32\DeleteDC", "Ptr", MemDC)
      return
  }
so both handles are released regardless of which failed and the function always bails on failure.
- **Test** : headless-unit: factor the failure cleanup into a tiny pure helper _TooltipBorderCleanupOnFail(hbmp, memdc) that returns a sentinel and increments delete-call spy counters; assert that for (hbmp=0, memdc=valid) it deletes memdc and signals 'abort'. Alternatively a meta/static test asserting the block uses braces and contains an unconditional 'return' not nested under 'if HBmp'.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-wpm-webview-temp-dir-leak"></a>
#### `wpm-webview-temp-dir-leak` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tooltip-widget (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/wpm_widget.ahk:603`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/wpm_widget.ahk:604`
- **Description** : WPMWidget_AttachWebView creates udir := A_Temp . "\ergopti_wpm_wv2_" . A_TickCount and DirCreate()s it, but there is no sweep of prior ergopti_wpm_wv2_* directories and no DirDelete on teardown/reload. Other WebView2 hosts in the codebase (keylogger_ui.ahk:151-158) explicitly sweep stale '_*' dirs before creating a new one; the WPM widget does not.
- **Cause racine** : WebView2 user-data folders accumulate one per process lifetime in graph mode; on Reload (which the driver does for updates/config changes) the old folder is orphaned because A_TickCount changes, and WebView2 caches can be tens of MB each.
- **Scénario** : A user who keeps the widget in graph mode and reloads the driver repeatedly (updates, config edits) slowly accumulates ergopti_wpm_wv2_* folders in %TEMP%.
- **Impact** : Bounded-but-unbounded-over-time disk usage in %TEMP%; no functional failure. Low because graph mode is opt-in and the widget is off by default.
- **Correctif** : Mirror the keylogger_ui.ahk pattern: before DirCreate(udir), 'loop files, udir_root . "_*", "D" { try DirDelete(A_LoopFileFullPath, true) }' where udir_root := A_Temp . "\ergopti_wpm_wv2". Wrap in try since a locked dir from a still-running webview process cannot always be removed.
- **Test** : headless-unit: extract the sweep into a helper WPMWidget_SweepWvTempDirs(root) and assert that, given a temp root with two fabricated '<root>_<n>' dirs, the helper removes the ones not equal to the current udir. Keeps the test off live WebView2.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-menu-dispatch-callbacks-unbounded-growth"></a>
#### `menu-dispatch-callbacks-unbounded-growth` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tray-llm (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/menu_main.ahk:43`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:88-93`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:118-160`
- **Description** : LLM_Tray_Build() begins with `try _LLM_Tray_Menu.Delete()` and then re-creates every submenu and every RegisterMenuItem from scratch. It is invoked on essentially every state change: every setter (SetModel/SetN/SetProfile/ToggleBool...), every reset-row click, the async health-probe status flip (_LLM_Tray_OnHealthProbeDone), the deferred boot build, and a SetTimer(-3000) after each model pull. RegisterMenuItem records each callback in the global _MenuDispatchCallbacks / _MenuDispatchLastFire Maps keyed by Win32 menu-item ID, but nothing ever removes entries on Menu.Delete(). The number of LLM items per build varies (warning row, thinking row, per-N reset rows, per-setting reset rows, variable model catalogue, variable user-profile/api-entry counts), so item count fluctuates and the set of live IDs shifts; stale IDs from larger past builds linger in both Maps for the life of the process.
- **Cause racine** : The dispatch-bypass layer has no de-registration hook tied to Menu.Delete(); IDs that no longer correspond to a live item are never pruned, and because item counts vary between rebuilds the high-water-mark set of IDs accumulates. AHK reuses freed IDs so live clicks don't misfire (RegisterMenuItem resets _MenuDispatchLastFire[ItemId]:=0 on each re-add), so this is growth, not a wrong-callback bug.
- **Scénario** : A power user who leaves the script running for days and frequently tweaks LLM settings / pulls models triggers hundreds-to-thousands of LLM_Tray_Build() passes; _MenuDispatchCallbacks/_MenuDispatchLastFire slowly accumulate dead ID entries. Not user-visible short term, but unbounded over a long session and across the whole tray (other menus rebuild too).
- **Impact** : Slow memory growth proportional to (number of rebuilds × item-count variance). No incorrect dispatch observed, but the leak is permanent and grows for the process lifetime; on long-lived sessions it is wasted memory and a slow GetMenuItemID/Map bloat.
- **Correctif** : Give the dispatcher a prune step keyed off the live HMENU: either expose RegisterMenu_ResetForMenu(MenuObj) that, before a rebuild, drops _MenuDispatchCallbacks/_MenuDispatchLastFire entries whose ItemId is no longer in GetMenuItemID(HMENU) for that menu; or track item IDs per owning menu and clear that bucket at the top of LLM_Tray_Build() (and other rebuilders) right after _LLM_Tray_Menu.Delete().
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/ exercising menu_dispatcher: register N items on a Menu, capture _MenuDispatchCallbacks.Count, Delete() + rebuild with N-3 items several times, and Assert the Count does not grow without bound (stays within a small constant of the live item count) after the new prune hook runs.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-async-requests-not-cancelled-on-channel-reload"></a>
#### `async-requests-not-cancelled-on-channel-reload` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : updater-infra (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:152`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:532`
- **Description** : Updater_SetChannel persists the channel and immediately calls 'Reload'. It does not call Updater_StopBackgroundChecks/_Updater_CancelAsyncChecks first. An in-flight async WinHttp request (its COM object held in _UpdaterAsyncRequests) and its pending SetTimer poll are abandoned to AHK's Reload teardown. Reload does tear down the process state, so this is mostly benign, but a still-pending _Updater_PollAsync timer referencing the old request can fire during the brief window before Reload completes, and the WinHTTP COM object is released non-deterministically.
- **Cause racine** : Reload re-parses and restarts the script but the transition is not instantaneous; OnMessage(0x404) is also re-registered fresh by the new instance while the old one may still post. The async registry and its poll timers are not explicitly cancelled, so cleanup depends on Reload's implicit teardown rather than the explicit _Updater_CancelAsyncChecks path that exists precisely for this.
- **Scénario** : User switches channel from the changelog while a background async check is mid-flight. Updater_SetChannel calls Reload; the in-flight WinHTTP object is dropped without WaitForResponse, and a queued poll timer may fire a callback against a half-torn-down state. Low impact because Reload restarts everything, but it is an avoidable non-deterministic teardown.
- **Impact** : Minor: non-deterministic COM/timer teardown on channel switch. No keystroke or data-corruption impact; defensive cleanliness only.
- **Correctif** : Call Updater_StopBackgroundChecks() (which already calls _Updater_CancelAsyncChecks and stops the timer) at the top of Updater_SetChannel before 'Reload', mirroring what Updater_SetCheckInterval already does for the in-process restart path.
- **Test** : headless-unit: call _Updater_FetchLatestJsonAsync to populate _UpdaterAsyncRequests (with a stub http object), then assert that a Stop/Cancel before Reload empties _UpdaterAsyncRequests (Count == 0). Verifies the cancel is invoked on the channel-change path.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-cb-save-text-only-data-loss"></a>
#### `cb-save-text-only-data-loss` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : adapters-os (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/clipboard.ahk:63`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/clipboard.ahk:75`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:93`
- **Description** : CB_Save() returns `A_Clipboard`, which in AHK v2 is the TEXT form of the clipboard only — binary/CF_BITMAP/CF_HDROP/RTF formats coerce to empty string. TextSend's clipboard path saves via CB_Save, overwrites the clipboard with the payload, pastes, then CB_Restore writes back the text snapshot. If the user's clipboard held an image, copied files, or rich text, that content is permanently lost and replaced with empty/plain text after the expansion.
- **Cause racine** : A_Clipboard is the plain-text projection of the clipboard. Preserving arbitrary formats requires ClipboardAll() (a binary snapshot of all formats), which the hotstring engine correctly uses (hotstring_engine.ahk:285 OldClipboard := ClipboardAll()). The Clipboard port adapter has no ClipboardAll equivalent, so the round-trip is lossy by construction.
- **Scénario** : User copies a screenshot or a set of files (Explorer Ctrl+C), then accepts a long LLM suggestion (or any clipboard-mode TextSend). After the paste-restore, their clipboard no longer holds the image/files — it holds empty/plain text. The user pastes expecting their screenshot and gets nothing.
- **Impact** : Silent user-data loss (clipboard content the user explicitly placed there). Same blast radius as the ClipWait finding — only the clipboard branch of TextSend — so bounded, but a real correctness regression versus the hotstring engine which preserves all formats.
- **Correctif** : Add a binary-safe save/restore to the Clipboard port (e.g. CB_SaveAll/CB_RestoreAll backed by ClipboardAll()) and have TextSend's clipboard path use it, matching SendInstant/GetSelection in hotstring_engine.ahk. Keep CB_Save text-only for callers that genuinely want text.
- **Test** : live or headless-with-real-clipboard: place a non-text format (or simulate via a ClipboardAll stub that returns a sentinel object), run the clipboard-mode send, and assert the restored clipboard equals the original ClipboardAll snapshot, not a plain-text degradation. At minimum a headless test asserting TextSend's clipboard path calls the ClipboardAll-backed save rather than CB_Save.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the actual source. CB_Save() (clipboard.ahk:63-69) returns A_Clipboard, which in AHK v2 is the plain-text projection of the clipboard — non-text formats (CF_BITMAP, CF_HDROP file lists, RTF) coerce to empty string. TextSend's clipboard branch (text_sender.ahk:93-100) does Saved := CB_Save(); CB_Write(Text); paste ^v; then SetTimer(() => CB_Restore(SavedForTimer)). CB_Restore (clipboard.ahk:75-82) writes Saved back into A_Clipboard. So if the user had an image/files/RTF on the clipboard, the snapshot is empty/plain text and the deferred restore overwrites the original content with that degraded value: silent, permanent user-data loss. The finding's premise is correct line-for-line.

The contrast with the hotstring engine is accurate: SendInstant (lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:285) and GetSelection (line 308) both snapshot with OldClipboard := ClipboardAll() — a binary all-formats blob — and restore via A_Clipboard := OldClipboard inside try/finally, so those paths are lossless. The port adapter has no ClipboardAll equivalent, so its round-trip is lossy by construction. Confirmed.

The AHK cooperative-threading skepticism does NOT apply here: this is not a race/lost-keystroke claim, it is a pure data-fidelity defect in a save/restore round-trip. Single-threadedness is orthogonal — the loss happens deterministically whenever the branch runs with non-text clipboard content, no interruption required.

Blast radius is narrower than 'medium' suggests, which I verified by enumerating callers. The only two real TextSend callers — LLM_Bridge_OnAccept (modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:489) and inline autotype LLM_Engine_OnResults (modules/llm/prediction_engine.ahk:1125) — both pass Opts = 0, so Mode stays 'auto' and resolves to 'clipboard' ONLY when StrLen(text) > TEXT_CLIPBOARD_THRESHOLD (1000). No call site forces mode:'clipboard'. Grep for '"mode"' / 'clipboard' confirms no other forcing caller. So the bug fires only on an LLM completion/prediction exceeding 1000 chars landing on the clipboard branch AND the user holding non-text clipboard content — two conditions on a low-frequency path. The high-frequency hotstring path is already correct. That double gating, plus the low-frequency trigger, is why I drop severity to low (it is real and silent, so not info; but not medium given how rarely it can fire and the fact that the common expansion path is the lossless hotstring engine). Existing tests (tests/test_adapter_compliance_new.ahk:93-108) only assert CB_Save returns a string and CB_Restore returns 1 — no format-fidelity guard exists, so a fix would close a genuinely untested gap.

<a id="f-roi-current-word-unbounded-growth"></a>
#### `roi-current-word-unbounded-growth` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : gap-keylogger-roi-onchar-race-and-pause (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:160`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:166`
- **Description** : KL_Roi_OnChar appends every non-boundary character to KLRoi.current_word (line 166) and only resets it at a boundary (space, tab, newline, CR, '.', ',', '!', '?'). A long stream with none of those delimiters — e.g. a pasted URL/base64/hex blob the InputHook observes character-by-character, or typing in a language/context that does not emit those specific delimiters — makes current_word grow without bound, and the per-char `.= c` string concat is O(len) so the cost grows quadratically over the run. There is no MAX length cap on the in-progress word.
- **Cause racine** : Per-keystroke string accumulation with no upper bound and an O(n) concatenation; the only flush is a fixed small set of boundary chars. Worst-case input (no boundary char) turns a per-key O(1) into O(n) per key on the keystroke thread.
- **Scénario** : A long token with no spaces/punctuation is entered (paste of a 10 KB JWT, or sustained typing in a context whose separators are not in the hard-coded boundary set). current_word climbs into the thousands of chars; each subsequent character pays an ever-growing concat cost on the keyboard thread, adding latency exactly the dropped-keystroke audit cares about.
- **Impact** : Minor, bounded by how long a delimiter-free run is, but it is on the hot path and combines with the other ROI hot-path costs. Also a (small) privacy nuance: a long secret-like token is held in RAM in current_word until a boundary appears.
- **Correctif** : Cap current_word: if StrLen(KLRoi.current_word) exceeds a sane bound (e.g. 2*MAX_WORD_LEN or a dedicated MAX_INPROGRESS_WORD_LEN constant) reset it (treat the over-long run as not-a-word and discard) before appending. Add the constant to the KLRoiConst block per the no-magic-numbers rule.
- **Test** : headless-unit: call KL_Roi_OnChar with the RequireInit guard satisfied for, say, 5000 non-boundary chars and assert StrLen(KLRoi.current_word) stays bounded by the new cap (and that no candidate is emitted for the over-long run).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below threshold.

### Catégorie : Erreurs avalées silencieusement (9)

<a id="f-dispatch-swallows-subscriber-errors"></a>
#### `dispatch-swallows-subscriber-errors` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : input-dispatch (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:196`
- **Description** : The fan-out loop runs `try cb(args*)` with a bare `try` — no catch, no logging. A subscriber that throws on every keystroke fails completely silently: no WARNING, no ERROR, nothing in the file logger. The module docstring claims try/catch isolation so one bad callback can't silence the others (true), but the project invariant is explicit that errors thrown inside InputHook/OnMessage callbacks are already swallowed and that this is where silent failures hide.
- **Cause racine** : AHK `try` with no `catch` discards the exception object entirely. Because this is the per-keystroke dispatch invoked from the InputHook callback chain, a recurring subscriber fault produces zero diagnostics, exactly the failure mode the project memory warns about.
- **Scénario** : A subscriber (e.g. keylogger char handler) starts throwing because of a corrupt buffer or a Map key error. The dispatcher keeps eating the exception on every keystroke. The feature is dead, the log is clean, and the maintainer has no signal to investigate.
- **Impact** : Diagnosability black hole on the hottest code path; a broken feature can run dead for the entire session with no trace, violating the 'fail loud / no silent failure' invariant.
- **Correctif** : Add a catch that logs at WARNING with throttling, e.g. `try cb(args*) catch as e { try LoggerWarn("HookDispatcher", "Subscriber for '{1}' threw: {2}.", event_type, e.Message) }`. Keep the loop continuing to the next subscriber. Optionally dedupe identical messages so a per-keystroke fault does not flood the log.
- **Test** : headless-unit: register a subscriber that throws and a second that increments a counter; call Dispatch and assert (a) the second subscriber still ran (counter incremented) AND (b) a logger spy captured a WARNING mentioning the event type. Asserts both isolation and that the error is no longer silent.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source. lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:195-197 is exactly `for cb in HookDispatcher._subscribers[event_type] { try cb(args*) }` — a bare `try` with no `catch` and no logging. In AHK v2 a `try` with no catch swallows the thrown error object entirely, so a subscriber that throws produces zero diagnostics.

This is genuinely the per-keystroke hot path: Dispatch is called by HookDispatcher._OnChar/_OnKeyDown/_OnKeyUp (lines 210-222), which are bound to the single process-wide InputHook in Start() (lines 289-292). The real registered subscribers on EVT_KB_CHAR/EVT_KB_DOWN are concrete and fault-capable: modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:120-121 (LLM bridge char/key handlers), adapters/keyboard_hook.ahk:68-69 (_KH_DispatchChar/_KH_DispatchKey), and modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:398-399 (KLHook.cb_char/cb_down). A corrupt buffer or a Map-key error in any of these throws on every keystroke and the dispatcher eats it silently — exactly the scenario described, and exactly the failure mode the project invariant warns about ("Errors thrown inside SetTimer / InputHook / OnMessage callbacks are SWALLOWED — silent failures hide there").

The concurrency model does not refute this. It is not a race claim; it is an error-swallow claim, and AHK's single-threaded cooperative model is irrelevant to whether a synchronously-caught exception is logged. The catch path only runs on the abnormal (throwing) path, so adding a logged catch imposes zero overhead on the normal per-keystroke path — the hot path stays `try cb(args*)`.

The docstring's claim of try/catch isolation (one bad callback can't silence the others) is true and preserved — the loop already continues to the next subscriber because each cb() is in its own try. The defect is purely the missing diagnostic, not broken isolation.

LoggerWarn is globally available and already used in this very file (line 278), so the fix has no new dependency.

<a id="f-timer-callback-errors-to-outputdebug"></a>
#### `timer-callback-errors-to-outputdebug` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : adapters-os (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/timer_scheduler.ahk:152`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/timer_scheduler.ahk:165`
- **Description** : Both timer wrappers catch callback exceptions and report them via `OutputDebug(...)` (lines 152, 165). OutputDebug writes to the Win32 debugger channel, which goes nowhere unless a debugger (DebugView) is attached. The project mandates the central logger (LoggerError/LoggerWarn) and warns that 'Errors thrown inside SetTimer/InputHook/OnMessage callbacks are SWALLOWED — they never reach the file logger.' The menu_dispatcher's own bypass path correctly uses LoggerError (menu_dispatcher.ahk:297); this adapter does not.
- **Cause racine** : AHK swallows uncaught exceptions thrown in timer threads. The adapter does catch them (good) but routes the message to OutputDebug instead of the persistent file logger, so in normal operation a failing scheduled callback produces zero diagnostic evidence on disk.
- **Scénario** : A scheduled task (once the TimerScheduler port is used in production) starts throwing — e.g. a null-deref after a config reload. The timer keeps firing and failing every interval; the file log shows nothing, the START/SUCCESS pairing the project relies on to spot silent failures never trips, and the maintainer has no trail to debug from.
- **Impact** : Silent failures in scheduled work, exactly the class the project's logging conventions exist to prevent. Severity capped at medium because the adapter is not yet on the production timer path, but it is the designated future path.
- **Correctif** : Replace the OutputDebug calls with `try LoggerError("TimerScheduler", "one-shot callback threw: {1}", Err.Message)` (and the repeating equivalent), guarded with `try` so a logger failure cannot re-crash the timer thread. Keep OutputDebug only as an additional channel if desired.
- **Test** : headless-unit: install a Logger test double that records LoggerError invocations, arm a TimerEvery with a callback that throws, invoke H["Fn"](), and assert the recorder captured one LoggerError with the error message — not merely that no exception propagated.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source. timer_scheduler.ahk:152 (_TimerAdapterMakeOneShot) and :165 (_TimerAdapterMakeRepeating) both `catch as Err` and report via `OutputDebug(...)`, which writes only to the Win32 debugger channel and goes nowhere on disk unless DebugView is attached. This is a pure observability/error-swallow defect, NOT a concurrency claim — so the AHK cooperative-threading skepticism filters do not apply: the catch correctly isolates the exception (the timer thread keeps running, which is desired), it just routes the diagnostic to the wrong sink.

The finding is accurate on every load-bearing point. (a) The central logger exists and is global: logger.ahk:320 `LoggerError(Tag, Msg, Args*)`. (b) The established sanctioned pattern is real and identical: lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:297 wraps its swallow-prone catch with `try LoggerError("MenuDispatcher", "Bypass dispatch ... threw: {2}.", ItemId, Err.Message)` — the proposed fix is a verbatim match. The only factual imprecision is the cited path for menu_dispatcher: it lives in lib/, not adapters/ — but the code at line 297 is exactly as described, so the comparison stands.

Severity (medium) is correct, arguably generous. The adapter IS compiled into production (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:131 `#Include adapters/timer_scheduler.ahk`), but a grep for TimerAfter(/TimerEvery(/TimerCancel( call sites shows NO production module invokes it yet — only tests do. The keylogger, tap_holds and llm READMEs declare TimerScheduler as the designated future scheduling port (ingest tick/idle/flush, tap-vs-hold thresholds, LLM debounce), confirming it is the intended production path. So 'not yet on the production timer path' is true and the medium cap is justified.

OutputDebug is not an intentional architectural choice: there is no dependency-free-adapter policy (the TimerScheduler spec says nothing of the kind), and a sibling adapter (tooltip_renderer.ahk:58) repeats the identical OutputDebug anti-pattern, while the lib-layer menu_dispatcher in the same swallow-prone situation correctly uses LoggerError. It is an oversight inconsistent with the project's own convention and the documented AHK timer-callback-swallow gotcha.

<a id="f-uia-error-swallowed-silently"></a>
#### `uia-error-swallowed-silently` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : layout (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:441`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:457`
- **Description** : GetUIASelection wraps the UIA chain in `try { ... }` with no catch/finally and returns "" after the block. Any UIA exception (element gone, pattern unavailable, COM RPC failure) is silently swallowed with no Logger call, so recurring UIA failures are invisible in ErgoptiPlus logs.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 `try` without catch/finally discards the exception and continues; combined with no logging, the failure mode is completely silent. This also violates project rule 5.3 (no silent failures / log pcall failures).
- **Scénario** : On a system where UIA is chronically failing (sandboxed app, broken automation provider), every wrap key silently falls through to plain symbol output. The maintainer has no log signal that the selection probe is failing and slow.
- **Impact** : Diagnosability only — the feature degrades to plain output (acceptable), but a real, possibly slow/failing UIA path is undetectable from logs.
- **Correctif** : Convert to `try { ... } catch as e { try LoggerWarn("Layout", "UIA selection probe failed: {1}", e.Message) }` (using the try-prefixed logger pattern used elsewhere in the layout files), then return "".
- **Test** : meta-static test asserting GetUIASelection's source contains a catch that logs (no bare catch-less try over a UIA call), mirroring the existing source-scan meta tests in test_layout_tables.ahk.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-ui-launch-error-msgbox-on-timer-thread"></a>
#### `ui-launch-error-msgbox-on-timer-thread` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : keylogger-sensors (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_ui.ahk:188`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_webview.ahk:191`
- **Description** : KLUI_LaunchWindow shows a blocking MsgBox on Run() failure (line 189). The WebView2 path swallows controller-create / navigate failures into a FileAppend log only (keylogger_webview.ahk:193-198, 232-234) with no user feedback and no central Logger call. The two failure modes are inconsistent (one screams modally, the other is silent), and the launch path also does synchronous KL_IngestOnce() + KLPF_BuildAndWrite (line 113, 131) which on a cold DB the webview comment itself says can take 30-75 s.
- **Cause racine** : MsgBox is a blocking, message-pumping modal; if KLUI_Toggle* is ever invoked from a timer/menu re-entrancy it blocks that pseudo-thread. The WebView2 errors are written only to webview.log, bypassing the central logger, so a failed controller create is invisible to the standard diagnostics the project mandates (rule 5.3: never swallow without a Logger.error).
- **Scénario** : Edge is mid-update or WebView2 runtime is half-installed: the user toggles the dashboard, KLUI_LaunchWindow's Run throws and a modal MsgBox blocks until dismissed; on the WebView2 path the controller create fails silently and the user just sees nothing happen, with the only trace buried in webview.log.
- **Impact** : Inconsistent UX and a diagnostics gap (silent WebView2 failures). The synchronous pre-launch build can also briefly stall the thread on a cold DB, though it is off the keystroke path.
- **Correctif** : Route both failure paths through the central logger (LoggerError) in addition to any user-facing message, and use a non-blocking notification (tray balloon / TooltipShow) rather than a modal MsgBox so the calling thread is never blocked. Confirm KLPF_BuildAndWrite at launch uses the documented 'manifest' fast mode, never a synchronous cold full build on the launching thread.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert keylogger_webview.ahk's catch blocks on controller-create and navigate include a LoggerError/LoggerWarn call (not only FileAppend), pinning rule 5.3 'no silent swallow' for the webview lifecycle.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-coalesced-job-callbacks-dropped"></a>
#### `coalesced-job-callbacks-dropped` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : llm-api (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:351`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:405`
- **Description** : LLM_OllamaGenerate_Async stores a single pending job in _LLM_Ollama_Pending when one is already in-flight. If a newer call arrives before the slot frees, the previous pending job is overwritten (line 352 reassigns _LLM_Ollama_Pending) and its on_fail/on_success closures are never invoked. The contract of an async API is that exactly one of the callbacks fires; here neither does for a coalesced-then-superseded job.
- **Cause racine** : Latest-only coalescing keeps only one pending job by overwriting the variable, with no 'cancelled' notification to the displaced job's callbacks.
- **Scénario** : Today this is benign: the engine dispatches one variant at a time and waits on its callback, so two concurrent Ollama jobs from the engine never coexist, and any new request bumps request_id so stale callbacks would bail anyway. But a future caller (or re-enabled streaming, or a second concurrent consumer) that relies on always-one-callback to advance a state machine would stall forever on a coalesced job.
- **Impact** : Latent stall/leak risk for any future code that depends on the documented 'exactly one callback' async contract; no user-visible impact in the current single-consumer wiring.
- **Correctif** : When overwriting _LLM_Ollama_Pending, invoke the displaced job's on_fail() (wrapped in try) before replacing it, so the async contract holds; or document explicitly that coalesced jobs may drop callbacks and have the engine never rely on them (it already uses request_id, so just call on_fail for safety).
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub _LLM_Ollama_DispatchAsync to a no-op that leaves _LLM_Ollama_Async non-empty, call LLM_OllamaGenerate_Async twice with distinct on_fail spies, assert the first job's on_fail was invoked exactly once when the second call displaced it.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-ext-builder-fn-dynamic-call-swallow"></a>
#### `ext-builder-fn-dynamic-call-swallow` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tray-menu (module) · **Fichiers** : `ui/tray_menu.ahk:2016`, `ui/tray_menu.ahk:2017`, `ui/tray_menu.ahk:2019`
- **Description** : _SC_Extensions builds a function name string `"BuildExtMenu_" . StrReplace(ExtId, "-", "_")` (tray_menu.ahk:2016) and calls it via `%BuilderFn%(ExtMenu, ExtName)` guarded by `IsSet(%BuilderFn%) and HasMethod(%BuilderFn%)`. The call is wrapped in try/catch that only LoggerWarns. Any exception inside a third-party extension's BuildExtMenu (which itself may call RegisterMenuItem, file reads, etc.) is swallowed and the extension's menu silently renders empty/partial.
- **Cause racine** : Dynamic name resolution of an externally-supplied builder, executed during menu build, with broad try/catch that downgrades a real failure to a warning. Combined with the fact that errors thrown inside the deferred build (BuildTrayMenuDeferred runs under Critical) never surface to the user, a broken extension menu is indistinguishable from an absent one.
- **Scénario** : A bundled/third-party extension ships a menu.ahk whose BuildExtMenu_<id> throws (bad TOML, missing global, typo). The user sees a present-but-empty extension submenu with no error dialog; the only trace is a WARNING line in the log they are unlikely to read.
- **Impact** : Silent partial menu for extensions; debugging requires reading logs. Low blast radius (extensions are opt-in) but violates the project's fail-loud convention for user-actionable surfaces.
- **Correctif** : On catch, render a visible disabled placeholder row in ExtMenu (e.g. t('menu.extensions.load_error') with the ExtId) so the failure is user-visible, in addition to the LoggerError (upgrade from Warn). Validate that the resolved builder actually populated at least one item; if zero items were added after a successful call, also show the error/empty marker.
- **Test** : headless-unit: define a fake BuildExtMenu_test that throws, drive the catch branch, and assert a disabled 'load error' row was added to the menu object (the test can inspect Menu item count/text via the same GetMenuItemCount/GetMenuStringW helpers the dispatcher uses).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-plc-onfocuschange-drops-closures-boundfuncs"></a>
#### `plc-onfocuschange-drops-closures-boundfuncs` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : updater-infra (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/process_lifecycle.ahk:75`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/process_lifecycle.ahk:88`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/process_lifecycle.ahk:100`
- **Description** : The callback registration guard is 'if Type(Callback) = "Func"'. In AHK v2 a fat-arrow closure that captures variables, a BoundFunc (from .Bind()), a Closure, and any callable Object have Type() values 'Closure', 'BoundFunc', etc. — NOT 'Func'. Only a top-level named function reference is Type 'Func'. So a perfectly valid callable is silently discarded with no log.
- **Cause racine** : Type() returns the concrete class name ('Func' vs 'Closure' vs 'BoundFunc'), not a generic callable category. The correct callable test in AHK v2 is HasMethod(Callback, "Call") or 'Callback is Func' is also too narrow because Closure/BoundFunc do not derive a Type string of 'Func'. The guard rejects them and the try/catch wrapper means even an exception would be swallowed.
- **Scénario** : A domain module registers a focus callback as 'PLC_OnFocusChange((app, title) => self.OnFocus(app, title))' (a closure capturing self) or 'PLC_OnFocusChange(obj.Handler.Bind(obj))'. The callback is silently dropped — focus changes never reach that subscriber, with no error logged. The feature appears wired but is dead.
- **Impact** : Silent feature breakage for any subscriber that registers a closure/bound method (the idiomatic way to register instance handlers). No log, so the failure is invisible until someone notices focus-driven behavior never fires.
- **Correctif** : Replace 'if Type(Callback) = "Func"' with a generic callable check: 'if (Callback is Func) or HasMethod(Callback, "Call")' — or simply 'if HasMethod(Callback, "Call")'. Apply to all three registration functions. Log a WARNING when a non-callable is rejected instead of silently returning.
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/test_adapter_compliance_new.ahk: register a closure 'x := 1, cb := () => x' and a BoundFunc via .Bind(), then assert PLC_FocusCallbacks.Length increased for each (currently it stays 0). Fails before the fix, passes after.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code (adapters/process_lifecycle.ahk:72-105) — the guard is exactly `if Type(Callback) = "Func"` on all three registration functions, wrapped in try/catch with a silent `return`. This is not a concurrency issue (registration runs once at wiring time, not on the keyboard hot path), so the AHK cooperative-threading model is irrelevant here; it is a pure language-semantics + error-swallow claim. I verified the semantics empirically by running AutoHotkey v2.0.26 (C:\Program Files\AutoHotkey\v2\AutoHotkey64.exe) against closures, capturing closures, BoundFuncs, and Maps.

Result: the finding is PARTIALLY WRONG and overstated.
- FALSE (its lead scenario): a fat-arrow closure `(app,title)=>self.OnFocus(...)`, even one capturing variables, has `Type()=="Func"` in AHK v2 — confirmed: `capturingClosure Type=Func isFunc=1`. There is NO `"Closure"` Type string in AHK v2. So the idiomatic closure case the finding leads with is ACCEPTED today, not dropped. The root_cause assertion "Closure/BoundFunc do not derive a Type string of 'Func'" is half false — closures do.
- TRUE: a BoundFunc from `.Bind()` has `Type()=="BoundFunc"` (confirmed: `boundMethod Type=BoundFunc isFunc=1`), so it IS silently rejected by `Type(Callback)="Func"`. That is a real latent bug. The proposed fix (`is Func`, or `HasMethod(Callback,"Call")`) correctly accepts both: I confirmed `BoundFunc is Func == 1` and rejects Map (`Map isFunc=0 hasCall=0`).

Impact is much smaller than "medium": a codebase-wide grep shows ZERO real callers of PLC_OnFocusChange/OnAppLaunch/OnAppQuit. The only caller is the test (passing a named function, which is Type "Func") and the ADAPTER_* contract map. Launch/quit never fire by design (no WMI); focus has no subscriber. So this is dead/latent wiring, not active feature breakage. The error-swallow angle is valid as a consistency gap: the macOS/Linux Lua siblings reject non-callables WITH `Logger.warn`, while the AHK version returns silently with no log. The try/catch is also dead code: Type() never throws and Array.Push won't throw for any value, so "even an exception would be swallowed" is technically true but unreachable.

Verdict uncertain (real-but-overstated): the specific BoundFunc rejection is real and worth fixing for correctness + parity with the other platforms, but the finding's headline claim about closures is factually wrong and the severity is inflated given no current subscribers.

<a id="f-json-number-misleading-error"></a>
#### `json-number-misleading-error` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : core-utils (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/json.ahk:221-245`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/json.ahk:85-100`
- **Description** : _JsonParseValue treats any character that is not {,[,",t,f,n as the start of a number and calls _JsonParseNumber, which collects a run and returns `s + 0`. For input where the run is empty or non-numeric (a lone '-', a stray 'e', or a leading 'x' that falls through the dispatch), `s + 0` throws AHK's 'Expected a Number but got a String/empty string' rather than the parser's descriptive position-bearing Error. Confirmed empirically: JsonParse('-'), JsonParse('e'), JsonParse('x') each threw the low-level numeric-coercion error (no infinite loop — the parser always terminates).
- **Cause racine** : _JsonParseNumber does not validate that it consumed at least one digit before `return s + 0`; the value dispatch uses a catch-all number branch for any unrecognised leading char, so genuinely invalid input reaches the numeric coercion and throws AHK's internal message instead of `Error("JSON: invalid number at position ...")`.
- **Scénario** : A malformed LLM/WebView2 payload (e.g. a truncated stream chunk that ends on a lone '-' or a stray character) is parsed; the caller's try swallows it, so behaviour is the same as a clean parse failure — but if logged, the diagnostic blames numeric coercion, not JSON, making field debugging slower. No crash beyond the already-swallowed throw.
- **Impact** : Diagnostic-quality only: the parser's stated 'throws a descriptive Error' contract is not honoured for these inputs, so a future unwrapped caller or a logged failure is harder to triage. No lost keystrokes, no corruption.
- **Correctif** : After building `s` in _JsonParseNumber, if `pos == start` (no characters consumed) or `s` does not match `^-?\d`, `throw Error("JSON: invalid number at position " . start . ".", -1)` before the `s + 0` coercion. Optionally wrap `s + 0` in try and re-throw the descriptive error.
- **Test** : headless-unit: AssertThrows(() => JsonParse('-')) and assert the message contains 'JSON' and a position; same for JsonParse('e') and JsonParse('x'). Fails before (message mentions 'Number'/'String'), passes after.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-global-error-handler-sendevent-storm"></a>
#### `global-error-handler-sendevent-storm` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : e2e-keystroke-pipeline (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:74-99`
- **Description** : ErgoptiGlobalErrorHandler loops over 8 modifier keys and, for each currently-physically-down one, issues `SendEvent('{Mod Up}')`. It then may pop a CrashReport prompt and a blocking MsgBox. Errors thrown inside SetTimer/InputHook/OnMessage callbacks are swallowed by AHK and never reach OnError, so this net only catches main-thread/hotkey errors — but when it does fire it injects synthetic modifier-up events into the live input stream.
- **Cause racine** : Releasing modifiers the user is legitimately holding (e.g. an error fires mid-chord while Ctrl/Shift is physically down) sends `{LShift Up}` etc. via interruptible SendEvent; the synthetic Up can race the user's still-held physical key and desync modifier state, and the subsequent blocking MsgBox starves the keyboard hook (any key pressed while the dialog is up is dropped or queued). The handler is best-effort and otherwise sound, but the modifier-storm + blocking dialog on the input thread is a lost-keystroke window.
- **Scénario** : A hotstring callback throws while the user holds Shift to type a capital; the handler sends `{LShift Up}`, the user's still-held Shift is now logically released mid-word, and the modal MsgBox blocks the next few keystrokes until dismissed.
- **Impact** : Rare, but on any uncaught hot-path error the user can lose the next keystrokes (blocked by the dialog) and see broken capitalisation from the forced modifier release.
- **Correctif** : Only release modifiers that are LOGICALLY (not physically) stuck, or skip the release entirely when the user is still physically holding them; and surface the error via a non-blocking tray notification instead of a modal MsgBox so the keyboard hook is never starved. Keep the file logging.
- **Test** : headless-unit: factor the modifier-release decision into `_ShouldReleaseModifier(mod)` and Test() that it does NOT release a modifier the user is physically holding. meta-static: assert the handler routes the user-facing surface through the notifier adapter, not a bare MsgBox, so a regression to the blocking dialog fails.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

### Catégorie : Ordre d’initialisation / lifecycle (2)

<a id="f-healthcheck-init-dead-reference"></a>
#### `healthcheck-init-dead-reference` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : boot-main (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/healthcheck.ahk:34`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/healthcheck.ahk:147`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/crash_reporter.ahk:113`
- **Description** : The module header and HealthCheck_Run docstring repeatedly reference HealthCheck_Init() as the call that sets _HealthCheckStartMs and defines 'uptime', but no such function is defined and none is called. _HealthCheckStartMs is instead set by `global _HealthCheckStartMs := A_TickCount` at the point healthcheck.ahk is #Included during the parse/include phase. Crash_reporter.ahk also reads _HealthCheckStartMs for uptime.
- **Cause racine** : A planned init function was never implemented (or was removed) but its references survive in comments and the contract. Because healthcheck.ahk is #Included fairly late in the include list, the module-load assignment happens during the (invisible) parse phase, not at a controlled boot point — so 'uptime' actually measures time since include-parse, which is close to but not the documented semantics.
- **Scénario** : A maintainer adds code that relies on calling HealthCheck_Init() (per the docs) to reset/anchor uptime — it silently does nothing because the function doesn't exist, and uptime keeps its parse-time origin. Purely a maintenance/accuracy footgun, not a runtime crash.
- **Impact** : Misleading documentation/contract; uptime semantics differ slightly from what's documented. No functional break today.
- **Correctif** : Either implement HealthCheck_Init() (set _HealthCheckStartMs := A_TickCount) and call it once at a defined boot point near LoggerInit, OR delete every HealthCheck_Init reference from the comments/docstrings and document that _HealthCheckStartMs is captured at module load. Keep the single-source-of-truth principle.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert that the string 'HealthCheck_Init' either appears as a real function definition (regex `HealthCheck_Init\s*\(`) AND is called from the boot file, or appears nowhere in healthcheck.ahk comments — preventing the contract from referencing a non-existent function again.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-dispatcher-start-unguarded-hotkeys-leak-ih"></a>
#### `dispatcher-start-unguarded-hotkeys-leak-ih` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : input-dispatch (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:284`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:310`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hook_dispatcher.ahk:321`
- **Description** : Start() creates and Starts the InputHook (line 292-293), then issues ten Hotkey(...) calls (310-319) with NO try/catch, then sets _started := true (321). If any Hotkey call throws (locked-down environment, an OS that rejects WheelLeft/WheelRight, group policy), the exception propagates out of Start(); _ih is already created and running but _started stays false. A retry of Start() (or the idempotency guard relying on _started) would then create a SECOND InputHook — a hook leak and potential double-processing.
- **Cause racine** : The success flag is set only after all side effects, and the side effects are not atomic nor individually guarded, so a partial failure leaves inconsistent state (live hook, _started=false, some hotkeys registered).
- **Scénario** : On a hardened machine or unusual input stack where one wheel/button hotkey registration is rejected, Start throws mid-way; if anything later calls Start again, two InputHooks now race for the key stream — precisely the multi-hook contention this module was built to avoid.
- **Impact** : Edge-environment robustness: possible double InputHook and event-ordering corruption after a partial Start failure. Wheel hotkeys do register on standard Windows, so this is conditional.
- **Correctif** : Wrap each Hotkey() in `try` (logging a WARNING on failure) so a single unsupported mouse event cannot abort the whole Start, and set _started := true immediately after the InputHook is up (before the optional mouse hotkeys) OR use a try/finally so _started reflects the InputHook being live. Guard against re-creating the InputHook if _ih is already an InputHook.
- **Test** : headless-unit: monkeypatch a Hotkey shim to throw for one name, call Start(), then assert exactly one InputHook was created (no duplicate on a second Start) and that the failure was logged. Validates partial-failure atomicity.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

### Catégorie : Crashes (4)

<a id="f-json-unicode-escape-raw-throw"></a>
#### `json-unicode-escape-raw-throw` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : core-utils (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/json.ahk:192-196`
- **Description** : In _JsonParseString the \u case does `hex := SubStr(text, pos, 4); pos += 4; out .= Chr("0x" . hex)` with no validation of hex. When hex is empty (escape at end of input), non-hex (\uZZZZ), or partially non-hex (a short escape that runs into the closing quote), Chr() throws 'Parameter #1 of Chr requires a Number, but received a String.' This is NOT caught by the surrounding switch's `default` (which only fires for unknown escape letters), so the exception unwinds JsonParse entirely.
