- **Cause racine** : AHK v2's Chr() requires an integer; `"0x" . hex` is only coercible to a number when hex is a valid 1-4 digit hex string. `Chr("0xZZZZ")`, `Chr("0x")`, and `Chr("0x00" . <non-hex>)` all throw a type-coercion error rather than the parser's own `Error("JSON: invalid escape sequence...")`. Empirically confirmed on AutoHotkey64.exe v2: inputs `"abc\u00"`, `"\uZZZZ"`, `"\u"` each threw the raw Chr error; valid `é` decoded to 'é'. Additionally `pos += 4` blindly can overshoot the closing quote on a truncated escape, mis-aligning the rest of the parse.
- **Scénario** : An LLM streaming/non-streaming response (modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:1216/1236, api_remote.ahk:526, models.ahk:292) or a WebView2 postMessage (ui/llm_model_browser.ahk:533, lib/changelog_window.ahk:247) contains a truncated or malformed \u sequence (common when a stream chunk is cut mid-escape, or a model emits an invalid escape). The whole JsonParse throws; because every caller wraps it in `try`, the payload is silently dropped: the user's prediction/tooltip never renders or a model selection silently no-ops, with no diagnostic beyond the swallowed exception. The 'descriptive Error' contract in the module header (feature 4) is also violated — the surfaced message points at Chr, not the JSON position.
- **Impact** : Untrusted-input robustness hole on the LLM/WebView2 boundary: a single bad byte in a network/IPC payload silently discards an entire response. No data corruption of user files, but a confusing, undiagnosable feature failure and a violated parser contract.
- **Correctif** : Validate the 4 hex digits before decoding and align position to what was actually consumed: `hex := SubStr(text, pos, 4); if !RegExMatch(hex, "^[0-9A-Fa-f]{4}$") throw Error("JSON: invalid \\u escape at position " . pos . ".", -1); pos += 4; out .= Chr("0x" . hex)`. This converts the cryptic Chr type error into the parser's own descriptive, position-bearing Error (which callers already expect), and prevents `pos += 4` from overshooting on a truncated escape.
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/ (add to a json test file included by run_all.ahk): AssertThrows(() => JsonParse('"\uZZZZ"')) and assert the thrown message contains 'JSON' and a position (not 'Chr'); AssertThrows(() => JsonParse('"\u"')); and a positive case AssertEqual(Chr(0x00e9), JsonParse('"é"')). Fails before the fix (message is the raw Chr error / or for the truncated case the parse mis-aligns), passes after.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code and reproduced the behavior on the real AutoHotkey64.exe v2.

CODE: lib/json.ahk:192-196, case "u": does `hex := SubStr(text, pos, 4); pos += 4; out .= Chr("0x" . hex)` with no validation. The throw originates from Chr() INSIDE the case "u" body, so the switch's `default` (which only fires for an unknown escape *letter*) never catches it — the exception unwinds JsonParse entirely, exactly as the finding claims.

EMPIRICAL (AutoHotkey64.exe v2, /ErrorStdOut): JsonParse('"\uZZZZ"'), JsonParse('"\u"'), JsonParse('"abc\u00"'), and JsonParse('{"a":"\u00","b":1}') each threw `Parameter #1 of Chr requires a Number, but received a String.` — NOT the parser's `Error("JSON: invalid escape sequence...")`. Valid '"é"' decoded to one char. This violates the module header's Feature 4 ("descriptive Error rather than silently producing a corrupted half-tree").

CALLER: _LLM_Ollama_ParseStreamLine (api_ollama.ahk:1215-1226) wraps JsonParse in `try { ... } catch { return "" }` — a bare catch, zero logging. Confirmed: a malformed \u in the payload is silently discarded with no diagnostic.

CONCURRENCY: irrelevant — this is a synchronous parse-robustness bug, not a race. AHK's cooperative threading model does not gate or excuse it.

SKEPTICAL CORRECTIONS to the finding (why I downgrade to medium, not high):
1. The streaming path (:1216) parses NDJSON line-by-line; each line is a complete object, so a bad escape drops ONE token chunk, not "an entire response." The whole-payload drop is only real for the non-streaming/file/WebView2 paths (:1236, :526, :292, browser :533, changelog :247). "Single bad byte silently discards an entire response" is true only for those, overstated for streaming.
2. The secondary "pos += 4 overshoots and mis-aligns the rest of the parse" rationale is essentially moot. I tested it: any truncated/short escape ('"\u41"') includes the closing quote (a non-hex char) inside the 4-char slice, so Chr throws on the same line BEFORE pos influences later parsing. A silently-mis-aligning case would need 4 valid hex digits present — which is a correct escape with no overshoot. So the fix corrects the error-message contract, not a real mis-alignment bug.
3. No crash of the driver and no corruption of user files: callers' try/catch already contain the blast radius. It is an untrusted-input diagnostics/robustness hole, which lands at medium, not high.

The proposed fix (RegExMatch '^[0-9A-Fa-f]{4}$' guard throwing the parser's own position-bearing Error) is correct and converts the cryptic Chr error into the contracted descriptive Error.

<a id="f-parser-ord-empty-token-crash"></a>
#### `parser-ord-empty-token-crash` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : llm-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/parser.ahk:581-591`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/parser.ahk:678-679`
- **Description** : In the advanced-path anchor/visual-op logic, expressions like `Ord(stripped_ops[ak]["t1"]) >= 128`, `Ord(ops[ak]["t1"]) >= 128`, and `Ord(w1) >= 128` call Ord on token-text strings. Ord("") on an empty string returns 0 in AHK v2 (so not a hard throw there), but the `~= "[\w…]"` regex combined with multi-codepoint tokens fed to Ord (Ord only reads the FIRST code unit) and the RegExMatch-driven token arrays can produce edge inputs. The whole ProcessPrediction runs inside _LLM_Engine_ParseSlots, which runs inside the async on_success/on_batch_success callbacks.
- **Cause racine** : Errors thrown inside SetTimer/InputHook/async-poll callbacks are SWALLOWED by AHK and never reach the file logger. ProcessPrediction has no top-level try; an unexpected throw on adversarial model output (e.g. a token array shape the prefix/suffix loops did not anticipate) would silently abort the variant with no log line — appearing to the user as 'the model failed / no suggestion' with zero diagnostics.
- **Scénario** : A remote model returns a TAIL_CORRECTED/NEXT_WORDS block whose tokenization yields a token shape the anchor loops mis-handle (e.g. only-punctuation tokens around a 4-byte emoji), the parser throws mid-alignment, and because it runs inside the async poll callback the exception vanishes — the user just never sees a prediction and the log has no clue why.
- **Impact** : Silent, undiagnosable prediction failures on edge model output. Low likelihood (the code is a careful port and most paths are guarded) but the swallowing makes any latent crash invisible.
- **Correctif** : Wrap LLM_Parser_ProcessPrediction's body (or _LLM_Engine_ParseSlots) in try/catch that LoggerWarn("LLM.parser", ...) the message + a snippet of the offending block and returns "" — so an edge-input crash degrades to a failed variant WITH a log line instead of a silent swallow. Defensive nil/empty guards before each Ord() call.
- **Test** : headless-unit in test for parser: feed LLM_Parser_ProcessPrediction adversarial blocks (only-punctuation tc, emoji-only nw, empty-after-clean) and AssertThrows-free (returns "" not throw); add a wrapper test asserting a malformed block logs a warning rather than propagating. Encodes the swallow-prevention root cause.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-personal-ext-scan-unbounded-recursion"></a>
#### `personal-ext-scan-unbounded-recursion` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tray-menu (module) · **Fichiers** : `ui/tray_menu.ahk:1224`, `ui/tray_menu.ahk:1229`, `ui/tray_menu.ahk:1244`
- **Description** : _HS_ScanExt (nested in _HS_PreScanPersonal, tray_menu.ahk:1224) recurses into every subdirectory it finds via `Loop Files CurrentDir "\*", "DF"` and a self-call (line 1229) with no depth cap and no visited-path set. AHK's `Loop Files` with the D flag follows directory junctions/symlinks. A cyclic junction anywhere under the user's PersonalHotstringsDir makes the recursion descend forever until the call stack is exhausted.
- **Cause racine** : No cycle detection / max-depth guard on a user-controlled directory tree that is walked at every menu (re)build (InitSubMenus -> _HS_PreScanPersonal). AHK has no tail-call optimisation; deep recursion throws an unrecoverable stack-overflow-class error. Because the first scan runs on the deferred boot thread (BuildTrayMenuDeferred under Critical) the crash can take down the tray-menu build silently, and on a live rebuild it crashes the toggle thread.
- **Scénario** : User (or a sync tool like OneDrive/Dropbox/Git) places a directory junction inside ~/.../personal hotstrings that points to an ancestor. On next menu rebuild the recursive scan never terminates and the script dies or hangs while building the menu.
- **Impact** : Tray menu fails to build / driver crashes on an attacker- or accident-induced directory cycle in a user-writable folder. Low probability but a hard failure with no graceful degradation.
- **Correctif** : Pass a depth counter (cap at e.g. 16) and/or maintain a Map of canonical absolute paths already visited; skip a directory whose resolved path is already in the set, and stop descending past the cap with a LoggerWarn. Wrap the top-level _HS_ScanExt call in try/catch so a runaway scan degrades to 'no extension hotstrings' instead of crashing the menu build.
- **Test** : headless-unit: extract the directory-walk into a pure helper that takes a 'list children' injectable; feed it a fake tree whose node A lists B and B lists A (a cycle) and assert the walk terminates and returns a bounded result rather than recursing forever (test would otherwise stack-overflow before the fix).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-numeric-prompt-throws-on-nonnumeric"></a>
#### `numeric-prompt-throws-on-nonnumeric` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : tray-llm (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/menu_settings.ahk:360`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/menu_settings.ahk:456`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/menu_settings.ahk:470`
- **Description** : LLM_Tray_PromptNumeric does `val := Integer(ib.Value)` with no IsInteger/IsNumber guard (only checks ib.Value != ""). In AHK v2, Integer("abc") and Float("x.y") throw a TypeError/ValueError. Same unguarded pattern in LLM_Tray_PromptMaxWords (Integer, line 456) and LLM_Tray_PromptTemperature (Float, line 470). By contrast LLM_Tray_PromptOllamaPort correctly does `if !IsInteger(ib.Value) return` before converting — proving the safe pattern is known but inconsistently applied. PromptNumeric backs debounce_ms, ctx_chars, min_words.
- **Cause racine** : The conversion functions are called on raw InputBox text without validating it is numeric first; AHK v2 Integer()/Float() raise on non-numeric strings rather than returning 0.
- **Scénario** : User opens Trigger -> Debounce (or Generation -> Max words / Temperature), types a stray non-numeric value like 'abc' or '12,5' (comma decimal — common on a French locale, the project's UI language) and clicks OK. Integer()/Float() throws. Because this runs in a menu-callback thread (not a SetTimer/InputHook), the error is NOT swallowed — the user gets an AHK error dialog / the script may exit if uncaught, an ugly failure for a trivial typo.
- **Impact** : A single typo in any of five numeric tray dialogs throws an unhandled exception; on a French keyboard the comma-decimal habit makes the temperature/float case especially easy to trigger.
- **Correctif** : Guard before converting, mirroring LLM_Tray_PromptOllamaPort: in PromptNumeric `if !IsInteger(ib.Value) return` (or show a localized 'invalid number' TrayTip); in PromptMaxWords same; in PromptTemperature `if !IsNumber(ib.Value) return` and accept comma decimals via StrReplace(ib.Value, ',', '.') before Float(). Keep the existing range checks after conversion.
- **Test** : headless-unit: extract the validate-and-parse core into a pure helper (e.g. _LLM_ParseNumericInput(text, min, max)) and AssertThrows is replaced by AssertEqual(parse('abc', ...), "") / a sentinel meaning rejected; assert parse('12,5') for temperature yields 12.5 (or is rejected deterministically) rather than throwing. meta-static: assert menu_settings.ahk contains IsInteger/IsNumber guards adjacent to each Integer()/Float() call on an InputBox value.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the actual cited code and verified the runtime behavior empirically with AutoHotkey v2.0.19.

THE BUG IS REAL. In menu_settings.ahk the three sites convert raw InputBox text without a numeric guard:
- line 360 (LLM_Tray_PromptNumeric, backs debounce_ms/ctx_chars/min_words): `val := Integer(ib.Value)` after only `ib.Value == ""`.
- line 456 (LLM_Tray_PromptMaxWords): `val := Integer(ib.Value)`.
- line 470 (LLM_Tray_PromptTemperature): `val := Float(ib.Value)`.
By contrast line 421 (LLM_Tray_PromptOllamaPort) correctly does `if !IsInteger(ib.Value) return` before Integer() — the safe pattern is known but applied to only one of four prompts. I ran a probe under AutoHotkey64.exe v2 and confirmed: Integer("abc") and Integer("12,5") THROW TypeError; Float("x.y") and Float("12,5") THROW TypeError; Integer("12.5")=12 (truncates, no throw); Integer("")/Float("") throw but those are gated by the empty-string check. So the French comma-decimal scenario (Float("12,5") on the temperature prompt) genuinely throws — the most realistic trigger given the project's French UI.

CONCURRENCY MODEL CHECK (the finding's claim about thread context): These prompts fire from tray-menu callbacks via RegisterMenuItem. There are two dispatch paths (lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk):
1. AHK native dispatch (the common path): the wrapper _TrackedDispatch calls Callback.Call() with NO surrounding try/catch, so a throw propagates uncaught. The finding is right that this is a menu-callback thread, NOT a swallowed SetTimer/InputHook thread.
2. The bypass dispatch path _DispatchIfMissed (fires on the ~30-50% of clicks AHK drops): it wraps the call in `try { Callback.Call("bypass_dispatch") } catch as Err { LoggerError... }` at menu_settings.ahk's dispatcher lines 294-299, so the SAME throw is here swallowed and only logged.
Net effect is non-deterministic per click: native path -> reaches the global handler; bypass path -> silently swallowed (typo just does nothing).

SEVERITY CORRECTION (this is why I refute "crash"/exit, not the bug itself): ErgoptiPlus.ahk:99 installs OnError(ErgoptiGlobalErrorHandler), which returns true. So an uncaught throw CANNOT exit the script and CANNOT leave modifiers stuck — the handler releases LControl/RControl/LShift/RShift/LAlt/RAlt/LWin/RWin, logs the error, builds an opt-in crash report, and shows ONE MsgBox with the message + stack. The finding's stated impact ("the script may exit if uncaught", "unhandled exception") is inaccurate. Real outcome: either a crash-report prompt + ugly error MsgBox with a stack trace for a trivial typo (native path), or a silent no-op (bypass path) — an annoying, unpolished UX defect, not a crash and not a keyboard lock. Hence medium is too high; this is low.

The proposed fix is sound and mirrors the existing safe prompt: add `if !IsInteger(ib.Value) return` (PromptNumeric, PromptMaxWords) and `if !IsNumber(ib.Value) return` for temperature, ideally normalizing comma decimals via StrReplace(ib.Value, ",", ".") before Float() so the French habit is accepted rather than rejected; keep the existing range checks. A localized TrayTip on rejection would be nicer than a silent return given the bypass path already eats the throw silently.

### Catégorie : Bugs de logique (49)

<a id="f-textpresskey-drops-string-modifiers"></a>
#### `textpresskey-drops-string-modifiers` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : input-dispatch (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:125`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:131`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/text_sender.ahk:136`
- **Description** : TextPressKey(Key, Modifiers) handles only two Modifiers shapes: the literal strings "Down"/"Up", and an Array of modifier names. Any OTHER string ("Shift", "Ctrl", "Alt", "Win", "Ctrl Shift", "Blind") fails the `Modifiers is Array` test, leaves Prefix empty, and emits the bare key. Reproduced on AHK64: TextPressKey("Tab","Shift") sends `{Tab}`, TextPressKey("Tab","Ctrl Shift") sends `{Tab}`, TextPressKey("BackSpace","Blind") sends `{BackSpace}`, TextPressKey("Delete","Ctrl") sends `{Delete}` — every modifier dropped.
- **Cause racine** : The adapter only resolves modifiers when `Modifiers is Array`; there is no branch for a space-delimited modifier string. Dozens of tap-hold and gesture call sites (rctrl.ahk:104-107, tab.ahk:73-76, capslock.ahk:116/223, lalt.ahk:331-367, win.ahk, base_modifier/altgr indirectly) pass the AHK-style string form, which the spec (TextSender.spec.js) does not define — so the boundary silently mismatches.
- **Scénario** : User holds RCtrl-as-Tab (the tap-hold) and adds Shift to get a back-Tab: `+SC11D:: TextPressKey("Tab","Shift")` sends a plain forward Tab instead of Shift+Tab. CapsLock-Backspace word-delete relies on `TextPressKey("BackSpace","Blind")` to preserve held modifiers; the Blind is dropped, so the active modifier state corrupts the keystroke. Win+Tab, Ctrl+Delete etc. via the string form all degrade to the unmodified key.
- **Impact** : Modified keystrokes from tap-holds/gestures fire WITHOUT their modifier — wrong action sent to the app (forward Tab vs back-Tab, Backspace without Blind, Delete without Ctrl). User-facing, reproducible, and broad across the tap-hold layer.
- **Correctif** : Add a string branch in TextPressKey: if Modifiers is a non-empty String that is not "Down"/"Up", split on spaces and either map known modifier tokens via _TextSenderModifierPrefix or pass through AHK-native prefixes/words (e.g. "Blind" -> `{Blind}` prefix). Simplest robust form: for a string, build Prefix by tokenising and translating each token; emit `Prefix . "{" . Key . "}"`. Keep Array and Down/Up handling intact.
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub _AHK_SendInput to capture payloads, call TextPressKey("Tab","Shift") and AssertEqual captured to "+{Tab}"; TextPressKey("Tab","Ctrl Shift") -> "^+{Tab}"; TextPressKey("BackSpace","Blind") -> a payload containing "Blind". Fails today (bare {Tab}/{BackSpace}), passes after.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited adapter (adapters/text_sender.ahk:125-137) and the real call sites. The finding is accurate.

TextPressKey(Key, Modifiers) has exactly three branches: (1) Modifiers == "Down"/"Up" → emit a sustained event; (2) Modifiers is Array → build Prefix from _TextSenderModifierPrefix; (3) fallthrough → emit Prefix . "{" . Key . "}" with Prefix STILL "". Any non-empty string that is not "Down"/"Up" — "Shift", "Ctrl", "Alt", "Win", "Ctrl Shift", "Blind" — fails branch 1, fails `is Array`, so Prefix stays "" and the bare key is sent. No normalization layer sits between these call sites and the adapter: _GestureParseAndPressKey (gestures.ahk:1049) correctly builds an Array, but the broken call sites bypass it and pass raw string literals directly (rctrl.ahk:104-107, tab.ahk:73-76, lalt.ahk:131/138-139/331-367, capslock.ahk:116/223). Verified each: e.g. rctrl.ahk:104 `+SC11D:: TextPressKey("Tab","Shift")`, capslock.ahk:223 `TextPressKey("BackSpace","Blind")`, lalt.ahk:361 `TextPressKey("BackSpace","Ctrl")`.

This is NOT masked by physically-held modifiers, and that is the decisive point. _AHK_SendInput is `(Keys)=>SendInput(Keys)` (line 42), which forces SendInput mode regardless of the global SendMode("Event"). AHK's default (non-Blind) Send behavior temporarily RELEASES any physically-held modifier that the sent keystroke does not request, then restores it. So when `+SC11D` fires (Shift physically down) and the adapter sends a bare `{Tab}`, AHK releases the physical Shift for the duration → a plain forward Tab is produced, not Shift+Tab. The same applies to the synthetic-modifier path: capslock _CapsLockDispatch (capslock.ahk:217-231) does `{LCtrl Down}` then intends `{Blind}{BackSpace}` so the held Ctrl survives; with "Blind" dropped, the bare `{BackSpace}` makes AHK release the (synthetic) LCtrl → plain BackSpace, and the word-delete degrades to a single-char delete. lalt BackSpaceLogic:360-368 (LCtrl physically held → intent Ctrl+BackSpace) likewise degrades to plain BackSpace. The whole reason "Blind" and "{Ctrl}" prefixing exist here is precisely because AHK would otherwise strip the modifier — so the dropped-modifier bug actively changes the emitted keystroke.

This is a pure logic bug on the keyboard dispatch path; the AHK cooperative-threading model is irrelevant (no race, no Critical, no timer interleave needed — it misfires deterministically every call). Note the heavily-used empty-string form `TextPressKey("X","")` happens to be correct by accident (no modifier wanted, Prefix "" → bare key), which is why the bug went unnoticed; only the non-empty modifier strings are broken — exactly the set the finding names.

<a id="f-word-delimiters-not-applied-live"></a>
#### `word-delimiters-not-applied-live` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : hotstrings-config (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_config.ahk:233`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_config.ahk:235`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_config.ahk:251`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_config_window.ahk:772`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_menu.ahk:1040`
- **Description** : HotstringsSetConsumedDelimiters updates the LIVE engine global HSE_CONSUMED_DELIMITERS (hotstrings_config.ahk:252-254) so consumed-delimiter changes apply immediately. Its sibling HotstringsSetWordDelimiters only mutates _HotstringsWordDelimiters and writes the file via _SaveGlobalWordDelimiters — it never touches HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS, and despite the docstring at line 233-234 ('then trigger a Reload so the engine picks up the change') it does NOT Reload. The engine's word-boundary detection reads HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS (hotstring_engine_main.ahk:74, 720, 732, 831, 1098, 1105), which was assigned once at boot in ErgoptiPlus.ahk:364 from HotstringsGetWordDelimiters().
- **Cause racine** : HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS is a boot-time snapshot global. The setter persists the new value and bumps nothing in the engine; the consumed-delimiter setter was given the live-update line but the word-delimiter setter was not. Every caller (config-window checkbox grid _HCW_OnDelimiterChanged, tray-menu _HS_DelimToggleEntry / _HS_DelimSetAll / _HS_DelimReset / _HS_DelimAddCustom / _HS_DelimRemoveCustom) then fires a 'delimiters saved' TrayTip, so the user is told it worked.
- **Scénario** : User opens the config window (or tray 'Word expanders' submenu), ticks a new terminator such as '/' or unticks ';', and sees the 'Delimiters saved' toast. They keep typing in the same session: expansions still fire on the OLD terminator set. The new boundary set silently does nothing until the script is reloaded or restarted, with no indication anything is pending.
- **Impact** : Persistent user-visible feature breakage: word-delimiter customisation appears to apply but doesn't, for the entire session. Consumed-delimiter changes apply live, so the two halves of the same menu behave inconsistently — confusing and hard to diagnose.
- **Correctif** : Make HotstringsSetWordDelimiters update the runtime global in lock-step with the consumed-delimiter setter: add `global HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS` and `HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS := HotstringsGetWordDelimiters()` (i.e. the effective string, falling back to the default when Delimiters=="") right after _SaveGlobalWordDelimiters, mirroring lines 252-254. Note _PREFIX_WORD_BOUNDARIES (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:100) is also derived from HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS at load; if the watcher must honour the new set live too, recompute it as well. Then correct the docstring (no Reload is performed).
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/test_terminators.ahk or test_hotstrings_config.ahk: set _HotstringsOverridesPath := "" (disable disk I/O), save a known HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS, call HotstringsSetWordDelimiters(" " . Chr(0x2605) . "/"), and AssertTrue(InStr(HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS, "/")) — fails today (global unchanged), passes after the fix. A companion test asserts HotstringsSetConsumedDelimiters already updates HSE_CONSUMED_DELIMITERS so the two stay symmetric.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source; this is a plain logic/wiring bug, not a concurrency claim, so the AHK cooperative-threading model doesn't refute it.

ASYMMETRY CONFIRMED (hotstrings_config.ahk):
- HotstringsSetConsumedDelimiters (lines 251-256) does `_HotstringsConsumedDelimiters := Consumed` AND `HSE_CONSUMED_DELIMITERS := Consumed` (the live engine global) AND persists. So consumed-delimiter edits apply live.
- HotstringsSetWordDelimiters (lines 235-239) does ONLY `_HotstringsWordDelimiters := Delimiters` + `_SaveGlobalWordDelimiters(Delimiters)`. It never assigns HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS and never Reloads — despite its docstring at line 234 claiming it triggers a Reload.

ENGINE READS THE STALE BOOT SNAPSHOT:
- `grep HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS` shows the ONLY non-test, non-boot assignment site is... none. It is assigned exactly once at boot: ErgoptiPlus.ahk:364 `HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS := HotstringsGetWordDelimiters()`.
- The engine reads HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS live in its hot paths: hotstring_engine_main.ahk lines 720, 732, 831, 1098, 1105 (word-boundary / end-char detection). It never calls HotstringsGetWordDelimiters() at runtime — only the config UI does, and only to compute the new string to hand back to the broken setter.

NO RELOAD ON ANY CALLER:
- tray_menu.ahk _HS_DelimToggleEntry (1040), _HS_DelimSetAll (1047), _HS_DelimReset (1054), _HS_DelimAddCustom (1088), _HS_DelimRemoveCustom (1117) and config-window _HCW_OnDelimiterChanged (772) / _HCW_ResetDelimiters (779) all call HotstringsSetWordDelimiters then immediately `TrayTip(t("hs_config.notify_delimiters_saved")...)`. None calls Reload or any engine rebuild. HotstringsConfigReload (line 857) only re-parses the override file and bumps a resolve generation counter — it does NOT touch HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS and isn't invoked by the delimiter setters regardless.

So: the user ticks/unticks a word terminator, gets a "saved" toast, the override file is updated, but the running engine keeps using the boot-time terminator set for the rest of the session. The bug is exactly as described.

CONCURRENCY ANGLE (why the adversarial model does not save it): there is no race, no interruption, no Critical/timer/InputHook subtlety here. The setter is synchronous and single-threaded; it simply omits the one assignment line that its sibling has. The defect exists deterministically on the very next keystroke after the edit, regardless of timing.

SEVERITY: high is correct — persistent, whole-session, user-visible feature breakage with a false "success" toast, made worse by the inconsistency that the consumed-delimiter half of the SAME menu does apply live (confusing and hard to diagnose). Not critical (no crash, no data loss, no input drop), but clearly more than medium given the misleading success report.

FIX COMPLETENESS: the proposed primary fix is correct — mirror lines 252-254 by adding `global HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS` and `HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS := HotstringsGetWordDelimiters()` right after _SaveGlobalWordDelimiters, so it resolves to the effective string (override, else HOTSTRINGS_DEFAULT_WORD_DELIMITERS) rather than the raw `Delimiters` arg (important because _SaveGlobalWordDelimiters stores "" when the value equals the default, so re-reading via the getter keeps the global and the persisted state consistent for the reset/set-all-off paths). The docstring at line 234 must be corrected (no Reload). I also confirmed the secondary note: _PREFIX_WORD_BOUNDARIES (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:100) is likewise a boot-time snapshot derived from HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS and is read live by the tooltip word-boundary scan (lines 836, 956, 1186), so a complete live-apply must also recompute it (`_PREFIX_WORD_BOUNDARIES := HSE_WORD_TERMINATORS . Chr(0x22) . Chr(0x201C) . Chr(0x201D)`) in the same setter — otherwise tooltip prefix anchoring keeps the old boundary set even after the engine is fixed.

<a id="f-dpapi-decrypt-fail-wipes-token"></a>
#### `dpapi-decrypt-fail-wipes-token` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : llm-api (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_token_crypto.ahk:85`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_token_crypto.ahk:91`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/menu_api_entries.ahk:332`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/menu_api_entries.ahk:378`
- **Description** : LLM_ApiToken_Decrypt returns _LLM_DPAPI_Unprotect(b64), and _LLM_DPAPI_Unprotect returns "" on ANY CryptUnprotectData failure (DllCall !ok, out_size 0, or out_ptr 0). The empty string is indistinguishable from a token that was genuinely empty. On load, menu_api_entries.ahk sets obj["Token"] := LLM_ApiToken_Decrypt(obj["Token"]). When decrypt fails the in-memory entry now holds "". The very next persist (_LLM_Tray_PersistApiEntries, fired on any CRUD action and on LLM_Tray_SaveConfig) re-encrypts only when val != "", so it writes an empty Token field back to api_entries.json — permanently overwriting the user's encrypted blob with nothing.
- **Cause racine** : DPAPI CryptUnprotectData fails whenever the blob was produced under a different Windows user account or on a different machine (DPAPI keys are user/machine bound) or the entropy/blob is corrupted. The decrypt contract collapses 'decrypt failed, value is still valid on disk' and 'value was empty' into the same "" return, and the persister treats "" as 'no token to encrypt' and overwrites the on-disk ciphertext.
- **Scénario** : User configures a paid API key (e.g. Anthropic) on laptop A. They sync config.toml + api_entries.json to laptop B (or restore from a backup, or a Windows profile rebuild changes the DPAPI master key). On laptop B the dpapi: blob can't be decrypted, so the token loads as "". The user opens the tray menu and toggles anything that calls SaveConfig — api_entries.json is rewritten with an empty token. The encrypted key is now gone from disk on BOTH machines after the next sync. Predictions silently stop (no tooltip) with no error.
- **Impact** : Irreversible loss of the user's API credential and silent loss of the API prediction feature. A privacy-improving feature (encryption) becomes a data-loss footgun precisely in the scenario it was meant to protect (file copied/synced).
- **Correctif** : Make decrypt failure distinguishable from an empty token. Either (a) have LLM_ApiToken_Decrypt return the original `stored` (still-encrypted) string on failure so a round-trip re-persist preserves the ciphertext, plus log a WARN that the token can't be decrypted on this machine; or (b) return a distinct sentinel and have the persister skip rewriting the Token field (preserve the existing on-disk value) when decrypt failed. At minimum, guard the persister: never overwrite a non-empty on-disk Token with an empty one that resulted from a failed decrypt.
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/test_llm_api_token_crypto.ahk: stub _LLM_DPAPI_Unprotect to return "" (simulating cross-machine failure) for a value carrying the dpapi: prefix, call LLM_ApiToken_Decrypt('dpapi:GARBAGE'), and assert the result is NOT "" (it must return the original encrypted string under fix (a)) so a subsequent LLM_ApiToken_Encrypt round-trip on persist does not erase it. Add a meta/static assertion that _LLM_Tray_PersistApiEntries does not write an empty Token over a previously-encrypted one.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Read the actual source. The data-loss chain is real and exactly as described at the three layers:

1) api_token_crypto.ahk:85-92 — LLM_ApiToken_Decrypt(stored) for a "dpapi:"-prefixed value returns _LLM_DPAPI_Unprotect(b64) verbatim. _LLM_DPAPI_Unprotect returns "" on base64-decode failure (line 169), on CryptUnprotectData !ok (line 193), or on zero out_size/out_ptr (line 198). A blob produced under a different Windows user/machine fails CryptUnprotectData (DPAPI keys are user/machine bound) -> "". The empty string is indistinguishable from a genuinely empty token; the docstring even concedes "or "" when the decrypt failed."

2) menu_api_entries.ahk:332-333 — _LLM_Tray_LoadApiEntries overwrites the in-memory obj["Token"] with that "". After a cross-machine load the entry now holds "".

3) menu_api_entries.ahk:370-393 — _LLM_Tray_PersistApiEntries iterates ALL entries; at 378-379 it only re-encrypts when val != "", so for the wiped entry val stays "" and line 380 writes "Token":"" . The file is then deleted+rewritten (391-393), permanently destroying the on-disk dpapi: ciphertext. After the next sync the credential is gone on both machines and is irrecoverable (DPAPI is one-way without the original key).

This is a pure single-threaded logic bug in a user-driven dialog/CRUD flow. The AHK cooperative-threading model is irrelevant: no race, no Critical, no InputHook, no timer — nothing to gate or serialize. The bug is unconditional once decrypt fails and a CRUD persist runs.

One factual correction to the finding (does not change the verdict): the scenario claims "toggling anything that calls SaveConfig rewrites api_entries.json." That is inaccurate. LLM_Tray_SaveConfig (actions.ahk:106-112) calls _LLM_Tray_SyncToFeatures + SaveFullConfig, which write config.toml, NOT api_entries.json. _LLM_Tray_PersistApiEntries is reached ONLY from _LLM_Tray_PromptApiEntry (add/edit, line 196) and _LLM_Tray_RemoveActiveApiEntry (line 250); selecting an entry (line 91) does not persist. So the trigger surface is narrower than stated — but it is still a realistic and common surface: the natural user response to "predictions silently stopped on machine B" is to open the Edit dialog on the API entry to re-enter the key, and completing ANY add/edit/remove re-serializes the whole list and wipes the token of every entry whose decrypt failed (including ones the user never touched). The narrowed trigger does not reduce the impact: a single completed CRUD action destroys the ciphertext irreversibly. Severity high holds.

<a id="f-download-no-integrity-partial-safety"></a>
#### `download-no-integrity-partial-safety` — **high** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : updater-infra (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:1518`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:1532`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:1559`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:1593`
- **Description** : Updater_DownloadAndInstall checks only Req.Status == 200 and FileExist(NewExe) != empty before writing a swap .cmd that deletes the running exe and moves NewExe over it, then ExitApp. There is no content-length verification, no checksum/signature validation, and FileExist returns true even for a 0-byte or truncated file. If WinHttp returns 200 but the body is truncated (CDN cut the connection mid-transfer, receive timeout fired after partial bytes), the swap proceeds and replaces a working exe with a corrupt one.
- **Cause racine** : WinHttpRequest.Status can be 200 while ResponseBody is incomplete if the transfer is interrupted after headers; the receive-timeout path or a stream write error can leave a partial file. ADODB.Stream.SaveToFile writes whatever bytes arrived. FileExist() only checks presence, not size or validity. The swap .cmd then unconditionally 'move /y NEW_EXE CUR_EXE' and relaunches — bricking the install with no rollback.
- **Scénario** : User clicks Install on a flaky connection. The download stalls after 60% and the receive timeout (30s) fires, or the CDN drops the socket; WinHttp surfaces a 200 with a partial body (or the Stream write throws but the file already has partial bytes from a prior attempt). The swap script overwrites ErgoptiPlus.exe with a truncated binary; on next launch the exe fails to start and the user has no working driver and no automatic rollback.
- **Impact** : Bricked install: a partial/corrupt download silently replaces the working binary, leaving the user with a non-launching driver. High severity because it is destructive and self-inflicted by the updater.
- **Correctif** : Before swapping: (1) verify the downloaded file size against the asset's 'size' field from the release JSON (available in the assets array next to browser_download_url); (2) ideally verify a SHA-256 (CryptoSha256 already exists in this unit) against a digest published in the release; (3) keep a backup copy of CUR_EXE in the swap .cmd and restore it if 'start' of the new exe is not confirmed. At minimum, reject the swap when FileGetSize(NewExe) != expected asset size.
- **Test** : headless-unit: feed _Updater_FindAssetUrl/asset parsing a release JSON and assert a new helper extracts the asset 'size'; then a unit test that simulates a NewExe smaller than the expected size and asserts the swap is aborted (no .cmd written, error logged). The full download path needs live/integration coverage, but the size-mismatch guard is unit-testable.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : This is a logic/destructive-operation finding, not a concurrency claim, so the AHK cooperative-threading model neither gates nor refutes it — I evaluated it as a correctness bug against the real source.

Verified against updater.ahk:1472-1594 (Updater_DownloadAndInstall):
- The ONLY gates before the swap are `if (Req.Status != 200)` (line 1527) and `if !FileExist(NewExe)` (line 1543). There is NO Content-Length check, NO size verification, NO checksum/signature validation. Confirmed by grep: no "size", FileGetSize, Content-Length, backup, or rollback anywhere in updater.ahk.
- `FileExist()` is presence-only in AHK v2 — a 0-byte or truncated file passes line 1543.
- _Updater_FindAssetUrl (1168-1216) extracts only "browser_download_url" from the matched asset object; it never reads the GitHub asset "size" field sitting next to it, so no expected size is even available to compare against.
- The swap .cmd (1559-1574) is unconditionally destructive: `del /q "%CUR_EXE%"` removes the running exe, then `move /y "%NEW_EXE%" "%CUR_EXE%"`. The only post-move check is `if exist "%CUR_EXE%"` before `start` — which a truncated/0-byte/HTML-error-page file passes. No copy of the original exe is retained anywhere, so once `move` lands a corrupt binary the working exe is irreversibly gone. No rollback path. This is the high-blast-radius part: outcome is a non-launching driver, not a recoverable retry.
- CryptoSha256 (adapters/crypto.ahk:48) does exist as claimed, but it hashes a UTF-8 *string* via WriteText/text-mode — it cannot hash a binary file as-is, so the proposed checksum fix is feasible but needs a binary-read variant, not a drop-in call.

Honest caveat that tempers (but does not refute) the finding: the download uses a synchronous WinHttpRequest with finite timeouts (SetTimeouts at 1524). When the receive timeout actually fires mid-body, `Send()` typically THROWS and the `catch as e` at 1538 aborts the swap. So the specific "HTTP 200 with a silently truncated ResponseBody" path is narrower than the description suggests. But the defect does NOT depend on that exact path: a CDN/proxy returning 200 with a short body and clean early close, a 200 serving an HTML/JSON error page, or any non-exe payload all produce a file that passes both gates and is then destructively swapped in with no rollback. The absence of ANY integrity validation before an irreversible binary replacement is the real, present bug.

Not a false positive: nothing serializes, gates, or validates the swap, and the destructive operation has no backup. Core claim holds; only the truncated-200 trigger likelihood is overstated.

<a id="f-healthcheck-recordwarn-never-called"></a>
#### `healthcheck-recordwarn-never-called` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : boot-main (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/healthcheck.ahk:85`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/healthcheck.ahk:93`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/healthcheck.ahk:159`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/crash_reporter.ahk:143`
- **Description** : HealthCheck_RecordWarn() and HealthCheck_RecordError() are defined to bump _HealthCheckWarnCount / _HealthCheckErrCount, but a repo-wide grep finds ZERO call sites (the logger's WARNING/ERROR emit path does not call them). So HealthCheck_Run() always returns warn_count=0 and err_count=0, and the crash report's session_warnings/session_errors and the healthcheck window's '✅ 0 warnings / ✅ 0 errors' are always green — even when the session logged dozens of warnings/errors.
- **Cause racine** : Wiring gap: the logger emits WARNING/ERROR lines (and pushes them to the ring buffer) but never invokes the healthcheck counters that were designed to be called from the logger. The counters are dead inputs.
- **Scénario** : A user hits problems (many WARNING/ERROR log lines), then opens Debug > Diagnostic or triggers a crash report to send to the maintainer. The report confidently shows 0 warnings / 0 errors, masking the very signal that would tell the maintainer something went wrong — a misleading diagnostic exactly when it matters.
- **Impact** : False-clean diagnostics. The recent_issues array (parsed from the ring buffer) still shows the lines, but the headline counters and the ✅/❌ status lie. Undermines crash-report triage.
- **Correctif** : Call HealthCheck_RecordWarn() from the logger's WARNING emit and HealthCheck_RecordError(Msg) from the ERROR emit (guarded with `if IsSet(...)` since the logger loads before healthcheck). Alternatively, derive warn_count/err_count directly by counting [WARNING]/[ERROR] lines in the ring buffer inside HealthCheck_Run (the snapshot is already taken for recent_issues), removing the dead counters entirely.
- **Test** : headless-unit: emit N WARNING and M ERROR lines through the logger, then call HealthCheck_Run() and AssertEqual its warn_count==N and err_count==M (or, for the ring-buffer-derived variant, assert the counts match the number of [WARNING]/[ERROR] lines in LoggerRingBufferSnapshot()).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against source. Repo-wide grep for HealthCheck_RecordWarn|HealthCheck_RecordError returns only the definitions and one doc line inside healthcheck.ahk (lines 18, 85, 93, 106) — zero external callers. The two counter globals _HealthCheckWarnCount/_HealthCheckErrCount are referenced only within healthcheck.ahk (declared 36-37, incremented inside the dead Record* funcs 88/95, read in HealthCheck_Run 159-160). The logger's emit path _LoggerEmit (logger.ahk:379-453) handles WARNING/ERROR by pushing to the ring buffer (423), writing the errors-only sink (437-451) and fanning out (452) but NEVER calls the healthcheck counters. So both counters remain 0 for the whole session. The read sites then propagate the lie: HealthCheck_Run sets warn_count/err_count from the 0 counters; _HealthCheck_SnapshotToHtml (835-836) and the markdown (221-222) render the green checkmark when count==0; crash_reporter.ahk:143-144/207-208 copies them into session_warnings/session_errors. Result: diagnostic window and crash report always show 0 warnings / 0 errors with a green status no matter how many WARNING/ERROR lines were logged. This is a pure wiring/logic bug under the cooperative single-thread model — no concurrency, Critical, Suspend, or serialization defense applies; there is simply no code path that increments the counters. Severity stays medium rather than high because the report is not fully blind: recent_issues (healthcheck.ahk:744-760, parsed from the ring buffer) and the crash report's log_tail (crash_reporter.ahk:160-167) still carry the actual WARNING/ERROR lines, so a maintainer reading the body sees them — only the headline counters and the checkmark/cross status lie.

<a id="f-wrap-symbol-disabled-state-roundtrip-loss"></a>
#### `wrap-symbol-disabled-state-roundtrip-loss` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : hotstrings-config (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/wrap_symbols_config.ahk:330`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/wrap_symbols_config.ahk:363`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/wrap_symbols_config.ahk:253`
- **Description** : _WS_Save serialises the disabled set as a single comma-joined 'chars = "a,b,c"' string (wrap_symbols_config.ahk:363-369). _WS_Load splits on ',' and then does Trimmed := Trim(Ch, " `t") (line 330), stripping leading/trailing spaces before inserting into _WS_Disabled. The shared catalogue (shared/wrap_symbols.json) contains the guillemet pair with left = "« " — a trailing space is part of the opening token. WrapSymbols_Rebuild keys disabling on the exact left string (`if !_WS_Disabled.Has(L)` where L == "« ", line 253).
- **Cause racine** : Save writes the literal left token "« " into the comma list; load Trim()s the space off, producing key "«". On the next launch _WS_Disabled has "«" but the rebuild looks up "« ", so .Has() is false and the pair is re-enabled. The disabled flag round-trips lossily for any opening char with surrounding whitespace. The same comma delimiter would also corrupt any pair whose opening char is literally ',' (none ship today, but the format is fragile and a future catalogue entry or a hand-edited file would silently break).
- **Scénario** : User disables the « » (French quotes) wrap pair from the tray 'Wrap selection' menu. It is off for the current session (in-memory _WS_Disabled has "« "). After a restart/Reload, _WS_Load reads it back as "«", the rebuild does not match "« ", and « » comes back on — the user's disable silently reverts every launch.
- **Impact** : A user preference (disabling a specific wrap symbol) silently fails to persist for whitespace-bearing tokens; over a comma-bearing token it would corrupt the whole disabled list. Quietly reverts user intent on every restart.
- **Correctif** : Stop trimming significant characters: on load, do not Trim() the per-token whitespace (only skip genuinely empty tokens), or — more robustly — change the persistence format to one disabled entry per line (e.g. a [[disabled]] block with char = "…" like [[custom]]) so commas and spaces are never ambiguous. Whichever is chosen, keep _WS_EscapeToml/_WS_UnescapeToml in the loop so the stored token is byte-exact. Add a guard in _WS_Save that escapes/avoids the comma delimiter if the per-line format is not adopted.
- **Test** : headless-unit: point _WS_Config_Path at a temp file, WrapSymbols_DisableAll(), assert _WS_Disabled.Has("« ") is true, then re-run _WS_Load() into a fresh Map and assert the "« " key survives verbatim and WrapSymbols_IsEnabled("« ") stays false. Fails today (key trimmed to "«"), passes after the fix.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code and the shared catalogue. The finding is real and the described mechanism is exact.

VERIFICATION OF THE LOAD-BEARING CLAIM: shared/wrap_symbols.json:23 ships { "left": "« ", "right": " »" } — the opening token carries a trailing space and the closing a leading space. This is the canonical key for the per-symbol disabled state.

ROUND-TRIP TRACE (deterministic, no race involved):
1. In-session disable keys _WS_Disabled on Pair["left"] = "« " (wrap_symbols_config.ahk:167/184/204). The tray menu (tray_menu.ahk:2066/2077/2083) and the rebuild lookup (wrap_symbols_config.ahk:253 `if !_WS_Disabled.Has(L)` with L=="« ") all use the same space-bearing token, so the disable is consistent and WORKS for the live session — this is why the regression is invisible until restart.
2. _WS_Save (lines 363-369) joins the disabled keys with ',' into chars = "...,« ,...", preserving the trailing space.
3. _WS_Load (line 330) does `Trimmed := Trim(Ch, " `t")`, stripping the trailing space, then stores key "«" (line 332).
4. After restart, _WS_Rebuild looks up `_WS_Disabled.Has("« ")` (the catalogue token still has the space) but the map only holds "«". .Has() is false → the guillemet pair is silently re-enabled. The user's disable reverts on every launch.

Only the left token round-trips (right "« " variant / " »" is never inserted into the disabled set — disabling sets Pair["left"] keys only), so the corruption is confined to the « » pair among shipping symbols, exactly as the finding states. The secondary comma-delimiter fragility (a left token literally equal to ',' would split into empty tokens) is correctly noted as latent — none ship today.

CONCURRENCY MODEL CHECK (the adversarial angle): This is NOT a race / lost-keystroke / Critical / suspend issue. _WS_Save and _WS_Load run on the menu-callback thread and at WrapSymbols_Init; they never touch the keystroke hot path (the watcher only calls WrapSymbols_GetActivePairs which returns a map reference). AHK's cooperative single-threading neither causes nor masks this — it is a pure serialization-format / normalization logic bug, deterministic on every restart. There is no guard, serialization, or A_IsSuspended path that prevents it.

I could not refute it: there is no later code that re-normalizes the catalogue token to the trimmed form, no alias keying, and the catalogue token is fixed by the shared JSON. The bug stands.

<a id="f-screenshot-region-clipwait-clobber"></a>
#### `screenshot-region-clipwait-clobber` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : gestures (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:1353`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:1364`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:1367`
- **Description** : In 'save' mode, GestureScreenshotRegion sets A_Clipboard := "" (destroying whatever the user had on the clipboard), invokes Snip & Sketch, then ClipWait(30,2). If the user cancels (Esc) the ClipWait times out after 30s and the function returns — but the original clipboard contents are gone forever (no ClipboardAll() save/restore, unlike GetSelection which does save/restore). Even on success the user's prior clipboard is replaced by the screenshot.
- **Cause racine** : Direct A_Clipboard mutation without the save/restore pattern used elsewhere (GetSelection wraps in ClipboardAll() + try/finally). ClipWait blocks the calling pseudo-thread for up to 30s, and on timeout there is no restore.
- **Scénario** : User has important text on the clipboard, triggers a 'screenshot_region_save' gesture, then presses Esc to cancel the snip. Their clipboard text is now permanently lost, and the action thread was blocked for up to 30s.
- **Impact** : Silent data loss of the user's clipboard, plus a 30s thread block on cancel. Medium severity (data loss, but user-initiated action).
- **Correctif** : Wrap the capture in OldClip := ClipboardAll() / try ... finally A_Clipboard := OldClip, mirroring GetSelection. On timeout/cancel restore the clipboard. Consider lowering the 30s ClipWait or making it fire-and-forget like the clipboard-mode branch.
- **Test** : headless-unit is hard because ClipWait needs a real clipboard event; use a meta/static test asserting GestureScreenshotRegion contains a ClipboardAll() save and a restore in a finally block (the same shape the GetSelection regression relies on). Flag full behavior as live.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the actual source. GestureScreenshotRegion (gestures.ahk:1353) in "save" mode does `A_Clipboard := ""` (1364), `SendEvent("#+s")` (1365), then `ClipWait(30, 2)` (1367). There is NO `ClipboardAll()` capture and NO restore on any return path: on timeout/cancel (1368-1370) it returns leaving the clipboard blank; on success Snip & Sketch leaves the screenshot image on the clipboard, so the prior contents are gone either way. This is a genuine, unconditional clobber of the user's clipboard for a gesture whose intent is to save a PNG to disk. The finding correctly cites the contrasting proven pattern: GetSelection() in lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:305-320 wraps the same kind of clipboard mutation in `OldClipboard := ClipboardAll()` + `try { A_Clipboard := ""; ... } finally { A_Clipboard := OldClipboard }`, which is exactly what is missing here. The clipboard-mode branch (1354-1360) is correctly fire-and-forget, confirming the save branch is the outlier. On the AHK concurrency angle: the action runs synchronously inside the gesture hotkey thread (GestureDispatch -> `GESTURE_ACTIONS[ActionName].Fn()` at line 1702, bound to ^#+F1..F10), so ClipWait blocks that pseudo-thread for up to 30s. ClipWait is a yielding built-in, so the message pump and low-level keyboard hook keep running — this is NOT a dropped-keystroke (LowLevelHooksTimeout) event, so the 30s "thread block" is accurate but it is not a lost-keystroke bug. The data-loss claim is real and specific to save mode. One refinement: "even on success the prior clipboard is replaced" is only an unintended side effect in save mode (clipboard mode legitimately leaves the image there) — but save mode is exactly the branch flagged, so the conclusion stands.

<a id="f-av-mute-heuristic-and-doc-mismatch"></a>
#### `av-mute-heuristic-and-doc-mismatch` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : keylogger-sensors (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_av_state.ahk:179`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_av_state.ahk:122`
- **Description** : The module/function docstrings claim volume+mute are read via the IAudioEndpointVolume COM interface (lines 33-35, 129-131), but the implementation uses winmm waveOutGetVolume (line 171) which is a legacy per-waveOut-handle API, not the system master endpoint, and has no mute concept. KL_AV_GetMasterMuted (line 179-184) therefore fakes mute as `vol <= 0.01`, and it calls KL_AV_GetMasterVolume() a SECOND time (line 182) — so each fast tick queries volume twice and treats 'volume turned to zero' as 'muted', which is wrong (real mute keeps the volume level).
- **Cause racine** : waveOutGetVolume(0,...) reads the volume of waveOut device handle 0, which on modern Windows is the per-app session mapped through the audio engine, not the master endpoint, and returns 0xFFFF on many systems regardless of the real slider. The mute=vol<=0.01 heuristic conflates a genuine mute (which preserves the level) with the user dragging the slider to zero, producing false muted/unmuted volume_change events.
- **Scénario** : During a call the user clicks the mute button (level stays at e.g. 50). waveOutGetVolume still reports ~50, so no 'muted' event is logged — the meeting-activity signal the feature is designed for is missed. Conversely dragging the slider to 0 emits a spurious 'muted' event.
- **Impact** : Volume/mute events are unreliable; the meeting-activity correlation (the feature's stated primary purpose) is wrong in both directions. Minor double syscall per second.
- **Correctif** : Either implement the documented IAudioEndpointVolume path (CoCreateInstance MMDeviceEnumerator -> GetDefaultAudioEndpoint -> Activate IAudioEndpointVolume -> GetMasterVolumeLevelScalar + GetMute) wrapped in try, or fix the docstrings to describe the winmm heuristic and stop double-calling GetMasterVolume (pass the already-read vol into the mute decision). Prefer the COM path so mute is real. Cache the COM interface across ticks rather than re-creating it.
- **Test** : headless-unit: extract a pure `_KL_AV_ClassifyVolumeChange(prev_vol, prev_muted, vol, muted, delta_pct)` and assert it emits 'muted' only on a real mute-bit flip and 'level' only on >=VOLUME_DELTA_PCT change with no mute flip; include a vector where vol drops to 0 with mute unchanged and assert it is classified 'level', not 'muted'.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : All three core claims verified against source (no concurrency model needed — this is a pure logic/doc correctness bug, not a race). (1) Docstring lies: lines 33-35 and 129-131 say volume/mute come from "the IAudioEndpointVolume COM interface" / "CoCreateInstance IAudioEndpointVolume", but the implementation at line 171 is `DllCall("winmm\waveOutGetVolume", "Ptr", 0, "Ptr", buf.Ptr)` — the legacy waveOut API, not COM. Grep for IAudioEndpointVolume/GetMute/GetMasterVolumeLevelScalar finds ZERO implementation anywhere in the Windows tree; the COM path is aspirational docstring text only. (2) Fake mute: KL_AV_GetMasterMuted (lines 179-184) returns `(vol <= 0.01) ? 1 : 0`, and its own comment (lines 180-181) admits "No lightweight native path — default to 0... so the mute transition is surfaced at least when the volume drops to 0." winmm waveOut genuinely has no mute bit, so `vol<=0.01` cannot distinguish a real mute (which preserves the slider level) from a slider dragged to zero. The finding's scenario is directionally correct: a mute click that keeps level=50 emits no event (the meeting-activity signal is missed), and a drag-to-zero emits a spurious "muted". (3) Double syscall confirmed: line 132 calls KL_AV_GetMasterVolume(), then line 133 calls KL_AV_GetMasterMuted() which re-invokes KL_AV_GetMasterVolume() at line 182 — two waveOutGetVolume DllCalls per 1000ms tick. Caveat: the finding's root_cause overstates one technical detail — `waveOutGetVolume(0,...)` takes a device ID (0 = first device), not a "handle 0 mapped to a per-app session", and the blanket "returns 0xFFFF on many systems" claim is not generally true; on systems with per-device volume it does track a real level. That nuance does not undermine the core bug (no mute concept in waveOut). Severity: this is keylogger TELEMETRY feeding a dashboard correlation, not a keystroke path — it cannot drop keys, lock the keyboard, corrupt shared state, or break the pause invariant, so impact is bounded to inaccurate analytics plus a flatly-false docstring (a real maintenance hazard). Medium is defensible at the low end; I keep medium. Separately and OUT OF SCOPE: KL_AV_FastTick (lines 116-120) is a SetTimer that only checks `Keylogger.initialized`, not `A_IsSuspended` — a latent pause-invariant gap noted but not part of this finding, and the two volume DllCalls are fast/non-blocking so they do not cause lost keystrokes.

<a id="f-ergo-roi-count-synthetic-keystrokes"></a>
#### `ergo-roi-count-synthetic-keystrokes` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : keylogger-sensors (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_ergonomics.ahk:142`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:160`
- **Description** : KL_Hook_OnChar (keylogger_hook.ahk:242-250) stamps meta['s']=1 while Keylogger.synth_active (auto-typing from a hotstring expansion or accepted LLM prediction) so synthetic chars are excluded from the manual `chars` count — but it still calls KL_Ergo_OnKeystroke and KL_Roi_OnChar unconditionally. Neither module checks Keylogger.synth_active, so synthetic characters inflate the continuous-typing block char count, flow WPM, pinky/backspace runs, hesitation delays, and the ROI candidate-word frequencies.
- **Cause racine** : The synthetic-source guard exists only at the buffer-push level in the hook, not at the per-keystroke fan-out to the ergonomic/ROI sensors. When a hotstring expands a 30-char replacement, those 30 SendInput chars flow through OnChar with synth_active=true and are counted by KLErgo.block_chars and KLRoi.current_word/word_counts as if hand-typed.
- **Scénario** : A user with many active hotstrings types abbreviations that expand to long phrases. The ergonomics dashboard reports an inflated WPM / continuous-block char count and may emit a spurious flow_window or pinky_overload (e.g. an expansion containing repeated 'a'); the ROI candidate detector may flag expanded text as a 'new trigger candidate' it just produced.
- **Impact** : Ergonomic and ROI metrics are inflated/wrong precisely for power users who rely on expansions — the audience the feature targets. ROI can recommend automating text the automation itself generated.
- **Correctif** : Pass the synthetic flag into the fan-out and skip: in KL_Hook_OnChar guard `if !Keylogger.synth_active { KL_Ergo_OnKeystroke(...); KL_Roi_OnChar(c) }`, or pass is_synth and early-return inside KL_Ergo_OnKeystroke / KL_Roi_OnChar when set. Keep delay accounting consistent (treat the first manual key after a synth burst with a fresh delay).
- **Test** : headless-unit: call KL_Ergo_OnKeystroke / KL_Roi_OnChar (or their refactored is_synth-aware variants) with is_synth=true for a run of chars and AssertEqual KLErgo.block_chars and KLRoi.word_counts.Count stayed unchanged; with is_synth=false assert they advance. Encodes the synthetic-exclusion contract.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against source. The synthetic-source flag is real and active during expansions: KL_MarkSynthetic("hotstring") is set in hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1162/1275 (and engine.ahk:502) and KL_MarkSynthetic("llm") in llm_bridge.ahk:488, each cleared only on a DEFERRED timer (SetTimer((*) => KL_ClearSynthetic(), -delay)) precisely so Keylogger.synth_active stays true while the injected keys' InputHook callbacks run later off the message pump. The module docstring (keylogger_hook.ahk:7-18) and keylogger.ahk:124-129 both state the auto-typed output re-enters the InputHook. The synthetic backspace+replacement burst is SendInput(Burst) (hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1347), so both the {BackSpace N} chars and the replacement chars flow through the hook.

In KL_Hook_OnChar the synth guard exists ONLY at the buffer-push/meta level: lines 242-245 stamp meta["s"]=1 + meta["st"]. But lines 249-250 then call KL_Ergo_OnKeystroke(delay, last_vk) and KL_Roi_OnChar(c) UNCONDITIONALLY — no `if !Keylogger.synth_active` gate. Same in KL_Hook_OnKeyDown: line 318 calls KL_Ergo_OnKeystroke(delay, vk, vk=0x08) for synthetic backspaces unconditionally. I read both target functions in full: KL_Ergo_OnKeystroke (keylogger_ergonomics.ahk:142-158) and KL_Roi_OnChar (keylogger_trigger_roi.ahk:160-173). Neither reads Keylogger.synth_active nor meta["s"]; they only guard on Keylogger.initialized. So synthetic chars increment KLErgo.block_chars (ergonomics:212), feed flow WPM (ergonomics:219/256), bump KLErgo.bs_run toward backspace_burst (ergonomics:152 → CheckBsBurst threshold 5) and KLErgo.pinky_run toward pinky_overload (ergonomics:313, threshold 4), and bump KLRoi.word_counts / current_word toward new_trigger_candidate (roi:166/188, threshold 5).

The design intent that synthetic must be excluded from manual-typing metrics is proven by the offline walker (keylogger_walker.ahk:481,503,548-563): it reads is_synthetic from meta["s"] and routes synthetic chars to hs_chars/llm_chars (NOT manual `chars`), and even uses !is_synthetic to gate n-gram continuity (line 503). The two real-time sensors simply never received that same flag. This is a logic bug, not a race: AHK's cooperative model is irrelevant here because the flag is correctly held true across the burst — the sensors just don't consult it.

Severity medium is right (arguably the low end of medium). Metrics are corrupted for power users (the target audience), and short-threshold events (pinky_overload≥4, backspace_burst≥5) genuinely false-fire on expansions: any trigger ≥5 chars emits ≥5 synthetic backspaces → spurious backspace_burst attributed to the user; a replacement containing 4+ repeated pinky chars → spurious pinky_overload; repeated expanded words → spurious new_trigger_candidate recommending automation of text the automation produced. The long-window events (continuous_typing_block 20min, flow_window 5min) won't false-fire on their own but carry inflated char/WPM payloads. No data loss, no keystroke drop, no crash — purely metric integrity. I also noted a sibling instance outside the cited files: hook line 252 WPMWidget_Push(false,false) counts each synthetic char as a manual keystroke (is_hs=false) in the WPM ring; the fix should cover it for consistency.

<a id="f-no-profile-delete-path"></a>
#### `no-profile-delete-path` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : tray-llm (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/menu_profiles.ahk:224-245`
- **Description** : LLM_Tray_OnUserProfileClick shows a 3-button MsgBox whose text lists Use / Edit / Delete (the third line is t("menu.profiles.delete_profile")). The handler maps Yes->use, No->edit, and Cancel->nothing. A comment on line 244 explicitly says 'Cancel = delete — we use a separate confirm to avoid accidental deletion', but no such confirm or delete code exists anywhere: grepping the whole windows tree, user_profiles is only ever Push-ed (create/clone) and read — never RemoveAt/Delete-d. The delete menu line is therefore a dead affordance.
- **Cause racine** : The Cancel branch of the MsgBox("3 32") choice was left unimplemented; the deletion logic the comment promises was never written, and there is no other entry point that removes an element from _LLM_Tray["user_profiles"].
- **Scénario** : User creates a few custom profiles, then opens the profile submenu, clicks a custom profile, and picks the 'Delete' (Cancel) button expecting it to be removed. Nothing happens. The profile is permanently un-deletable from the UI; the user must hand-edit config.toml. Over time the profile menu and the Ctrl+1..9 hotkey order fill up with junk profiles they can't remove.
- **Impact** : A documented, user-visible feature (delete profile) is completely non-functional. Profiles accumulate with no in-app removal, and they consume Ctrl+<n> hotkey slots (LLM_Tray_GetHotkeyProfileOrder appends user profiles after built-ins up to LLM_PROFILE_HOTKEY_LIMIT), pushing real profiles out of reach.
- **Correctif** : Implement the delete path: on the Cancel branch (or, better, replace the tri-state MsgBox with an explicit 'Delete' confirm like _LLM_Tray_RemoveActiveApiEntry already does for API entries), find the profile by id in _LLM_Tray["user_profiles"], RemoveAt it, fix up _LLM_Tray["profile_id"] if it pointed at the deleted profile (fall back to a built-in), then LLM_Tray_SaveConfig() + LLM_Engine_Init(LLM_Tray_BuildOpts()) + LLM_Tray_Build(). Re-run LLM_Tray_BindProfileHotkeys() since the hotkey order changes.
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/test_llm_profiles.ahk: build a _LLM_Tray with two user profiles, call the (new) LLM_Tray_DeleteUserProfile(pid) helper extracted from the handler, AssertEqual(_LLM_Tray["user_profiles"].Length, 1) and AssertEqual that profile_id reset to a built-in when the active profile was the deleted one. Plus a meta-static test asserting menu_profiles.ahk actually contains a RemoveAt/Delete call against user_profiles so the dead-branch regression can't return.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the actual source. LLM_Tray_OnUserProfileClick (menu_profiles.ahk:224-245) shows a "3 32" MsgBox (Yes/No/Cancel + question icon) whose three lines are t("menu.profiles.use_profile"), t("menu.profiles.edit_profile"), t("menu.profiles.delete_profile"). The en.json text for the third is literally "🗑️ Delete…" (and Supprimer/Löschen/Eliminar in all 20+ locales), so a Delete affordance is genuinely advertised. The handler maps "Yes"->LLM_Tray_SetProfile, "No"->LLM_Tray_PromptEditProfile, and "Cancel"->nothing; line 244 comments "Cancel = delete — we use a separate confirm to avoid accidental deletion" but execution simply falls off the end of the function. Grepping the whole windows tree for any mutation of _LLM_Tray["user_profiles"] confirms it is only ever .Push-ed (create at :276, clone at :344) and edited in place (:305-308) or read — there is NO RemoveAt/Delete against it anywhere. The only .Delete in this file (:192) targets app_profile_overrides (a Map), not user_profiles. The promised "separate confirm" was never written: its i18n key dialog.llm.delete_profile_prefix ships in all 20+ locales yet has zero references in the Windows source. Decisive corroboration: the mirror macOS driver implements the full delete path (profiles_manager.lua:262-288) — confirm dialog, build a kept[] excluding pid, reassign state.llm_user_profiles, fall back to "basic" if the active profile was deleted — and the two drivers are explicitly designed to agree, so the Windows half is simply missing. The AHK concurrency model offers nothing to refute here: this whole flow is one blocking menu-callback thread (MsgBox blocks), there is no race, Critical/Suspend/serialization angle, and no keystroke-loss vector — it is a pure unimplemented-branch logic bug. The dead affordance is real and user-visible exactly as described; the secondary impact (deleted-but-undeletable profiles consuming Ctrl+1..9 slots up to LLM_PROFILE_HOTKEY_LIMIT, pushing real profiles out) is also real per LLM_Tray_GetHotkeyProfileOrder appending user profiles after built-ins.

<a id="f-nav-val-modifiers-not-wired"></a>
#### `nav-val-modifiers-not-wired` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : tray-llm (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/tab_accept.ahk:52-62`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ui/tray_llm/menu_settings.ahk:316-335`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/tooltip.ahk:1741-1765`
- **Description** : The Navigation submenu (LLM_Tray_PromptNavModifiers / LLM_Tray_PromptValModifiers) lets the user set nav_modifiers (arrow navigation modifier) and val_modifiers (digit-select modifier, default 'alt'). These values flow into LLM_Engine/BuildOpts and into the tooltip module, but lib/tooltip.ahk consumes _LLM_Tooltip_NavMods/_LLM_Tooltip_ValMods ONLY in _LLM_BuildNavHint / _LLM_BuildShortcutSuffix to render the footer hint text. The actual navigation hotkeys in tab_accept.ahk are hardcoded: ~Up/~Down (bare arrows, ignoring nav_modifiers) and !1..!9 (Alt+digits, ignoring val_modifiers). So changing the setting changes only what the hint *claims*, not what works.
- **Cause racine** : tab_accept.ahk registers static literal hotkeys at load time instead of (re)binding them from the configured modifier strings the way LLM_Tray_BindProfileHotkeys does for Ctrl+<n>. The tooltip module's nav/val mod globals are display-only.
- **Scénario** : User sets val_modifiers = 'ctrl' in the Navigation submenu. The tooltip footer now reads 'Ctrl+1 to select prediction 1', but pressing Ctrl+1 fires the profile-switch hotkey ^1 from menu_profiles.ahk (which is HotIf-active whenever the LLM is enabled) — switching profile instead of selecting a slot — while Alt+1 still silently selects the slot despite no hint saying so. The user's configured modifier is both ignored and actively misleading, and there is a hard collision between the displayed val_modifiers='ctrl' and the live ^1..^9 profile bindings.
- **Impact** : Two user-configurable settings are effectively no-ops with deceptive hint text; one common value ('ctrl') collides with the always-on profile hotkeys, producing a surprising profile switch when the user thinks they're selecting a prediction.
- **Correctif** : Drive the slot-nav bindings from configuration like the profile hotkeys do: add an LLM_Tray_BindNavHotkeys() that, under HotIf(LLM_Tooltip_GetText() != ''), registers Up/Down and 1..9 with the prefix derived from nav_modifiers/val_modifiers (reusing LLM_Tray_ShortcutToAhk's modifier parsing), Off-ing the previous set on each change. Call it from LLM_Tray_Init and after LLM_Tray_PromptNavModifiers/PromptValModifiers. Also detect and warn on the ctrl collision with the profile hotkeys.
- **Test** : meta-static in tests/meta/: assert tab_accept.ahk no longer contains the hardcoded '!1::' literal AND that a Bind function references _LLM_Tray["val_modifiers"]/["nav_modifiers"]. headless-unit: feed val_modifiers='ctrl' through LLM_Tray_ShortcutToAhk-style conversion and AssertEqual the produced hotkey string for digit 1 is '^1', then assert the binder skips/warns because '^1' is reserved by the profile hotkeys.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source; this is a pure logic/wiring bug, so the AHK cooperative-threading model is irrelevant (no race, no lost keystroke). tab_accept.ahk:52-62 registers STATIC literal hotkeys at load time — ~Up/~Down and !1..!9 — under #HotIf LLM_Tooltip_GetText() != "". They never read nav_modifiers/val_modifiers. The settings prompts (menu_settings.ahk:479-498) only mutate _LLM_Tray["nav_modifiers"]/["val_modifiers"], persist, then call LLM_Engine_Init(LLM_Tray_BuildOpts()) and LLM_Tray_Build(); none of these re-bind hotkeys. BuildOpts routes the values into the tooltip via LLM_TooltipSetDisplayOpts (tooltip.ahk:1795-1800), feeding the DISPLAY-ONLY globals _LLM_Tooltip_NavMods/_LLM_Tooltip_ValMods, which are consumed only by _LLM_FormatValModifiers→_LLM_BuildShortcutSuffix (slot suffix, tooltip.ahk:1757-1770) and _LLM_BuildNavHint (footer, tooltip.ahk:1874-1901). So changing the setting changes only what the hint claims, not what works — exactly as the finding states. The Ctrl collision is also real: LLM_Tray_BindProfileHotkeys (menu_profiles.ahk:490-507) binds ^1..^9 under HotIf(_LLM_Tray_IsProfileHotkeyActive) = enabled + >=1 profile, which is true whenever the LLM is on. With val_modifiers="ctrl" the footer renders the ctrl glyph + digit (_LLM_FormatValModifiers maps "ctrl"->the Ctrl symbol, tooltip.ahk:1751), so the hint says Ctrl+1 while ^1 actually fires the profile switch and the unadvertised Alt+1 silently still selects the slot. The codebase already proves it knows how to bind config-derived hotkeys dynamically — LLM_Tray_ApplyTriggerShortcut + LLM_Tray_ShortcutToAhk (menu_settings.ahk:532-596) do exactly that with Hotkey(...,"Off"/"On") — which makes the static nav bindings a genuine inconsistency, not an intentional design. No guard, suspend check, or serialization changes any of this; the two settings are genuine no-ops with deceptive hints.

<a id="f-appstate-orphaned-parallel-state"></a>
#### `appstate-orphaned-parallel-state` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : config-toml (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/app_state.ahk:54`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/app_state.ahk:99`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/app_state.ahk:118`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:124`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:338`
- **Description** : app_state.ahk declares itself the 'single-source-of-truth for all mutable runtime state' and is #Include'd in production (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:124). But NONE of its state is read or written by production code: AppState['last_sent_key_time'] / AppState_TouchLastSentKey / AppState_PruneLastSentKeyTime are shadowed by the global LastSentCharacterKeyTime + _PruneLastSentKeyTime in modules/layout.ahk; number_of_repetitions/activity_simulation/one_shot_shift_enabled/remapped_list are shadowed by the standalone globals NumberOfRepetitions, ActivitySimulation, OneShotShiftEnabled, RemappedList. The only references to AppState's accessors outside app_state.ahk are in test_stubs.ahk.
- **Cause racine** : The module was written as a consolidation target ('Replaces the scattered top-level globals') but the cut-over never happened, leaving two parallel copies of every conceptual state field. The hot-path prune logic is even duplicated verbatim (app_state.ahk:128 vs modules/layout.ahk:349). Because the duplication is silent, a future developer wiring one consumer to AppState['number_of_repetitions'] and leaving another on the global NumberOfRepetitions would get two diverging counters with no compile error.
- **Scénario** : A maintainer reads the authoritative-sounding app_state.ahk docstring and routes a new nav-layer feature through AppState_GetNumberOfRepetitions(), while the existing nav code keeps using the plain global NumberOfRepetitions. The two values silently diverge: the Vim-style repeat count is read as 1 by the new feature while the user actually pressed 5, so the action repeats the wrong number of times. Nothing logs or throws.
- **Impact** : Latent state-divergence bug + dead code that violates rules 5.2/5.6. It directly undermines the documented invariant of the module and is a landmine for any future state consolidation; also wastes the prune/reset machinery that runs on no real data.
- **Correctif** : Either (a) finish the cut-over: make the standalone globals (NumberOfRepetitions, LastSentCharacterKeyTime, ActivitySimulation, OneShotShiftEnabled, RemappedList) thin readers/writers of the AppState Map (or delete the globals and route all sites through AppState_* accessors), so there is exactly one copy; or (b) if the cut-over is abandoned, delete app_state.ahk's unused fields/accessors and the #Include, per rule 5.6 (no dead/duplicate state). Do not leave both alive.
- **Test** : meta-static (tests/meta): grep the production tree (excluding tests/ and app_state.ahk itself) for any read/write of AppState_GetNumberOfRepetitions/AppState_TouchLastSentKey/AppState['...']; assert the module is either fully wired (at least one production caller) OR fully removed. Encodes the root cause: 'AppState must not be a parallel unused copy of the live globals.'  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : Read all cited code. The finding's factual core is fully verified (and actually understated). This is a dead-code / single-source-of-truth claim, not a concurrency claim, so the AHK cooperative-threading skepticism does not refute it.

Verified facts:
- lib/app_state.ahk is #Include'd in production at ErgoptiPlus.ahk:124, and its docstring declares itself the "Single-source-of-truth for all mutable runtime state."
- Grep over the whole windows tree shows AppState appears in exactly TWO files: lib/app_state.ahk itself and tests/test_stubs.ahk. No production module reads or writes AppState or calls any AppState_* accessor. It is provably inert in production.
- Every conceptual field has a live standalone global that production actually uses: NumberOfRepetitions (nav_layer.ahk SC002..SC00B, ActionLayer counts; nav_layer_helpers.ahk owns the writer), LastSentCharacterKeyTime (layout.ahk:339, capslock.ahk:178, lalt.ahk:124/292, hotstring_engine.ahk:550), ActivitySimulation (win.ahk, gestures.ahk), OneShotShiftEnabled (one_shot_shift.ahk), RemappedList (layout.ahk:393, shortcuts/utils.ahk:31). 
- The prune logic is duplicated verbatim: AppState_PruneLastSentKeyTime (app_state.ahk:128-140) is byte-for-byte the same two-pass deletion as _PruneLastSentKeyTime (layout.ahk:349-362), each on a different backing Map.
- The finding UNDERCOUNTS the duplication on two axes the audit missed: (1) the threshold constants LAST_SENT_KEY_TIME_MAX_AGE_MS and LAST_SENT_KEY_TIME_PRUNE_AT are declared twice (app_state.ahk:45/49 AND ErgoptiPlus.ahk:289/292); (2) the toml_strict_canon_in_progress field is shadowed by the standalone global _TOML_STRICT_CANON_IN_PROGRESS (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:301, toml_helpers.ahk:372-385, onboarding.ahk:1294) — the AppState copy is never touched in production. So six fields + two constants + one prune function are all duplicated.
