- The only thing that ever writes AppState is the TEST stub UpdateLastSentCharacter (test_stubs.ahk:435-441), which mirrors into AppState_TouchLastSentKey. The REAL UpdateLastSentCharacter (layout.ahk:333-347) does NOT mirror into AppState. So the production write path bypasses AppState entirely.

This squarely violates the project's own rule 5.2 (single source of truth for defaults) and rule 5.6 (no dead/duplicate state), both codified in CLAUDE.md, and it actively undermines the module's own authoritative docstring — a genuine trap for a future maintainer.

Adversarial push-back on severity (the one place the finding overreaches): there is NO current runtime bug. AHK's cooperative model is irrelevant here because AppState is never read/written in production, so there is nothing to tear, drop, or diverge today. All live reads and writes go through the standalone globals consistently; no keystroke is lost, no counter diverges, nothing throws or mislogs at present. The finding's "scenario" (diverging repeat counts) is purely hypothetical and contingent on a future developer wiring a new consumer to AppState while leaving the old one on the global. A latent dead-code/style hazard with zero current incorrect behavior is a maintainability landmine, not a live defect — that is a medium, not a high.

<a id="f-pathstoml-read-missing-utf8-encoding"></a>
#### `pathstoml-read-missing-utf8-encoding` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : config-toml (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_helpers.ahk:512`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_helpers.ahk:497`
- **Description** : paths.toml is WRITTEN with FileOpen(FilePath, 'w', 'UTF-8') (line 497) but READ with `FileRead(FilePath)` (line 512) — no encoding argument. AHK's FileRead without an encoding uses A_FileEncoding (default CP0/system ANSI). Every other reader in this unit (ReadTomlFile, ParseTomlFile, ApplyConfigToml, ReadPersonalInfoToml) passes 'UTF-8' explicitly.
- **Cause racine** : Encoding asymmetry between writer and reader. The auto-generated file carries a UTF-8 BOM (which FileRead auto-detects regardless of the arg), so the common path works — but a BOM-less paths.toml (user hand-edits and saves as ANSI/UTF-8-no-BOM, or a file produced by a different tool) is decoded with the system codepage. A ConfigDirPath containing non-ASCII characters (accented Windows username/folder, e.g. C:\Users\Adrien-with-accent\...) is then mojibake, and StrReplace('/','\') yields a path that does not exist.
- **Scénario** : A user on a French/accented Windows profile points ConfigDirPath at a folder under their accented home directory and saves paths.toml without a BOM. On next boot the path is mis-decoded, the personal hotstrings/config directory resolves to a non-existent path, FileExist returns false everywhere downstream, and the user's entire personal configuration silently fails to load — with no error, just 'my custom hotstrings disappeared'.
- **Impact** : Silent total loss of personal config resolution for any user with non-ASCII characters in their config-dir path and a BOM-less paths.toml. Privacy/data-integrity adjacent: the custom dir often holds personal_info.toml.
- **Correctif** : Change line 512 to `FileRead(FilePath, 'UTF-8')` to match the writer and every other reader in the unit. Consider also reading via ReadTomlFile for cache consistency, but at minimum fix the encoding.
- **Test** : headless-unit (test_toml_helpers): write a paths.toml WITHOUT a BOM (FileOpen with 'UTF-8-RAW') containing a ConfigDirPath whose value embeds Chr(0x00E9) (e accent), call ReadPathsToml, and AssertEqual the round-tripped path contains the accented byte sequence intact. Fails on the ANSI-decoded mojibake before the fix, passes after.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against source. The encoding asymmetry is real: paths.toml is written with FileOpen(...,"w","UTF-8") in all three writers (toml_helpers.ahk:497, ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1980, onboarding.ahk:1274), but read at toml_helpers.ahk:512 with bare FileRead(FilePath) — no encoding arg — while every sibling reader in the unit passes "UTF-8" (e.g. ParseTomlFile at line 89). ReadPathsToml is genuinely the only un-encoded reader.

The AHK concurrency model is irrelevant here: this is a synchronous boot-time read (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:314, top-level global init), not a hotkey/timer/InputHook path — no race, no lost keystroke. It is a pure decode logic bug, but a real one.

Common path is safe (finding concedes this correctly): AHK v2 FileOpen(...,"UTF-8") writes a UTF-8 BOM by default (BOM-less needs "UTF-8-RAW"), and FileRead auto-detects a BOM regardless of the absent encoding arg. So app-written files decode fine. The auto-generated default also can't trip it because it writes a COMMENTED "# ConfigDirPath" line (line 504) which the "#" guard at line 514 skips before RegExMatch.

The bug bites only on a BOM-less paths.toml with a non-ASCII ConfigDirPath value. That vector is realistic, not exotic: the file's own comments (lines 500-501, "Edit this file to point to your personal configuration folder") explicitly invite hand-editing, and the default save mode of VS Code / most modern editors / POSIX tools is UTF-8 without BOM. Combined with this project's francophone audience (accented home dirs like C:\Users\Adrien-accent\...), a UTF-8 "e-accent" (bytes C3 A9) gets decoded under the system ANSI codepage to mojibake; StrReplace("/","\") then yields a non-existent path; FileExist fails everywhere downstream; personal config silently vanishes with no log. Matches the description, scenario and impact.

<a id="f-traymenu-separator-addstandard"></a>
#### `traymenu-separator-addstandard` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : adapters-os (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/tray_menu.ahk:58`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/tray_menu.ahk:60`
- **Description** : For an item flagged `separator:true`, TrayMenuSetMenu calls `A_TrayMenu.AddStandard()` (line 60). AddStandard() injects AHK's full default tray menu (Open, Help, Window submenu, Pause Script, Suspend Hotkeys, Reload Script, Edit Script, Exit) — not a horizontal separator line. The contract intent (per the doc comment at line 21 and 50) is clearly a separator.
- **Cause racine** : Wrong AHK v2 API: a menu separator is added with `Menu.Add()` (no arguments). `Menu.AddStandard()` is a distinct call that appends the standard script-control items.
- **Scénario** : Same dormant path as the raw-Add finding: if `setMenu` is used to build the tray menu from the shared item list, every intended separator instead dumps the default AHK control menu — including a live 'Exit' and 'Reload Script' the product UI deliberately hides — into the user's custom menu, and provides a one-click way to kill or reload the driver from an unexpected place.
- **Impact** : Cosmetically broken menu plus an unintended, user-reachable Exit/Reload/Suspend surface. Suspend Hotkeys in particular only half-pauses the driver (the documented partial-suspend foot-gun). Latent until setMenu is used in production.
- **Correctif** : Replace `try A_TrayMenu.AddStandard()` with `try A_TrayMenu.Add()` (no arguments) in the separator branch.
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub A_TrayMenu with a recorder object exposing Add(args*); call TrayMenuSetMenu([{separator:true}]) and assert the recorded call was Add() with zero arguments, not AddStandard().  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Read the cited code at static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/tray_menu.ahk:51-73. The separator branch is exactly as described: at line 58 `IsSep := Item.Has("separator") and Item["separator"]`, then at line 60 `try A_TrayMenu.AddStandard()`. The finding's AHK v2 API claim is correct: `Menu.Add()` with NO arguments adds a horizontal separator line, while `Menu.AddStandard()` is a distinct documented method that appends AHK's full default tray menu (Open / Help / Window / Pause Script / Suspend Hotkeys / Reload Script / Edit Script / Exit). So every item flagged `separator:true` injects the entire standard control menu instead of a separator. The intent is unambiguous: the adapter's doc comments (lines 21 and 50) list `separator` as a flag, and the cross-platform port contract at static/ergopti_plus/shared/ports/TrayMenu.spec.js:82 defines `separator:boolean // true = render as a separator line (ignores other fields)`. This is NOT a concurrency bug — there is no race, no lost-keystroke path, no Critical/InputHook/SetTimer interaction; TrayMenuSetMenu produces the wrong menu content deterministically regardless of which pseudo-thread invokes it, so the AHK cooperative-threading model offers no refutation. The wrapping `try` does not mask the bug: AddStandard() succeeds (it does not throw), so the wrong menu is actually rendered, not swallowed. On exposure: I grepped the whole windows tree — TrayMenuSetMenu is referenced only by its own definition, the ADAPTER_TRAY_MENU map (line 96), the healthcheck spec list (lib/healthcheck.ahk:67), and the test suite (tests/test_adapter_contract_vectors.ahk:358 calls it only with an empty array, so it never hits the separator branch). The live production tray menu in ui/tray_menu.ahk builds itself directly via RegisterMenuItem and deliberately exposes its own controlled Suspend/Reload entries (lines 2625-2626) rather than AddStandard(), so today the bug is dormant. It fires the instant any domain module routes a menu containing a separator through this adapter — which is the adapter's entire stated purpose. The impact claim therefore holds: a cosmetically broken menu plus an unintended one-click Exit/Reload/Suspend-Hotkeys surface, the last being the documented half-pause foot-gun. Medium severity is appropriate: real and contract-violating, but latent (not reachable on the current production path).

<a id="f-uridecode-multibyte-utf8-corruption"></a>
#### `uridecode-multibyte-utf8-corruption` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : core-utils (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/string_utils.ahk:30-45`
- **Description** : UriDecode treats each %XX as an independent codepoint: `Out .= Chr(\"0x\" . Hex)`. A real file:// URL encodes non-ASCII path characters as a sequence of UTF-8 BYTES, e.g. 'é' (U+00E9) → %C3%A9. UriDecode turns this into Chr(0xC3) . Chr(0xA9) (two separate UTF-16 code units U+00C3 U+00A9 = 'Ã©') instead of the single character 'é'. A 3-byte char (U+20AC € → %E2%82%AC) becomes three garbage code units.
- **Cause racine** : Percent-encoding is defined over bytes, but Chr() in AHK v2 produces a UTF-16 code unit from a codepoint. Decoding %XX directly to Chr(0xXX) only round-trips for ASCII (<= 0x7F). For >= 0x80, the correct decode requires collecting the raw bytes into a Buffer and StrGet(buf, 'UTF-8'). The current code never reassembles multibyte runs.
- **Scénario** : modules/shortcuts/win.ahk:570 calls UriDecode on a file:// URL returned by the browser location bar to convert it to a Windows path. A user opens or acts on a file whose path contains accented or non-Latin characters (extremely common in French/non-English installs — the project's primary audience). The decoded path is corrupted, so the subsequent file operation (open folder, reveal, etc.) targets a non-existent path and silently fails or errors.
- **Impact** : The Win-shortcuts 'open containing folder / act on current file' feature is broken for any path with non-ASCII characters — i.e. for the French-speaking primary user base whenever a folder/file has an accent. Functional failure, not a crash.
- **Correctif** : Decode into a byte Buffer and StrGet as UTF-8: accumulate consecutive %XX bytes (and literal ASCII bytes) into a Buffer via NumPut('UChar', value, buf, i), then `return StrGet(buf, byteLen, 'UTF-8')`. This correctly reassembles multibyte UTF-8 sequences while leaving ASCII unchanged. Keep the lone-'%' passthrough behaviour the existing tests assert.
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/test_string_utils.ahk: AssertEqual(Chr(0x00E9), UriDecode("%C3%A9")) [é], AssertEqual(Chr(0x20AC), UriDecode("%E2%82%AC")) [€], plus the existing ASCII/space/lone-% cases must still pass. Fails before the fix (returns the 2-/3-codeunit mojibake), passes after.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited source and verified the bug by live-running AHK v2 (installed at C:\Program Files\AutoHotkey\v2\AutoHotkey64.exe), not just by reasoning.

CODE (lib/string_utils.ahk:30-45): UriDecode iterates char by char and does `Out .= Chr("0x" . Hex)` for each `%XX`, then `Pos += 3`. Each percent-byte is converted to its own code unit. AHK v2 strings are native UTF-16 and Chr(n) yields the single code unit for code point n, so Chr(0xC3) is literally 'Ã' (U+00C3), not a raw byte that later gets reassembled.

LIVE PROOF: UriDecode("%C3%A9") returned len=2, cp0=U+00C3, cp1=U+00A9 (= "Ã©" mojibake), instead of the single U+00E9 ("é"). The proposed Buffer+StrGet(buf,'UTF-8') fix returned len=1 U+00E9 for "é" and U+20AC for "€", while preserving every existing case (hello%20world -> "hello world", "50%" -> "50%", "a%2Fb" -> "a/b"). This is exactly the percent-encoding-is-over-bytes vs Chr-is-over-codepoints mismatch the finding describes.

CALLER IS REAL: modules/shortcuts/win.ahk:562 DOMPathToFilesystem() converts a `file:///` LocationURL into a Windows path and calls UriDecode at line 570; win.ahk:537 feeds it the browser location URL. The surrounding code is explicitly accent-aware ("Téléchargements", GetKnownFolderDownloads with localized names), confirming non-ASCII paths are an expected, common input for the French primary audience. A corrupted decode yields a non-existent path -> the open/reveal silently fails. Functional failure, not a crash.

CONCURRENCY MODEL: irrelevant here — this is a deterministic pure-function logic bug, not a race. No Critical/Suspend/InputHook gating applies; the finding does not (and need not) claim a concurrency cause.

SMOKING GUN: the existing test tests/test_string_utils.ahk:62-71 (_SU_DecodesRealisticFileUrlSegment) already encodes "Téléchargements" as "T%C3%A9l%C3%A9chargements" and its OWN comment admits "Chr(0xC3) + Chr(0xA9) may not equal 'é'", so it was deliberately weakened to only assert StrLen(Result) > 0. The author knew the byte-wise decode is wrong and worked around it instead of fixing it. This corroborates the finding directly.

I could not refute it: code, caller, AHK semantics, live run, and a self-incriminating existing test all agree. Verdict: confirmed.

<a id="f-onkeydown-dead-llm-branch"></a>
#### `onkeydown-dead-llm-branch` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-keystroke-pipeline (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:924-939`
- **Description** : In _OnPrefixKeyDown the branch chain is `if (VK==0x09 or VK==0x0D) {...}` (924) then `else if (VK==0x1B or arrows) {...}` (928) then `else if (VK==0x09 or VK==0x0D or VK==0x1B) { LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive(VK) }` (936). The third clause's VKs (Tab/Enter/Escape) are all already captured by the two preceding clauses, so it is dead code: LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive is never called for Tab/Enter/Escape from this path.
- **Cause racine** : An else-if chain whose final condition is a subset of earlier conditions — AHK evaluates clauses top-to-bottom, so VK 0x09/0x0D hit clause 1 and VK 0x1B hits clause 2; clause 3 is unreachable. The Tab branch (924) only calls LLM_Tooltip_TryAcceptTab and then HSE_FeedReset, never LLM_Bridge_OnFlush; the arrow/Escape branch (928) does feed the bridge, but Enter (0x0D) never reaches any LLM flush.
- **Scénario** : LLM prediction tooltip is showing; the user presses Enter to end the line. The LLM bridge never receives the Enter as a flush via the prefix watcher (only the HookDispatcher fallback path could, and that is disabled once _PrefixInputHook exists). The pending prediction is not flushed/committed in lockstep with the keystroke, so a stale prediction can linger or the buffer desyncs.
- **Impact** : LLM prediction state can drift out of sync on Enter (and the intent to flush on Tab/Enter/Escape is partially lost), surfacing stale suggestions.
- **Correctif** : Collapse the LLM feed into the live branches: call `LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive(VK)` for Enter inside the Tab/Enter clause (after the Tab-accept early return), and remove the dead third else-if. Mirror the macOS flush-on-Enter contract.
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive to record calls, drive _OnPrefixKeyDown with VK 0x0D (Enter), 0x09 (Tab), 0x1B (Escape) and assert the bridge flush is invoked for each that should flush. The test fails today for Enter.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code at hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:920-939 and the dead-branch claim is correct. The else-if chain is: (1) `if VK==0x08` (Backspace, feeds bridge); (2) `else if VK==0x09 or VK==0x0D` (Tab/Enter); (3) `else if VK==0x1B or arrows` (Escape/arrows, feeds bridge via LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive); (4) `else if VK==0x09 or VK==0x0D or VK==0x1B`. Because AHK evaluates top-to-bottom, clause 4's VKs are all consumed by clauses 2-3, so clause 4 is unreachable dead code. Confirmed by inspection — this is plain control-flow, not a concurrency question, so the AHK single-threaded model is not a mitigating factor.

The user-impacting consequence is real for Enter (0x0D). Enter falls into clause 2 (line 924), which only runs `HSE_FeedReset(true)` and NEVER calls LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive. In the steady state the prefix watcher's InputHook IS the sole keystroke source: llm_bridge.ahk:79 documents "Keystrokes are delivered by PrefixWatcher", and the only alternate path — _LLM_Bridge_OnDispatcherKey (llm_bridge.ahk:138-141) — returns early whenever `_PrefixInputHook` is set (line 139), and the fallback is explicitly unregistered once the watcher activates (_LLM_Bridge_Activate line 104). LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive (llm_bridge.ahk:185-196) maps vk 0x0D to LLM_Bridge_OnFlush(), which clears _LLM_Bridge_Buffer and cancels/hides any stale prediction (lines 311-317). With Enter never reaching it, the LLM context buffer is not flushed at end-of-line: a stale prediction can linger and the buffer carries pre-Enter text into the next prediction context — exactly the desync the finding describes. Enter is NOT recoverable via OnChar either: AHK InputHook OnChar does not surface Enter/Tab/Escape as printable chars (they are control/end keys; the watcher relies on the `+N` OnKeyDown notify, line 629), and even if it did, LLM_Bridge_OnChar (line 252-257) would APPEND the char rather than flush — the opposite of the intent. macOS confirms the contract: llm_bridge.lua:734 explicitly handles Return/Enter (accept-prediction). So Windows is missing a behavior macOS has.

Two corrections to the finding's scope. (a) Escape (0x1B) is NOT lost — it is fed via the live clause 3 (lines 928-935). (b) Tab (0x09) is partly handled: clause 2 calls LLM_Tooltip_TryAcceptTab (line 925) which accepts a visible prediction; but when no tooltip is visible / its text is empty (TryAcceptTab returns false, tooltip_llm.ahk:174-178), clause 2 falls through to HSE_FeedReset only and skips LLM_Bridge_OnFlush — whereas LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive (line 193) WOULD flush in that case. So Tab also loses its no-accept flush, matching the Enter defect. Net: the title overstates "Tab/Enter/Escape" (Escape is fine), but the core bug — Enter never flushes, and Tab-without-accept never flushes — is real.

<a id="f-paste-plain-destroys-nontext-clip"></a>
#### `paste-plain-destroys-nontext-clip` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : e2e-send-clipboard (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/shortcuts/ctrl.ahk:43-50`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:743-750`
- **Description** : PasteWithoutFormatting and GesturePastePlain do `A_Clipboard := A_Clipboard` to strip formatting before SendFinalResult('^v'). Reading then re-assigning A_Clipboard coerces the clipboard to plain text, discarding any non-text formats. If the clipboard held an image or files, the round-trip wipes them to empty; the subsequent Ctrl+V then pastes nothing.
- **Cause racine** : A_Clipboard is text-only in AHK v2; `A_Clipboard := A_Clipboard` is the documented idiom to strip rich formatting, but it is destructive to non-text clipboard payloads — there is no guard for the case where the clipboard is non-text (an image/file the user intended to paste normally).
- **Scénario** : User copies an image, then (out of habit, or because the chord is muscle-memory) presses Ctrl+Shift+V. The image is destroyed and nothing pastes, instead of either pasting the image or being a no-op.
- **Impact** : Clipboard data loss when paste-plain is invoked over non-text content. Medium because paste-plain is a deliberate action, but the all-formats wipe is surprising and unrecoverable.
- **Correctif** : Guard the strip: only do `A_Clipboard := A_Clipboard` when CB_Read()/A_Clipboard is non-empty text (i.e. DllCall IsClipboardFormatAvailable(CF_UNICODETEXT) or a non-empty A_Clipboard). When the clipboard is non-text, fall through to a normal SendFinalResult('^v') without the destructive round-trip.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert PasteWithoutFormatting / GesturePastePlain guard the `A_Clipboard := A_Clipboard` strip with a text-availability check (grep the function body for a non-empty/text guard preceding the self-assignment). headless-unit if a clipboard stub is available: feed a non-text sentinel and assert the strip is skipped.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : The cited code matches the finding verbatim. ctrl.ahk:43-50 PasteWithoutFormatting and gestures.ahk:743-750 GesturePastePlain both run `A_Clipboard := A_Clipboard` then `SendFinalResult("^v")`, gated only by `WinActive("ahk_exe EXCEL.EXE")` (an app check, not a clipboard-format check). GesturePastePlain is wired directly to a gesture action at gestures.ahk:523 with no upstream guard.

The AHK v2 semantics are confirmed by the project's OWN clipboard adapter docstring at adapters/clipboard.ahk:37-38: "Non-text clipboard content (images, file lists) yields '' — A_Clipboard already coerces binary clipboard data to '' automatically in AHK v2." So with an image or file list on the clipboard: the RHS `A_Clipboard` evaluates to "" (non-destructive read coerced to empty), and the assignment `A_Clipboard := ""` then clears the clipboard entirely (assignment replaces all formats with the assigned text). The subsequent `^v` pastes nothing. The loss is silent and unrecoverable for that clipboard snapshot. The codebase demonstrably knows the format-preserving idiom — lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:285,308 use ClipboardAll() precisely to snapshot non-text content — but it is not applied on these two paste-plain paths.

This is a pure deterministic logic bug, NOT a concurrency bug — the AHK cooperative single-thread model is irrelevant here. There is no race, no serialization gate, and no A_IsSuspended interaction that changes the outcome. (Both handlers are registered as a hotkey / gesture action, so they already respect native Suspend, but that only controls WHETHER they fire, not the destructive behavior WHEN they fire.) No adversarial "the path is already guarded / cannot be interrupted" rebuttal applies: the destructive self-assignment is unconditional whenever the active window is not Excel. The bug is real.

<a id="f-time-activation-fails-open-on-missing-prev-char"></a>
#### `time-activation-fails-open-on-missing-prev-char` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : hotstrings-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:547-559`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1225-1234`
- **Description** : IsTimeActivationExpired reads CharacterSentTime from LastSentCharacterKeyTime[PreviousCharacter], but when the map has no entry it defaults CharacterSentTime := Now. That makes (Now - CharacterSentTime) == 0, which is never > delay, so the gate returns False (not expired) and the time-gated hotstring FIRES even though we have no evidence the prior char was typed recently. LastSentCharacterKeyTime is also pruned by age (_PruneLastSentKeyTime) and keyed by the char AS TYPED, so the entry can legitimately be absent.
- **Cause racine** : A time-activation gate is supposed to SUPPRESS the expansion when the trigger is typed too slowly. Defaulting an unknown timestamp to 'now' inverts the safe default: a missing/pruned timestamp is treated as 'just typed' rather than 'unknown / too old'. After a pause the previous char's timestamp may have been pruned (LAST_SENT_KEY_TIME_MAX_AGE_MS), so the gate that exists precisely to block slow-typed triggers lets them through.
- **Scénario** : A comma_j / SFB / deadkey trigger has TimeActivationSeconds set (e.g. ,a → ja only if typed within the window). The user types the comma, then is interrupted for longer than the prune-age window, then types the vowel. The prior char's timestamp has been pruned, so the gate defaults to 'now', the difference is 0, and the expansion fires anyway — turning a deliberately-paused ",a" into "ja" when the user expected the literal comma+a. The user perceives this as the driver 'eating' their comma after a pause.
- **Impact** : Time-gated reductions/deadkeys fire after a long pause when they should stay literal, producing wrong text exactly in the case the delay was configured to prevent. Subtle and intermittent (depends on prune timing), so hard for users to report precisely.
- **Correctif** : When LastSentCharacterKeyTime has no entry for PreviousCharacter, treat it as expired (return True) for any positive OptionTimeActivationSeconds — fail closed, not open. I.e. default CharacterSentTime to 0 (or A_TickCount - large) so the elapsed time exceeds the window, matching the gate's intent ('only fire when we can prove the prior char was typed recently').
- **Test** : Headless-unit: clear LastSentCharacterKeyTime, then call IsTimeActivationExpired("a", 1) (1 s window, no recorded timestamp for "a") and AssertTrue the result (gate treats unknown as expired). Add a positive case: set LastSentCharacterKeyTime["a"] := A_TickCount and assert it returns False (fires). This pins fail-closed semantics.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : The cited code is accurate. hotstring_engine.ahk:550 reads `CharacterSentTime := LastSentCharacterKeyTime.Has(PreviousCharacter) ? LastSentCharacterKeyTime[PreviousCharacter] : Now`, so a missing entry makes (Now - CharacterSentTime) == 0, which is never > delay, and IsTimeActivationExpired returns False (fires). The gate at hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1232 suppresses only when expired. So the described fail-OPEN-on-missing-entry is mechanically real, not a concurrency/lost-keystroke issue (this is pure single-threaded logic; no AHK interruption is involved).

BUT this is intentional, codified behavior, not an oversight, and the proposed fix directly contradicts the codebase's own asserted contract:
- THREE existing regression tests explicitly assert and document fail-open as the intended semantics: test_hotstring_engine.ahk:268-275 TestHE_TimeoutMissingKey ("missing key in map never triggers expiry"), test_hotstrings_full.ahk:448-459 TestHH_PriorKeyMissingNeverExpires ("prior key absent from timing Map never expires"), and TestHH_PriorKeyDifferentCharNeverBlocksOther:461-470. The proposed fix (default to 0 / treat missing as expired) would flip all three from green to red. Project rule 5.9 forbids weakening regression tests to land a change, so the fix is in head-on conflict with an encoded design decision.
- The design rationale for fail-open is defensible: in the common path the prev char IS freshly recorded by UpdateLastSentCharacter (modules/layout.ahk:333-347), so a missing entry is the abnormal/rare case. The maintainer biased toward "no timing evidence -> let the expansion fire" rather than silently swallowing the user's reduction. That is a UX judgment about the suppression heuristic, not a safety invariant being violated.
- The finding's headline scenario does NOT reproduce as written. HSE_Buffer has no time-based reset (hotstring_engine_main.ahk:622-654 reset only on terminators/cursor-move/focus/cut-paste-undo), so the comma survives the pause and the trigger does match. However _PruneLastSentKeyTime (layout.ahk:344-362) only runs INSIDE UpdateLastSentCharacter and only when the map exceeds LAST_SENT_KEY_TIME_PRUNE_AT=150 entries, deleting entries older than LAST_SENT_KEY_TIME_MAX_AGE_MS=60000ms. With a single comma then a 60s pause then the vowel and no intervening typing, the prune is never triggered, so the comma's stale-but-present (~60s) timestamp is read and the gate CORRECTLY expires (60s >> the 1-2s Delay windows; default DYN_HOTSTRINGS_DEFAULT_DELAY=2.0). The genuine fail-open only manifests when the entry is truly ABSENT: a >150-distinct-char session that pruned it, or a prev char emitted by a path that never calls UpdateLastSentCharacter. That is real but materially rarer than the description implies.

Net: the mechanism exists and the fail-open direction is arguably the unsafe default for the gate's stated intent, but it is a deliberate, test-locked behavior, and whether to invert it is a contested design call rather than a clear bug. It is not exploitable as a lost-keystroke/race issue.

<a id="f-starcover-ci-suppresses-cs-endchar-overbroad"></a>
#### `starcover-ci-suppresses-cs-endchar-overbroad` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : hotstrings-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine_main.ahk:964-979`
- **Description** : _HSE_StarTriggerCoversBody, for a case-INSENSITIVE end-char Spec, looks up HSE_StarPrefixSetCI[StrLower(trigger)] and tests StrLower(EndChar) membership. The comment states CI triggers shadow both CI and CS 'conservative suppression'. But the suppression decision drives a `continue` that DROPS the end-char match entirely. When a CI end-char trigger's lowercased body+endchar happens to be a prefix of some unrelated CI star trigger, the end-char trigger is suppressed for that endchar even if no star trigger the user could actually reach exists in the cased form they typed.
- **Cause racine** : The CI/CS split is asymmetric: the CS path correctly checks only HSE_StarPrefixSetCS, but the CI path's lowercase-everything probe can match a star prefix that only exists for a DIFFERENT casing, yielding a false 'covered' verdict and silently swallowing an end-char expansion that should have fired.
- **Scénario** : A user has a case-insensitive end-char trigger whose body+terminator lowercases onto the prefix of an unrelated star trigger registered in another section. Typing the body then the terminator yields neither the end-char expansion (suppressed as 'covered') nor the star expansion (the user didn't press the magic key) — the trigger silently does nothing, and the user just sees their literal text. Rare because it needs a body/prefix collision across the CI set, but when it bites it is a fully missing expansion with no feedback.
- **Impact** : Occasional silently-missing end-char expansion when an unrelated CI star trigger shares the lowercased prefix. Low frequency, no data loss, but a confusing 'this hotstring sometimes just doesn't fire' report.
- **Correctif** : Tighten the CI cover-check so it only suppresses when a star trigger that the typed casing could actually extend toward exists — i.e. confirm the matched star prefix corresponds to a registered star trigger reachable from the typed EndChar in the relevant case bucket, rather than suppressing on any lowercased-prefix hit. Alternatively, record on each prefix entry whether it came from a CI or CS star and require a casing-compatible match.
- **Test** : Headless-unit: register a CI end-char trigger (e.g. 'foo' end-char) and a CI star trigger whose lowercased prefix collides ('fooz★') in a way that should NOT cover a space-terminated 'foo'. Type the body + space via HSE_FeedChar and AssertTrue the end-char Spec is returned (not suppressed). Then register a genuinely-covering star ('foo★', magic-key endchar) and assert that magic-key feed IS suppressed in favour of the star — pinning the boundary between correct and over-broad suppression.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-space-altgr-double-raalt-down"></a>
#### `space-altgr-double-raalt-down` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : tap-holds (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/space.ahk:146`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/space.ahk:147`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/space.ahk:149`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/space.ahk:152`
- **Description** : _SpaceHoldAltGr does SendInput('{RAlt Down}'); then if captured!='' SendInput('{RAlt Down}' . captured); then KeyWait; then SendInput('{RAlt Up}'). The second '{RAlt Down}' is redundant (RAlt is already down) and there is a single matching '{RAlt Up}' at the end — but if the captured branch runs, RAlt was pressed Down twice and released once. Combined with the lack of try/finally, an interruption after either Down leaves RAlt latched. Also, unlike _SpaceHoldShift which deliberately drops the captured char to avoid double-translation, this re-sends the captured char with RAlt held, which double-applies AltGr to an already layout-translated character.
- **Cause racine** : Manual key-event bracketing without a balanced Down/Up count and without try/finally. SendInput's internal Down counter for RAlt is incremented twice but decremented once; on most layouts the OS treats repeated Down as idempotent, but if the second Down arrives as a distinct event the modifier can stay logically held after the single Up.
- **Scénario** : User holds Space+AltGr (hold=alt_gr) then a letter key to produce an AltGr-layer glyph. The captured char is re-sent with RAlt held, double-translating it (wrong glyph or no glyph), and if the user releases Space at the wrong instant the double-Down/single-Up leaves AltGr stuck so the next normal keystrokes are AltGr-mapped.
- **Impact** : Wrong character produced on Space+AltGr hold, and an occasional stuck AltGr modifier. Lower frequency than the LShift case because alt_gr-on-Space is a non-default hold option.
- **Correctif** : Remove the redundant second '{RAlt Down}': send '{RAlt Down}' once, send the captured char with the existing '{RAlt}'-down context, then release in a finally: SendInput('{RAlt Down}'); try { if captured... SendInput(captured); KeyWait('SC039','U T2') } finally { SendInput('{RAlt Up}') }. Mirror _SpaceHoldShift's decision about whether to re-send the captured char to avoid double-translation.
- **Test** : headless-unit: with _AHK_SendInput stubbed to record events, call _SpaceHoldAltGr('a') and assert the recorded sequence has exactly one '{RAlt Down}' and one '{RAlt Up}' (balanced), and that the release is recorded even when the KeyWait branch is simulated as interrupted.  _(faisabilité : hard)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code at static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/space.ahk:146-152, plus the sibling holds (_SpaceHoldCtrl/Shift/Alt/Win), the InputHook-capture dispatcher SpaceTapHold (lines 44-56), and the adapter (adapters/text_sender.ahk).

FACTUAL CHECK ON CLAIM 1 (double Down / single Up): Confirmed. Line 147 SendInput("{RAlt Down}"); line 149 (captured branch) SendInput("{RAlt Down}" . captured); line 151 SendInput("{RAlt Up}"). That is 2 explicit RAlt-down events and 1 RAlt-up. The second {RAlt Down} at line 149 is genuinely redundant — line 147 already armed RAlt. Every sibling avoids this: Ctrl/Alt/Win use the transient prefix syntax ("^"/"!"/"#" . captured) which auto-brackets the modifier, and enter.ahk/lalt.ahk use balanced TextPressKey(ModKey,"Down")/(…,"Up"). So _SpaceHoldAltGr is the lone asymmetric outlier. This part of the finding is real.

BUT THE IMPACT IS OVERSTATED — the "stuck/duplicated AltGr" claim does NOT hold under the AHK model. At the Windows level a modifier key-state is boolean, not a press-counter; AHK's explicit {RAlt Up} forces RAlt up regardless of how many Downs preceded it. The function cannot be re-entered (SC039:: defaults to #MaxThreadsPerHotkey 1; the comment at lines 162-164 confirms auto-repeat can't spawn a second thread), so it always runs start-to-finish and line 151 always executes. The "no try/finally → interruption leaves RAlt latched" argument is generic and does not map to this code: between line 147 and line 151 there is NO throwing operation — only SendInput with static/concatenated literals and KeyWait with static args. There is no Map access, no division, no user callback that could throw. So the interruption-leaves-it-stuck path is not realistic here.

CLAIM 2 (double-translation) IS A FALSE POSITIVE. The design comment (space.ahk lines 30-33) states the InputHook ("L1 T3" in SpaceTapHold) captures the next key WITHOUT any modifier active, so `captured` is the BASE layout glyph. Re-sending that base glyph with RAlt held applies AltGr exactly ONCE — which is precisely the intended semantics of an "AltGr on hold" feature (Space+AltGr+key → AltGr-layer glyph). It is NOT applied to an already-AltGr-translated char. The finding's analogy to _SpaceHoldShift is wrong: Shift drops the captured char for a different reason (Space+Shift is meant to capitalise SUBSEQUENT keys, not the trigger), not because re-sending would genuinely double-translate. So the proposed fix's suggestion to "mirror _SpaceHoldShift's decision about whether to re-send the captured char" is incorrect — for AltGr the char SHOULD still be re-sent.

NET: real defect = one redundant {RAlt Down} (code smell / asymmetry with siblings, minor robustness). Not a stuck modifier, not a double-translation, not a wrong-glyph bug. I land on "uncertain" rather than "confirmed" or "false-positive" because the literal code asymmetry the finding flags exists, but its severity, its stuck-modifier mechanism, and its double-translation rationale are all wrong.

<a id="f-lalt-altgr-capslock-no-priorkey-min-duration"></a>
#### `lalt-altgr-capslock-no-priorkey-min-duration` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tap-holds (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/capslock.ahk:177`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/capslock.ahk:179`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/lalt.ahk:123`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/lalt.ahk:125`
- **Description** : LShift/LCtrl/RShift handlers apply a TapMinDurationMs() lower bound ((TimeAfter-TimeBefore) >= TapMinDurationMs()) to filter spurious sub-threshold taps fired mid-chord. The CapsLock hold-layer block (2.4), the LAlt tab+layer block (4.2) and the LAlt generic hold-layer block (4.8) instead compute tap := (Now - CharTime <= duration*1000) with NO minimum-duration floor and (for capslock 2.4) no A_PriorKey guard. A very fast accidental brush of the layer key resolves as a tap and fires the configured tap action even when the key was used as part of a chord.
- **Cause racine** : Inconsistent tap-detection: the shift/ctrl handlers gate on both an upper bound and TapMinDurationMs() plus A_PriorKey, but the layer-based handlers gate only on the upper bound (and LAlt blocks add A_PriorKey but capslock 2.4 does not). The TapMinDurationMs floor exists precisely to suppress the spurious firing the brief warns about, but it is not applied uniformly.
- **Scénario** : With CapsLock hold=nav, the user momentarily presses CapsLock together with a letter as part of a fast roll; the layer activates and disables within the threshold, the elapsed time is under duration so tap resolves true with no A_PriorKey check, and _CapsLockDispatch fires the configured tap action (e.g. an unintended Enter or Backspace) mid-word.
- **Impact** : Occasional spurious tap-action injection (extra Enter/Backspace/char) when a layer key is brushed during fast typing. Cosmetic-to-annoying, not a hard lockup.
- **Correctif** : Apply the same guards uniformly: in capslock 2.4 add 'and A_PriorKey == "CapsLock"' to the tap condition, and add a '(Now - CharTime) >= TapMinDurationMs()' lower bound to capslock 2.4, lalt 4.2 and lalt 4.8 so an ultra-fast chord brush is not counted as an intentional tap.
- **Test** : headless-unit: extract the tap-resolution predicate into a pure helper TapHoldResolveTap(elapsedMs, durationMs, minMs, priorKeyMatches) and unit-test that elapsed < minMs returns false even when elapsed < durationMs, and that priorKeyMatches=false returns false — covering all four call sites.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-capslock-led-multiple-owners"></a>
#### `capslock-led-multiple-owners` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : shortcuts (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/shortcuts/capsword.ahk:23-39`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/tap_holds/one_shot_shift.ahk:78-85`
- **Description** : ToggleCapsWord()/DisableCapsWord() call UpdateCapsLockLED, which couples the LED to `CapsWordEnabled or LayerEnabled` via SetCapsLockState. ToggleCapsLock() (reachable from every 10-action dispatcher) instead drives SetCapsLockState directly from GetKeyState("CapsLock","T"). Three independent truth sources (CapsWordEnabled, LayerEnabled, hardware CapsLock toggle) all write the same LED with no single owner.
- **Cause racine** : There is no single owner of the CapsLock LED. UpdateCapsLockLED OR-s CapsWordEnabled and LayerEnabled into the LED, while the dispatcher's caps_lock action toggles the real hardware CapsLock and lets that drive the LED independently. Interleaving the three can leave the LED disagreeing with each individual logical state.
- **Scénario** : User enables CapsWord (LED on), enters the nav layer (LED stays on), exits CapsWord with Space → DisableCapsWord sees LayerEnabled true → LED stays on though CapsWord is off; or toggles real CapsLock via AltGr+CapsLock, after which the LED reflects hardware state, desynced from CapsWord/layer intent. The LED stops being a reliable indicator.
- **Impact** : Cosmetic/UX only: the CapsLock LED can misrepresent which of CapsWord/layer/CapsLock is active; no lost keystrokes. Low severity.
- **Correctif** : Make UpdateCapsLockLED the single LED owner and route ToggleCapsLock through it, computing desired LED = (CapsWordEnabled or LayerEnabled or real_capslock_toggle) consistently; or document that the hardware CapsLock toggle action and the CapsWord/layer LED are deliberately separate and never combined.
- **Test** : headless-unit: drive CapsWordEnabled/LayerEnabled combinations through UpdateCapsLockLED (capsword.ahk logic, SetCapsLockState stubbed) and assert the LED-state stub matches a documented truth table for every combination, including the post-DisableCapsWord-with-layer-active case.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-config-shortcuts-array-parse-escape-bug"></a>
#### `config-shortcuts-array-parse-escape-bug` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : shortcuts (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/config_shortcuts.ahk:120-146`
- **Description** : The array state machine toggles `in_str` on `c = '"' && SubStr(cur,-1) != "\"`, but it checks the backslash against `cur` (the accumulator) BEFORE the current char is appended, and each element is then re-coerced via `CS_CoerceValue(Trim(cur))` (a second unescape pass). A name containing an escaped quote (`\"`) or a comma inside quotes desyncs the in_str/comma-at-depth-0 logic and splits/corrupts the value. The header comment itself notes 'quotes never contain commas in practice'.
- **Cause racine** : Hand-rolled TOML array parsing: the escape lookbehind inspects the not-yet-updated accumulator instead of the raw stream, and double-unescaping (loop coercion + per-element CS_CoerceValue) compounds the error, so a quoted name with an embedded comma is wrongly split into two elements and a name with an escaped quote is mis-unescaped.
- **Scénario** : A user hand-edits config.toml to add a disabled-app/window-filter entry whose name contains a comma or quote (rare for exe names but possible for title-based filters). On next CS_Load the disabled_apps Map gets a corrupted/split key, so a metrics privacy filter silently fails to match the intended app — typing in that app is logged when the user expected it filtered.
- **Impact** : Privacy-relevant: a malformed-but-well-intentioned disabled-apps entry can silently fail to suppress keystroke metrics for the targeted app. Low severity because exe names rarely contain commas/quotes, but it is a silent privacy-filter failure.
- **Correctif** : Fix the tokenizer to track in_str from the raw character stream with a proper escape flag (check the previous raw char, not the accumulator), and do NOT re-run CS_CoerceValue's string-unescape on already-extracted quoted elements (unescape exactly once). Add a comment that array elements may contain commas inside quotes.
- **Test** : headless-unit: call CS_CoerceValue on `["a,b.exe", "c\"d.exe"]` and assert the returned Array has exactly two elements equal to `a,b.exe` and `c"d.exe` (currently fails). config_shortcuts.ahk is a lib file, unit-testable without hotkeys.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-lock-workstation-lambda-implicit-concat"></a>
#### `lock-workstation-lambda-implicit-concat` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : layout (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:419`
- **Description** : RemapKey registers `(*) => DllCall("LockWorkStation") UpdateLastSentCharacter(Character)` for the L key. A fat-arrow body is a single expression; this works only because AHK v2 evaluates both operands of the implicit (space) string-concatenation `DllCall(...) UpdateLastSentCharacter(...)`. It is fragile and non-obvious, and unlike every other RemapKey branch it does not go through _RemapEmit (so it neither serialises under Critical nor benefits from the ordering guarantee).
- **Cause racine** : Implicit concatenation of two function-call expressions happens to evaluate both, so the side effects fire — but the intent (sequence two statements) is not expressed; a future refactor that 'simplifies' the dead concatenation could drop one call. It also bypasses the _RemapEmit Critical-serialised emit path used by all other remaps.
- **Scénario** : Pressing Win+L (remapped L) locks the workstation and bumps the last-sent character. Today it works; a maintainer cleaning up the 'pointless concatenation' could silently delete the UpdateLastSentCharacter call, or the lack of serialisation could let it reorder relative to a neighbouring remap.
- **Impact** : No user-visible bug today; latent maintenance hazard and an inconsistency with the documented per-key ordering invariant.
- **Correctif** : Replace the arrow with a named helper (e.g. _LockWorkstationEmit(Character)) that calls DllCall("LockWorkStation") then UpdateLastSentCharacter(Character) on explicit separate lines, ideally routed through the same Critical-serialised pattern as _RemapEmit, and bind it like the other handlers.
- **Test** : headless-unit: bind/extract the L-key handler and assert that invoking it (with DllCall stubbed) calls UpdateLastSentCharacter exactly once with the expected character — guarding against a refactor that drops the second statement.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-gesturepickcolor-clipboard-clobber"></a>
#### `gesturepickcolor-clipboard-clobber` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : gestures (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:604`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/gestures.ahk:743`
- **Description** : GesturePickColor sets A_Clipboard := HexColor (intentional, but undocumented data loss of prior clipboard). GesturePastePlain does 'A_Clipboard := A_Clipboard' to strip formatting — this round-trips through the clipboard and can lose non-text clipboard payloads (the reassignment coerces to the text representation, discarding e.g. an image or rich object that was on the clipboard).
- **Cause racine** : Direct clipboard mutation. 'A_Clipboard := A_Clipboard' reads the text form and writes it back, dropping any non-text formats; for plain-text paste this is the goal, but it silently destroys image/HTML/RTF-only clipboard contents the user may have intended to keep.
- **Scénario** : User copies an image, then uses the paste_plain gesture in a text field — the image is replaced on the clipboard by an empty/text-coerced value, so a subsequent paste into an image-capable app fails.
- **Impact** : Minor, action-scoped clipboard surprise. Low severity; behavior is arguably intended for paste_plain but the data-loss edge is undocumented.
- **Correctif** : For GesturePickColor, optionally save/restore or just document. For GesturePastePlain, save OldClip := ClipboardAll() before the coercion and restore in a finally after the ^v settle, so the user's original (possibly non-text) clipboard survives.
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub A_Clipboard/ClipboardAll and SendFinalResult; call GesturePastePlain and assert the original clipboard object is restored after the paste (mirrors GetSelection's restore guarantee).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-lost-tick-after-filtered-keystroke"></a>
#### `lost-tick-after-filtered-keystroke` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : keylogger-core (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:209`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:222`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_hook.ahk:293`
- **Description** : KL_Hook_OnChar returns early when MF_ShouldFilter() is true (line 211) BEFORE reaching `KLHook.last_tick := now` (line 224). Same in OnKeyDown for special keys. So while the user types in a filtered context (password field, private window, disabled app), last_tick stays frozen at the last NON-filtered keystroke's time.
- **Cause racine** : Early-return ordering: the filter short-circuits before the last_tick watermark and before KL_Watchers_OnKeystroke, so the watermark reflects pre-filter activity rather than the user's most recent (filtered) keypress.
- **Scénario** : User types a password (filtered) for 20 s then clicks into their editor and types. The first editor keystroke computes delay = now - last_tick = 20 000+ ms. KL_FlushBuffer caps it for WPM, but the walker's burst-gap / session-gap / think-pause logic sees a giant gap, breaks the burst, fabricates a think-pause, and KL_Watchers_OnKeystroke may emit a spurious retroactive session_end/idle.
- **Impact** : Metric distortion (false think-time, broken bursts, spurious idle/session events) after every filtered interlude. Not a lost user keystroke, purely data quality — hence low.
- **Correctif** : Update KLHook.last_tick (and drive KL_Watchers_OnKeystroke) BEFORE the MF_ShouldFilter early-return, since the physical key WAS pressed — only the content must be dropped, not the timing watermark. Move the watermark update to the top of both callbacks, guarded only by A_IsSuspended and Keylogger.initialized.
- **Test** : headless-unit: refactor the watermark update into KL_HookNoteActivity() and assert it runs on the filtered path; or with a stubbed MF_ShouldFilter returning true, assert KLHook.last_tick advanced after a filtered keystroke so the next unfiltered keystroke computes a small delay.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-topo-checkvirtualdesktop-stale-prev-hwnd"></a>
#### `topo-checkvirtualdesktop-stale-prev-hwnd` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : keylogger-sensors (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_window_topology.ahk:133`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_window_topology.ahk:243`
- **Description** : In KL_Topo_Tick the foreground change is detected with the OLD KLTopo.prev_hwnd (line 133), KL_Topo_CheckVirtualDesktop(hwnd) is called, and only AFTER that does line 136 set prev_hwnd := hwnd. But KL_Topo_CheckVirtualDesktop checks `WinExist('ahk_id ' . KLTopo.prev_hwnd)` (line 247) — at that point prev_hwnd is still the OLD window, which is correct; however the function name argument is `new_hwnd` and the seen_hwnds lookup uses new_hwnd while the alive-check uses prev_hwnd, and seen_hwnds[hwnd] is only updated at line 137 AFTER the check, so on the very first switch to a window seen on a prior tick the gap math is off by one tick.
- **Cause racine** : Ordering: the seen_hwnds timestamp for the incoming hwnd is updated after the heuristic runs, and the heuristic mixes prev_hwnd (old) with new_hwnd (incoming) for two different lookups. The logic is internally inconsistent about whether it is reasoning over the outgoing or incoming window, producing the documented false positives on Alt+Tab plus an additional off-by-one on the first re-visit.
- **Scénario** : User Alt+Tabs back to a window they used a few ticks ago. The gap computed from the not-yet-updated seen_hwnds can exceed 3000 ms and emit a virtual_desktop_switch event when it was a plain Alt+Tab.
- **Impact** : Extra false-positive virtual_desktop_switch events (the docstring already admits false positives on Alt+Tab; this makes them slightly worse). Data-only, low impact.
- **Correctif** : Decide the reference window explicitly: compute `last_seen := KLTopo.seen_hwnds.Has(hwnd) ? KLTopo.seen_hwnds[hwnd] : 0` BEFORE updating seen_hwnds, pass it into the heuristic, and only then set seen_hwnds[hwnd] and prev_hwnd. Document that the alive-check is on the OUTGOING window and the gap is on the INCOMING window.
- **Test** : headless-unit: refactor the decision into `_KL_Topo_IsLikelyDesktopSwitch(prev_alive, incoming_last_seen, now)` and assert it returns false for a recent re-visit (gap < 3000) and true only for a long-unseen incoming hwnd while the outgoing one is still alive.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-av-focus-mode-dead-code"></a>
#### `av-focus-mode-dead-code` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : keylogger-sensors (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_av_state.ahk:200`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_av_state.ahk:341`
- **Description** : KL_AV_SlowTick intentionally never calls KL_AV_PollFocusMode (line 200-204, because the `loop reg 'KR'` walk blocked the main thread). The whole KL_AV_PollFocusMode function (270-300) and the focus_active state are therefore dead: focus_mode_start/end events are never emitted, yet KL_AV_Stop (line 341) still tries to emit a focus_mode_end if focus_active — which can never be true. The CAPTURE_EXES list also double-counts: discord.exe/teams.exe etc. are both 'capture' here and 'communication' in app_categories, and obs has no end event if it is replaced by a different capture exe between ticks.
- **Cause racine** : A blocking probe was disabled by commenting out its only caller, but the rest of the focus-mode machinery (function, state field, shutdown emit) was left in place per 5.6 'No Unused Fallback Code' which forbids keeping removed functionality as dead code. The feature is now permanently inert with no log trace.
- **Scénario** : A maintainer reading the dashboard wonders why focus_mode_* events never appear; the code looks complete and even has a shutdown emit, hiding that the feature is disabled. A future edit might re-enable PollFocusMode and reintroduce the documented 30 s keyboard lockup.
- **Impact** : Dead code that misleads maintainers and risks reintroducing a known main-thread-blocking regression if naively re-enabled. No runtime harm today.
- **Correctif** : Either remove KL_AV_PollFocusMode, focus_active, and the focus_mode_end shutdown emit entirely (per rule 5.6) until a non-blocking focus-state source exists, or replace the registry walk with the non-blocking WNF/notification API and re-enable it. If kept disabled, leave a single explanatory comment, not the full machinery.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert KL_AV_SlowTick does NOT call KL_AV_PollFocusMode AND that no `loop reg` appears on a hot timer path in keylogger_av_state.ahk (pins the no-blocking-registry-walk regression the comment warns about).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-stale-cache-survives-reset-and-pause"></a>
#### `stale-cache-survives-reset-and-pause` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : llm-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/prediction_engine.ahk:291-299`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/prediction_engine.ahk:424-465`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:354-368`
- **Description** : LLM_Engine_StopGeneration (called from LLM_Bridge_ResetPredictions on navigation/pointer/flush) bumps request_id and cancels async work but does NOT clear _LLM_Engine["last_ctx"]/"last_results". Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter likewise leaves them intact. The FirePrediction exact-match (line 424) and prefix-match (line 439) cache branches re-render last_results whenever the new context matches — even after an explicit reset or a pause/resume cycle.
- **Cause racine** : The cache is keyed only on context string equality, not on a 'still valid' generation/epoch. Reset bumps request_id (which invalidates in-flight async callbacks) but the cache lookup in FirePrediction does not consult request_id — it just compares ctx. So a context the user returns to (same word re-typed) replays a prediction the user already dismissed via navigation, and a prediction generated before pause can re-appear after resume.
- **Scénario** : User types "bonjour", a prediction shows, they press an arrow key (reset_on_nav fires LLM_Bridge_ResetPredictions, dismissing it). They navigate back and re-type the same trailing context; the next debounce fire hits the exact-match cache and instantly re-displays the prediction the user explicitly dismissed — feels like a 'ghost' suggestion. Same after pause/resume if the buffer reconstructs the cached ctx.
- **Impact** : Confusing UX (dismissed suggestions reappear) and a minor privacy wrinkle (a prediction generated before pause can surface after resume). Not a crash or data race.
- **Correctif** : In LLM_Engine_StopGeneration (and from Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter), clear _LLM_Engine["last_ctx"]:="" and _LLM_Engine["last_results"]:=[] (and last_result:="") so the cache cannot replay across an explicit reset/pause. If a soft-cache across pause is desired, gate the cache branches on an epoch counter bumped by reset/suspend.
- **Test** : headless-unit: set _LLM_Engine["last_ctx"]:="bonjour" and last_results:=["!"]; call LLM_Engine_StopGeneration(); assert last_ctx=="" and last_results.Length==0. Then a second test: prime cache, call StopGeneration, call LLM_Engine_FirePrediction("...bonjour") with a dispatch stub and assert the cache branch did NOT fire LLM_Engine_OnResults (i.e. it went to the network path). Fails today.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : The finding is accurate against the real source. This is a logic bug, not a concurrency claim, so the AHK cooperative-threading model does not gate it — it manifests on the single keyboard thread regardless of interruption.

Verified facts:
1. The cache fields exist and default to empty: prediction_engine.ahk:61-63 (`last_ctx:=""`, `last_result:=""`, `last_results:=[]`).
2. The cache is written ONLY in `_LLM_Engine_FinalizeRequest` (965-969): `last_ctx:=state["ctx"]`, `last_results:=state["slots"]`. I grepped every `last_ctx`/`last_results` reference across the whole `modules/llm` tree — there is no clear-on-reset, clear-on-stop, or clear-on-suspend anywhere.
3. `LLM_Engine_StopGeneration` (291-299) bumps `request_id` and cancels async/streams, but does NOT touch the cache. `LLM_Bridge_ResetPredictions` (llm_bridge.ahk:354-368) calls StopGeneration + Tooltip_Hide, also leaving the cache intact.
4. `Ergopti_OnSuspendEnter` (ErgoptiPlus.ahk:2006-2010) calls TooltipHide + LLM_Tooltip_Hide + LLM_Engine_CancelTimer — it does NOT clear the cache either.
5. The cache branches actively RE-RENDER, not merely dedup: the exact-match branch (424-430) and prefix-match branch (439-465) both call `LLM_Engine_OnResults(...)`, which (1111-1154) goes straight to `LLM_Tooltip_Show` with no network call. So a dismissed/pre-pause prediction is genuinely repainted.
6. Reachability is deterministic: `ctx` is `PromptBuilder._CapContext(buffer, maxWords)` (_generated/prompt_builder.ahk:200-213), a pure function of the buffer. Re-typing the same trailing context reproduces the same `ctx`, hitting the exact-match branch. `request_id` is bumped on FirePrediction but the cache lookup never consults it, so the bump does not invalidate the cache — confirming the stated root cause.

Parity check (decisive on intent): the macOS reference driver this code claims to mirror has NO string-keyed re-render cache. macOS prediction_engine.lua uses `last_buffer_signature` purely as a fire-skip dedup guard, and crucially clears it in `M.reset()` (line 712) along with `pending_predictions={}`. macOS never re-renders from a signature match — it just skips. So the AHK driver both (a) added a re-rendering cache with no macOS equivalent and (b) failed to clear it on the reset/stop path that macOS DOES clear. This confirms a parity defect, not intended design.

The pause sub-claim also holds but is narrower: FirePrediction has `if A_IsSuspended return` (345), so nothing fires during pause; the privacy wrinkle requires the user to resume and reconstruct the identical `ctx` (the LLM buffer is not cleared on resume — `Ergopti_OnSuspendResume` only resets the prefix buffer), after which the pre-pause prediction can repaint.

<a id="f-remote-parse-first-content-match"></a>
#### `remote-parse-first-content-match` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : llm-api (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_remote.ahk:466`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_remote.ahk:471`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_remote.ahk:477`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_remote.ahk:482`
- **Description** : _LLMRemoteParseResponse uses a single non-anchored regex that returns the first matching string value: anthropic/gemini both match the first "text":"..." and openai matches the first "content":"...". For Anthropic with extended-thinking enabled the first content block is a thinking/redacted_thinking block whose "text" precedes the answer; for a tool_use turn there may be no "text" at all; for OpenAI the first "content" could belong to a non-primary field. Unlike the Ollama parser (which uses JsonParse + structured navigation and even has a thinking-fallback), the remote parser is a flat regex with no structural awareness.
- **Cause racine** : Regex-based first-match extraction rather than JSON-structured navigation to choices[0].message.content / content[].type=='text' / candidates[0].content.parts[].text. Works for the minimal common-case response shape these providers return today with thinking off, but is fragile against the documented multi-block shapes.
- **Scénario** : User points the API backend at Anthropic with a model/prompt that triggers a thinking block, or at a gateway (OpenRouter) that returns a reasoning field before content. The tooltip shows the model's chain-of-thought fragment (or empty) instead of the actual completion.
- **Impact** : Wrong/garbled prediction text shown to the user; usually low because the engine ships thinking-off prompts and these providers default to no thinking block, but it silently mis-parses when the shape changes.
- **Correctif** : Parse with JsonParse and navigate the canonical path per format (openai: choices[1].message.content; anthropic: first content[] entry with type=='text'; gemini: candidates[1].content.parts[1].text), falling back to the current regex only if structured parse misses. Reuse the same JsonParse the Ollama path already uses.
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/test_llm_api_remote.ahk: feed an anthropic body with a leading thinking block then a text block — assert _LLMRemoteParseResponse returns the answer text, not the thinking text; feed an openai body containing a non-content "content"-like decoy field before choices and assert correct extraction.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-insert-id-discovery-label-collision"></a>
#### `insert-id-discovery-label-collision` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tray-menu (module) · **Fichiers** : `lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:191`, `lib/menu_dispatcher.ahk:207`, `ui/tray_menu.ahk:617`
- **Description** : RegisterMenuItemInsert (menu_dispatcher.ahk:177) discovers the just-inserted item's Win32 ID by walking the menu and matching the FIRST entry whose text equals ItemName (_FindMenuItemIdByName, line 207, returns on first match). Unlike RegisterMenuItem (which uses position Count-1 and is collision-proof), this is text-based. AddCategoryToggleItem (tray_menu.ahk:617) uses RegisterMenuItemInsert with category on/off labels that, while per-category-worded, are not guaranteed globally unique, and any future caller inserting an item whose label duplicates an existing one in the same menu will bind the bypass to the wrong (earlier) item.
- **Cause racine** : Name-based ID discovery instead of position-based. When two items in the same HMENU share a label, GetMenuStringW matches the first occurrence, so the bypass records the older item's ID against the new callback; a dropped click on the new item then re-dispatches the old item's intended callback (or no-ops).
- **Scénario** : A submenu ends up with two entries sharing the same localized label (e.g. two '(default)'-suffixed delay rows, or a future toggle reusing a generic label). The category toggle / inserted item's bypass binds to the wrong ID; on a dropped click the wrong row's action fires or nothing happens, reintroducing exactly the drop symptom the bypass exists to cure.
- **Impact** : Bypass coverage silently binds to the wrong item under label collisions, causing wrong-action or no-action on insert-based items. Confined to RegisterMenuItemInsert call sites (currently the category toggles).
- **Correctif** : Resolve the inserted item's position deterministically: BeforeItem already encodes the 1-based insert position (e.g. '1&'), so compute the inserted item's index from that and read its ID by position via GetMenuItemID, instead of matching text. If position cannot be derived, fall back to matching but require the match to be unique (count occurrences; return 0 and log if >1) so a collision degrades to native dispatch rather than binding the wrong ID.
- **Test** : headless-unit on a real detached Menu(): Insert two items with the SAME label, call the position-based discovery for the second, and assert it returns the SECOND item's GetMenuItemID(pos) — not the first's. Fails with the current first-match-by-name implementation.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-counttoml-overcounts-personal-meta-sections"></a>
#### `counttoml-overcounts-personal-meta-sections` — **low** · `uncertain`
- **Unité** : config-toml (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:102`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:94`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_config_window.ahk:1205`
- **Description** : _TomlWarmFileCounts excludes only the exact sections '_meta' and '_meta.sections' from counting (line 102). But _HCW_PatchTomlMeta writes per-section overrides into DOTTED headers `[_meta.sections.<name>]` (hotstrings_config_window.ahk:1205). The header regex (line 94) captures the whole dotted name, so CurrentSec becomes e.g. '_meta.sections.accents', which is NOT in the two-item exclusion list. Each metadata line inside (`delay = 0.5`, `color = "#fff"`, `priority = 30`, `description = "..."`) matches the entry regex `^(?:"[^"]+"|[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+)\s*=` (line 103) and is tallied as a hotstring entry.
- **Cause racine** : The exclusion checks for equality against two fixed strings rather than a prefix. The bundled shared category files happen to use the flat `[_meta.sections]` form (which IS excluded), so the bug is masked there — but personal files customised through the hotstrings config window use the dotted-per-section form the parser elsewhere (ParseTomlGroupConfig:495) expects, and those slip through.
- **Scénario** : User opens the hotstrings config window and sets a custom delay+color on two sections of their Personal category. That writes two `[_meta.sections.foo]` blocks (delay + color = ~4 metadata lines). The tray menu then shows the Personal hotstring count inflated by ~4, so 'Personal (12)' is rendered as 'Personal (16)'. Counts drift further with each per-section customisation.
- **Impact** : Wrong hotstring counts shown to the user for any personalised category (cosmetic but persistent and confusing). No crash, but it erodes trust in the UI numbers.
- **Correctif** : Change the exclusion test from equality to a prefix/startswith check: skip any section whose lowercased name == '_meta' OR starts with '_meta.' (covers '_meta.sections', '_meta.sections.<x>', '_meta.section_delays'). e.g. `if (CurrentSec != '' and SubStr(CurrentSec,1,5) != '_meta')` plus the exact '_meta' case, mirroring ParseTomlFile's general '[_*] is metadata' convention used by toml_config_loader.
- **Test** : headless-unit (test_toml_loader): build a temp category TOML with a `[_meta.sections.accents]` block containing delay/color/priority/description lines plus a real `[[accents]]` section with exactly 3 entries; AssertEqual(3, CountTomlHotstrings('x', TmpPath)). Fails (returns 7) before the fix.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : The defect in the code is real but its stated impact is wrong, and the live path that would surface it does not exist. Verified against source:

REAL part of the finding:
- toml_loader.ahk:102 excludes only the exact strings "_meta" and "_meta.sections", not the prefix "_meta.".
- toml_loader.ahk:94 regex ^\[+([^\[\]]+)\]+$ captures the full dotted name; on `[_meta.sections.accents]` CurrentSec becomes "_meta.sections.accents", which slips past the exclusion.
- The entry regex at :103 matches delay/color/priority/description lines, tallying them into Counts["_meta.sections.accents"].
- hotstrings_config_window.ahk:1205 (_HCW_PatchTomlMeta) genuinely writes the dotted [_meta.sections.<sec>] form, and it operates on Entry.Path/E.Path (lines 640-810), which can be a shared category file, not only the personal file.

WHY IT IS INERT (refutation of the user-facing claim):
- The over-count lands in a Map key named "_meta.sections.<sec>". The ONLY production consumer of _TomlWarmFileCounts is CountTomlSection, which returns Counts.Has(SName)?Counts[SName]:0 where SName is always a REAL v2 section id passed from tray_menu.ahk:343, :1869, :1891 (e.g. "accents"). The phantom key is never requested, so the displayed per-section count for the real section is unchanged. The inflated entry is dead weight.
- CountTomlHotstrings — the whole-file sum that WOULD expose the inflation — has ZERO production callers (grep finds only its definition in toml_loader.ahk:135 and a reference in tests/test_session_regressions.ahk:58). It is effectively test-only.
- The finding's headline scenario "Personal (12) -> Personal (16)" is factually wrong: the Personal count is computed in _HS_ComputeGrandTotal (tray_menu.ahk:832-844) via ReadPersonalToml() summing ["entries"].Length per real section, completely bypassing _TomlWarmFileCounts.

So this is a genuine latent correctness bug in _TomlWarmFileCounts that produces wrong intermediate Map data, but no count shown to the user is currently wrong through any live path. It is a latent trap: if anyone later wires CountTomlHotstrings (or iterates _TomlWarmFileCounts totals) into the UI — a plausible refactor — the inflation goes live. The AHK concurrency model is irrelevant; this is pure synchronous parse logic, no thread/Critical/InputHook interaction.

<a id="f-strict-canon-does-not-drop-stale-keys"></a>
#### `strict-canon-does-not-drop-stale-keys` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : config-toml (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_helpers.ahk:368`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_helpers.ahk:262`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/ErgoptiPlus.ahk:1560`
- **Description** : TOML_RunStrictCanonicalization's docstring (line 368-370) says it 'rebuild[s] the file from the in-memory state via SaveFullConfig so stale sections/keys are dropped.' But SaveFullConfig delegates to TOML_BatchWrite, which starts with `Sections := ParseTomlFile(Path)` (line 262) and only adds/overwrites the keys in the update list — it never clears sections absent from the updates. So any section not collected by SaveFullConfig (e.g. [ahk.personal_editor], or any key from a since-removed feature) is preserved verbatim, not dropped.
- **Cause racine** : BatchWrite is a read-modify-merge, while the canonicalization contract assumes it is a from-scratch rebuild. The two are incompatible. The benign side effect is that legitimately-needed-but-uncollected sections (ahk.personal_editor) survive (good), but the stated 'strict schema enforcement / stale-key pruning' is a no-op (the documented behaviour never happens).
- **Scénario** : A feature is removed from the manifest in a driver update. Its old keys remain in the user's config.toml indefinitely across every save, despite the canonicalization step that claims to purge them. ApplyConfigToml then WARNs 'unknown section path' on every boot for those stale keys. No functional break, but the file accumulates cruft the design says it removes.
- **Impact** : Documentation/behaviour mismatch (rule 5.7) and unbounded accumulation of stale keys plus recurring boot WARNs; misleads maintainers who rely on canonicalization to clean the file.
- **Correctif** : Either (a) make the contract true — have SaveFullConfig pass a 'rebuild from scratch' flag to a variant of BatchWrite that starts from an empty Sections map (and explicitly re-add every section that must persist, including ahk.personal_editor), or (b) correct the docstrings on TOML_RunStrictCanonicalization and SaveFullConfig to state that untouched sections are PRESERVED (merge semantics) and remove the 'stale keys are dropped' claim. Pick one; do not keep the false claim.
- **Test** : headless-unit: seed config.toml with a bogus `[ahk.removed_feature] foo = 1`, run SaveFullConfig, then re-parse. If fix (a): assert the bogus section is gone. If fix (b): assert it is preserved AND add a meta-static test asserting the docstring no longer contains 'dropped'. Either way the test pins the chosen, documented behaviour.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-format-toml-stale-path-deadcode"></a>
#### `format-toml-stale-path-deadcode` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : config-toml (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_helpers.ahk:362`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_helpers.ahk:363`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/personal_toml_editor.ahk:342`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/personal_toml_editor.ahk:431`
- **Description** : All three formatter call sites compute RepoRoot by stripping a `\static\drivers\autohotkey$` suffix from A_ScriptDir and then look for `tools\format_toml.py`. The actual driver lives at static\ergopti_plus\windows (the regex never matches, so RepoRoot keeps the full A_ScriptDir + an erroneous trailing '\'), and the real formatter is at tools\dev\format_toml.py, not tools\format_toml.py. FileExist therefore returns false and the RunWait is skipped at every site.
- **Cause racine** : Stale paths left over from a previous repo layout (the 'drivers/autohotkey' tree the file-path-header comments still reference). Because the only effect is a guarded RunWait that never fires, the dead path is silent — the TOML files are simply never reformatted by the Python canonicaliser.
- **Scénario** : A maintainer expects config.toml / personal hotstrings TOML to be normalised (=== headers ===, sorted keys) by the shared Python formatter after every save, as the comments promise. It never happens; files keep the AHK-side BatchWrite formatting only. If someone later 'fixes' the path so the RunWait DOES fire, it would introduce a synchronous blocking python RunWait on the SaveFullConfig path (which runs on a 500 ms boot timer and on every menu toggle) — a latent hot-adjacent blocking call.
- **Impact** : Dead formatting feature today; a latent message-pump-blocking RunWait the moment the path is corrected without also moving it off the save path. Also a correctness footgun: code says it formats, it does not.
- **Correctif** : Fix the path to the real layout: derive RepoRoot by stripping `\static\ergopti_plus\windows$` and look for `tools\dev\format_toml.py`; OR if the Python pass is intentionally retired, delete all three RunWait blocks and their comments (rule 5.6). If kept and wired live, ensure it never runs synchronously inside SaveFullConfig's save path during typing — run it deferred/off-thread.
- **Test** : meta-static (tests/meta): grep the three files for `static\drivers\autohotkey` and for the literal `tools\format_toml.py`; assert neither stale token exists (the path must resolve against the real layout or the block must be removed). Encodes the layout-drift root cause.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-parsetomlgroupconfig-missing-file-cache-key-mismatch"></a>
#### `parsetomlgroupconfig-missing-file-cache-key-mismatch` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : config-toml (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:463`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:479`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:552`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:158`
- **Description** : ParseTomlGroupConfig computes CacheKey := (FilePath != '') ? FilePath : LowerCat (line 463) and the success path stores under CacheKey (line 552). But the file-not-found branch stores the empty Config under LowerCat (line 479), ignoring CacheKey. When called with an explicit FilePath whose file is missing, the cache entry lands under the wrong key.
- **Cause racine** : Key inconsistency between the two store sites.
