Two consequences: (1) the cache check on line 463-464 (`HotstringGroupConfig.Has(CacheKey)`) never matches the LowerCat-keyed empty Config, so every call with an explicit missing path re-reads/re-FileExists the file (minor perf); (2) _ParseTomlGroupConfig_InvalidatePath(FilePath) deletes HotstringGroupConfig[FilePath] (line 163-165) but cannot reach the LowerCat-keyed entry — so if a file is created after a missing-file call, a stale empty Config keyed under LowerCat could linger (e.g. when called as ParseTomlGroupConfig('personal', PersonalPath) before the personal file exists, then the user creates it).
- **Scénario** : A user with no personal.toml triggers a code path that calls ParseTomlGroupConfig('personal', personalPath) (caches empty Config under 'personal'). They then create personal hotstrings via the editor (which writes the file and calls _ParseTomlGroupConfig_InvalidatePath(personalPath), deleting the FilePath-keyed entry that does not exist). A subsequent call keyed by the bare category 'personal' (FilePath=='') would still see the stale empty Config until a Reload — so per-group delay/color/priority for personal hotstrings appears unset.
- **Impact** : Stale empty group-config can mask user-set per-group delay/color/priority on a category that did not exist at first probe, until the next Reload; plus redundant disk re-reads for explicit-path missing files. Low frequency, no crash.
- **Correctif** : Store the missing-file Config under CacheKey, not LowerCat: change line 479 to `HotstringGroupConfig[CacheKey] := Config`. This makes the cache key consistent across both branches and lets _ParseTomlGroupConfig_InvalidatePath reach it.
- **Test** : headless-unit (test_toml_loader): call ParseTomlGroupConfig('', TmpPath) on a non-existent TmpPath (caches empty), then create the file with a [_meta] delay, call _ParseTomlGroupConfig_InvalidatePath(TmpPath), then ParseTomlGroupConfig('', TmpPath) again and assert Delay reflects the new file. Fails before the fix because the empty Config was cached under '' and not invalidated.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-reg-keyexists-false-negative-value-only-key"></a>
#### `reg-keyexists-false-negative-value-only-key` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : config-toml (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/registry.ahk:291`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/registry.ahk:298`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/registry.ahk:302`
- **Description** : Reg_KeyExists first does `Loop Reg, keyPath, 'K'` (subkeys); if the key has no subkeys the loop body never runs, so it falls through to `RegRead(keyPath, '')` (line 298) probing the unnamed default value. A key that exists but has named values only (no subkeys) and no default value makes RegRead throw ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND (Extra=2), which line 302 treats as 'key absent' -> returns false.
- **Cause racine** : The probe conflates 'the (Default) value is absent' with 'the key is absent'. A REG_SZ/REG_DWORD-bearing key with no default value and no subkeys is a perfectly valid existing key, but the chosen existence proxy (default-value read) fails on it.
- **Scénario** : Code checks Reg_KeyExists on a key like an app's settings key that holds only named values (the common case) and no default and no subkeys; the function reports the key does not exist, leading callers to recreate it or skip logic that depended on its presence. Currently only used in tests, so production impact is latent.
- **Impact** : Latent false-negative existence check; harmless today (test-only) but a trap for the first production caller that probes a value-only key, which would then wrongly conclude the key is absent.
- **Correctif** : After the subkey loop, also enumerate values: `Loop Reg, keyPath, 'V' { return true }` before falling back to the default-value RegRead. Only return false on Extra=2/3 from BOTH the K and V enumerations. This makes existence independent of whether a (Default) value is set.
- **Test** : headless-unit (test_registry): under HKCU\Software\...\ErgoptiTest create a key with one named DWORD value and no subkeys and no default; AssertTrue(Reg_KeyExists(thatKey)). Fails before the fix; clean up the key after.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-ssid-utf8-misdecode-and-signal-offset-fragility"></a>
#### `ssid-utf8-misdecode-and-signal-offset-fragility` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : updater-infra (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/network_info.ahk:136`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/network_info.ahk:138`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/network_info.ahk:139`
- **Description** : _NI_QueryWlan reads ssid_len at offset 520 and decodes the SSID as 'UTF-8' from offset 524, and reads signal at fixed offset 576. DOT11_SSID's ucSSID is a raw 32-byte octet array, not necessarily UTF-8 — an SSID with non-UTF-8 bytes (legacy/Latin-1 APs, or binary) StrGet-decodes incorrectly, producing a different SHA-256 than the true bytes. The offsets are also hardcoded with no struct-version/length validation.
- **Cause racine** : 802.11 SSIDs are opaque octet strings; treating ucSSID as UTF-8 is a heuristic that fails for non-UTF-8 access points, yielding a hash that does not stably identify the network. The fixed offsets (520/524/576) assume a specific 64-bit WLAN_CONNECTION_ATTRIBUTES layout with no guard if cbData is smaller than expected.
- **Scénario** : User connects to a Wi-Fi network whose SSID contains non-UTF-8 bytes. NI_GetSsidHash returns a hash derived from a mojibake decode; the keylogger's per-network grouping treats the same physical network inconsistently, or two distinct networks collide. Low impact (privacy hash is best-effort) but the value is wrong/unstable.
- **Impact** : Incorrect/unstable SSID hash for non-UTF-8 networks; analytics grouping by network silently degrades. No keystroke impact.
- **Correctif** : Hash the raw SSID octets (copy ssid_len bytes from pData+524 into a Buffer and feed the bytes to the hash) rather than StrGet-then-hash, so the digest is over the true network identifier regardless of encoding. Add a bounds check that cbData >= 580 before reading the signal offset, returning an empty Map otherwise.
- **Test** : headless-unit: factor the SSID-bytes->hash step into a pure helper and assert that a byte sequence with non-UTF-8 bytes produces a stable hash equal to hashing those exact bytes (not the UTF-8 re-decode). Live hardware needed for the full WLAN path; the byte-hashing helper is unit-testable.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-ni-isvpnactive-missing-return-on-no-match"></a>
#### `ni-isvpnactive-missing-return-on-no-match` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : updater-infra (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/network_info.ahk:212`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/network_info.ahk:247`
- **Description** : NI_IsVpnActive returns true inside the loop when a VPN adapter is found, and the catch returns false, but when the while loop completes with no match there is no explicit return — the function returns an empty string (AHK's default for falling off the end). Callers using strict comparison (=== true / Type checks) may misbehave; '' is falsy in boolean context but is not the documented 'false' return value the port contract promises.
- **Cause racine** : AHK v2 functions that reach the end without a return statement return an empty string. The contract (NetworkInfo.spec.js) says isVpnActive() returns a boolean; the no-match path returns '' instead of false.
- **Scénario** : On a machine with no VPN adapter, NI_IsVpnActive returns '' rather than false. A downstream consumer that does 'if (NI_IsVpnActive() === false)' or logs the typed value gets the wrong type; a JSON serialization of the network context emits '' where false is expected.
- **Impact** : Contract violation / type inconsistency on the common no-VPN path. Low impact since '' is falsy, but it can break strict-equality consumers and pollutes serialized diagnostics.
- **Correctif** : Add an explicit 'return false' after the while loop (before the closing of the try) and ensure all exit paths return a genuine boolean.
- **Test** : headless-unit in tests/test_adapter_compliance_new.ahk: assert that NI_IsVpnActive() returns a value whose Type is 'Integer' (AHK boolean) and specifically === false on a machine/CI without a VPN adapter (the common case), failing when '' is returned.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-updater-setcheckinterval-type-guard-rejects-non-integer"></a>
#### `updater-setcheckinterval-type-guard-rejects-non-integer` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : updater-infra (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:195`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/updater.ahk:183`
- **Description** : Updater_SetCheckInterval guards with 'if (Type(Seconds) != "Integer" or Seconds < 0) return'. The menu presets (_MakeFreqSetter(Preset.Seconds)) pass integer literals so the menu path is fine, but Updater_LoadCheckInterval coerces persisted config via 'Integer(raw + 0)' and any other caller passing a numeric string or a Float (e.g. from a future config source) is silently dropped with no log. A silently-rejected setter contradicts the project's 'setters must log' and 'fail loud' rules.
- **Cause racine** : Type() == 'Integer' is an exact-type test; a value like 60.0 (Float) or '60' (String) fails it even though it is a valid non-negative cadence. The early 'return' is silent — no LoggerWarn — so a mis-typed value disappears without trace.
- **Scénario** : A future caller or a config-migration passes the interval as a string or float (entirely plausible given Updater_LoadCheckInterval reads strings from TOML). Updater_SetCheckInterval returns immediately, the cadence is not applied, the timer keeps the old interval, and nothing is logged — the user's chosen cadence silently does not take effect.
- **Impact** : Silent no-op on a valid-but-differently-typed value; violates fail-loud/setter-logging conventions. Low because the only current caller passes integer literals.
- **Correctif** : Coerce defensively: 'Seconds := Integer(Seconds)' inside a try, reject only genuinely invalid (negative or non-numeric) input, and LoggerWarn on rejection so a bad value is visible in the log rather than silently dropped.
- **Test** : headless-unit: call Updater_SetCheckInterval('300') and Updater_SetCheckInterval(300.0) and assert UPDATER_CHECK_INTERVAL becomes 300 (currently it stays unchanged). Fails before the coercion fix, passes after.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-prefix-onkeydown-tab-llm-feed-gap"></a>
#### `prefix-onkeydown-tab-llm-feed-gap` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : e2e-lost-keystroke (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:924`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:936`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:185`
- **Description** : In _OnPrefixKeyDown, the branch handling VK 0x09 (Tab) / 0x0D (Enter) at line 924 calls HSE_FeedReset(true) and returns WITHOUT calling LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive. A later else-if branch at line 936 (`VK == 0x09 or 0x0D or 0x1B`) is the only place that forwards Tab/Enter/Escape to the LLM bridge, but it is unreachable: every VK it tests has already been consumed by the earlier 0x09/0x0D branch (924) or the 0x1B/arrows branch (928). So the LLM bridge's OnFlush (clear rolling context on Enter/Tab/Escape) is driven for Tab/Enter/Escape only via the dispatcher-fallback path, not via the PrefixWatcher hook that the bridge is normally attached to.
- **Cause racine** : Dead-branch ordering: AHK evaluates the if/else-if chain top-down; the 0x09/0x0D case (and the 0x1B/arrow case) match and return/handle first, so the trailing else-if at line 936 testing the same VKs can never execute. The LLM feed for Enter was therefore dropped on the PrefixWatcher path (Tab is partially salvaged by the LLM_Tooltip_TryAcceptTab early-return at 925; Escape is salvaged by the 0x1B arrow branch at 934 which does forward to the bridge).
- **Scénario** : LLM bridge is active and attached to the PrefixWatcher (the normal case). User finishes a sentence and presses Enter, expecting the rolling LLM context buffer to flush so the next prediction starts fresh. _OnPrefixKeyDown handles Enter via HSE_FeedReset and returns at line 924 without LLM_Bridge_OnFlush; the LLM buffer keeps the pre-Enter context, so the next prediction is seeded with stale cross-paragraph context (reset_on_nav silently does nothing for Enter on this path).
- **Impact** : No lost keystroke, but a real correctness gap: LLM context is not flushed on Enter (and Tab when not accepting a suggestion) as the reset_on_nav contract promises, degrading prediction relevance after line breaks. Low severity because predictions still work, just with staler context.
- **Correctif** : Fold the LLM feed into the live branches: in the 0x09/0x0D branch (line 924), after the Tab-accept early-return, call LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive(VK) before/with HSE_FeedReset; delete the unreachable else-if at line 936. LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive already maps 0x0D/0x1B->OnFlush and 0x09->TryAcceptTab-or-OnFlush, so routing every nav VK through it once is sufficient and removes the dead code.
- **Test** : headless-unit: stub LLM_Bridge_FeedKeyDownIfActive into a recorder, set _LLM_Bridge_Active := true, call _OnPrefixKeyDown(IH, 0x0D, sc) and assert the recorder saw VK 0x0D (Enter flush reached the bridge). Add the same assertion for Tab when LLM_Tooltip_TryAcceptTab returns false. The test fails today because the Enter VK never reaches the bridge through the watcher hook.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-win-l-lock-no-watcher-reset"></a>
#### `win-l-lock-no-watcher-reset` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : e2e-keystroke-pipeline (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/layout.ahk:415-421`
- **Description** : The special-cased `#<scancode>` handler for the remapped 'l' key calls `DllCall('LockWorkStation') UpdateLastSentCharacter(Character)`. It does not reset HSE_Buffer / _PrefixBuffer, and UpdateLastSentCharacter pushes 'l' as if a literal letter were typed.
- **Cause racine** : Locking the workstation is a focus-destroying / context-unknown event (like a mouse click or Ctrl+V) that should set HSE_FeedReset(false) and _ResetPrefixBuffer, but instead the handler records 'l' into the last-sent ring and leaves the buffers intact. After unlock the engine still believes the pre-lock word context abuts the cursor.
- **Scénario** : User types `contro` then Win+L to lock, unlocks later and resumes typing in a different field; the HSE buffer still holds `contro` (plus a phantom 'l'), so the next characters are matched against a stale word context and a hotstring can mis-fire or fail to fire where it should.
- **Impact** : Minor: stale word context across a lock/unlock boundary causing an occasional mis-/non-fire on the first word typed after unlocking.
- **Correctif** : In the Win+L handler call `HSE_FeedReset(false)` and `_ResetPrefixBuffer()` (and drop the misleading `UpdateLastSentCharacter('l')`, since no 'l' was emitted) so the lock is treated as the context-unknown boundary it is.
- **Test** : headless-unit: seed HSE_Buffer with a word, invoke the lock handler's reset path (factored into a testable helper that stubs DllCall), and assert HSE_Buffer is empty and HSE_StartIsWordBoundary is false afterwards.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-per-entry-priority-divergence-cache-vs-toml"></a>
#### `per-entry-priority-divergence-cache-vs-toml` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : gap-hotstrings-cache-toml-fallback-parse-divergence (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_cache.ahk:230`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_cache.ahk:259`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_cache.ahk:377`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:345`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstring_engine.ahk:354`
- **Description** : LoadHotstringsSection's TOML-parse fallback resolves a full priority cascade per entry: ResolvedPriority (HotstringsResolve section/file/source) then EntryPriority := _ParseEntryPriority(Line, ResolvedPriority) which reads an inline `priority = N` override on that very line (toml_loader.ahk:345), and passes it as Options["Priority"]. The cache path does NOT: _HotstringsCacheBuildRows stores only 6 fields per row [flags,trigger,output,final,repeat,caseSens] (hotstrings_cache.ahk:230) and the .tsv serializer writes only 8 columns with NO priority column (line 259-262). _HsCacheRegisterSection builds Opts WITHOUT a "Priority" key (line 377), so CreateHotstring falls back to _HSE_ResolveRegistrationPriority(Category,Section) (hotstring_engine.ahk:381->354) which folds in ONLY the section/file/source cascade via HotstringsResolve and can NEVER see an individual entry's `priority = N`.
- **Cause racine** : The cache is a lossy projection of the TOML: the .tsv schema omits the per-entry priority that the TOML fallback computes from the raw line via _ParseEntryPriority. The two paths therefore resolve different Priority values for any entry carrying an inline priority override, and the cache cannot even represent it. HSE collision precedence among equal-length triggers is decided by Spec.Priority (hotstring_engine_main.ahk:314-319), so a wrong Priority silently changes which of two overlapping hotstrings wins.
- **Scénario** : A maintainer adds an inline override to a bundled TOML, e.g. distancesreduction.toml: `"qu" = { output = "que", ..., priority = 60 }` to force it to win over a colliding common-tier entry. On first launch (cache miss) the rebuild reads the row but drops the priority -> the entry registers at the section/source default (e.g. 10). On every subsequent FAST boot it also registers at 10. But if the user instead has the bundled category disabled in the cache and that section falls to the TOML fallback, OR the personal/extension equivalent of the same entry goes through LoadHotstringsSection, it registers at 60. Different boots / paths register the SAME TOML at DIFFERENT collision priorities -> a hotstring that wins on one boot loses on another, non-deterministically by which path served it.
- **Impact** : Silent, hard-to-debug collision-priority divergence between fast-cache, rebuilt-cache and TOML-fallback registration of the identical bundled TOML. Dormant today (no bundled entry currently carries an inline `priority =`, confirmed by grep over shared/hotstrings/*.toml), but the cache physically cannot honour the documented "individual > section > file > source" cascade the TOML path implements, so the feature is a silent no-op for bundled categories and a latent divergence the moment such an override is added.
- **Correctif** : Either (a) add a 9th priority column to the .tsv: in _HotstringsCacheBuildRows compute EntryPriority := _ParseEntryPriority(Line, _HSE_SourcePriority(Category) or the resolved section value) and push it into the row; serialize/deserialize it (it is a small integer, identifier-safe, no escaping); have _HsCacheRegisterSection set Opts["Priority"] from it so the cache reproduces LoadHotstringsSection 1:1 as the module docstring claims. Or (b) if bundled categories are intentionally NEVER to carry per-entry priority, document and assert that with a meta-test and make the build/registrar explicitly pass the resolved section priority so both paths provably agree. Bump the cache format version so old .tsv files are rebuilt.
- **Test** : headless-unit: write a temp TOML section with two equal-length colliding triggers where the second carries `priority = 60`; build rows via _HotstringsCacheBuildRows, round-trip through _HotstringsCacheWriteTsv/_HotstringsCacheReadTsv, register via _HsCacheRegisterSection with the _HotstringRegistrar recorder, and assert the recorded Spec.Priority for the overridden trigger == 60 — and equals the Priority that LoadHotstringsSection's TOML fallback registers for the same line. Fails today (cache drops it), passes after the fix. Plus a meta-static test asserting the .tsv writer emits a priority column.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : This is a pure boot-time logic/data-loss bug, not a concurrency bug — all hotstring registration runs single-threaded at startup, so the AHK cooperative-threading model is irrelevant. I verified the data flow line-by-line against the real source.

CACHE PATH DROPS PRIORITY (confirmed): _HotstringsCacheBuildRows pushes a 6-field row [Flags,Trigger,Output,FinalResult,IsRepeat,IsCaseSens] (hotstrings_cache.ahk:230) — no priority. _HotstringsCacheWriteTsv serialises exactly 8 columns (cat,sec,flags,trigger,output,final,repeat,caseSens) with no priority column (lines 259-262), and _HotstringsCacheReadTsv reads back the same 6 fields (line 287). _HsCacheRegisterSection builds Opts = Map("TimeActivationSeconds","FinalResult","IsRepeat","Category","Section") with NO "Priority" key (lines 361-362). So CreateHotstring's ternary (hotstring_engine.ahk:381-383) takes the else branch -> _HSE_ResolveRegistrationPriority(Category,Section) (line 354), which only folds the section/file/source cascade via HotstringsResolve and falls back to HSE_PRIORITY_COMMON=10. It can never see an inline `priority = N`.

TOML FALLBACK HONOURS IT (confirmed): LoadHotstringsSection computes EntryPriority := _ParseEntryPriority(Line, ResolvedPriority) (toml_loader.ahk:340), reading the inline override via regex [,{]\s*priority\s*=\s*([0-9]+) (line 58), and passes Options["Priority"] (line 347). LoadExtTomlFile does the same (line 421). So the two paths resolve different Priority for any entry with an inline override.

PRIORITY IS LOAD-BEARING (confirmed): _HSE_Beats breaks equal-length-trigger collisions by higher Priority then lower Seq (hotstring_engine_main.ahk:778-783), and Spec.Priority defaults to HSE_PRIORITY_COMMON (line 319). A wrong Priority silently flips which of two overlapping triggers wins.

DORMANT (confirmed): grep for `[,{] priority = N` over static/ergopti_plus/shared/hotstrings/*.toml returns no matches, so nothing exercises this today. The module docstring's claim that the cache reproduces the old loaders / LoadHotstringsSection "1:1" and that "rows carry the identical fields fed to CreateHotstring" is therefore false specifically for the priority field — the per-entry cascade is a silent no-op for the 5 bundled categories.

WHERE THE FINDING OVERSTATES (severity-relevant): The scenario's "different boots register the SAME bundled TOML at different priorities, non-deterministically" is not accurate. All 5 bundled categories are unconditionally in HS_BUNDLED_CATEGORIES, so HotstringsCacheEnsure builds rows for every one of them and populates _GENERATED_HOTSTRINGS; LoadHotstringsSection (lines 274-282) then ALWAYS takes the cache fast-path for a bundled, non-personal category. There is no per-category "disable in the cache" mechanism, and a bundled category only falls through to the TOML parse on a genuine miss (e.g. _SharedDir unset in a harness, or a rebuild yielding zero rows). So the real divergence is between the bundled entry served from the cache (priority dropped, lands at 10) and the SAME entry copied into a personal/extension TOML, which goes through LoadHotstringsSection/LoadExtTomlFile and honours the override. That is still a genuine, surprising inconsistency and a footgun, but it is a single deterministic path per bundled file, not a per-boot race. The bug is real; the "non-deterministic across boots of the identical file" framing is not.

<a id="f-isrepeat-section-name-mismatch-latent-divergence"></a>
#### `isrepeat-section-name-mismatch-latent-divergence` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : gap-hotstrings-cache-toml-fallback-parse-divergence (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_cache.ahk:225`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:347`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:424`, `static/ergopti_plus/shared/hotstrings/magickey.toml:38`
- **Description** : Three inconsistent treatments of IsRepeat for the same bundled data. (1) The cache build computes IsRepeat := (category=="magickey" and CurrentSection=="repeatcorrections" and InStr(Trigger,★)) — but the real section header is [[repeat_corrections]] (magickey.toml:38), so StrLower gives "repeat_corrections" WITH an underscore and the literal "repeatcorrections" can never match. (2) The TOML-parse fallback LoadHotstringsSection (toml_loader.ahk:347-353) does not put IsRepeat in its Options Map at all, so CreateHotstring defaults it to False. (3) The extension loader LoadExtTomlFile (toml_loader.ahk:424) DOES compute it but also against "repeatcorrections" (no underscore). All three resolve to False today only because (a) the section name never matches the no-underscore literal AND (b) no bundled trigger in that section contains ★ (verified: the entries are arrê/ccê/… fixed corrections, and the old generated bundle emitted IsRepeat=false for every row, confirmed via git show 63331a2a9^).
- **Cause racine** : Copy-pasted, never-firing predicate with a section-name typo (repeatcorrections vs repeat_corrections, a casualty of the snake_case rename in commit 85e967816) combined with the main fallback path simply omitting IsRepeat. The cache module's docstring claims it 'replicates the generator's exact test' and reproduces LoadHotstringsSection 1:1, but LoadHotstringsSection does not compute IsRepeat, so the cache build's per-row IsRepeat is dead-but-divergent intent.
- **Scénario** : A future TOML edit adds a ★-bearing repeat trigger (e.g. "x★" => "xx") to a magic-key section, or the [[repeat_corrections]] section is renamed back to a form matching the no-underscore literal. The cache path would then set IsRepeat=true and register it through the word-boundary repeat gate (_HSE_WordBoundaryAllows, hotstring_engine_main.ahk:1091), while the same entry served by the TOML fallback would register with IsRepeat=false and bypass that gate — a fast-boot vs fallback behavioural divergence (the repeat fires at start-of-word on one path, not the other).
- **Impact** : Currently inert (all paths agree at False by accident), so no user-visible effect today. But it is a booby-trap: the cache and the TOML fallback have structurally different IsRepeat logic, so they are NOT 1:1, and the divergence will silently activate on a plausible future TOML/section edit. Also actively misleading to maintainers reading the 'reproduces 1:1' claim.
- **Correctif** : Pick one source of truth. Best: delete the IsRepeat computation from _HotstringsCacheBuildRows (and from LoadExtTomlFile) entirely if repeat is now fully owned by the engine-level HSE_TryRepeatKey fallback (it is — see hotstring_engine_main.ahk:701/750), storing IsRepeat=false unconditionally; OR if registered-hotstring IsRepeat is still meaningful, fix the section literal to "repeat_corrections" in BOTH places AND make LoadHotstringsSection compute and pass the identical IsRepeat so the two paths provably match. Either way add a comment that the cache and the TOML fallback must use byte-identical IsRepeat logic.
- **Test** : meta-static (tests/meta): assert that the IsRepeat predicate string in hotstrings_cache.ahk and the one (if any) in toml_loader.ahk's LoadHotstringsSection are byte-identical, and that whatever section literal they test against actually exists as a [[section]] header in magickey.toml (parse the real TOML for the header). This fails the moment the two paths' IsRepeat logic drifts or the section literal stops matching a real header.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below threshold.

<a id="f-freshness-same-second-edit-window"></a>
#### `freshness-same-second-edit-window` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : gap-hotstrings-cache-toml-fallback-parse-divergence (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_cache.ahk:86`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_cache.ahk:92`
- **Description** : _HotstringsCacheIsFresh treats the .tsv as fresh unless a TOML's mtime is STRICTLY GREATER than the .tsv's (line 92: `FileGetTime(TomlPath,"M") > TsvTime` => stale). FileGetTime "M" returns YYYYMMDDHH24MISS at one-SECOND resolution (the test suite even notes this in test_i18n.ahk: 'FileGetTime has 1-second resolution'). So a TOML whose mtime equals the .tsv's mtime (same second) is judged fresh and its content is NOT picked up. The prompt hypothesised minute granularity; it is actually second granularity, which narrows but does not eliminate the window.
- **Cause racine** : Second-granularity mtime compared with strict `>`; the previous rebuild writes the .tsv at second T after reading the TOMLs, so a TOML re-saved within that same second T compares equal and loses the freshness race.
- **Scénario** : A developer or a packaging/sync script rewrites a bundled TOML programmatically in the same second the .tsv was last written (or restores files from an archive that normalises mtimes to a single second). Next boot serves the STALE cache; the TOML change (a corrected expansion, a removed entry) is silently absent until some later boot where the mtimes differ. Hard to diagnose because re-running often 'fixes' it once a second has elapsed.
- **Impact** : Rare, narrow (one-second) window where a legitimate TOML edit is silently ignored for one or more boots. Affects developers/packagers more than end users (who edit personal TOML, which is not in HS_BUNDLED_CATEGORIES and always goes through the TOML path). Low severity but a genuine correctness edge in a self-healing cache whose whole contract is 'rebuild after a TOML edit'.
- **Correctif** : Treat equal-second mtimes as stale: change the comparison to rebuild when the TOML is `>=` the .tsv, i.e. require the .tsv to be strictly newer (`FileGetTime(TomlPath,"M") >= TsvTime => return false`). Equivalent to the i18n contract phrased the other way. The only cost is one extra rebuild on the rare equal-second boot, which is the safe direction. Optionally also stamp a content hash of the TOML set into the .tsv header and compare hashes instead of mtimes for an exact freshness signal.
- **Test** : headless-unit: write a temp .tsv, then write a temp TOML and force both mtimes to the identical second via FileSetTime; call _HotstringsCacheIsFresh and assert it returns false (rebuild) so a same-second edit is never served stale. Fails with the current strict `>`, passes after switching to `>=`.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below threshold.

<a id="f-kh-intercept-dead-flag"></a>
#### `kh-intercept-dead-flag` — **info** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : input-dispatch (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/keyboard_hook.ahk:63`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/adapters/keyboard_hook.ahk:64`
- **Description** : KHStart records Opts["intercept"] into _KH_INTERCEPT, but the shared InputHook runs in visible ("V") mode and the adapter never consumes _KH_INTERCEPT anywhere. A caller requesting intercept=true to suppress events gets no suppression and no error — the request is silently ignored (the docstring does say so).
- **Cause racine** : The intercept capability cannot be honored per-subscriber on a shared visible InputHook, so the flag is inert. It is documented as a no-op but still presents a misleading API surface that could mask a wrong expectation.
- **Scénario** : A future feature calls KHStart with intercept=true expecting the key to be swallowed; the key still passes through to the app, and the bug looks like a 'lost suppression' rather than an unsupported capability.
- **Impact** : No current functional break (documented), but a latent footgun: a behavioral option that silently does nothing violates the project's 'no hardcoded/silent behavioral fallback' principle.
- **Correctif** : Either remove _KH_INTERCEPT entirely (per the 'no unused fallback code' rule) or, if the capability is desired, log a WARNING when intercept=true is requested so the unsupported path is loud, and track it in the spec.
- **Test** : meta/static: assert _KH_INTERCEPT is either unreferenced after assignment (candidate for removal) or that KHStart emits a WARNING when Opts["intercept"] is true. Cheap grep-based guard.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-delay-edit-non-integer-personal-truncation"></a>
#### `delay-edit-non-integer-personal-truncation` — **info** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : hotstrings-config (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_config_window.ahk:1270`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_config.ahk:421`
- **Description** : _HCW_TomlValue formats a personal-file delay as Format("{:.3f}", seconds), i.e. 3 decimal places = millisecond granularity. The common/extension override store (_ParseOverrides line 421 / _SaveOverrides) keeps the raw number with full precision. The delay Edit is entered in whole ms (DelayEdit.Value + 0, divided by 1000), so in practice both paths land on ms multiples and the truncation is harmless — but the two backends are not symmetric, and a programmatically-set or hand-edited sub-ms delay on a personal file is silently rounded while the same value on a common category is not.
- **Cause racine** : Two independent serialisers (_HCW_TomlValue for personal [_meta], _SaveOverrides for the shared override file) with different precision rules for the same logical field.
- **Scénario** : Only reachable via hand-edited TOML or a future non-ms input path; the current GUI feeds integer ms so the divergence is masked. Flagged for completeness as a single-source-of-truth smell rather than a live defect.
- **Impact** : No user-visible bug today (GUI always feeds ms). Future risk: personal vs common delay precision diverge for the same entered value.
- **Correctif** : Route both backends through one delay-serialisation helper, or document that delays are ms-quantised everywhere and clamp the override store the same way. Low priority — only worth doing if a non-integer-ms delay path is ever introduced.
- **Test** : headless-unit asserting _HCW_TomlValue("delay", 2.0) and the override-store serialisation of 2.0 produce the same on-disk numeric string (modulo trailing zeros), pinning the two paths to one rule.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-wpm-graph-label-json-injection"></a>
#### `wpm-graph-label-json-injection` — **info** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tooltip-widget (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/wpm_widget.ahk:991`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/wpm_widget.ahk:1005`
- **Description** : WPMWidget_PushGraphUpdate builds a JSON string by raw concatenation: label := wpm_str . " " . t("menu.metrics.wpm_unit"); json := '{... ,"label":"' . label . '" ...}' and passes it into ExecuteScriptAsync("...updateGraph(" . json . ")"). A JSON_Escape helper exists (line 1005) but is never called; the label is interpolated verbatim.
- **Cause racine** : If the localized unit string t("menu.metrics.wpm_unit") (or any future label content) ever contains a double-quote or backslash, it breaks the JSON literal and the ExecuteScriptAsync payload, silently failing the graph update (and in principle allowing script injection into the canvas page if the string were attacker-controlled — it is not today, it comes from the bundled translation table).
- **Scénario** : A future translation or a user-editable unit label containing a quote/backslash silently breaks the graph rendering with no error (ExecuteScriptAsync swallows it).
- **Impact** : Today the label is a controlled translation string, so no live bug; latent robustness gap and dead-code smell (unused JSON_Escape).
- **Correctif** : Route the label (and any other interpolated string fields) through the existing JSON_Escape() helper when building the JSON, or assemble the payload with a proper JSON builder; remove JSON_Escape if it is intentionally not needed.
- **Test** : headless-unit: assert JSON_Escape('a"b\\c') == '"a\\"b\\\\c"' and that the label-building path uses it (e.g. feed a unit string containing a quote and assert the produced JSON is parseable / contains the escaped sequence).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-sensors-cpu-state-stale-across-reload"></a>
#### `sensors-cpu-state-stale-across-reload` — **info** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : e2e-async-background (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_sensors.ahk:75`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_sensors.ahk:116`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_sensors.ahk:181`
- **Description** : KLSensors.prev_idle/prev_kernel/prev_user are class statics initialised to -1 (keylogger_sensors.ahk:75). KL_Sensors_Tick computes a CPU delta only when prev_idle >= 0 (line 116); the first tick after process start records the baseline and emits no cpu_pct. KL_Sensors_Stop (line 191) cancels the timer but does NOT reset the prev_* baseline. On a KL_Sensors_Stop()/KL_Sensors_Start() cycle within the same process (feature toggled off then on) the next tick computes a delta against a stale baseline spanning the entire off-period, producing a meaningless cpu_pct for that one sample.
- **Cause racine** : Statics persist for the lifetime of the AHK instance regardless of Stop/Start; the lifecycle Stop does not invalidate the CPU baseline, so the delta math straddles a gap where the timer was not running. Not a hot-path issue and not a crash — purely a one-sample data-quality artefact in the system_load metric.
- **Scénario** : User toggles the metrics feature off for ten minutes, then back on. The first system_load event after re-enabling reports a CPU percentage averaged over the whole ten-minute gap (almost always near a flat idle figure or a misleading spike), polluting the dashboard's CPU trend with one wrong point.
- **Impact** : Minor metric inaccuracy on a Stop/Start cycle; no effect on typing, no crash, no leak.
- **Correctif** : Reset KLSensors.prev_idle/prev_kernel/prev_user to -1 in KL_Sensors_Stop (or at the top of KL_Sensors_Start) so the first tick after a restart re-establishes a fresh baseline and skips the bogus delta, exactly as it does on cold start.
- **Test** : headless-unit: call KL_Sensors_Stop() then assert KLSensors.prev_idle == -1 (and prev_kernel/prev_user), proving the baseline is invalidated so the next tick takes the >= 0 guard's 'record baseline only' branch.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-count-regex-vs-entry-pattern-divergence"></a>
#### `count-regex-vs-entry-pattern-divergence` — **info** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : gap-hotstrings-cache-toml-fallback-parse-divergence (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:103`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:321`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_cache.ahk:203`
- **Description** : CountTomlSection/CountTomlHotstrings (via _TomlWarmFileCounts, toml_loader.ahk:103) count any line matching the loose `^(?:"[^"]+"|[A-Za-z0-9_.-]+)\s*=` — i.e. ANY key=value line in a non-_meta section. But the actual registration (both the cache build at hotstrings_cache.ahk:203 and the TOML fallback at toml_loader.ahk:321) requires the FULL _HOTSTRING_ENTRY_PATTERN inline-table shape (output=…, is_word=…, auto_expand=…, is_case_sensitive=…, final_result=…). Any non-conforming key=value line in a section (e.g. a simple `key = "value"` roll entry, or a stray scalar) is counted but never registered as a hotstring, so the displayed count can diverge from the cache row count and from the number of Specs actually registered.
- **Cause racine** : Two different regexes for 'count this entry' vs 'register this entry', with the counting one strictly looser. The cache row count (rows that match _HOTSTRING_ENTRY_PATTERN) is the truthful figure; the count helpers over-count.
- **Scénario** : The tray/config window shows 'N hotstrings' for a category using CountTomlHotstrings, but the engine registered fewer (only the inline-table entries). A user comparing the advertised count to observed behaviour, or a future assertion comparing CountTomlSection to _HS_CACHE_ROWS[key].Length, sees a mismatch. Purely a reporting/consistency issue — no keystroke or registration effect.
- **Impact** : Cosmetic/diagnostic only: hotstring counts shown in UI can be slightly inflated versus what is actually active, and any code that trusts CountTomlSection as the registered-row count would be wrong. No functional divergence in what gets registered.
- **Correctif** : Make _TomlWarmFileCounts count with the same _HOTSTRING_ENTRY_PATTERN used for registration (or, for sections that legitimately use the simple key="value" form handled by LoadExtTomlFile, count both shapes exactly as that loader registers them). Then the displayed count equals the registered/cached row count by construction.
- **Test** : headless-unit: for a representative bundled section, assert CountTomlSection(category, section) == _HotstringsCacheBuildRows()["category.section"].Length, so the count helper and the actual registration can never drift. Fails today for any section containing a non-conforming key=value line; passes once the count regex matches the registration regex.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below threshold.

### Catégorie : Sécurité / vie privée (4)

<a id="f-inline-autotype-not-synthetic"></a>
#### `inline-autotype-not-synthetic` — **medium** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : llm-engine (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/prediction_engine.ahk:1119-1130`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/llm_bridge.ahk:480-509`
- **Description** : When inline_autotype is on, LLM_Engine_OnResults types the prediction via TextSend(text, 0, 0) on the final render WITHOUT calling KL_MarkSynthetic("llm")/KL_ClearSynthetic, and WITHOUT appending the typed text to _LLM_Bridge_Buffer. The Tab-accept path (LLM_Bridge_OnAccept) does both correctly (KL_MarkSynthetic + buffer append). The two acceptance surfaces are therefore inconsistent.
- **Cause racine** : TextSend in the inline branch emits real keystrokes that flow back through the PrefixWatcher InputHook (synthetic input re-enters the hook). Because the synthetic flag is not set, the keylogger stamps these as MANUAL keystrokes (counted in `chars`, attributed to the user's n-gram source esrc) — exactly the PII/metrics pollution KL_MarkSynthetic exists to prevent. Separately, because _LLM_Bridge_Buffer is not advanced by the inserted text, the rolling context the engine sees diverges from the document: the very next keystroke fires a prediction against a buffer that is missing the words just auto-typed, so corrections/next-words are computed against stale context.
- **Scénario** : User enables inline auto-type (Copilot-style). They type "je voudrais " and the engine inserts "vous remercier" directly into the document. The keylogger records "vous remercier" as words the human typed (inflating WPM, polluting n-gram stats with model output). Then the user types " pour"; the engine predicts from buffer "je voudrais  pour" (missing the inserted "vous remercier"), producing an irrelevant or duplicated suggestion.
- **Impact** : Keylogger PII corruption (model output indistinguishable from human typing in the persisted SQLite/JSONL), inflated metrics, and degraded prediction quality in inline mode because the context buffer desyncs from the actual document.
- **Correctif** : In the inline branch of LLM_Engine_OnResults, mirror LLM_Bridge_OnAccept: call KL_MarkSynthetic("llm") before TextSend, schedule SetTimer((*) => KL_ClearSynthetic(), -SYNTH_CLEAR_DELAY_MS) after, and append the typed text to _LLM_Bridge_Buffer (or route the inline insertion through a shared helper that LLM_Bridge_OnAccept also calls, so the synthetic-tag + buffer-advance logic lives in exactly one place per rule 5.2).
- **Test** : headless-unit: in test_llm_prediction_engine.ahk, set _LLM_Engine["inline_autotype"]:=true, stub TextSend and KL_MarkSynthetic/KL_ClearSynthetic to record calls, call LLM_Engine_OnResults(["vous remercier"], ctx, 1, true) and assert (a) KL_MarkSynthetic("llm") was invoked exactly once before TextSend, and (b) _LLM_Bridge_Buffer now ends with the inserted text. Fails today (no mark, no append).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code. The PII half of the finding is REAL and is not a race — it follows directly from the AHK observation-hook model.

INLINE PATH (prediction_engine.ahk:1119-1130): on the final render with inline_autotype on, it does `TextSend(text, 0, 0)` then `return`, with NO `KL_MarkSynthetic("llm")` and no buffer mutation. `TextSend` routes to `SendText` (adapters/text_sender.ahk:88,104) for short payloads.

WHY THE SYNTHETIC OUTPUT IS MIS-RECORDED: the keylogger uses an observation InputHook configured `"V L0"` with NO `I0` (keylogger_hook.ahk:13-18 header and the process-wide hook at :391). The header explicitly says it observes "the OUTPUT of ... hotstring expansions" — i.e. synthetic SendText/SendInput re-enters this hook by design. In `KL_Hook_OnChar` the synthetic meta tag (`meta["s"]=1`, `meta["st"]`) is stamped ONLY `if Keylogger.synth_active` (keylogger_hook.ahk:242-245); otherwise the char is pushed to `buffer_events`/`buffer_text` as a normal manual keystroke (:247-248). The walker then counts it in `chars` and attributes it to the user's n-gram esrc histogram (keylogger_walker.ahk:517-559). Since the inline branch never raises `Keylogger.synth_active`, every auto-typed model character is recorded as if the human typed it. The Tab-accept surface (LLM_Bridge_OnAccept, llm_bridge.ahk:486-491) correctly wraps its TextSend with `KL_MarkSynthetic("llm")` + a deferred `KL_ClearSynthetic`, so the two acceptance surfaces are genuinely inconsistent. This is unconditional on every inline auto-type, exactly the pollution KL_MarkSynthetic was built to prevent — confirmed.

BUFFER half — the finding's MECHANISM is partly WRONG. The finding asserts `_LLM_Bridge_Buffer` ends up "missing the inserted text," causing stale context. But there is NO `_PrefixWatcherSuppressed`/`HSE_Suppressed` flag set by the LLM module (grep of modules/llm: no matches). The PrefixWatcher `_OnPrefixChar` skips `LLM_Bridge_FeedCharIfActive` ONLY under those suppress flags (hotstring_prefix_watcher.ahk:661-675); the watcher hook is also `"V L0"` with NO `I0` and the comment at :624-627 says injected keystrokes must reach it. So the synthetic SendText output DOES re-enter the watcher and IS appended to `_LLM_Bridge_Buffer` char-by-char via `LLM_Bridge_OnChar` (llm_bridge.ahk:257). The buffer therefore advances — it is not left stale/missing as described. Worse in the opposite direction: each re-entrant synthetic char also calls `LLM_Engine_OnKeystroke(_LLM_Bridge_Buffer)` (:284), so the inline burst fires a cascade of fresh predictions and tooltip churn, unlike the Tab path's single clean `_LLM_Bridge_Buffer .= text` with no per-char re-prediction. So a real defect exists on this surface too, but the finding's stated rationale ("buffer not advanced -> stale context") is inaccurate.

NET: bug is real; primary harm is keylogger/metrics PII pollution (local DB only). The buffer-append fix is still directionally correct (route both surfaces through one helper per rule 5.2), but the justification must be corrected.

<a id="f-clipboard-reads-entire-clipboard-into-ram"></a>
#### `clipboard-reads-entire-clipboard-into-ram` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : keylogger-sensors (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_clipboard.ahk:104`
- **Description** : KL_Clip_OnChange computes `Min(StrLen(A_Clipboard), MAX_CHAR_COUNT)`. Reading the A_Clipboard built-in variable forces AHK to materialise the ENTIRE clipboard text into a process string just to take its length, even though only the count is ever stored. The module docstring promises 'the raw clipboard text is never written to any log' but it is still fully read into the keylogger process's address space.
- **Cause racine** : A_Clipboard is a magic variable: any read renders the full CF_UNICODETEXT into memory. There is no length-only path here. OnClipboardChange also bypasses Suspend (see sensors-bypass-pause-suspend), so this read happens even while paused. For very large clipboards this is a multi-MB allocation; for sensitive copies (passwords, tokens) the secret transits the keylogger process memory.
- **Scénario** : User copies a password from their password manager (which is on the privacy exclusion list as the FOREGROUND app, but the copy may complete after focus shifts, or the manager isn't excluded). KL_Clip_OnChange fires and pulls the entire secret into the keylogger process to measure StrLen — a secret-in-memory exposure the 'only the count is stored' comment does not cover.
- **Impact** : Sensitive clipboard contents (passwords/tokens) transit the keylogger process memory; large clipboards cause avoidable multi-MB allocations on the clipboard thread. Privacy posture weaker than documented.
- **Correctif** : Get the length without materialising the text: use the Win32 clipboard API — OpenClipboard/GetClipboardData(CF_UNICODETEXT)/GlobalSize then GlobalUnlock/CloseClipboard via DllCall, computing char count from the buffer size, never assigning A_Clipboard to a variable. Wrap in try (clipboard may be locked by another app). Also gate KL_Clip_OnChange on A_IsSuspended so no clipboard read happens while paused.
- **Test** : headless-unit: extract a pure `_KL_Clip_CharCountFromByteSize(bytes)` helper and assert it converts GlobalSize bytes to wide-char count and caps at MAX_CHAR_COUNT; assert KL_Clip_OnChange never references the A_Clipboard variable (source-text meta scan) so the no-materialise invariant cannot regress.  _(faisabilité : meta-static)_
- **Verdict** : I read the cited code. The factual core is accurate but the severity is overstated.

CONFIRMED FACTS:
- keylogger_clipboard.ahk:104 is exactly `try char_count := Min(StrLen(A_Clipboard), KLClipConst.MAX_CHAR_COUNT)`. Reading the magic `A_Clipboard` variable does force AHK to OpenClipboard/GetClipboardData(CF_UNICODETEXT) and copy the entire text into a freshly-allocated AHK string just to measure its length; only the integer count is then stored (lines 108, 116). So the secret-transits-process-memory and avoidable-large-allocation claims are technically true.
- No length-only path exists anywhere: a Grep for A_Clipboard shows every consumer (adapters/clipboard.ahk CB_Read/CB_Save, hotstring_engine.ahk, healthcheck, gestures) materialises the full text. The adapter offers read/write/save/restore but no length() method, so there is no helper to reuse.
- The Suspend-bypass sub-claim is REAL and verified by contrast: keylogger_hook.ahk gates its callbacks with `if A_IsSuspended` (lines 206, 256) and notes "Dispatch gates on A_IsSuspended" (394), but KL_Clip_OnChange has NO such guard. Its only early-outs are `!Keylogger.initialized` (90) and MF_ShouldFilter (92-94). So while paused, OnClipboardChange still fires and still reads A_Clipboard at 104. KL_AppendLog later also lacks an A_IsSuspended check (618-654), so suspend never reaches this path at all.

WHERE THE FINDING OVERREACHES (severity correction):
1. The docstring promise it cites — "the raw clipboard text is never written to any log" (line 26/24) — is literally STILL TRUE. The text is read into a transient expression operand and freed immediately; it is never persisted. The finding silently upgrades "never logged" to "never read into RAM," a stricter bar the code never claimed. Persisted privacy posture is unchanged.
2. MF_ShouldFilter() (lines 92-94) runs BEFORE the read at 104. If the password manager is the foreground app and is excluded OR detected as a secure field via UIA (MetricsFilters.secure_field -> KL_IsFocusedFieldPassword, metrics_filters.ahk:175-180), the function returns at 94 and A_Clipboard is never touched. The finding's own scenario concedes this and retreats to "copy completes after focus shifts" / "manager not excluded." The focus probe is cached for 250 ms (MF_FOCUS_TTL_MS), so a copy landing just after a focus change can indeed read with a stale-but-still-correct or already-shifted focus — a genuine but narrow window. The exposure is real but conditional, not unconditional.
3. AHK concurrency / lost-keystroke angle (the user's #1 concern): NOT applicable here, and the finding correctly does not claim it. OnClipboardChange runs as its own cooperative pseudo-thread; a multi-MB string copy is sub-millisecond to low-ms, well under the default 300 ms LowLevelHooksTimeout, so it will not drop physical keystrokes. This is purely a privacy/memory-hygiene issue, correctly categorised security-privacy.

Net: the bug is real (avoidable full-clipboard materialisation, including while paused), but it is a defence-in-depth / memory-hygiene improvement, not a documented-promise violation, and the worst-case exposure is gated by MF_ShouldFilter and is a narrow timing window. Medium is too high; low is right.

<a id="f-curl-payload-pii-temp-leak"></a>
#### `curl-payload-pii-temp-leak` — **low** · `confirmed`
- **Unité** : llm-api (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:369`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:371`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:846`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:1068`
- **Description** : _LLM_Ollama_DispatchAsync (the live Ollama prediction path) writes the full JSON payload — which embeds the user's typed buffer/context as the prompt — to A_Temp\ergopti_ollama_<uid>.json via FSWrite, then has curl --data-binary @file read it. On normal completion/timeout/cancel, _LLM_Ollama_CleanupCurlFiles deletes it. But the only call to the orphan sweeper _LLM_Ollama_StreamCleanupOrphans (which deletes ergopti_ollama_*.json/.out older than 60 s) is inside LLM_OllamaGenerate_Streaming (line 846), and streaming is hard-disabled in the engine (prediction_engine.ahk:542 sets streaming_enabled := false for ollama). Therefore the orphan sweep never runs in practice, and any payload file left behind by a hard process kill / power loss / crash persists in A_Temp containing the user's typed text in cleartext.
- **Cause racine** : Cleanup of crash-orphaned temp files is coupled to a code path (streaming) that is currently dead, so the non-streaming hot path that actually creates these PII files has no orphan reaper. Per-request cleanup only covers graceful completion.
- **Scénario** : User is typing a private message; a prediction fires, writing their sentence into A_Temp\ergopti_ollama_42_3.json. AHK is then hard-killed (Task Manager, BSOD, power loss) before _LLM_Ollama_CleanupCurlFiles runs. The file with the user's text remains readable by any process/user with temp access until manual cleanup. Repeated crashes accumulate many such files.
- **Impact** : Leak of potentially sensitive typed content to a predictable, world-readable-ish temp location, with no bounded lifetime. Privacy regression vs. keeping the prompt in memory.
- **Correctif** : Call _LLM_Ollama_StreamCleanupOrphans() (or a shared sweeper) from _LLM_Ollama_DispatchAsync before writing the new payload, and/or run it once at LLM bridge init. Better: write payloads to a dedicated subdir under A_Temp and sweep that dir at startup. Optionally shorten the orphan window for payloads specifically.
- **Test** : headless-unit: create A_Temp\ergopti_ollama_<old>.json with a backdated FileSetTime (>60 s), call the sweeper (invoked from the dispatch path), and assert the stale file is gone while a fresh one is kept. meta-static: assert _LLM_Ollama_DispatchAsync's source contains a call to the orphan-cleanup helper so the reaper can never again be orphaned to a dead code path.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Verified against the real source, not the description.

(1) The active path writes PII: _LLM_Ollama_DispatchAsync (api_ollama.ahk:362) builds the payload from job["full_text"] (the user's typed context/buffer) and writes it via FSWrite to A_Temp\ergopti_ollama_<uid>.json (lines 365-371). Confirmed.

(2) Per-request cleanup only covers graceful exits: _LLM_Ollama_CleanupCurlFiles runs on cancel (550), timeout (561), and normal completion (580) inside _LLM_Ollama_PollCurl. A hard kill / crash / power loss before any of those leaves the file behind. Confirmed.

(3) The orphan sweeper has exactly ONE call site: a project-wide grep for _LLM_Ollama_StreamCleanupOrphans returns only its definition (1068) and a single invocation at line 846, inside LLM_OllamaGenerate_Streaming. There is no init-time / startup call and no other caller.

(4) Streaming is dead for the ollama backend: prediction_engine.ahk:542 unconditionally sets streaming_enabled := false for backend=="ollama", and the only branch that could call LLM_OllamaGenerate_Streaming (prediction_engine.ahk:815) is gated on state["streaming"]. So LLM_OllamaGenerate_Streaming — and therefore _LLM_Ollama_StreamCleanupOrphans — is never invoked in practice. The reaper is orphaned to a dead code path while the live path that actually creates the PII files has no orphan reaper.

Net: stale ergopti_ollama_*.json files containing the user's typed text accumulate after any non-graceful termination and are never cleaned. The finding's mechanism is real and correctly described.

The AHK cooperative-threading model does not apply here — this is not a race/lost-keystroke claim; it is a cleanup-coupling/dead-code defect, so none of the usual false-positive escape hatches (Critical, serialization, A_IsSuspended gating) are relevant.

One factual correction to the finding's impact wording: it calls A_Temp "world-readable-ish". On Windows A_Temp resolves to the per-user %LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp, whose default ACL grants the owning user, SYSTEM, and Administrators — not Everyone. The leak is genuine (unbounded-lifetime cleartext PII on disk, readable by anything running as the same user and by local admins/malware) but the blast radius is local-user, not literally world-readable. That softens the impact slightly without negating it.

Also verified the other two grep hits for ergopti_ollama_ are unrelated: ollama_webview.ahk:234 uses a different prefix (ergopti_ollama_wv_, a WebView2 user-data dir), and the test-file hits are incidental substrings, not a competing sweeper.

<a id="f-ollama-curl-temp-pii-plaintext"></a>
#### `ollama-curl-temp-pii-plaintext` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : e2e-async-background (e2e) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:369`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:371`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:848`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/llm/api_ollama.ahk:1184`
- **Description** : Both the async (_LLM_Ollama_DispatchAsync, api_ollama.ahk:369-371) and streaming (LLM_OllamaGenerate_Streaming, api_ollama.ahk:848-849) paths build the request payload via LLM_BuildOllamaPayload — which embeds the full typed buffer (full_text) and tail_text (the user's actual prose, api_ollama.ahk:1184) — and FSWrite it to `A_Temp\ergopti_ollama_<uid>.json`. A_Temp is a shared, non-ACL-restricted location; the file contains the user's keystrokes in cleartext for the duration of the request (1-3 s typically, up to the 3-min timeout) and is only swept on success/fail/cancel or by the 60 s orphan cleanup after a crash/Reload.
- **Cause racine** : WinHTTP async Send(string) returned HTTP 200 with empty content on this driver (api_ollama.ahk:361 comment), so the dispatch was moved to a curl child reading --data-binary @file. That requires the prompt — including raw user context — to land on disk. There is no encryption and no restrictive ACL on the temp file, and the keylogger's own privacy filters (private browsing / password fields, MF_ShouldFilter) do not gate the LLM context capture, so text the metrics layer would refuse to log can still be written here.
- **Scénario** : User types sensitive text (a password typed into a non-detected field, private message, medical note) with LLM predictions enabled. For each prediction a temp file containing that text exists in %TEMP% readable by any process running as the same user (and, on shared machines with lax temp ACLs, potentially others). A forensic tool / clipboard manager / sync agent watching %TEMP% can capture it; a crash leaves it up to 60 s.
- **Impact** : Transient plaintext exposure of user keystrokes on disk for a privacy-sensitive feature. Low likelihood of exploitation but inconsistent with the keylogger's otherwise-strict privacy posture (secure-field / private-browsing filters).
- **Correctif** : Write the curl payload to a per-instance subdirectory created with restrictive ACLs (or under %LOCALAPPDATA%\Ergopti with user-only access), delete it immediately after curl has opened it rather than waiting for the poll/orphan sweep, and consider gating LLM context capture through MF_ShouldFilter so password/private-browsing windows that the keylogger refuses to record also suppress sending their text to the model. At minimum, document the on-disk exposure and tighten the cleanup to fire as soon as the child has consumed the file.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert the temp payload path used by _LLM_Ollama_DispatchAsync / LLM_OllamaGenerate_Streaming is NOT a bare A_Temp join (e.g. assert it routes through a hardened-dir helper), and a headless-unit test that calls the dir-resolution helper and asserts the returned path is under a per-instance/user-restricted directory rather than the shared A_Temp root.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

### Catégorie : Performance (5)

<a id="f-config-window-delay-write-per-keystroke"></a>
#### `config-window-delay-write-per-keystroke` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : hotstrings-config (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_config_window.ahk:418`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_config_window.ahk:577`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/hotstrings/hotstrings_config_window.ahk:1261`
- **Description** : DelayEdit and PriorityEdit bind OnEvent('Change') to _HCW_OnDelayChanged / _HCW_OnPriorityChanged, each of which calls _HCW_SetOverride → for common/extension entries HotstringsSetOverride → _SaveOverrides (full FileOpen/Write of hotstrings_config.toml), and for personal entries _HCW_PatchTomlMeta (full read + rewrite of the personal TOML). The 'Change' event fires on every character/spin, so typing '2000' into the delay box writes the file four times, and each write also runs HotstringsResolveBumpGen (invalidating the per-keystroke resolve cache).
- **Cause racine** : Edit 'Change' fires per keystroke, but the handler persists immediately with no debounce. _HCW_SetOverride is a synchronous file rewrite; for personal entries _HCW_PatchTomlMeta re-reads and re-serialises the whole file each time.
- **Scénario** : User types a 4-digit delay or a 3-digit priority in the config window: the override file is rewritten on each digit, and the resolve memo is flushed each time. On a personal TOML with many sections, each keystroke re-reads+rewrites the entire file. Runs on the GUI thread, so it manifests as sluggish typing inside the field and unnecessary disk churn, not lost keystrokes.
- **Impact** : Wasteful disk I/O and repeated cache invalidation; minor UI sluggishness while editing numeric fields. No correctness loss, but it amplifies the next finding's hot-path cost (every change bumps the global resolve generation).
- **Correctif** : Debounce the numeric-field persistence: on 'Change', cancel and re-arm a short SetTimer (e.g. -250 ms) that performs the single _HCW_SetOverride/_HCW_LoadCurrent once the user pauses; or persist on focus-loss / Enter instead of per-keystroke. Personal-file patches especially should not run per digit.
- **Test** : headless-unit on the pure layer: wrap _SaveOverrides with a call counter (or assert via _HSResolveGen delta) and simulate the sequence of values a 4-digit entry would produce; assert the persist count is 1 after the debounce window rather than 4. Encodes 'numeric edits coalesce into a single write'.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-parsetomlgroupconfig-missing-file-cache-key"></a>
#### `parsetomlgroupconfig-missing-file-cache-key` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : hotstrings-config (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:478`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/toml/toml_loader.ahk:552`
- **Description** : ParseTomlGroupConfig computes CacheKey := (FilePath != "") ? FilePath : LowerCat (line 462) and on success stores under CacheKey (line 552). But the file-missing branch stores the empty Config under LowerCat (line 479), not CacheKey. When called with an explicit FilePath whose file is absent (HotstringsResolveExt and the config window's __personal__/__ext__ paths), LowerCat is the bare category (often "" for ext lookups), so the next call with the same FilePath misses the cache and re-runs FileExist + the parse attempt.
- **Cause racine** : Copy/paste of the cache-store key: the success path uses CacheKey, the early-return uses LowerCat. The lookup at line 463 keys on CacheKey, so the missing-file entry is unreachable for FilePath-based calls.
- **Scénario** : An extension or personal file referenced in config but missing on disk (renamed/deleted) is resolved repeatedly. HotstringsResolve memoises per (category|section) within a generation, so the cost only surfaces on the first resolve of each key per generation — but the config window's _HCW_TomlDefaults / _HCW_ReadTomlMeta / _HCW_UserOverride call ParseTomlGroupConfig directly (no resolve memo) and will re-stat the missing file on every GUI refresh.
- **Impact** : Redundant FileExist/parse on missing TOMLs; negligible during steady typing (resolve memo absorbs it) but real churn in the config-window refresh loop. Latent correctness trap if a future caller relies on the missing entry being cached under FilePath.
- **Correctif** : Change line 479 to `HotstringGroupConfig[CacheKey] := Config` so the missing-file result is cached under the same key the lookup uses.
- **Test** : headless-unit: call ParseTomlGroupConfig("", "C:\does\not\exist.toml") twice; after a fix, assert HotstringGroupConfig.Has("C:\does\not\exist.toml"). Fails today (cached under ""), passes after.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-loader-toml-injection-readfile-hotpath"></a>
#### `loader-toml-injection-readfile-hotpath` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tap-holds (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/tap_hold/tap_hold_loader.ahk:103`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/tap_hold/tap_hold_loader.ahk:109`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/tap_hold/tap_hold_writer.ahk:284`
- **Description** : _TapHold_ParseFileInto reads the entire tap_hold.toml via ReadTomlFile then loop-parses every line with two RegExMatch calls each. This runs synchronously at boot/reload (not on the per-keystroke path), so it does not drop keystrokes, but there is no upper bound on file size or line count and no try around the parse — a corrupt multi-megabyte file (or one a user hand-edits badly) makes the reload pause. The writer's FileDelete-then-FileAppend (no atomic rename) can also leave a truncated tap_hold.toml if the process is killed between delete and append, which the loader then parses as empty, silently clearing all tap-holds on next boot.
- **Cause racine** : Non-atomic write (FileDelete then FileAppend) plus unbounded parse. The loader's graceful-empty fallback masks a truncated-file failure as 'no config', so a crash mid-write silently disables every tap-hold with no error surfaced to the user.
- **Scénario** : User toggles a tap-hold in the tray (triggering _TH_WriteTapHoldToml), the machine loses power between FileDelete and FileAppend; on next boot tap_hold.toml exists but is empty, the loader returns the empty scaffold, and ALL of the user's tap-holds (CapsLock=Enter, etc.) are silently gone with no warning.
- **Impact** : Silent total loss of tap-hold configuration after an ill-timed crash/power loss during a tray toggle. Low probability but high surprise (the keyboard 'forgot' all its remaps).
- **Correctif** : Write atomically: FileAppend to a temp path then FileMove/rename over the target (rename is atomic on NTFS), so the loader never sees a half-written file. Add a sanity check in LoadTapHoldToml: if FileExist(FilePath) but the parsed result has 0 keys AND inherit_defaults was not explicitly false, log a WARNING rather than silently returning empty (distinguishes 'user disabled everything' from 'file was truncated').
- **Test** : headless-unit (extends tests/test_tap_hold_loader.ahk): write a file containing only the truncated header (no keys, no inherit_defaults=false) and a defaults file with keys; assert LoadTapHoldToml falls back to defaults (or logs a warning) instead of returning an empty keys map — encodes the truncated-write regression.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-klr-builddatabase-debug-fileappend-hot"></a>
#### `klr-builddatabase-debug-fileappend-hot` — **low** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : keylogger-core (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_reader.ahk:116`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_reader.ahk:124`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/modules/keylogger/keylogger_reader.ahk:331`
- **Description** : KLR_BuildDatabase opens with several `try FileAppend(... logPath ...)` calls (lines 116-145) and KLR_ApplyIncremental appends another (line 331). These run on every build — every 5 s ingest tick and every live-push debounce — even in normal operation. Each FileAppend re-opens/closes the log file. No log-level gate.
- **Cause racine** : Leftover unconditional debug instrumentation on a path the rest of the code keeps fast. Each FileAppend is open+write+close — the very cost KL_OpenTodayFh was created to avoid — paid 6+ times per build, and the build can be on the keystroke-thread timer (see ingest-tick-blocks-keyboard-thread).
- **Scénario** : Continuous operation with a dashboard open writes thousands of lines/hour to prefetch.log and pays the NTFS+AV open/close tax on the same thread that can be servicing keystrokes, compounding the stall in the high-severity finding.
- **Impact** : Unbounded log growth and added latency on the rebuild path. Low alone but it amplifies the keyboard-thread blocking finding.
- **Correctif** : Gate every KLR_BuildDatabase / KLR_ApplyIncremental FileAppend behind a debug-level check (route through LoggerDebug which no-ops below threshold), or write them through a single cached FileObject handle like KL_OpenTodayFh rather than re-opening per line.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert KLR_BuildDatabase contains no unconditional FileAppend outside a debug guard (grep that each FileAppend on this path is inside an `if (LoggerLevel >= DEBUG)`-style gate or uses LoggerDebug).  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-wpm-push-unguarded-debug-arg-build"></a>
#### `wpm-push-unguarded-debug-arg-build` — **info** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tooltip-widget (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/lib/metrics/wpm_widget.ahk:237`
- **Description** : WPMWidget_Push (called once per accepted keystroke when the widget is visible, and per replacement char on end-char hotstring fires) calls LoggerDebug("WPMWidget", "Push hs: ...", category, section, ...) on every HS push. LoggerDebug short-circuits internally when DEBUG is off, but the call site still evaluates the four interpolated arguments and builds the variadic args array each time.
- **Cause racine** : logger.ahk:254-258 explicitly documents that high-frequency per-keystroke call sites should gate with 'if LoggerIsDebugEnabled()' to avoid even the arg-array build; the prefix watcher follows this (every DBG line is wrapped in 'if LoggerIsDebugEnabled()'), but this WPMWidget_Push call site does not.
- **Scénario** : Hot path while the widget is visible and many hotstrings fire in a burst; a tiny per-event cost is paid even when DEBUG logging is disabled.
- **Impact** : Negligible measured cost (string concat of a few short values); flagged only as a consistency/perf nit against the project's own documented convention for per-keystroke sites.
- **Correctif** : Wrap line 237 in 'if LoggerIsDebugEnabled()' so the arg array is built only when DEBUG is actually on, matching the prefix-watcher pattern.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert that LoggerDebug call sites inside WPMWidget_Push are guarded by LoggerIsDebugEnabled() (grep-style invariant test in tests/meta), consistent with the existing hot-path-logging convention.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

### Catégorie : Divers (2)

<a id="f-tint-test-stale-constants-comment"></a>
#### `tint-test-stale-constants-comment` — **info** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tooltip-widget (module) · **Fichiers** : `static/ergopti_plus/windows/tests/test_tooltip_tint_contract.ahk:37`, `static/ergopti_plus/windows/tests/test_tooltip_tint_contract.ahk:91`
- **Description** : The header comment (lines 37-39) states the expected vectors were computed at DEFAULT_LIGHTNESS=0.10 / DEFAULT_SATURATION=0.40, but _RunTooltipTintContractTests seeds UI_TINT_LIGHTNESS := 0.13 and UI_TINT_SATURATION := 0.85 (lines 91-92), which is what actually matches the hard-coded ExpectedHex values (e.g. red_accent -> 3D0505).
- **Cause racine** : The seed values were updated to track constants.toml [tint] (0.13/0.85) but the explanatory comment that tells a future maintainer how to regenerate the vectors was not updated, so the documented regeneration parameters are wrong.
- **Scénario** : A maintainer changes the tint algorithm, follows the comment to regenerate vectors at 0.10/0.40, and produces values that no longer match the test's actual 0.13/0.85 seed — wasting time or masking a real drift.
- **Impact** : Documentation-only; no runtime effect. Flagged because the tint contract is a cross-driver invariant and the regeneration instructions must be accurate to keep the guard trustworthy.
- **Correctif** : Update the comment at lines 37-39 to state L=0.13 / S=0.85 (matching the seeds and constants.toml), and ideally derive the seeds from UI_TINT_LIGHTNESS/UI_TINT_SATURATION loaded from the TOML rather than hard-coding, so comment and seed cannot diverge again.
- **Test** : meta-static: assert the seeded UI_TINT_LIGHTNESS/UI_TINT_SATURATION in the test match the values referenced in its own header comment (or are read from constants.toml), so the comment can never drift from the seed.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.

<a id="f-app-picker-t-variable-shadows-i18n"></a>
#### `app-picker-t-variable-shadows-i18n` — **info** · `unverified-low`
- **Unité** : tray-menu (module) · **Fichiers** : `app_picker.ahk:166`, `app_picker.ahk:167`
- **Description** : AppPicker_BuildRows assigns `t := WinGetTitle(...)` (app_picker.ahk:166) inside a function that also calls the global i18n function `t(...)` (e.g. lines 150, 210). In AHK v2 variable and function namespaces are separate so `t(...)` still resolves to the function and this does not currently break, but the collision is a latent footgun: a future refactor that captures `t` into a closure or passes it as a Func, or any tooling that assumes `t` is the i18n callable in this scope, will silently misbehave.
- **Cause racine** : Single-letter local variable name colliding with the project's most-used global function identifier. No functional defect today purely due to AHK v2's separate name resolution for `name(...)` calls vs `name` variable reads.
- **Scénario** : Not user-facing today. Bites a future maintainer who writes `cb := t` (expecting the i18n func) inside this function and gets the window-title string instead.
- **Impact** : Maintainability/latent-bug only; no current runtime effect.
- **Correctif** : Rename the local to `winTitle` (it is already shadowed implicitly): `winTitle := WinGetTitle(...)` and use winTitle in the `if (winTitle = "")` guard. Trivial, removes the footgun.
- **Test** : meta-static: grep app_picker.ahk and assert there is no assignment of the form `\bt\s*:=` (i.e. the i18n identifier is never used as an lvalue) so the shadow can't be reintroduced.  _(faisabilité : headless-unit)_
- **Verdict** : Below verification threshold; carried through as reported.
